# Shadleaf components

> Every Shadleaf component: examples as Thymeleaf source, and attribute tables.

Shadleaf 1.0.1.

## Components

> Every Shadleaf component, in the groups of the sidebar, with one line on what each is for.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/

Every component is a Thymeleaf tag with the `sl:` prefix, rendered on the server. Each page shows examples, the
markup to write, and a table of the component's attributes. Whole pages built from them are in
[Layouts](../layouts/.

### Actions

- [Button](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/button.md): A button, or a link that looks like one.
- [Theme Toggle](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/theme-toggle.md): Switches the page between the light and the dark theme, and remembers the choice in the browser.

### Forms

- [Field](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md): A label, a control, a description and the binding's error messages, wired together from one th:field.
- [Form errors](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/form-errors.md): The errors that belong to the whole form rather than one field, or all of the form's errors, read from the binding and shown above the fields.
- [Label](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/label.md): A label for a form control.
- [Input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md): A text input, bound to a form field with th:field.
- [Input Group](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input-group.md): Text, icons and buttons inside the border of an input or textarea.
- [Textarea](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/textarea.md): A multi-line text input, bound to a form field with th:field.
- [Select](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/select.md): shadcn/ui's Select on a real select element, bound with th:field; the browser's customizable select where it has one.
- [Select (Native)](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/select-native.md): The browser's own select, styled, bound to a form field with th:field.
- [Combobox](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/combobox.md): shadcn/ui's Combobox on a real select element, bound with th:field; type to filter, choose one value or several as chips, and load the options from the server as you type.
- [Checkbox](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/checkbox.md): The browser's own checkbox, styled, bound to a form field with th:field.
- [Radio Group](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/radio-group.md): A set of the browser's own radio buttons, styled, bound to a form field with th:field.
- [Switch](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/switch.md): An on/off control, bound to a boolean form field with th:field.
- [Toggle](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toggle.md): A button that is on or off, as a checkbox drawn as a button, bound to a boolean form field with th:field.
- [Toggle Group](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toggle-group.md): A set of toggles of which one or several can be on, as radio buttons or checkboxes bound with th:field.
- [Calendar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/calendar.md): shadcn/ui's Calendar over native date inputs, bound with th:field; a date or a range of dates.
- [Date Picker](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/date-picker.md): A button showing the chosen date that opens a calendar in a popover; a date or a range, bound with th:field.
- [File Upload](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/file-upload.md): A drop zone with a Browse button and the list of the files picked, around a file input bound with th:field.

### Overlays

- [Dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md): A modal window over the page, on the native dialog element.
- [Alert Dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/alert-dialog.md): A modal dialog that interrupts the user with something important and waits for an answer.
- [Sheet](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sheet.md): A dialog that slides in from an edge of the screen.
- [Popover](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/popover.md): Rich content in a panel next to the button that opens it, on the browser's Popover API.
- [Dropdown Menu](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dropdown-menu.md): A menu of actions or links that opens from a button, on the browser's Popover API.
- [Tooltip](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/tooltip.md): A short text that shows while the pointer rests on an element or the keyboard focuses it.

### Feedback

- [Alert](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/alert.md): A callout for a message the user should notice.
- [Toast](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toast.md): A short message in a corner of the page that closes itself, sent by the server; shadcn/ui's Sonner.
- [Skeleton](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/skeleton.md): A placeholder that pulses while content loads.
- [Spinner](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/spinner.md): A spinning icon that says something is loading.
- [Empty](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/empty.md): What a list, a table, a search or a page shows when there is nothing to show yet.

### Data

- [Table](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/table.md): A table in shadcn/ui's look, whose column headers sort it on the server.
- [Pagination](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/pagination.md): Links to the other pages of a list, built from the Spring Data Page the controller returns.
- [Query params](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/query-params.md): Hidden inputs that keep a table's order, page size and filters when a search form is sent.
- [Month view](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/month-view.md): A month rendered on the server, with content of your own per day and a link per day; no picking.
- [Chart](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/chart.md): shadcn/ui's Chart, Recharts drawing bars, lines and areas from rows the controller puts in the model; labels and colours on the series, named Intl formats.

### Application shell

Start with the [overview](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/application-shell.md): Choosing between a sidebar and a header for the frame around every page, and what every layout needs whichever you pick.

- [Sidebar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md): The application's navigation beside the page, and a panel over it on a phone.
- [Site Header](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/site-header.md): A header bar across the top of the page, or at the top of the sidebar layout's page.
- [User Menu](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/user-menu.md): The signed-in user with a menu that signs out, or a Sign in link, for the sidebar's footer or the site header.
- [Breadcrumb](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/breadcrumb.md): The trail of pages above the one shown now, with a menu for the levels a long trail leaves out.
- [Skip Link](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/skip-link.md): A link past the navigation to the page's content, for keyboard users.

### Disclosure

- [Tabs](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/tabs.md): Panels of content shown one at a time, chosen from a row of tabs.
- [Accordion](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/accordion.md): A stack of sections, each with a heading that shows or hides its content, on the browser's details element.
- [Collapsible](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/collapsible.md): Content that a button shows and hides, on the browser's details element.

### Display

- [Card](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/card.md): A container for related content and actions, built from its parts.
- [Attachment](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/attachment.md): A file or an image attached to a message, a form or a record, with its name, its size or state, and actions.
- [Badge](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/badge.md): A small label for a status, a count or a category.
- [Avatar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/avatar.md): A picture of a person, with initials when there is no picture.
- [Icon](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/icon.md): Inline SVG icons from lucide, or your own.
- [Kbd](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/kbd.md): A key on the keyboard, or a combination of keys.
- [Separator](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/separator.md): A thin line between content.

---

## Button

> A button, or a link that looks like one.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/button/

**Example: Button.**

```html
<sl:button>Save</sl:button>
```

### A button never submits by accident

`<sl:button>` renders `type="button"`, not HTML's default `submit`. A button inside a form therefore does nothing
unless you say so: write `type="submit"` on the one that should submit.

**Example: In a form.** Only the button that says type="submit" submits the form.

```html
<form method="post" th:action="@{/orders}">
  <sl:button type="submit">Save</sl:button>
  <sl:button variant="outline">Preview</sl:button>
</form>
```

### Variants

**Example: Variants.**

```html
<sl:button>Default</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="outline">Outline</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="ghost">Ghost</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="destructive">Destructive</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="secondary">Secondary</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="link">Link</sl:button>
```

### Sizes

**Example: Sizes.**

```html
<sl:button size="xs">Extra small</sl:button>
<sl:button size="sm">Small</sl:button>
<sl:button>Default</sl:button>
<sl:button size="lg">Large</sl:button>
```

The `icon` sizes are square, for a button holding only an icon. Such a button has no text for a screen reader, so it
must have an `aria-label` (or `aria-labelledby`); rendering fails without one.

**Example: Icon-only sizes.** An icon-only button needs an aria-label; rendering fails without one.

```html
<sl:button size="icon-xs" variant="outline" aria-label="Add"><sl:icon name="plus"/></sl:button>
<sl:button size="icon-sm" variant="outline" aria-label="Add"><sl:icon name="plus"/></sl:button>
<sl:button size="icon" variant="outline" aria-label="Add"><sl:icon name="plus"/></sl:button>
<sl:button size="icon-lg" variant="outline" aria-label="Add"><sl:icon name="plus"/></sl:button>
```

#### Hit area on a touch screen

The `icon`, `icon-xs` and `icon-sm` buttons are smaller than the 44 by 44 pixels that touch guidelines ask for. On a
touch screen (`@media (pointer: coarse)`) such a button gets an invisible area around it, so that it is at least 44 by
44 pixels to tap, without looking any bigger: a tap just outside the button still presses it. With a mouse, the hit area
is the button itself. Buttons that touch each other (a row without a gap) only grow in height, so one never takes a tap
from its neighbour, and a button in an `sl:input-group` is not enlarged. `icon-lg` is large enough as it is.

### With an icon

Put an [`<sl:icon>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/icon.md) in the `icon-start` or `icon-end` slot. It takes the button's colour and a 16 pixel size.

**Example: With an icon.**

```html
<sl:button variant="destructive">
  <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="trash"/></sl:slot>
  Delete order
</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="outline">
  Next
  <sl:slot name="icon-end"><sl:icon name="arrow-right"/></sl:slot>
</sl:button>
```

### As a link

`as="a"` renders an anchor that looks the same, for navigation.

**Example: As a link.**

```html
<sl:button as="a" href="/orders" variant="outline">View orders</sl:button>
<sl:button as="a" href="/orders" variant="link">View orders</sl:button>
```

### As a label

`as="label"` renders a `<label>` that looks the same, for a control that is visually hidden: `for` points at it, and
a click on the label activates the control. [`<sl:file-upload>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/file-upload.md)'s Browse button is one, for its file
input. The label takes no focus and no `disabled`; the control does, so draw the focus ring and the disabled look from
the control's state (`:has()`), as the file upload does.

```html
<input type="file" id="photo" name="photo" class="sl-sr-only">
<sl:button as="label" for="photo" variant="outline">Choose a photo</sl:button>
```

### Disabled and loading

A disabled button gets the native `disabled` attribute. A disabled link loses its `href`, leaves the tab order and is
marked `aria-disabled`, so it cannot be followed. `loading` shows a spinner in place of the start icon, announces the
busy state with `aria-busy` and a visually hidden "Loading" label, and disables the button.

**Example: Disabled.**

```html
<sl:button disabled>Save</sl:button>
<sl:button as="a" href="/orders" variant="outline" disabled>View orders</sl:button>
```
**Example: Loading.**

```html
<sl:button loading>Saving</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="outline" loading>
  <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="save"/></sl:slot>
  Save
</sl:button>
<sl:button size="icon" variant="outline" aria-label="Refresh" loading><sl:icon name="refresh-cw"/></sl:button>
```

The "Loading" label comes from the message `sl.button.loading`; define it in your own `messages.properties` to
translate or reword it, as [Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md) explains.

### Props from expressions

Every prop also has a `th:` form that takes a Thymeleaf expression.

**Example: Props from an expression.**

```html
<sl:button th:variant="${order.cancellable ? 'destructive' : 'secondary'}"
           th:text="#{orders.cancel}">Cancel order</sl:button>
```

### Everything else passes through

Attributes that are not props, such as `hx-*`, `x-*`, `aria-*` and `data-*`, land on the rendered element unchanged,
so the button works with htmx and Alpine. `class` is appended to the component's own class.

**Example: Pass-through attributes.** Anything that is not a prop lands on the rendered element; class is appended.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" class="app-wide"
           hx-post="/orders/42/archive" hx-target="#orders"
           data-testid="archive">Archive</sl:button>
```

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:button>`: A button, or with `as="a"` an anchor that looks the same. Every attribute that is not a prop (`hx-*`, `x-*`, `aria-*`, `data-*`, `href`) passes through; `class` is appended to `button`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline`, `ghost`, `destructive`, `secondary`, `link` | `default` | Visual style of the button. |
| `size` | `xs`, `sm`, `default`, `lg`, `icon`, `icon-xs`, `icon-sm`, `icon-lg` | `default` | Size of the button. The `icon` sizes are square, for a button that holds only an icon, and need an `aria-label`. |
| `as` | `button`, `a`, `label` | `button` | Element to render: a `button`, an anchor (`a`) for navigation, or a `label` for a visually hidden control (`for`), as `sl:file-upload`'s Browse button for its file input. A label takes no focus and no `disabled`: the control does. |
| `type` | `button`, `submit`, `reset` | `button` | Submit behaviour. Defaults to `button`, unlike HTML, so a button in a form never submits by accident. |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | Disable the button. A disabled anchor loses its `href` and leaves the tab order. |
| `loading` | *boolean* | — | Show a spinner, announce the busy state and disable the button. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended. An `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` is required when `size` is `icon` or `icon-xs` or `icon-sm` or `icon-lg`; rendering fails without one.

---

## Theme Toggle

> Switches the page between the light and the dark theme, and remembers the choice in the browser.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/theme-toggle/

**Example: Theme toggle.** An icon button opening a menu with Light, Dark and System; the icon shows the page's theme.

```html
<sl:theme-toggle/>
```

### Usage

Put the theme script in the `<head>` of your layout, as [Getting started](https://shadleaf.dev/current/getting-started.md) shows, and the
toggle wherever it belongs, usually the page header:

```html
<head>
  <th:block th:replace="~{sl/layout :: theme-script}"></th:block>
  <th:block th:replace="~{sl/layout :: assets}"></th:block>
</head>
<body>
<header>
  ...
  <sl:theme-toggle/>
</header>
```

The toggle is an icon button that opens a menu with **Light**, **Dark** and **System**. Light and Dark are stored in
the browser (`localStorage`, key `shadleaf-theme`); System removes the key, and the page follows the operating system
again. The theme script reads that key before the first paint, so every page after the choice starts in the right
theme, with no flash of the other one. A dark page prints in the light theme: the script takes the `dark` class off
while the browser prints and puts it back afterwards.

The button shows a sun in the light theme and a moon in the dark one. A screen reader hears something like "Theme, menu
button"; in the menu, "Light, checked, menu item radio, 1 of 3" (the wording differs between screen readers).

### One button

`as="button"` makes it one button that switches between light and dark. It is a toggle button for dark mode: a screen
reader hears "Dark mode, toggle button, pressed" while the page is dark.

**Example: One button.** as="button" switches between light and dark with one press (aria-pressed is on while dark).

```html
<sl:theme-toggle as="button" variant="ghost"/>
```

Pressing it stores light or dark, so once pressed the page no longer follows the operating system. Use the menu where
that matters.

### What the server renders

The server cannot know what the browser stored, so neither the menu's check mark nor the button's pressed state is in
the markup: the toggle's Alpine component sets them when it starts. Nothing shows wrong in the meantime. The icon comes
from the `dark` class the theme script put on `<html>` before the first paint, and the menu is closed until someone
opens it. A toggle that htmx swaps in, or brings back from its history cache, works the same way.

### Several toggles, several tabs

Every toggle on the page shows the same state, and a choice made in another tab of the same site changes this page
too. While the choice is System, the page follows the operating system when it switches between light and dark (at
sunset, say). Each toggle on a page needs its own `id` when it is a menu:

```html
<sl:theme-toggle id="header-theme" variant="ghost"/>
...
<sl:theme-toggle id="footer-theme" side="top"/>
```

### Labels

The labels are messages, which your `messages.properties` can reword or translate (see
[Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md)):

```properties
sl.theme-toggle.label=Theme
sl.theme-toggle.dark-mode=Dark mode
sl.theme-toggle.light=Light
sl.theme-toggle.dark=Dark
sl.theme-toggle.system=System
```

`sl.theme-toggle.label` names the menu's button and `sl.theme-toggle.dark-mode` the single button; an `aria-label` on
`<sl:theme-toggle>` replaces either for one toggle.

There is no tooltip. The sun and the moon are what people look for, and a [tooltip](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/tooltip.md) would only repeat the
button's name to a screen reader, as its description.

### Without JavaScript

Nothing can store a choice without JavaScript, and the theme script cannot run either, so the page stays light. The
toggle is then not shown at all (`@media (scripting: none)`), rather than shown and doing nothing.

### Left out

- **More themes than light and dark.** The theme script and the skins know the `dark` class only.
- **A choice stored on the server** (a user setting). Render `class="dark"` on `<html>` yourself and leave out the
  theme script and the toggle; the toggle stores in the browser only.
- **The keyboard shortcut** shadcn's own site has (D). Your application can add one that clicks the toggle.

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:theme-toggle>`: Switches the page between the light and the dark theme, and remembers the choice in the browser under the key the theme script (`sl/layout :: theme-script`) reads before the first paint, so the next page starts in it. By default an icon button opening a menu with Light, Dark and System; with `as="button"` one button that switches between light and dark. Its icon shows the theme the page is in. Needs Alpine; without JavaScript it is not shown. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through to the button.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `as` | `menu`, `button` | `menu` | `menu`: a menu with Light, Dark and System (`menuitemradio`s). `button`: one toggle button for dark mode (`aria-pressed`), which cannot go back to following the system. |
| `id` | *string* | `theme-toggle` | The menu's id (its button gets the id with `-trigger` added). Two menus on a page need different ids. With `as="button"` it is the button's id, rendered only when set. |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline`, `ghost`, `destructive`, `secondary`, `link` | `outline` | Visual style of the button, as on `sl:button`. |
| `size` | `icon`, `icon-xs`, `icon-sm`, `icon-lg` | `icon` | Size of the button: one of `sl:button`'s icon sizes. |
| `side` | `top`, `right`, `bottom`, `left` | `bottom` | The side of the button the menu opens on, as on `sl:dropdown-menu-content`. |
| `align` | `start`, `center`, `end` | `end` | How the menu lines up with the button along that side, as on `sl:dropdown-menu-content`: `end` lines up the end edges. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:as="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

### JavaScript

A small Alpine component, `slThemeToggle`, does the work: `<sl:theme-toggle>` renders as
`<div class="theme-toggle" x-data="slThemeToggle">`, around a [dropdown menu](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dropdown-menu.md) (whose own
`slDropdownMenu` handles the keyboard) or a button.

- **It needs Alpine**, and no Alpine plugin: it writes the key itself, not with `$persist`, which would store
  `"dark"` as JSON, quotes included, where the theme script expects `dark`. Alpine is included by default; see
  [Alpine](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/alpine.md).
- **Attributes go to the button**, `class` and your own `x-data` included; the wrapper around it has no box.
- **It changes nothing when it starts.** The page is already in the stored theme; a page that sets the `dark` class
  some other way keeps it until someone chooses.

| Event | On | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sl-theme-change` | the document | Fired after the theme changed: by a toggle, in another tab, or by the operating system while the choice is System. The detail is `{theme: 'light' \| 'dark' \| 'system', dark: true \| false}`. For a chart that has to redraw, say. |

---

## Field

> A label, a control, a description and the binding's error messages, wired together from one th:field.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field/

**Example: Field.**

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{username}">
  <sl:field-label>Username</sl:field-label>
  <sl:input autocomplete="username"/>
  <sl:field-description>Letters, digits and dashes.</sl:field-description>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

### One th:field for the whole field

Put `th:field` on `<sl:field>`, not on the control. The field reads the binding and hands it to its parts, so you
write the field once and get:

- the control bound to the field: `name`, value and `id`, as `th:field` on the control would give;
- `<sl:field-label>` with a `for` that points at the control;
- `<sl:field-description>` with an `id`, and the control's `aria-describedby` pointing at it;
- `<sl:field-error>` showing the binding's messages, and the control described by them while there are any;
- `aria-invalid` on the control and `data-invalid` on the field when the binding has errors.

```html
<form th:object="${signup}" th:action="@{/signup}" method="post" novalidate>
  <sl:field th:field="*{email}">
    <sl:field-label>Email</sl:field-label>
    <sl:input type="email"/>
    <sl:field-description>We never share it.</sl:field-description>
    <sl:field-error/>
  </sl:field>
</form>
```

With an error on `email`, that renders:

```html
<div class="field" data-invalid="true">
  <label class="label field-label" for="email">Email</label>
  <input class="input" type="email" aria-invalid="true" id="email"
         aria-describedby="email-description email-error" name="email" value="wim@">
  <p class="field-description" id="email-description">We never share it.</p>
  <div class="field-error" id="email-error">must be a well-formed email address</div>
</div>
```

The parts go in any order. A field holds one control: a second one keeps its own `th:field` and id.

### Errors

`<sl:field-error>` renders nothing while the field has no errors. One message is shown as text, several as a list,
each message once. Content of your own replaces the messages and always shows, so wrap it in a `th:if` when it should
only show with errors.

**Example: With errors.** A second submit renders the form again. The field shows the binding's messages and is marked data-invalid; the input is aria-invalid and described by the message.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{workEmail}">
  <sl:field-label>Work email</sl:field-label>
  <sl:input type="email"/>
  <sl:field-description>Your company address.</sl:field-description>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

**Example: Several errors.**

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{newPassword}">
  <sl:field-label>Password</sl:field-label>
  <sl:input type="password" autocomplete="new-password"/>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

The error has no `role="alert"`. After a submit the whole page loads again, and a screen reader would read out every
error at once. The control is `aria-invalid` and described by its error instead, so the message is read when the user
reaches the control. When htmx swaps the whole form in, `<sl:form-errors autofocus/>` moves focus to the first
control with an error, which reads it out; see [Forms with htmx](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/htmx.md). If you swap a single field in
while focus stays elsewhere, for example to validate it as the user leaves it, add `role="alert"` to its error
yourself.

Errors that belong to the whole form rather than one field, such as `reject(...)` in the controller, go in a
[`<sl:form-errors/>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/form-errors.md) above the fields.

### Ids

The control's id is the field's name, the id `th:field` gives a text input: `email`, or `address.street` for a nested
property. The description and error ids add `-description` and `-error`. Set `for` on the field to use another id:

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{email}" for="work-email">
```

An `aria-describedby` you put on the control is kept, and the field's ids are added after it.

For a checkbox or switch, Spring numbers the id (`terms1`), so that several checkboxes bound to one list field get
different ids. In a field, the checkbox gets the field's name instead, and the label points at it.

### Other controls

A textarea and a native select work as the input does:

**Example: Textarea.**

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{bio}">
  <sl:field-label>Bio</sl:field-label>
  <sl:textarea rows="3"/>
  <sl:field-description>A few words for your profile page.</sl:field-description>
</sl:field>
```

**Example: Native select.**

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{country}">
  <sl:field-label>Country</sl:field-label>
  <sl:native-select>
    <option value="">Select a country</option>
    <option value="be">Belgium</option>
    <option value="nl">Netherlands</option>
  </sl:native-select>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

### Checkbox and switch

`orientation="horizontal"` puts the control first and the label beside it. `<sl:field-content>` stacks the label with
a description or an error.

**Example: Switch.** A horizontal field puts the control first. field-content stacks the label with its description.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{productUpdates}" orientation="horizontal">
  <sl:switch/>
  <sl:field-content>
    <sl:field-label>Product updates</sl:field-label>
    <sl:field-description>One email a month, no more.</sl:field-description>
  </sl:field-content>
</sl:field>
```

**Example: Checkbox.** In a field, the checkbox gets the field's name as its id, not Spring's numbered one, so the label points at it.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{acceptTerms}" orientation="horizontal">
  <sl:checkbox/>
  <sl:field-content>
    <sl:field-label>I accept the terms and conditions</sl:field-label>
    <sl:field-error/>
  </sl:field-content>
</sl:field>
```

### Radio buttons and checkbox groups

A set of options that answers one question is a `<sl:field-set>`, with a `<sl:field-legend>` as its question.
`th:field` goes on the field set, and the options inside share it. A description and an error directly inside the field
set describe the whole group: the `fieldset` gets the `aria-describedby`, while each radio button is still marked
`aria-invalid` from the field.

**Example: Radio group.** The field-set holds the th:field, which its radio buttons share. The description and the error describe the fieldset.

```html
<sl:field-set th:field="*{billingPlan}">
  <sl:field-legend variant="label">Plan</sl:field-legend>
  <sl:field-description>You can change it at any time.</sl:field-description>
  <sl:radio-group>
    <sl:label><sl:radio-group-item value="monthly"/>Monthly</sl:label>
    <sl:label><sl:radio-group-item value="yearly"/>Yearly</sl:label>
  </sl:radio-group>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field-set>
```

For checkboxes, make each option a horizontal field without a `th:field` of its own. It takes the field set's, and the
next of Spring's numbered ids (`toppings1`, `toppings2`), which its label points at.

**Example: Checkbox group.** Each option is a field without th:field of its own. It takes the field-set's, with the next of Spring's numbered ids.

```html
<sl:field-set th:field="*{toppings}">
  <sl:field-legend variant="label">Toppings</sl:field-legend>
  <sl:field orientation="horizontal">
    <sl:checkbox value="cheese"/>
    <sl:field-label>Cheese</sl:field-label>
  </sl:field>
  <sl:field orientation="horizontal">
    <sl:checkbox value="olives"/>
    <sl:field-label>Olives</sl:field-label>
  </sl:field>
  <sl:field orientation="horizontal">
    <sl:checkbox value="basil"/>
    <sl:field-label>Basil</sl:field-label>
  </sl:field>
</sl:field-set>
```

`variant="label"` on the legend gives it the size of a field label; the default is a section heading.

### Layout

`<sl:field-group>` stacks fields with even spacing. A field set without `th:field` groups related fields under a
heading.

**Example: Field set and field group.**

```html
<sl:field-set>
  <sl:field-legend>Shipping address</sl:field-legend>
  <sl:field-description>Where we deliver your order.</sl:field-description>
  <sl:field-group>
    <sl:field th:field="*{street}">
      <sl:field-label>Street</sl:field-label>
      <sl:input autocomplete="street-address"/>
    </sl:field>
    <sl:field th:field="*{city}">
      <sl:field-label>City</sl:field-label>
      <sl:input autocomplete="address-level2"/>
    </sl:field>
  </sl:field-group>
</sl:field-set>
```

`orientation="responsive"` puts the label and the control side by side when the enclosing field group is at least
28rem wide, and stacks them when it is narrower.

**Example: Responsive.** Side by side in a wide field-group, stacked in a narrow one.

```html
<sl:field-group>
  <sl:field th:field="*{displayName}" orientation="responsive">
    <sl:field-content>
      <sl:field-label>Display name</sl:field-label>
      <sl:field-description>Shown next to your comments.</sl:field-description>
    </sl:field-content>
    <sl:input/>
  </sl:field>
</sl:field-group>
```

### Disabled and read-only

A field notices a disabled control inside it and fades its label: there is no `data-disabled` to set, as there is in
shadcn. The description keeps its colour, since it often says why the control is disabled.

**Example: Disabled.** The label fades with a disabled control, found by the field itself; the description stays legible, as it often says why.

```html
<sl:field-group>
  <sl:field th:field="*{companyName}">
    <sl:field-label>Company name</sl:field-label>
    <sl:input disabled/>
    <sl:field-description>Only an administrator can change it.</sl:field-description>
  </sl:field>
  <sl:field th:field="*{twoFactor}" orientation="horizontal">
    <sl:switch disabled/>
    <sl:field-content>
      <sl:field-label>Two-factor authentication</sl:field-label>
      <sl:field-description>Required by your organisation.</sl:field-description>
    </sl:field-content>
  </sl:field>
</sl:field-group>
```

`disabled` on a `<sl:field-set>` disables every control in it, as it does on any `fieldset`. The legend and the
options' labels fade with them.

**Example: Disabled field set.** disabled on the field set disables every control in it, as on a fieldset; the legend and labels fade with them.

```html
<sl:field-set th:field="*{billingCycle}" disabled>
  <sl:field-legend variant="label">Billing cycle</sl:field-legend>
  <sl:field-description>Fixed until your contract ends.</sl:field-description>
  <sl:radio-group>
    <sl:label><sl:radio-group-item value="monthly"/>Monthly</sl:label>
    <sl:label><sl:radio-group-item value="yearly"/>Yearly</sl:label>
  </sl:radio-group>
</sl:field-set>
```

A read-only control changes nothing in the field: its label stays as it is. See the [input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md#read-only) for
how it looks.

**Example: Read-only.** A read-only input keeps its label as it is. It still takes focus, and its value is submitted.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{accountNumber}">
  <sl:field-label>Account number</sl:field-label>
  <sl:input readonly/>
  <sl:field-description>Quote it when you contact us.</sl:field-description>
</sl:field>
```

A disabled control is not submitted, so an error on it usually comes from something the user cannot change in this
form. It still shows: the control keeps its destructive border, faded, next to the field's error. With
`<sl:form-errors autofocus/>`, a control with a `disabled` attribute is passed over and the next control with errors
takes the focus; one disabled by `th:disabled` or by a disabled field set is not known to be disabled when the page is
rendered, and then nothing takes the focus.

### Without th:field

A field without `th:field` is only layout. Set `for` to give the control its id, the label its `for`, and the
description and error their ids. Without a binding, `data-invalid` and `aria-invalid` are yours to set.

**Example: Without th:field.** for gives the control its id, and the description and error their ids.

```html
<sl:field for="field-search">
  <sl:field-label>Search orders</sl:field-label>
  <sl:input type="search" name="q"/>
  <sl:field-description>An order number or a customer name.</sl:field-description>
</sl:field>
```

### How the parts find the field

`<sl:field>` renders its content with a local variable, `slField`, that holds the `th:field`, the ids and the
messages. Every part and control inside it reads that variable, which is why nothing has to be repeated. To know
whether it has a description or an error, the field looks at its content as written: a part added with `th:replace` is
not seen, and a part with a `th:if` that is false still counts, so the control would point at a description that is
not there.

### Attributes

#### sl:field

Attributes of `<sl:field>`: A form field: a `sl:field-label`, one control, and optionally a `sl:field-description` and a `sl:field-error`, in any order. Put `th:field` on the field, not on the control: the control takes it, gets the `id` the label points at (the field's name, `email`, also for a checkbox or switch), and an `aria-describedby` listing the description and, when the binding has errors, the error. The error shows the binding's messages. A field with errors gets `data-invalid`. Without `th:field`, set `for` to the control's id.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `orientation` | `vertical`, `horizontal`, `responsive` | `vertical` | Stack the label above the control, or put them side by side (a checkbox or switch, then its label). `responsive` stacks them in a narrow `sl:field-group` and puts them side by side in a wide one. |
| `for` | *string* | — | The id of the control: for a field without `th:field`, or to use another id than the field's name. The control gets it when it has no id of its own, and the description and error ids derive from it. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:orientation="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:field-label

Attributes of `<sl:field-label>`: The label of a `sl:field`. Its `for` points at the field's control; a `for` of your own wins.

`sl:field-label` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:field-description

Attributes of `<sl:field-description>`: Help text for a `sl:field` or `sl:field-set`. It gets the id the control's (or fieldset's) `aria-describedby` points at.

`sl:field-description` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:field-error

Attributes of `<sl:field-error>`: The error messages of a `sl:field` or `sl:field-set`, read from its `th:field`'s binding: one message as text, several as a list. It renders nothing while there are none. Content of its own replaces the messages, and always shows. It gets the id the control's (or fieldset's) `aria-describedby` points at while it shows messages.

`sl:field-error` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:field-content

Attributes of `<sl:field-content>`: Stacks a `sl:field-label` with its `sl:field-description` or `sl:field-error`, beside the control of a horizontal `sl:field`.

`sl:field-content` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:field-set

Attributes of `<sl:field-set>`: A group of related fields or options under a `sl:field-legend`, as a `fieldset`. With `th:field`, the options inside share it: radio buttons in a `sl:radio-group`, or checkboxes each in a `sl:field` of their own (which then need no `th:field` and get Spring's numbered ids, `toppings1`). A `sl:field-description` and `sl:field-error` directly inside describe the fieldset, and it gets `data-invalid` when the binding has errors.

`sl:field-set` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:field-legend

Attributes of `<sl:field-legend>`: The caption of a `sl:field-set`, as its `legend`. Put it first in the fieldset.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `legend`, `label` | `legend` | A section heading, or the size of a field label (for a set of options that answers one question). |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:field-group

Attributes of `<sl:field-group>`: Stacks `sl:field`s and `sl:field-set`s with even spacing. Fields with `orientation="responsive"` inside it go side by side when it is wide enough.

`sl:field-group` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Form errors

> The errors that belong to the whole form rather than one field, or all of the form's errors, read from the binding and shown above the fields.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/form-errors/

**Example: Form errors.** The binding has a global error, from a class-level constraint or reject(...) in the controller. It shows above the fields (here first in the field group, which spaces it like a field); the field errors stay with their fields.

```html
<sl:field-group>
  <sl:form-errors/>
  <sl:field th:field="*{signupPassword}">
    <sl:field-label>Password</sl:field-label>
    <sl:input type="password" autocomplete="new-password"/>
  </sl:field>
  <sl:field th:field="*{signupRepeat}">
    <sl:field-label>Repeat the password</sl:field-label>
    <sl:input type="password" autocomplete="new-password"/>
  </sl:field>
</sl:field-group>
```

### Errors that belong to the form

Some errors are about no single field: two passwords that do not match, a date range that ends before it starts, or a
service that refused the request. Spring keeps them in the `BindingResult` as global errors. `<sl:form-errors/>`
shows them as a destructive [alert](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/alert.md), and renders nothing while there are none:

```html
<form th:object="${signup}" th:action="@{/signup}" method="post" novalidate>
  <sl:field-group>
    <sl:form-errors/>
    <sl:field th:field="*{password}">...</sl:field>
    <sl:field th:field="*{repeatPassword}">...</sl:field>
  </sl:field-group>
</form>
```

With one global error, that renders:

```html
<div class="form-errors alert" data-variant="destructive" tabindex="-1" id="signup-errors">
  <svg class="icon" aria-hidden="true" ...>...</svg>
  <div class="alert-title">There is a problem</div>
  <div class="alert-description">The passwords do not match.</div>
</div>
```

As the first thing in a `<sl:field-group>`, it gets the same space as a field. One message is shown as text, several
as a list, each message once. By default field errors are not repeated: they stay with
their [fields](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md). To list them too, see [All errors](#all-errors).

**Example: Several errors.**

```html
<sl:form-errors id="order-errors"/>
```

It must be inside the element with `th:object`: that is the form object whose errors it reads. Outside one, rendering
fails with a message that says so.

### Where global errors come from

Anything that calls `reject(...)` on the `BindingResult` instead of `rejectValue(field, ...)`:

- **The controller**, for a rule that needs a service or a second look at the form:

  ```java
  @PostMapping("/signup")
  String signup(@Valid @ModelAttribute("signup") SignupForm signup, BindingResult bindingResult) {
    if (accounts.isBlocked(signup.getEmail())) {
      bindingResult.reject("signup.blocked", "We cannot create an account for this address.");
    }
    if (bindingResult.hasErrors()) {
      return "signup";
    }
    ...
  }
  ```

  The code (`signup.blocked`) is looked up in your `messages.properties` first; the second argument is the default.

- **A class-level constraint**: a Bean Validation annotation on the form class, such as a `@PasswordsMatch` of your
  own. Its violation has no property path, so Spring adds it as a global error.

A failure the controller catches and does not want in the binding can go in as content of its own (below).

### Title

The title defaults to the message `sl.form-errors.title`, "There is a problem". Define that key in your own
`messages.properties` to reword or [translate](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md) it for every form, or set `title` (or `th:title="#{...}"`) on one.
`level` makes the title a heading that screen reader users can jump to.

**Example: A title of its own.** title replaces the default title (the message sl.form-errors.title); level makes it a heading.

```html
<sl:form-errors id="transfer-errors" title="The transfer was not made" level="2"/>
```

### Content of your own

Content of your own replaces the messages and always shows, so wrap it in a `th:if` when it should only show
sometimes.

**Example: Content of its own.** Content of its own replaces the messages and always shows, for an error the controller does not put in the binding.

```html
<sl:form-errors id="payment-errors">
  <p>The payment service did not answer. Your card was not charged.</p>
  <p>Try again in a minute.</p>
</sl:form-errors>
```

### Announcement and focus

`<sl:form-errors>` is not announced, and takes focus only when you ask for it with `autofocus`.

- **No `role="alert"`.** After a submit the whole page loads again. A live region that is there when the page loads
  is read out by some screen readers and ignored by others, and when it is read, it talks over the page itself. The
  [field errors](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md#errors) leave out `role="alert"` for the same reason. When focus moves to the problem, as
  below, the screen reader reads it there, and a live region would only say it a second time.
- **No `autofocus` by default.** Moving focus on load skips the reader past the page's heading, so it fits a form
  that is the page's only task, or one that htmx swaps in, where the submit button that had the focus is gone. It is
  your call per form.

With `autofocus`, focus goes to the problem when the form comes back with errors:

```html
<form th:object="${signup}" th:action="@{/signup}" method="post" novalidate>
  <sl:form-errors autofocus/>
  ...
</form>
```

| The form comes back with | Focus goes to |
| --- | --- |
| a global error (or, with `show="all"`, any error) | the summary, which is read out; <kbd>Tab</kbd> continues into the form |
| field errors only | the first control with an error, in the order of the form: its label, its invalid state and its error are read out through `aria-describedby` |
| no errors | nowhere: the first, empty form keeps the focus where it was |

Content of your own always shows, so with it the summary takes the focus. The summary decides before the controls
below it render, so keep it above the fields, where it belongs anyway. It works on any control with a `th:field` in
the form, inside a field or not. A control with a `disabled` attribute cannot take focus, so it is passed over (see
[disabled and read-only fields](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md#disabled-and-read-only)); a read-only one takes it.

For a form that reloads the page, also put the outcome in the page title, such as `Error: Sign up`: it is the first
thing a screen reader reads after a page load.

htmx honours `autofocus` in the content it swaps in, after its own attempt to keep focus on the element that had it.
The [htmx guide](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/htmx.md) shows a form that posts with htmx and falls back to a plain POST.

### Id

The summary's id is the form object's name followed by `-errors`: `signup-errors` for `th:object="${signup}"`. Set
`id` to use another one, for instance when two forms on a page bind objects of the same name. The id is there for
a link (`<a href="#signup-errors">`) or a script. The form's `aria-describedby` does not point at it:
screen readers read a form's description, if at all, when the user enters the form, which is not where anyone is
after a submit.

### All errors

`show="all"` lists the field errors too, after the global ones, in the order they were rejected. Each field still
shows its own error next to its control. A message is listed once per field, so two fields that both say "must not be
blank" both appear.

**Example: All errors.** show="all" lists the field errors too, after the global ones. Each field still shows its own error.

```html
<sl:field-group>
  <sl:form-errors id="account-errors" show="all"/>
  <sl:field th:field="*{accountName}">
    <sl:field-label>Name</sl:field-label>
    <sl:input autocomplete="name"/>
    <sl:field-error/>
  </sl:field>
  <sl:field th:field="*{accountEmail}">
    <sl:field-label>Email</sl:field-label>
    <sl:input type="email" autocomplete="email"/>
    <sl:field-error/>
  </sl:field>
</sl:field-group>
```

The messages are listed as the binding has them, without the field's label, so write them to make sense on their own:
"Enter your name" rather than "must not be blank". Spring looks up a message code per field before the constraint's
default, so your `messages.properties` can do that without touching the form class:

```properties
NotBlank.contactForm.name=Enter your name.
Email.contactForm.email=Enter an email address like you@example.com.
```

A title with the number of errors takes `#fields` from your own `messages.properties`; the component's attributes are
evaluated inside your `th:object`:

```html
<sl:form-errors show="all" th:title="#{errors.summary.title(${#fields.errors().size()})}"/>
```

```properties
errors.summary.title=There were {0} errors with your submission
```

With every error in it, the summary is where a keyboard or screen reader user can take stock after a submit, so with
`autofocus` it takes the focus whenever the form has errors.

The messages are not links to their controls (GOV.UK's summary has those), at least for now:

- The links need the controls' ids, which the fields decide as they render, below the summary. Radio buttons and
  checkbox groups have numbered ids (`replyBy1`), so the field's name is not always an id to link to.
- Each field error is already next to its control, and read out with it through `aria-describedby`.
- Without `show="all"`, `autofocus` takes the user straight to the first field with an error, which is what a link
  from the summary would do.

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:form-errors>`: The errors of the form object the enclosing `th:object` selects, shown as a destructive alert: by default the errors that belong to the whole form rather than one field (global errors, from `bindingResult.reject(...)` or a class-level constraint); with `show="all"` the field errors too. One message as text, several as a list. It renders nothing while there are none. Content of its own replaces the messages, and always shows. Not announced, and not focused unless it has `autofocus`; it has an id (`signup-errors` for `th:object="${signup}"`) and `tabindex="-1"`, so a link or a script can move focus to it too.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `show` | `global`, `all` | `global` | Which errors to list: the global errors only, or all of them, the global ones first and then every field's (each field keeps its own error too). Field messages are listed as they are, without the field's label, so write them to make sense on their own. |
| `title` | *string* | — | The title above the messages. Defaults to the message `sl.form-errors.title` ("There is a problem"); use `th:title` for a message of your own. |
| `level` | `1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5`, `6` | — | Heading level of the title. With it the title is a heading (`role="heading"` and `aria-level`) that screen reader users can jump to; without it, plain text. |
| `autofocus` | *boolean* | — | Move focus to the problem when the form comes back with errors: to the summary when it shows, otherwise to the first control with errors below it (in a field or with a `th:field` of its own). Nothing takes focus while the form has no errors. htmx honours it in swapped content too. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:show="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Label

> A label for a form control.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/label/

**Example: Label.**

```html
<sl:label for="label-email">Email</sl:label>
<sl:input type="email" id="label-email" placeholder="you@example.com"/>
```

### Pointing at a control

Give the label a `for` with the control's `id`. A control with `th:field="*{email}"` gets `id="email"` from Spring, so
`for="email"` matches it without writing the `id` yourself. Clicking the label focuses the control, and a screen
reader announces the label's text as the control's name.

A label can also hold its control, and then needs no `for`:

**Example: Around its control.** A label that holds its control needs no for.

```html
<sl:label><input type="checkbox" name="newsletter"> Send me the newsletter</sl:label>
```

In a [field](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md), use `<sl:field-label>`: it points at the field's control by itself.

### Disabled

A label fades with a disabled control it holds, or one right before or after it, including a control disabled by a
`<fieldset disabled>` around it. There is nothing to add to the label.

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:label>`: A label for a form control. Point it at the control with `for` (the control's `id`; `th:field` gives the control an `id` equal to the field name), or put the control inside it.

`sl:label` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Input

> A text input, bound to a form field with th:field.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input/

**Example: Input.**

```html
<sl:label for="email">Email</sl:label>
<sl:input type="email" th:field="*{email}" placeholder="you@example.com"/>
```

### Binding to a form field

Inside a form with `th:object`, write `th:field` as on a plain `<input>`. Spring sets the input's `id`, `name` and
`value`, so a [label](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/label.md) with `for` set to the field name points at it:

```html
<form th:object="${profile}" th:action="@{/profile}" method="post">
  <sl:label for="email">Email</sl:label>
  <sl:input type="email" th:field="*{email}"/>
</form>
```

An explicit `id` wins over the one `th:field` gives. In a [field](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md), put `th:field` on the field instead: it
adds the label, description and error messages and wires them to the input.

### Types

`type` defaults to `text` and takes the text-like input types. Checkboxes, radio buttons and switches get components of
their own.

**Example: Types.**

```html
<sl:label for="input-password">Password</sl:label>
<sl:input type="password" id="input-password" name="password"/>
<sl:label for="input-date">Delivery date</sl:label>
<sl:input type="date" id="input-date" name="deliveryDate"/>
<sl:label for="input-file">Invoice</sl:label>
<sl:input type="file" id="input-file" name="invoice"/>
```

### Invalid

When the field has errors after a `@Valid` submit, the input marks itself `aria-invalid="true"`, which the skins draw
in the destructive colour. A [field](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md) shows the error message and links it with `aria-describedby`; outside
one, an `aria-describedby` you write passes through.

**Example: Invalid.** With th:field, a field that has errors marks the input aria-invalid by itself.

```html
<sl:label for="email">Email</sl:label>
<sl:input type="email" th:field="*{email}"/>
```

Without `th:field`, write `aria-invalid="true"` yourself.

### Disabled

A disabled input fades and cannot be focused, as in shadcn; the lyra and nova skins also give it a grey background. Its
value is not submitted, and its label fades with it. In Windows' high-contrast mode it turns the system's grey text
colour instead of fading.

**Example: Disabled.**

```html
<sl:label for="input-disabled">Customer number</sl:label>
<sl:input id="input-disabled" name="customerNumber" value="C-1042" disabled/>
```

### Read-only

`readonly` keeps the value but stops the user from changing it. Unlike a disabled input, a read-only one still takes
focus, its value can be selected and copied, and it is submitted with the form: use it for a value the user needs to
see or quote, such as a reference number. It gets a muted background and loses its shadow, but keeps its full text
colour, so it reads differently from a disabled input and its text keeps its contrast (a read-only input is not
exempt from WCAG's contrast minimum, a disabled one is). Errors still show: the destructive border and ring win over
the read-only look. shadcn has no read-only style.

**Example: Read-only.** The value cannot be changed, but it can be focused, selected and copied, and it is submitted.

```html
<sl:label for="input-reference">Order reference</sl:label>
<sl:input id="input-reference" name="reference" value="ORD-2026-0042" readonly/>
```

### With htmx

Every attribute that is not a prop lands on the `<input>`:

**Example: With htmx.** Every attribute that is not a prop lands on the input.

```html
<sl:input type="search" name="q" aria-label="Search orders"
          th:hx-get="@{/orders}" hx-trigger="input changed delay:300ms" hx-target="#orders"/>
```

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:input>`: A text input. Every attribute that is not a prop (`th:field`, `name`, `placeholder`, `required`, `hx-*`) passes through; `class` is appended to `input`. With `th:field`, the input is marked `aria-invalid` when its field has errors. In a `sl:field`, it takes the field's `th:field`, id and `aria-describedby`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `type` | `text`, `email`, `password`, `number`, `search`, `tel`, `url`, `date`, `datetime-local`, `month`, `week`, `time`, `file` | `text` | Type of the input. Checkboxes and radio buttons have components of their own. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:type="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Input Group

> Text, icons and buttons inside the border of an input or textarea.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input-group/

**Example: Input group.**

```html
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input type="search" name="q" placeholder="Search..." aria-label="Search"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon><sl:icon name="search"/></sl:input-group-addon>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end">12 results</sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
```

### Composition

```text
sl:input-group
├── sl:input-group-input or sl:input-group-textarea
└── sl:input-group-addon
    ├── sl:input-group-text
    ├── sl:input-group-button
    ├── sl:icon
    └── sl:kbd
```

The group draws the border, and its control has none of its own: the group shows the control's focus ring, its error
and its read-only look. A disabled control fades the whole group. A click on an addon, outside a button or link in it,
focuses the control.

### Align

Write the addons **after** the control, so the control comes first in the tab order and is read before them; `align`
on the addon places it. `inline-start`, the default, puts it before the control on its line:

**Example: Inline start.** The default align. The addon is written after the input, so the input comes first in the tab order.

```html
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input name="query" placeholder="Search..." aria-label="Search"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon><sl:icon name="search"/></sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
```

#### inline-end

**Example: Inline end.**

```html
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input type="password" name="apiKey" placeholder="Enter password" aria-label="Password"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end"><sl:icon name="eye-off"/></sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
```

#### block-start

`block-start` puts the addon above the control, across the whole group. `border` draws a line between the addon and
the control. In such a row an `sl:input-group-text` takes the free space, so what follows it sits at the end.

**Example: Block start.** Above the input, across the whole group. border draws a line between the addon and the control.

```html
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input id="input-group-full-name" name="fullName" placeholder="Jane Doe"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="block-start">
    <sl:label for="input-group-full-name">Full name</sl:label>
  </sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-textarea name="script" rows="3" aria-label="Script">console.log('Hello, world!');</sl:input-group-textarea>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="block-start" border>
    <sl:input-group-text><sl:icon name="file-code"/> script.js</sl:input-group-text>
    <sl:input-group-button size="icon-xs" aria-label="Copy"><sl:icon name="copy"/></sl:input-group-button>
  </sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
```

#### block-end

**Example: Block end.** Below the input, across the whole group.

```html
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input name="price" placeholder="Enter amount" aria-label="Amount"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="block-end">
    <sl:input-group-text>USD</sl:input-group-text>
  </sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-textarea name="comment" rows="3" placeholder="Write a comment..." aria-label="Comment"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="block-end" border>
    <sl:input-group-text>0/280 characters</sl:input-group-text>
    <sl:input-group-button variant="default" size="sm" type="submit">Post</sl:input-group-button>
  </sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
```

### Text

`sl:input-group-text` holds muted text: a currency, a unit, a domain. The input still needs a name of its own, as the
text is not its label: `aria-label="Amount in US dollars"`.

**Example: Text.** A currency, a unit or a domain before or after the input.

```html
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input type="number" name="amount" placeholder="0.00" aria-label="Amount in US dollars"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon><sl:input-group-text>$</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end"><sl:input-group-text>USD</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input name="website" placeholder="example" aria-label="Website"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon><sl:input-group-text>https://</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end"><sl:input-group-text>.com</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input type="number" name="weight" aria-label="Weight in kilograms"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end"><sl:input-group-text>kg</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
```

### Button

`sl:input-group-button` is an `sl:button`, ghost and `xs` by default, with sizes that fit inside the group: `xs`,
`sm`, `icon-xs` and `icon-sm`. An icon button needs an `aria-label`. As every `sl:button`, it does not submit unless
you write `type="submit"`.

**Example: Button.** An icon button needs an aria-label.

```html
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input name="shareUrl" value="https://shadleaf.dev" readonly aria-label="Link"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end">
    <sl:input-group-button size="icon-xs" aria-label="Copy"><sl:icon name="copy"/></sl:input-group-button>
  </sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input type="search" name="docsSearch" placeholder="Type to search..." aria-label="Search the docs"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end">
    <sl:input-group-button variant="secondary" type="submit">Search</sl:input-group-button>
  </sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
```

### Kbd

The key is decoration: say the shortcut with `aria-keyshortcuts` on the input, and hide the addon from screen readers.

**Example: Kbd.**

```html
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input type="search" name="commandSearch" placeholder="Search..." aria-label="Search"
                        aria-keyshortcuts="Control+K"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon><sl:icon name="search"/></sl:input-group-addon>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end" aria-hidden="true"><sl:kbd>⌘K</sl:kbd></sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
```

### Loading

`loading` on the button shows a spinner, disables it and announces the busy state.

**Example: Loading.** A loading button shows a spinner, announces the busy state and is disabled.

```html
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input name="username" value="wim" aria-label="Username"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end">
    <sl:input-group-button loading>Check</sl:input-group-button>
  </sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
```

### In a field

Put `th:field` on the [field](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md), as for an [input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md). The `sl:input-group-input` (or
`sl:input-group-textarea`) inside the group takes it: Spring sets its `id`, `name` and value, the label points at it,
and it is described by the field's description and error.

```html
<form th:object="${order}" th:action="@{/orders}" method="post">
  <sl:field th:field="*{budget}">
    <sl:field-label>Budget</sl:field-label>
    <sl:input-group>
      <sl:input-group-input type="number" placeholder="0.00"/>
      <sl:input-group-addon><sl:input-group-text>€</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
      <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end"><sl:input-group-text>EUR</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
    </sl:input-group>
    <sl:field-description>Excluding VAT.</sl:field-description>
    <sl:field-error/>
  </sl:field>
</form>
```

**Example: In a field.** Put th:field on the field. The input in the group takes it, and the label, description and error point at the input.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{budget}">
  <sl:field-label>Budget</sl:field-label>
  <sl:input-group>
    <sl:input-group-input type="number" placeholder="0.00"/>
    <sl:input-group-addon><sl:input-group-text>€</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
    <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end"><sl:input-group-text>EUR</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
  </sl:input-group>
  <sl:field-description>Excluding VAT.</sl:field-description>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

Outside a field, write `th:field` on the `sl:input-group-input` itself.

### Invalid

When the field has errors, the input marks itself `aria-invalid="true"`, and the group draws the destructive border
and ring.

**Example: Invalid.** With th:field, a field that has errors marks the input aria-invalid, and the group draws the error.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{packageWeight}">
  <sl:field-label>Weight</sl:field-label>
  <sl:input-group>
    <sl:input-group-input type="number"/>
    <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end"><sl:input-group-text>kg</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
  </sl:input-group>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

### Disabled

**Example: Disabled.** A disabled input fades the whole group.

```html
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input name="discount" value="10" disabled aria-label="Discount"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end"><sl:input-group-text>%</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
```

### Read-only

As a read-only [input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md#read-only): the group gets a muted background, and the value can still be focused,
selected and copied.

**Example: Read-only.**

```html
<sl:input-group>
  <sl:input-group-input name="volume" value="1.5" readonly aria-label="Volume"/>
  <sl:input-group-addon align="inline-end"><sl:input-group-text>l</sl:input-group-text></sl:input-group-addon>
</sl:input-group>
```

### Attributes

#### sl:input-group

Attributes of `<sl:input-group>`: An input with text, icons or buttons inside its border: one `sl:input-group-input` or `sl:input-group-textarea`, and `sl:input-group-addon`s around it. Put the addons after the control in the markup, so the control comes first in the tab order and is read first; `align` on the addon places it. The group draws the control's focus ring, error and disabled look. In a `sl:field`, the control takes the field's `th:field`, id and `aria-describedby`.

`sl:input-group` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:input-group-addon

Attributes of `<sl:input-group-addon>`: Text, an icon, a `sl:kbd` or `sl:input-group-button`s beside or around the control of a `sl:input-group`. Clicking it, outside a button or link, focuses the control. Write it after the control: `align` places it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `align` | `inline-start`, `inline-end`, `block-start`, `block-end` | `inline-start` | Where the addon goes: before or after the control on its line, or above or below it across the whole group. |
| `border` | *boolean* | — | With `align` `block-start` or `block-end`, a dividing line between the addon and the control, with matching spacing. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:align="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:input-group-text

Attributes of `<sl:input-group-text>`: Muted text in a `sl:input-group-addon`: a currency symbol, a unit, a domain. It may hold an `sl:icon`. In an addon above or below the control, it takes the free space, so what follows it sits at the end.

`sl:input-group-text` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:input-group-button

Attributes of `<sl:input-group-button>`: A button in a `sl:input-group-addon`: a `sl:button`, ghost and extra small by default, sized to fit inside the group. Any other attribute passes through to the `sl:button` (`type`, `loading`, `disabled`, `hx-*`).

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline`, `ghost`, `destructive`, `secondary`, `link` | `ghost` | Visual style of the button, as on `sl:button`. |
| `size` | `xs`, `sm`, `icon-xs`, `icon-sm` | `xs` | Size of the button. The `icon` sizes are square, for a button that holds only an icon, and need an `aria-label`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended. An `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` is required when `size` is `icon-xs` or `icon-sm`; rendering fails without one.

#### sl:input-group-input

Attributes of `<sl:input-group-input>`: The input of a `sl:input-group`: a `sl:input` without a border of its own, as the group draws it. Every attribute that is not a prop (`th:field`, `name`, `placeholder`, `required`, `hx-*`) passes through; `class` is appended to `input`. With `th:field`, the input is marked `aria-invalid` when its field has errors. In a `sl:field`, it takes the field's `th:field`, id and `aria-describedby`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `type` | `text`, `email`, `password`, `number`, `search`, `tel`, `url`, `date`, `datetime-local`, `month`, `week`, `time` | `text` | Type of the input. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:type="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:input-group-textarea

Attributes of `<sl:input-group-textarea>`: The textarea of a `sl:input-group`: a `sl:textarea` without a border of its own, as the group draws it, that grows with its content where the browser supports it. Addons go above or below it (`align` `block-start` or `block-end`). The content is the initial text, unless `th:field` sets it. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through; `class` is appended to `textarea`. With `th:field`, the textarea is marked `aria-invalid` when its field has errors. In a `sl:field`, it takes the field's `th:field`, id and `aria-describedby`.

`sl:input-group-textarea` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Textarea

> A multi-line text input, bound to a form field with th:field.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/textarea/

**Example: Textarea.**

```html
<sl:label for="message">Message</sl:label>
<sl:textarea th:field="*{message}" placeholder="Type your message here."/>
```

The textarea grows with its content in browsers that support `field-sizing`; elsewhere it keeps its height and
scrolls. `rows` sets a starting height.

### Initial text

With `th:field`, Spring fills the textarea from the field. Without it, the component's content is the initial text:

**Example: Initial text.** Without th:field, the content is the initial text.

```html
<sl:label for="textarea-notes">Notes</sl:label>
<sl:textarea id="textarea-notes" name="notes">Leave the parcel at the back door.</sl:textarea>
```

### Invalid

As with the [input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md), a field with errors marks the textarea `aria-invalid="true"`.

**Example: Invalid.**

```html
<sl:label for="message">Message</sl:label>
<sl:textarea th:field="*{message}"/>
```

### Disabled

As the [input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md#disabled): faded, not focusable and not submitted; lyra and nova add a grey background.

**Example: Disabled.**

```html
<sl:label for="textarea-disabled">Message</sl:label>
<sl:textarea id="textarea-disabled" name="message" disabled/>
```

### Read-only

As the [input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md#read-only): a muted background and full text colour; it takes focus and is submitted.

**Example: Read-only.**

```html
<sl:label for="textarea-instructions">Delivery instructions</sl:label>
<sl:textarea id="textarea-instructions" name="instructions" readonly>Leave the parcel with the neighbours at number 12.</sl:textarea>
```

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:textarea>`: A multi-line text input that grows with its content where the browser supports it. The content is the initial text, unless `th:field` sets it. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through; `class` is appended to `textarea`. With `th:field`, the textarea is marked `aria-invalid` when its field has errors. In a `sl:field`, it takes the field's `th:field`, id and `aria-describedby`.

`sl:textarea` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Select

> shadcn/ui's Select on a real select element, bound with th:field; the browser's customizable select where it has one.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/select/

**Example: Select.** A placeholder until an option is chosen; th:field marks the bound one.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{fruit}">
  <sl:field-label>Fruit</sl:field-label>
  <sl:select placeholder="Select a fruit">
    <sl:select-group label="Fruits">
      <sl:select-item value="apple">Apple</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="banana">Banana</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="blueberry">Blueberry</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="grapes">Grapes</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="pineapple">Pineapple</sl:select-item>
    </sl:select-group>
  </sl:select>
</sl:field>
```

### Built from parts

A select is `<sl:select>` holding `<sl:select-item>`s, optionally in `<sl:select-group>`s with
`<sl:select-separator>`s between them. It renders a real `<select>` with `<option>`, `<optgroup>` and `<hr>`
elements, so `th:field` binds it as it binds any select: Spring marks the bound item as selected, and the form submits
the chosen value.

```html
<form th:object="${order}" th:action="@{/orders}" method="post">
  <sl:field th:field="*{fruit}">
    <sl:field-label>Fruit</sl:field-label>
    <sl:select placeholder="Select a fruit">
      <sl:select-group label="Fruits">
        <sl:select-item th:each="fruit : ${fruits}" th:value="${fruit.id}">[[${fruit.name}]]</sl:select-item>
      </sl:select-group>
    </sl:select>
    <sl:field-error/>
  </sl:field>
</form>
```

Give an item its text as content (`[[${fruit.name}]]`), not with `th:text`: that attribute passes through to the
`<option>` and replaces its check mark too. (The select still works; the browser's own check mark shows instead.)

### Select, native select or combobox?

All three are a real `<select>` underneath, bind with `th:field` and submit with the form.

- **`<sl:select>`** looks like shadcn/ui's Select: its list is drawn in the page's style, with a check mark on the
  chosen item, and items can start with an icon. Use it where the look matters and the list is short enough to scan:
  a few to a few dozen items.
- **[`<sl:native-select>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/select-native.md)** styles only the closed control and leaves the list to the operating
  system: on a phone that is the system's own picker wheel or sheet, which many people find easier to use. Use it for
  long lists (countries, years), inside dense forms, and for `multiple`, which `<sl:select>` does not support.
- **[`<sl:combobox>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/combobox.md)** is a select people type into to filter the list, for one value or several
  (`multiple`), with options from the server if you like.

### Placeholder

`placeholder` adds a first option with an empty value, hidden from the list. It is chosen while the field has no
value, and shown in the muted colour. A `required` select with the placeholder chosen is invalid, as for any select
whose first option has an empty value; with server-side validation (`@NotBlank`, `@NotNull`) the field shows its
error instead.

### Groups, separators and icons

`<sl:select-group label="...">` is an `<optgroup>`: its label shows above its items and is read with them.
`<sl:select-separator/>` is an `<hr>`, which browsers draw as a line in a select's list.

**Example: Groups and separators.**

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{food}">
  <sl:field-label>Food</sl:field-label>
  <sl:select>
    <sl:select-group label="Fruits">
      <sl:select-item value="apple">Apple</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="banana">Banana</sl:select-item>
    </sl:select-group>
    <sl:select-separator/>
    <sl:select-group label="Vegetables">
      <sl:select-item value="carrot">Carrot</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="broccoli">Broccoli</sl:select-item>
    </sl:select-group>
  </sl:select>
</sl:field>
```

An item may start with an [icon](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/icon.md). The closed select shows the chosen item's icon and text.

**Example: Icons.** An item may start with an icon; the select shows it with the chosen item's text. Older browsers, which drop markup inside an option, show the text alone.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{chartType}">
  <sl:field-label>Chart</sl:field-label>
  <sl:select>
    <sl:select-item value="bar"><sl:icon name="chart-bar"/>Bar</sl:select-item>
    <sl:select-item value="line"><sl:icon name="chart-line"/>Line</sl:select-item>
    <sl:select-item value="pie"><sl:icon name="chart-pie"/>Pie</sl:select-item>
  </sl:select>
</sl:field>
```

Long lists scroll; the list never grows taller than the window.

**Example: Long lists.** The list scrolls when it is taller than the window.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{timezone}">
  <sl:field-label>Timezone</sl:field-label>
  <sl:select placeholder="Select a timezone">
    <sl:select-group label="Europe &amp; Africa">
      <sl:select-item value="gmt">Greenwich Mean Time (GMT)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="cet">Central European Time (CET)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="eet">Eastern European Time (EET)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="west">Western European Summer Time (WEST)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="cat">Central Africa Time (CAT)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="eat">East Africa Time (EAT)</sl:select-item>
    </sl:select-group>
    <sl:select-group label="Asia">
      <sl:select-item value="msk">Moscow Time (MSK)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="ist">India Standard Time (IST)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="cst_china">China Standard Time (CST)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="jst">Japan Standard Time (JST)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="kst">Korea Standard Time (KST)</sl:select-item>
    </sl:select-group>
    <sl:select-group label="Americas">
      <sl:select-item value="est">Eastern Standard Time (EST)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="cst">Central Standard Time (CST)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="mst">Mountain Standard Time (MST)</sl:select-item>
      <sl:select-item value="pst">Pacific Standard Time (PST)</sl:select-item>
    </sl:select-group>
  </sl:select>
</sl:field>
```

### Sizes

**Example: Sizes.**

```html
<sl:select size="sm" name="density" aria-label="Density (small)">
  <sl:select-item value="compact">Compact</sl:select-item>
  <sl:select-item value="comfortable">Comfortable</sl:select-item>
</sl:select>
<sl:select name="density" aria-label="Density">
  <sl:select-item value="compact">Compact</sl:select-item>
```
