```html
  <sl:select-item value="comfortable">Comfortable</sl:select-item>
</sl:select>
```

`size` picks the height, as in shadcn/ui. The list opens below the select, lined up with its start and at least as
wide as it, and above it when there is no room below.

### Width

The select sits in a wrapper that also holds the chevron. `class` goes to that wrapper, so `class="w-full"` widens
both; every other attribute goes to the `<select>`.

### In a form

- **`th:field`**, on the select or on its [field](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md), binds it. In a field, the select also takes the field's
  `id` (the label points at it) and `aria-describedby` (the description and, while there are errors, the error).
- **Errors.** A field with errors marks the select `aria-invalid="true"`, and
  [`<sl:form-errors autofocus/>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/form-errors.md) gives it `autofocus` when it is the first control with errors.
- **In a field set** with a `th:field` of its own (`<sl:field-set th:field="*{plan}">`), the select takes the field
  but no id; give it an `aria-label`, as the legend names the group, not the select.
- **Events.** Choosing an option fires `input` and `change` on the `<select>`, in every browser and with Shadleaf's
  list box too, so `hx-trigger="change"` and your own listeners work unchanged. A script that sets the select's value
  should fire `change` itself, as it should for any select; the list box then shows the new value.
- **Form reset** puts the select back to its rendered value, and the list box follows.

**Example: With errors.** In a field with errors, the select is aria-invalid and described by the message.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{shipping}">
  <sl:field-label>Shipping</sl:field-label>
  <sl:select placeholder="Select a shipping method">
    <sl:select-item value="standard">Standard</sl:select-item>
    <sl:select-item value="express">Express</sl:select-item>
  </sl:select>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

### Disabled

`disabled` on the select, or on a `<sl:field-set>` around it, disables it: it fades with its chevron, and the lyra and
nova skins also give it a grey background. `disabled` on an item leaves it in the list, faded; the keyboard and the
pointer skip it.

HTML has no read-only select: `readonly` does nothing on one, and Shadleaf's list box ignores it too. To show a choice
that cannot be changed, use a read-only [input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md#read-only) with the item's text, and a hidden input if the
value has to be submitted.

**Example: Disabled.** A disabled select, and a disabled item that the keyboard and the pointer skip.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{region}">
  <sl:field-label>Region</sl:field-label>
  <sl:select disabled>
    <sl:select-item value="eu">Europe</sl:select-item>
  </sl:select>
</sl:field>
<sl:field th:field="*{seat}">
  <sl:field-label>Seat</sl:field-label>
  <sl:select>
    <sl:select-item value="aisle">Aisle</sl:select-item>
    <sl:select-item value="middle" disabled>Middle</sl:select-item>
    <sl:select-item value="window">Window</sl:select-item>
  </sl:select>
</sl:field>
```

### Keyboard

With the focus on the select:

| Key | Closed | Open |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Arrow down, arrow up | Opens the list on the chosen item | Next, previous item |
| Enter, Space | Opens the list | Chooses the highlighted item and closes |
| Home, End | Opens the list on the first, last item | First, last item |
| A letter | Chooses (customizable select) or highlights (list box) the next item that starts with it | Highlights the next item that starts with it; typing quickly matches a prefix |
| Escape | | Closes without choosing; the value stays as it was |
| Tab | Moves on | The customizable select closes without choosing, the focus staying on it; Shadleaf's list box chooses the highlighted item and moves on, as the ARIA pattern describes |

Disabled items, group labels and separators are skipped. The item under the pointer is highlighted, and only keyboard
focus draws the focus ring as well. Focus stays on the select (or on the list box's button) while the list is open,
and is back there when it closes.

With the customizable select the browser handles the keys itself; `slSelect` only adds what it leaves out: Enter
opens the list, Home and End open it on the first and last item, and the item under the pointer takes the focus. It
also works around a Chrome bug: after a form reset the select showed the option chosen before, not the reset one.

### With htmx

- A response that renders the form again with errors keeps the chosen value (`th:field` marks it), and focus goes
  where `autofocus` says, the select included; see [Forms with htmx](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/htmx.md).
- A swap that replaces an open select leaves nothing open behind.
- Back restores the select as htmx saved it; Shadleaf's list box builds itself again from the restored select.
- A select can trigger a request itself: `hx-get="/cities" hx-trigger="change" hx-target="#city-field"`. Swapping in
  new items (`hx-target` on the select) is picked up by the list box.

### Forced colours

The select and its list keep a visible border, and the highlighted item an outline, when the system's colours
replace the page's (Windows High Contrast). A disabled select turns `GrayText` instead of fading.

### Left out

These shadcn/Radix parts and props have no counterpart, on purpose:

- **`SelectTrigger`, `SelectValue` and `SelectContent`.** In HTML the select is the trigger, shows its own value and
  holds its options, so `<sl:select>` covers all three: `size` from the trigger, `placeholder` from the value.
- **`SelectLabel` on its own.** A group's heading is the `label` of `<sl:select-group>` (an `<optgroup>` needs one);
  there is no heading outside a group, which no browser would show.
- **`SelectScrollUpButton`, `SelectScrollDownButton`.** The list scrolls like any other, with the wheel, the keys or a
  finger.
- **`position="item-aligned"`, `side`, `align`.** shadcn's default lays the list over the select with the chosen item
  on top of it; the customizable select always opens below the select (above when there is no room), and the list box
  does the same, so both look alike.
- **State in the browser** (`value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange`, `open`/`onOpenChange`): the server renders the
  value with `th:field`, and the select's `change` event reports a new one.
- **`multiple`**, which shadcn does not have either: use [`<sl:combobox multiple>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/combobox.md#multiple),
  [`<sl:native-select multiple>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/select-native.md) or a [field set](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md) of checkboxes.
- **`SelectItem`'s `textValue`**: typeahead reads the item's text.

### How it is drawn

Browsers are halfway through gaining the
[customizable select](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Learn_web_development/Extensions/Forms/Customizable_select)
(`appearance: base-select`), which lets CSS draw a select's list. Checked on 30 September 2026:

| Browser | Customizable select | What Shadleaf does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Chrome, Edge 135 and later | Yes | The browser draws the list in the skin's style, above everything else, placed with anchor positioning. |
| Safari 27 and later | Yes | The same. |
| Firefox (155 at the time of writing) | Behind a setting | Once Alpine runs, the `slSelect` component hides the select and shows a button and a list box of its own in its place, built from the select's options. |
| Older browsers, or no Alpine | No | The select is drawn like the native select: the closed control has the skin's look, and the list is the browser's own. |

The server always renders the same markup, a real select, so the choice happens in the browser and needs nothing
from you. That also means the form works before Alpine starts and without it: the select submits, binds, validates
and takes the keyboard like any select (rule 4 of [Alpine](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/alpine.md)).

The customizable select is used wherever the browser has it, rather than Shadleaf's list box everywhere, because the
browser's own is simply the better control: the keyboard, the screen reader, the top layer, the placing next to the
select and the form all come from the browser, and keep working if the page's JavaScript fails. The list box exists
only for the browsers still missing it, and follows the same
[ARIA select-only combobox pattern](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/combobox/examples/combobox-select-only/)
the browsers follow.

Only options with text work everywhere: an older browser drops any markup inside an `<option>` and keeps its text, so
an item's icon is extra, never its only label.

### Attributes

#### sl:select

Attributes of `<sl:select>`: A select drawn like shadcn/ui's: a real `select` with `sl:select-item`, `sl:select-group` and `sl:select-separator` as content, so `th:field`, submitting and the keyboard work as for any select. Where the browser supports the customizable select (`appearance: base-select`) it draws the list itself; elsewhere `slSelect` puts a list box in its place once Alpine runs. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through to the `select`, except `class`, which goes to the wrapper around it. In a `sl:field`, it takes the field's `th:field`, id and `aria-describedby`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `size` | `sm`, `default` | `default` | Height of the select. |
| `placeholder` | *string* | — | Text shown while no option is chosen: a first option with an empty value, hidden from the list. A required select with it selected is invalid. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:size="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:select-item

Attributes of `<sl:select-item>`: An option in a `sl:select`: an `option` with a check mark that shows while it is chosen. `value` (or `th:value`) and `disabled` pass through; `th:field` on the select marks the bound one. Its content may start with an icon; always give it text, which is all an older browser keeps of it.

`sl:select-item` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:select-group

Attributes of `<sl:select-group>`: A group of `sl:select-item`s under a heading: an `optgroup`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` | *string* | **required** | The group's heading, shadcn/ui's `SelectLabel`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:label="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:select-separator

Attributes of `<sl:select-separator>`: A line between items or groups of a `sl:select`: an `hr`, which every current browser draws in a select's list.

`sl:select-separator` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

### JavaScript

A small Alpine component, `slSelect`, sits on the select's wrapper:
`<div class="select-wrapper" x-data="slSelect">`.

- **Without Alpine the select works** as the browser's own, customizable or not.
- **Where the customizable select is missing**, `slSelect` hides the `<select>` (it stays in the form, and still
  submits, resets and validates) and adds a `<button role="combobox" class="select select-trigger">` and a
  `<div role="listbox" class="select-content" popover>` after it. The button takes the select's label (its `<label>`s,
  `aria-labelledby` or `aria-label`), `aria-describedby`, `aria-invalid`, `required` and `disabled`, and follows them
  when they change. Focus that reaches the hidden select, from a click on its label, `autofocus` or the browser's
  validation, moves on to the button.
- **It needs no Alpine plugin.** If you load Alpine yourself (`shadleaf.assets.alpine=external`), plain Alpine is
  enough.
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:select>`**: `class` is the only attribute that reaches the wrapper, and an element
  has one `x-data`. Listen for `change` on the select instead.

---

## Select (Native)

> The browser's own select, styled, bound to a form field with th:field.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/select-native/

**Example: Native select.**

```html
<sl:label for="status">Status</sl:label>
<sl:native-select th:field="*{status}">
  <option value="">Select status</option>
  <option th:each="status : ${statuses}" th:value="${status}" th:text="#{|status.${status}|}">Todo</option>
</sl:native-select>
```

### Native select or select?

Both are a real `<select>` underneath, bind with `th:field` and submit with the form. This one styles only the closed
control and leaves the list to the operating system: on a phone that is the system's own picker, which many people
find easier to use. Use it for long lists (countries, years), in dense forms, and for `multiple`.
[`<sl:select>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/select.md) draws its list in the page's style, like shadcn/ui's Select, with a check mark on the
chosen item and room for an icon; use it where the look matters and the list is short enough to scan.

### Options

Write plain `<option>` and `<optgroup>` elements as the content, with `th:each` for options from the model. With
`th:field` on the select, Spring marks the option that matches the field's value as selected.

**Example: Groups.**

```html
<sl:label for="select-department">Department</sl:label>
<sl:native-select id="select-department" name="department">
  <optgroup label="Engineering">
    <option value="frontend">Frontend</option>
    <option value="backend">Backend</option>
  </optgroup>
  <optgroup label="Sales">
    <option value="sales-rep">Sales representative</option>
    <option value="account-manager">Account manager</option>
  </optgroup>
</sl:native-select>
```

### Sizes

**Example: Sizes.**

```html
<sl:native-select size="sm" name="priority" aria-label="Priority (small)">
  <option>Low</option>
  <option>High</option>
</sl:native-select>
<sl:native-select name="priority" aria-label="Priority">
  <option>Low</option>
  <option>High</option>
</sl:native-select>
```

`size` picks the height, as in shadcn/ui. It replaces HTML's own `size` attribute, which turns a select into a list box;
`multiple` still works, and hides the chevron.

### Width

The select sits in a wrapper that also holds the chevron. `class` goes to that wrapper, so `class="w-full"` widens
both; every other attribute goes to the `<select>`.

### Invalid

As with the [input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md), a field with errors marks the select `aria-invalid="true"`.

**Example: Invalid.** With th:field, a field that has errors marks the select aria-invalid by itself.

```html
<sl:native-select th:field="*{status}" aria-label="Status">…</sl:native-select>
```

### Disabled

The select fades with its chevron; the lyra and nova skins also give it a grey background, as they do a disabled input.
HTML has no read-only select: `readonly` does nothing on one. To show a choice that cannot be changed, use a read-only
[input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md#read-only) with the option's text, and a hidden input if the value has to be submitted.

**Example: Disabled.**

```html
<sl:native-select name="status" aria-label="Status" disabled>
  <option>Todo</option>
</sl:native-select>
```

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:native-select>`: The browser's own `select`, styled, with its `option` and `optgroup` elements as content. `th:field` marks the bound option. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through to the `select`, except `class`, which goes to the wrapper around it (for its width). With `th:field`, the select is marked `aria-invalid` when its field has errors. In a `sl:field`, it takes the field's `th:field`, id and `aria-describedby`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `size` | `sm`, `default` | `default` | Height of the select. It replaces HTML's `size` attribute, which turns a select into a list box. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:size="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Combobox

> shadcn/ui's Combobox on a real select element, bound with th:field; type to filter, choose one value or several as chips, and load the options from the server as you type.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/combobox/

**Example: Combobox.** Type to filter the list; th:field marks the bound item.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{framework}">
  <sl:field-label>Framework</sl:field-label>
  <sl:combobox placeholder="Select a framework">
    <sl:combobox-item value="next">Next.js</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="sveltekit">SvelteKit</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="nuxt">Nuxt.js</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="remix">Remix</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="astro">Astro</sl:combobox-item>
  </sl:combobox>
</sl:field>
```

### Built from parts

A combobox is `<sl:combobox>` holding `<sl:combobox-item>`s, optionally in `<sl:combobox-group>`s with
`<sl:combobox-separator>`s between them. Like [`<sl:select>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/select.md), it renders a real `<select>` with
`<option>`, `<optgroup>` and `<hr>` elements, so `th:field` binds it as it binds any select and the form submits the
chosen value. Once Alpine runs, a text input and a list box take the select's place; typing filters the list.

```html
<form th:object="${project}" th:action="@{/projects}" method="post">
  <sl:field th:field="*{framework}">
    <sl:field-label>Framework</sl:field-label>
    <sl:combobox placeholder="Select a framework">
      <sl:combobox-item th:each="framework : ${frameworks}"
                        th:value="${framework.id}">[[${framework.name}]]</sl:combobox-item>
    </sl:combobox>
    <sl:field-error/>
  </sl:field>
</form>
```

Give an item its text as content (`[[${framework.name}]]`): the input, the chips and the filter use it.

### Combobox or select?

- **[`<sl:select>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/select.md)** for a short list people scan: a few to a few dozen items.
- **`<sl:combobox>`** for a list people would rather search: many items, items whose names people know, or several
  values at once (`multiple`). With `search-url`, the options can come from the server, for lists too long to put in
  the page.
- **[`<sl:native-select>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/select-native.md)** to leave the list to the operating system, as phones show it.

### Multiple

`multiple` lets people choose any number of items. The chosen ones are chips before the input, each with a button
that removes it; the list stays open while choosing, with a check mark on every chosen item. The select is a
`<select multiple>`, so `th:field` binds a collection (`List<String>`, `Set<Long>`, ...) and the form submits one
value per chosen item. Spring's hidden marker input comes with it, so choosing none binds an empty collection.

**Example: Multiple.** With multiple, the chosen items are chips before the input and th:field binds a collection.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{frameworks}">
  <sl:field-label>Frameworks</sl:field-label>
  <sl:combobox multiple auto-highlight placeholder="Add a framework">
    <sl:combobox-item value="next">Next.js</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="sveltekit">SvelteKit</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="nuxt">Nuxt.js</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="remix">Remix</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="astro">Astro</sl:combobox-item>
  </sl:combobox>
</sl:field>
```

### Placeholder and clearing

`placeholder` is the input's text while nothing is chosen. For a single value it is also a first option with an empty
value, hidden from the list, which is chosen while the field has no value: a `required` combobox with nothing chosen
is invalid, as for a select.

Emptying the input and leaving it clears the choice. `show-clear` adds a button that does the same, in place of the
chevron while there is something to clear; in a multiple combobox it removes every chip.

**Example: Clear button.** show-clear puts a button that clears the choice in place of the chevron while there is one.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{stack}">
  <sl:field-label>Stack</sl:field-label>
  <sl:combobox placeholder="Select a framework" show-clear>
    <sl:combobox-item value="next">Next.js</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="sveltekit">SvelteKit</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="nuxt">Nuxt.js</sl:combobox-item>
  </sl:combobox>
</sl:field>
```

### Groups, separators and icons

`<sl:combobox-group label="...">` is an `<optgroup>`: its label shows above its items, and hides while none of them
match what was typed. `<sl:combobox-separator/>` is an `<hr>`, hidden while typing.

**Example: Groups and separators.** Some 80 time zones, too many to scan, so you type a city instead (bru finds Brussels). A group's heading hides while none of its items match; separators hide while you type.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{meetingZone}">
  <sl:field-label>Timezone</sl:field-label>
  <sl:combobox placeholder="Select a timezone">
    <sl:combobox-group label="Americas">
      <sl:combobox-item value="Pacific/Honolulu">(GMT-10) Honolulu</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Anchorage">(GMT-9) Anchorage</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Los_Angeles">(GMT-8) Los Angeles</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Tijuana">(GMT-8) Tijuana</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Vancouver">(GMT-8) Vancouver</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Denver">(GMT-7) Denver</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Edmonton">(GMT-7) Edmonton</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Phoenix">(GMT-7) Phoenix</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Chicago">(GMT-6) Chicago</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Guatemala">(GMT-6) Guatemala</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Mexico_City">(GMT-6) Mexico City</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Winnipeg">(GMT-6) Winnipeg</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Bogota">(GMT-5) Bogota</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Havana">(GMT-5) Havana</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Lima">(GMT-5) Lima</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/New_York">(GMT-5) New York</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Toronto">(GMT-5) Toronto</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Caracas">(GMT-4) Caracas</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Halifax">(GMT-4) Halifax</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/La_Paz">(GMT-4) La Paz</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Santiago">(GMT-4) Santiago</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/St_Johns">(GMT-3:30) St. John's</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Argentina/Buenos_Aires">(GMT-3) Buenos Aires</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Montevideo">(GMT-3) Montevideo</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="America/Sao_Paulo">(GMT-3) São Paulo</sl:combobox-item>
    </sl:combobox-group>
    <sl:combobox-separator/>
    <sl:combobox-group label="Europe">
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/Dublin">(GMT+0) Dublin</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/Lisbon">(GMT+0) Lisbon</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/London">(GMT+0) London</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Atlantic/Reykjavik">(GMT+0) Reykjavik</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/Amsterdam">(GMT+1) Amsterdam</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/Berlin">(GMT+1) Berlin</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/Brussels">(GMT+1) Brussels</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/Budapest">(GMT+1) Budapest</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/Copenhagen">(GMT+1) Copenhagen</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/Madrid">(GMT+1) Madrid</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/Oslo">(GMT+1) Oslo</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/Paris">(GMT+1) Paris</sl:combobox-item>
      <sl:combobox-item value="Europe/Prague">(GMT+1) Prague</sl:combobox-item>

```

An item may start with an [icon](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/icon.md). The list shows it; the input and the chips show the item's text.

**Example: Custom items.** An item may start with an icon. The input and the chips show its text.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{issueStatus}">
  <sl:field-label>Status</sl:field-label>
  <sl:combobox placeholder="Set a status">
    <sl:combobox-item value="backlog"><sl:icon name="circle-dashed"/>Backlog</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="todo"><sl:icon name="circle"/>Todo</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="in-progress"><sl:icon name="circle-dot"/>In progress</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="done"><sl:icon name="circle-check"/>Done</sl:combobox-item>
  </sl:combobox>
</sl:field>
```

### Filtering and auto-highlight

The list shows the items whose text contains what was typed, ignoring case and accents (`turk` finds Türkiye). By
default nothing is highlighted while typing, so Enter does not choose by accident; `auto-highlight` highlights the
first match, so typing a few letters and pressing Enter chooses it. Long lists scroll; the list never grows taller
than the window.

**Example: Long lists.** The list scrolls when it is taller than the window; auto-highlight makes Enter choose the first match.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{destination}">
  <sl:field-label>Country</sl:field-label>
  <sl:combobox placeholder="Select a country" auto-highlight>
    <sl:combobox-item value="argentina">Argentina</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="australia">Australia</sl:combobox-item>

    <sl:combobox-item value="canada">Canada</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="chile">Chile</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="china">China</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="colombia">Colombia</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="czechia">Czechia</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="denmark">Denmark</sl:combobox-item>

    <sl:combobox-item value="vietnam">Vietnam</sl:combobox-item>
  </sl:combobox>

```

### Options from the server

`search-url` makes the combobox ask the server for the options as people type, for lists too long to put in the page.
300 ms after the last key, it sends a `GET` to that URL with the text as `q`, and expects the HTML of
`<sl:combobox-item>`s (and groups and separators, if you like) back. A Thymeleaf fragment renders them:

```html
<sl:combobox th:field="*{country}" placeholder="Search a country" th:search-url="@{/countries/search}">
  <!-- The chosen country, which the controller looked up: the combobox shows it before anyone types. -->
  <sl:combobox-item th:if="${chosenCountry != null}"
                    th:value="${chosenCountry.code}">[[${chosenCountry.name}]]</sl:combobox-item>
</sl:combobox>
```

```java
@GetMapping("/countries/search")
String searchCountries(@RequestParam String q, Model model) {
  model.addAttribute("countries", countryService.search(q, 20));
  return "countries :: options";
}
```

```html
<!-- countries.html -->
<th:block th:fragment="options">
  <sl:combobox-item th:each="country : ${countries}"
                    th:value="${country.code}">[[${country.name}]]</sl:combobox-item>
</th:block>
```

- **The answer replaces the options** that are not chosen. A chosen option stays, so the value does not change; when
  the answer leaves it out, it stays hidden from the list. Render the chosen option(s) in the page, as above, so the
  combobox can show and submit them before anyone types.
- **The server filters.** The browser shows what the answer holds, without filtering it again; an empty answer shows
  "No items found." (or `empty`). Limit the number of results on the server.
- **An empty input** brings back the options the page came with: render a first few there, or none.
- **A newer query aborts the request before it**, so a slow answer never replaces a newer one. While waiting, the list
  says "Searching…"; an answer that is not a 2xx, or that was redirected (a session that expired and sent the request
  to the login page), shows "The options could not be loaded."
- **To add parameters**, such as another field's value for options that depend on it, listen for
  `sl-combobox-search` on the select. It fires before each request, with `detail.query` and `detail.url`, a `URL`
  whose `searchParams` you can change; `preventDefault()` skips the request.

```js
document.addEventListener('sl-combobox-search', event => {
  if (event.target.id === 'city') {
    event.detail.url.searchParams.set('country', document.getElementById('country').value);
  }
});
```

Searching needs JavaScript. Without it, the combobox is a select with the options in the page, so only those can be
chosen.

### Width

The combobox sits in a wrapper that also holds the input and the list. `class` goes to that wrapper, so
`class="w-full"` widens it; every other attribute goes to the `<select>`. The list is at least as wide as the input.

### In a form

- **`th:field`**, on the combobox or on its [field](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md), binds it. In a field, the select takes the field's `id`
  (the label points at it) and `aria-describedby`; the input takes them over from the select, so the label names the
  input and the description and error describe it.
- **Errors.** A field with errors marks the combobox `aria-invalid="true"`, and
  [`<sl:form-errors autofocus/>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/form-errors.md) gives it `autofocus` when it is the first control with errors; the
  focus goes on to the input.
- **No free text.** Only the options can be chosen: what was typed without choosing is not submitted, and the input
  shows the choice again when it loses the focus. For a text field with suggestions, use an
  [input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md) with a `<datalist>`.
- **Events.** Choosing fires `input` and `change` on the `<select>`, so `hx-trigger="change"` and your own listeners
  work as for any select. A script that changes the select's options or value should fire `change` itself; the input
  and the chips follow.
- **Form reset** puts the combobox back to its rendered value.

**Example: With errors.** In a field with errors, the combobox is aria-invalid and described by the message.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{language}">
  <sl:field-label>Language</sl:field-label>
  <sl:combobox placeholder="Select a language">
    <sl:combobox-item value="java">Java</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="kotlin">Kotlin</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="groovy">Groovy</sl:combobox-item>
  </sl:combobox>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
<sl:field th:field="*{languages}">
  <sl:field-label>Other languages</sl:field-label>
  <sl:combobox multiple placeholder="Add a language">
    <sl:combobox-item value="java">Java</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="kotlin">Kotlin</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="groovy">Groovy</sl:combobox-item>
  </sl:combobox>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

### Disabled

`disabled` on the combobox, or on a `<sl:field-set>` around it, disables it: it fades with its chips. `disabled` on an
item leaves it in the list, faded; the keyboard and the pointer skip it.

**Example: Disabled.** A disabled combobox, and disabled items that the keyboard and the pointer skip.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{runtime}">
  <sl:field-label>Runtime</sl:field-label>
  <sl:combobox disabled>
    <sl:combobox-item value="jvm">JVM</sl:combobox-item>
  </sl:combobox>
</sl:field>
<sl:field th:field="*{plugins}">
  <sl:field-label>Plugins</sl:field-label>
  <sl:combobox multiple disabled>
    <sl:combobox-item value="lombok">Lombok</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="mapstruct">MapStruct</sl:combobox-item>
  </sl:combobox>
</sl:field>
<sl:field th:field="*{build}">
  <sl:field-label>Build tool</sl:field-label>
  <sl:combobox placeholder="Select a build tool">
    <sl:combobox-item value="maven">Maven</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="ant" disabled>Ant</sl:combobox-item>
    <sl:combobox-item value="gradle">Gradle</sl:combobox-item>
  </sl:combobox>
</sl:field>
```

### Keyboard

The focus stays in the input; the highlighted item is announced through `aria-activedescendant`, as the
[ARIA combobox pattern](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/combobox/examples/combobox-autocomplete-list/)
describes.

| Key | Does |
| --- | --- |
| Typing | Opens the list and filters it |
| Arrow down, arrow up | Opens the list on the chosen item; next, previous item |
| Alt + arrow down | Opens the list without highlighting |
| Page down, page up | Ten items further, back |
| Enter | Chooses the highlighted item; in a multiple combobox, adds or removes it and keeps the list open |
| Alt + arrow up | Chooses the highlighted item and closes the list |
| Escape | Closes the list, putting back the text of the choice |
| Tab | Closes the list without choosing and moves on |
| Home, End | Move the caret, as in any text field |
| Backspace (multiple, empty input) | Removes the last chip |
| Arrow left (multiple, at the start of the input) | Moves to the chips' remove buttons: arrow left and right between them, Backspace or Delete removes one |

The number of matches is announced once typing pauses ("3 results"; the texts are messages, `sl.combobox.*`, see
[Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md)). The clear and chevron buttons stay out of the tab order: each
has a key. The item under the pointer is highlighted.

### With htmx

- A response that renders the form again with errors keeps the chosen values (`th:field` marks them).
- A swap that replaces an open combobox leaves nothing open behind.
- Back restores the combobox as htmx saved it; it builds its input and list again from the restored select.
- A combobox can trigger a request itself: `hx-get="/cities" hx-trigger="change" hx-target="#city-field"`. Options
  swapped into the select are picked up by the list.

### Forced colours

The input and the list keep a visible border, the highlighted item an outline and the chips a border, when the
system's colours replace the page's (Windows High Contrast). A disabled combobox turns `GrayText` instead of fading.

### Left out

These shadcn/Base UI parts and props have no counterpart, on purpose:

- **`ComboboxInput`, `ComboboxContent`, `ComboboxList`, `ComboboxChips`, `ComboboxChip`, `ComboboxChipsInput`,
  `ComboboxValue` and `ComboboxTrigger`.** `<sl:combobox>` draws them all: `showClear`, `showTrigger` and
  `autoHighlight` are its `show-clear`, `show-trigger` and `auto-highlight`.
- **`items` and the render function.** Items are written as `<sl:combobox-item>`s (with `th:each`): `value` and their
  text take the place of `itemToStringValue`.
- **`ComboboxEmpty`** is the `empty` attribute; **`ComboboxLabel`** is a group's `label`, and there is no
  `ComboboxCollection`.
- **The popup layout**, with a button as the trigger and the input inside the list, and **addons** in the input.
- **Free text**, which Base UI's Combobox does not allow either (its Autocomplete does).
- **Removing chips is always possible** (`showRemove`), and a chip shows the item's text.
- **`side`, `align`**: the list opens below the input, or above it when there is no room.
- **State in the browser** (`value`/`defaultValue`/`onValueChange`, `open`/`onOpenChange`): the server renders the
  value with `th:field`, and the select's `change` event reports a new one.

### Without JavaScript

The server renders a real select, drawn like the [native select](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/select-native.md): a single combobox is a select
with a chevron, a multiple one a list box (Ctrl- or Cmd-click chooses several). Both bind, submit and validate as any
select. With Alpine, `slCombobox` puts its input and list in the select's place.

### Attributes

#### sl:combobox

Attributes of `<sl:combobox>`: A text input that filters a list of options as you type: shadcn/ui's combobox. Like `sl:select`, it is a real `select` with `sl:combobox-item`, `sl:combobox-group` and `sl:combobox-separator` as content, so `th:field` binds it (a collection with `multiple`) and the form submits it. Once Alpine runs, `slCombobox` hides the select and puts an input and a list box in its place; without it, the select is drawn like `sl:native-select`. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through to the `select`, except `class`, which goes to the wrapper around it. In a `sl:field`, it takes the field's `th:field`, id and `aria-describedby`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `placeholder` | *string* | — | The input's text while nothing is chosen. For a single value also the text of a first option with an empty value, hidden from the list: a required combobox with it chosen is invalid. |
| `multiple` | *boolean* | — | Choose any number of options, shown as chips before the input. The select is a `select multiple`, so `th:field` binds a collection. |
| `show-clear` | *boolean* | — | A button that clears the choice, in place of the chevron while there is one. |
| `show-trigger` | *boolean* | `true` | The chevron button that opens and closes the list. A multiple combobox has none. |
| `auto-highlight` | *boolean* | — | Highlight the first match while typing, so Enter chooses it. |
| `empty` | *string* | — | The text shown when no option matches. By default "No items found." |
| `search-url` | *string* | — | Ask the server for the options as you type: a `GET` to this URL with the text as `q`, answered with the HTML of `sl:combobox-item`s (and groups and separators), which replace the options that are not chosen. Needs JavaScript; without it, only the options in the page can be chosen. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:placeholder="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:combobox-item

Attributes of `<sl:combobox-item>`: An option in a `sl:combobox`: an `option`. `value` (or `th:value`) and `disabled` pass through; `th:field` on the combobox marks the bound one. The list shows its content, which may start with an icon, with a check mark while it is chosen; the input, the chips and the filter use its text, which is all an older browser keeps of it.

`sl:combobox-item` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:combobox-group

Attributes of `<sl:combobox-group>`: A group of `sl:combobox-item`s under a heading: an `optgroup`. The list hides the heading while none of its items match.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` | *string* | **required** | The group's heading, shadcn/ui's `ComboboxLabel`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:label="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:combobox-separator

Attributes of `<sl:combobox-separator>`: A line between items or groups of a `sl:combobox`: an `hr`. The list hides it while you type.

`sl:combobox-separator` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

### JavaScript

A small Alpine component, `slCombobox`, sits on the wrapper: `<div class="combobox" x-data="slCombobox">`.

- **It hides the `<select>`** (it stays in the form, and still submits, resets and validates) and adds what the
  wrapper's `<template>` holds after it: a `<div class="combobox-control">` with an
  `<input role="combobox" class="combobox-input">` (and the chips, the clear and the chevron buttons), and a
  `<div class="combobox-content" popover="manual">` with the `<div role="listbox" class="combobox-list">`. The input
  takes the select's label (its `<label>`s, `aria-labelledby` or `aria-label`), `aria-describedby`, `aria-invalid`,
  `required` and `disabled`, and follows them when they change. Focus that reaches the hidden select moves on to the
  input.
- **The list is a popover** in the top layer, so it shows above dialogs and sheets, placed with anchor positioning
  (with a stand-in for browsers without it). Escape in the input closes the list, not the dialog around it.
- **It works with Alpine's CSP build** and a strict Content Security Policy: no inline styles, no `eval`. A search
  request is a `fetch` to the same origin (`connect-src 'self'`).
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:combobox>`**: `class` is the only attribute that reaches the wrapper. Listen for
  `change` on the select instead.

---

## Checkbox

> The browser's own checkbox, styled, bound to a form field with th:field.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/checkbox/

**Example: Checkbox.**

```html
<sl:label><sl:checkbox th:field="*{terms}"/>Accept the terms and conditions</sl:label>
```

### Binding to a form field

`th:field` on a boolean field sets `checked` and adds the hidden `_terms` input Spring uses to see an unchecked box.
For a collection field, give each checkbox a `value` (or `th:value`), as with a plain checkbox.

```html
<form th:object="${signup}" th:action="@{/signup}" method="post">
  <sl:label><sl:checkbox th:field="*{terms}"/>Accept the terms and conditions</sl:label>
</form>
```

### Labelling it

Put the checkbox inside its [label](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/label.md): clicking the text toggles it, and it needs no `id`. Spring numbers the
`id` of a checkbox (`terms1`), so a label with `for="terms"` would miss it. For a label beside the checkbox, give the
checkbox an explicit `id`, which wins over the numbered one:

**Example: With a separate label.** Give the checkbox an explicit id for the label's for; th:field would number it.

```html
<sl:checkbox id="checkbox-notify" name="notify"/>
<sl:label for="checkbox-notify">Notify me by email</sl:label>
```

In a [field](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md) with `th:field`, the checkbox gets the field's name as its `id` (`terms`), and the field's
label points at it. The field also shows the error message.

### Checked

**Example: Checked.**

```html
<sl:label><sl:checkbox name="newsletter" checked/>Send me the newsletter</sl:label>
```

### Indeterminate

Neither checked nor unchecked: some of a group is selected. HTML has no attribute for it, only the DOM property
`indeterminate`. `indeterminate` on `<sl:checkbox>` renders `data-indeterminate="true"`, from which Shadleaf's script
sets the property when the page loads or htmx swaps the checkbox in; the box then shows a minus, and a screen reader
says "mixed". A click clears it and checks the box, as with any checkbox. A script of yours can set the property on
any checkbox at any time; the minus follows it (the CSS reads `:indeterminate`). The script is not an Alpine
component, so it also runs with `shadleaf.assets.alpine=external`; without JavaScript the checkbox is unchecked.

**Example: Indeterminate.** Some of a group selected. A click clears the state, as on any checkbox; a script can set it again.

```html
<sl:label><sl:checkbox name="toppings-all" indeterminate/>All toppings</sl:label>
```

[Select-all in a table](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/table.md#selecting-rows) is the common case, and sets the state itself.

### Invalid

A field with errors marks the checkbox `aria-invalid="true"`, as with the [input](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/input.md).

**Example: Invalid.** With th:field, a field that has errors marks the checkbox aria-invalid by itself.

```html
<sl:label><sl:checkbox th:field="*{terms}"/>Accept the terms and conditions</sl:label>
```

### Disabled

A disabled checkbox fades with its check mark, and a label fades with the disabled checkbox it holds, or sits next
to. In Windows' high-contrast mode the browser draws its own disabled checkbox. HTML has no read-only checkbox:
`readonly` does nothing on one.

**Example: Disabled.**

```html
<sl:label><sl:checkbox name="archived" checked disabled/>Archived</sl:label>
<sl:label><sl:checkbox name="deleted" disabled/>Deleted</sl:label>
```

### How it is drawn

The check mark and the minus are icons inside a wrapper around the `<input>`, not CSS background images, so the checkbox renders
under a strict `img-src` policy. `class` goes to that wrapper; every other attribute to the `<input>`. In Windows'
high-contrast mode the browser draws its own checkbox, with its own dash when it is indeterminate.

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:checkbox>`: A checkbox: the browser's own, styled. Every attribute that is not a prop (`th:field`, `name`, `value`, `checked`, `required`) passes through to the `input`, except `class`, which goes to the wrapper that holds the check mark. With `th:field`, Spring numbers the id (`terms1`), so put the checkbox inside its label, in a `sl:field` (which gives it the field's name as id), or give it an explicit `id`; the checkbox is marked `aria-invalid` when its field has errors. A checkbox in the indeterminate state (neither checked nor unchecked: some of a group selected) shows a minus.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `indeterminate` | *boolean* | — | Start in the indeterminate state. HTML has no attribute for it, only the DOM property `indeterminate`: this renders `data-indeterminate`, from which Shadleaf's script sets the property when the page loads (or htmx swaps the checkbox in). A click clears it, as it does on every checkbox. A script can set the property on any checkbox; the minus follows it. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:indeterminate="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Radio Group

> A set of the browser's own radio buttons, styled, bound to a form field with th:field.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/radio-group/

**Example: Radio group.**

```html
<p id="plan-question">Plan</p>
<sl:radio-group aria-labelledby="plan-question">
  <sl:label th:each="plan : ${plans}">
    <sl:radio-group-item th:field="*{plan}" th:value="${plan}"/>
    <th:block th:text="#{|plan.${plan}|}">Free</th:block>
  </sl:label>
</sl:radio-group>
```

### Binding to a form field

Write `th:field` on every item, with the item's own `value` or `th:value`. Spring gives them the field's `name`,
numbers their ids (`plan1`, `plan2`, ...) and checks the one whose value matches the field. Because of the numbered
ids, put each item inside its [label](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/label.md).

The group is announced as a radio group. Name it with `aria-labelledby`, pointing at the question, or with
`aria-label`.

In a [field set](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md#radio-buttons-and-checkbox-groups), `th:field` goes on the field set once, and the items
take it. Its legend asks the question, and it shows the error message and describes the group with it.

### Horizontal

**Example: Horizontal.**

```html
<sl:radio-group orientation="horizontal" aria-label="Density">
  <sl:label><sl:radio-group-item name="density" value="compact"/>Compact</sl:label>
  <sl:label><sl:radio-group-item name="density" value="comfortable" checked/>Comfortable</sl:label>
  <sl:label><sl:radio-group-item name="density" value="spacious"/>Spacious</sl:label>
</sl:radio-group>
```

### Invalid

A field with errors marks every item `aria-invalid="true"`.

**Example: Invalid.** With th:field, a field that has errors marks every item aria-invalid by itself.

```html
<sl:radio-group aria-label="Delivery">
  <sl:label><sl:radio-group-item th:field="*{delivery}" value="standard"/>Standard</sl:label>
  <sl:label><sl:radio-group-item th:field="*{delivery}" value="express"/>Express</sl:label>
</sl:radio-group>
```

### Disabled

A disabled radio button and its label fade, and the keyboard skips it. To disable the whole group, put it in a
[field set](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md#disabled) with `disabled`.

**Example: Disabled item.**

```html
<sl:radio-group aria-label="Shipping">
  <sl:label><sl:radio-group-item name="shipping" value="pickup" checked/>Pick up in store</sl:label>
  <sl:label><sl:radio-group-item name="shipping" value="drone" disabled/>Drone (not available)</sl:label>
</sl:radio-group>
```

### Attributes

#### sl:radio-group

Attributes of `<sl:radio-group>`: A group of `sl:radio-group-item`s, announced as a radio group. Name it with `aria-labelledby` (pointing at the question) or `aria-label`. The items share their `name`, or their `th:field`, which is what makes them one choice.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `orientation` | `vertical`, `horizontal` | `vertical` | Stack the items, or put them on a row that wraps. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:orientation="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:radio-group-item

Attributes of `<sl:radio-group-item>`: One radio button in `sl:radio-group`: the browser's own, styled. Give it a `value` and the group's `name`, or `th:field` with a `value` or `th:value`; Spring then numbers the ids (`plan1`, `plan2`), so put the item inside its label. In a `sl:field-set` with `th:field`, the items take its field. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through; `class` is appended to `radio-group-item`. With `th:field`, the item is marked `aria-invalid` when its field has errors.

`sl:radio-group-item` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Switch

> An on/off control, bound to a boolean form field with th:field.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/switch/

**Example: Switch.**

```html
<sl:label><sl:switch th:field="*{airplaneMode}"/>Airplane mode</sl:label>
```

A switch is a checkbox with `role="switch"`, so a screen reader announces it as on or off and a form submits it as a
checkbox. `th:field` on a boolean field works as it does on the [checkbox](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/checkbox.md), including the numbered `id`:
put the switch inside its label.

Use a switch for a setting that takes effect as it is, and a checkbox for a choice the user confirms by submitting a
form.

### Sizes

**Example: Sizes.**

```html
<sl:label><sl:switch size="sm" name="compact" checked/>Small</sl:label>
<sl:label><sl:switch name="regular" checked/>Default</sl:label>
```

### Invalid

**Example: Invalid.**

```html
<sl:label><sl:switch th:field="*{marketing}"/>Marketing emails</sl:label>
```

### Disabled

Track and thumb fade together, and so does the label. A disabled switch is not submitted, so Spring leaves out the
hidden marker input it adds for a `th:field` checkbox: the bound value stays as it was.

**Example: Disabled.**

```html
<sl:label><sl:switch name="sso" checked disabled/>Single sign-on (managed by your organisation)</sl:label>
<sl:label><sl:switch name="betaFeatures" disabled/>Beta features (not in your plan)</sl:label>
```

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:switch>`: A switch: a checkbox with `role="switch"`, announced as on or off. It submits like a checkbox, so `th:field` on a boolean works as it does there (numbered id, hidden marker input; in a `sl:field`, the field's name as id). Every attribute that is not a prop passes through; `class` is appended to `switch`. With `th:field`, the switch is marked `aria-invalid` when its field has errors.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `size` | `sm`, `default` | `default` | Size of the switch. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:size="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Toggle

> A button that is on or off, as a checkbox drawn as a button, bound to a boolean form field with th:field.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toggle/

**Example: Toggle.**

```html
<sl:toggle name="bookmarked" size="sm" variant="outline">
  <sl:icon name="bookmark"/> Bookmark
</sl:toggle>
```

A toggle is a checkbox drawn as a button: a `<label>` with the checkbox inside it, covering it. A click anywhere on the
toggle checks or unchecks it, the form submits it as a checkbox, and it works without JavaScript. A screen reader
announces it as a checkbox, checked or not.

The content is the toggle's label. A toggle that holds only an icon needs an `aria-label`. Every attribute that is not
a prop goes to the checkbox (`name`, `value`, `checked`, `disabled`, `aria-label`, `x-on:change`), except `class`,
which goes to the label.

For several toggles that belong together, one of which (or any of which) can be on, use a
[toggle group](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toggle-group.md).

### Outline

**Example: Outline.**

```html
<sl:toggle variant="outline" name="italicText" aria-label="Italic"><sl:icon name="italic"/></sl:toggle>
<sl:toggle variant="outline" name="underlineText" aria-label="Underline"><sl:icon name="underline"/></sl:toggle>
```

### With text

**Example: With text.**

```html
<sl:toggle name="italicLabel"><sl:icon name="italic"/> Italic</sl:toggle>
```

### Sizes

**Example: Sizes.**

```html
<sl:toggle size="sm" variant="outline" name="sizeSm">Small</sl:toggle>
<sl:toggle variant="outline" name="sizeDefault">Default</sl:toggle>
<sl:toggle size="lg" variant="outline" name="sizeLg">Large</sl:toggle>
```

### In a form

`th:field` on a boolean field checks the toggle when the field is `true`, as on a [checkbox](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/checkbox.md): Spring
numbers the `id` (`starred1`) and adds a hidden marker input, so unchecking it submits `false`. In a
[field](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md), the toggle takes the field's name as its `id` and is described by the field's description and
error.

**Example: In a form.** th:field on a boolean checks the toggle from the form object, as on a checkbox.

```html
<sl:toggle th:field="*{starred}" variant="outline"><sl:icon name="star"/> Starred</sl:toggle>
```

### Invalid

**Example: Invalid.** With th:field, a field that has errors marks the toggle aria-invalid by itself.

```html
<sl:toggle th:field="*{pinned}" variant="outline"><sl:icon name="bookmark"/> Pin to the top</sl:toggle>
```

### Disabled

**Example: Disabled.**

```html
<sl:toggle name="boldLocked" disabled aria-label="Bold"><sl:icon name="bold"/></sl:toggle>
<sl:toggle name="underlineLocked" variant="outline" checked disabled aria-label="Underline"><sl:icon
    name="underline"/></sl:toggle>
```

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:toggle>`: A button that is on or off: a checkbox drawn as a button, so it submits, binds and works as one without JavaScript, and a screen reader announces it as checked or not. The content (text, icons) is its label; give a toggle that holds only an icon an `aria-label`. Every attribute that is not a prop (`th:field`, `name`, `value`, `checked`, `disabled`, `aria-label`, `x-*`) passes through to the `input`, except `class`, which goes to the `label` around it, and `th:text` and `th:utext`, which set the label. `th:field` on a boolean works as on a checkbox; the toggle is marked `aria-invalid` when its field has errors.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline` | `default` | Visual style of the toggle. |
| `size` | `sm`, `default`, `lg` | `default` | Size of the toggle. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Toggle Group

> A set of toggles of which one or several can be on, as radio buttons or checkboxes bound with th:field.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toggle-group/

**Example: Toggle group.**

```html
<sl:toggle-group type="multiple" name="textStyle" aria-label="Text style">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="bold" aria-label="Bold"><sl:icon name="bold"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="italic" aria-label="Italic"><sl:icon name="italic"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="underline" aria-label="Underline"><sl:icon name="underline"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
```

The items of a toggle group are [toggles](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toggle.md) around radio buttons (`type="single"`, the default: one item is
on at a time) or checkboxes (`type="multiple"`: any number). The form submits them as such inputs, and they work
without JavaScript. In a `single` group the arrow keys move between the items and Tab leaves the group, as in any
group of radio buttons; in a `multiple` group every item is a tab stop.

Name the group with `aria-label`, or with `aria-labelledby` pointing at its label. Give each item a `value`; an item
that holds only an icon needs an `aria-label`.

### Outline

**Example: Outline.**

```html
<sl:toggle-group variant="outline" name="callFilter" aria-label="Calls">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="all" checked>All</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="missed">Missed</sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
```

### In a form

Write `th:field` on the group, once. The items share it: Spring gives them the field's `name`, numbers their ids
(`layout1`, `layout2`, ...) and checks the one whose value matches the field. Without a form object, `name` on the
group gives the items their name. A `single` group with neither gets a generated name, so its items still exclude
each other; give it a `name` when the form should submit it.

**Example: In a form.** th:field on the group: the items are radio buttons that share it, and the one with the field's value is on.

```html
<p id="layout-label">Layout</p>
<sl:toggle-group th:field="*{layout}" variant="outline" aria-labelledby="layout-label">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="list"><sl:icon name="list"/> List</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="grid"><sl:icon name="layout-grid"/> Grid</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="table"><sl:icon name="table-2"/> Table</sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
```

In a [field set](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md#radio-buttons-and-checkbox-groups) with `th:field`, the group takes the field set's, and
the legend labels it.

### Multiple

With `type="multiple"`, bind a collection (a `List`, a `Set`, an array). Spring checks every item whose value is in
it, and adds a hidden marker input, so a form with no item on clears the collection.

**Example: Multiple.** type="multiple" makes the items checkboxes: th:field binds a collection, and Spring's hidden marker input lets the form clear it.

```html
<sl:toggle-group type="multiple" th:field="*{workdays}" variant="outline" aria-label="Workdays">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="MONDAY">Mon</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="TUESDAY">Tue</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="WEDNESDAY">Wed</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="THURSDAY">Thu</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="FRIDAY">Fri</sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
```

### Sizes

**Example: Sizes.**

```html
<sl:toggle-group size="sm" variant="outline" spacing="0" name="positionSm" aria-label="Position, small">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="top" checked>Top</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="bottom">Bottom</sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
<sl:toggle-group variant="outline" spacing="0" name="positionDefault" aria-label="Position">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="top" checked>Top</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="bottom">Bottom</sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
<sl:toggle-group size="lg" variant="outline" spacing="0" name="positionLg" aria-label="Position, large">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="top" checked>Top</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="bottom">Bottom</sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
```

### Spacing

`spacing` sets the gap between the items, in Tailwind's spacing steps: `2` (`0.5rem`) by default, `0` to join them
into one control.

**Example: Spacing.** spacing="0" joins the items into one control; the default is 2.

```html
<sl:toggle-group variant="outline" spacing="0" name="alignment" aria-label="Alignment">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="left" checked aria-label="Align left"><sl:icon name="align-left"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="center" aria-label="Align center"><sl:icon name="align-center"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="right" aria-label="Align right"><sl:icon name="align-right"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
<sl:toggle-group variant="outline" spacing="4" name="alignmentSpaced" aria-label="Alignment, spaced">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="left" checked aria-label="Align left"><sl:icon name="align-left"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="center" aria-label="Align center"><sl:icon name="align-center"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="right" aria-label="Align right"><sl:icon name="align-right"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
```

### Vertical

**Example: Vertical.**

```html
<sl:toggle-group orientation="vertical" variant="outline" spacing="0" name="rowDensity" aria-label="Density">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="compact">Compact</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="comfortable" checked>Comfortable</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="spacious">Spacious</sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
```

### Invalid

A field with errors marks every item `aria-invalid="true"`.

**Example: Invalid.** With th:field, a field that has errors marks every item aria-invalid by itself.

```html
<sl:toggle-group th:field="*{shippingSpeed}" variant="outline" aria-label="Shipping">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="standard">Standard</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="express">Express</sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
```

### Disabled

**Example: Disabled.** disabled on the group disables every item; on an item, only that one.

```html
<sl:toggle-group type="multiple" disabled name="textStyleLocked" aria-label="Text style (locked)">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="bold" checked aria-label="Bold"><sl:icon name="bold"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="italic" aria-label="Italic"><sl:icon name="italic"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
<sl:toggle-group variant="outline" name="billingPeriod" aria-label="Plan">
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="monthly" checked>Monthly</sl:toggle-group-item>
  <sl:toggle-group-item value="yearly" disabled>Yearly</sl:toggle-group-item>
</sl:toggle-group>
```

### With tooltips

Put an item in a [tooltip](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/tooltip.md). The group still joins the items, the tooltip shows on hover and when the
keyboard focuses the item, and its text describes the item's input. An icon-only item already has its
`aria-label`: give the `sl:tooltip` `mode="label"`, so a screen reader does not say it twice.

**Example: With tooltips.** A sl:tooltip around an item. The tooltip shows next to the item, on hover and on keyboard focus; mode="label" because it repeats the item's aria-label.

```html
<sl:toggle-group variant="outline" spacing="0" name="tooltipAlign" aria-label="Alignment with tooltips">
  <sl:tooltip mode="label">
    <sl:toggle-group-item value="left" checked aria-label="Align left"><sl:icon name="align-left"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
    <sl:tooltip-content>Align left</sl:tooltip-content>
  </sl:tooltip>
  <sl:tooltip mode="label">
    <sl:toggle-group-item value="center" aria-label="Align center"><sl:icon name="align-center"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
    <sl:tooltip-content>Align center</sl:tooltip-content>
  </sl:tooltip>
  <sl:tooltip mode="label">
    <sl:toggle-group-item value="right" aria-label="Align right"><sl:icon name="align-right"/></sl:toggle-group-item>
    <sl:tooltip-content>Align right</sl:tooltip-content>
  </sl:tooltip>
</sl:toggle-group>
```

### Attributes

#### sl:toggle-group

Attributes of `<sl:toggle-group>`: A row of `sl:toggle-group-item`s, of which one (`type="single"`, radio buttons) or several (`type="multiple"`, checkboxes) can be on. The items submit, bind and work as those inputs, without JavaScript. Name the group with `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`. Give it the `th:field` (or the `name`) its items share, or put it in a `sl:field-set` with `th:field`; each item then only needs a `value`. Every other attribute that is not a prop passes through to the group.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `type` | `single`, `multiple` | `single` | `single`: one item is on at a time, as radio buttons (the arrow keys move between them). `multiple`: any number, as checkboxes. |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline` | `default` | Visual style of the items. |
| `size` | `sm`, `default`, `lg` | `default` | Size of the items. |
| `spacing` | `0`, `1`, `2`, `3`, `4` | `2` | The gap between the items, in Tailwind's spacing steps (`2` is `0.5rem`). `0` joins them into one control. |
| `orientation` | `horizontal`, `vertical` | `horizontal` | Put the items on a row, or in a column. |
| `name` | *string* | — | The `name` every item submits under, for a group without `th:field`. A `single` group without either gets a generated one, so its items still exclude each other. |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | Disable every item. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:type="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:toggle-group-item

Attributes of `<sl:toggle-group-item>`: One item of a `sl:toggle-group`, drawn as a `sl:toggle`: a radio button in a `single` group, a checkbox in a `multiple` one. Give it a `value`; it takes the group's `th:field` or `name`, variant, size and `disabled`. The content is its label; give an item that holds only an icon an `aria-label`. Every attribute that is not a prop (`value`, `checked`, `disabled`, `aria-label`) passes through to the `input`, except `class`, which goes to the `label` around it, and `th:text` and `th:utext`, which set the label. With `th:field`, the item is marked `aria-invalid` when its field has errors.

`sl:toggle-group-item` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Calendar

> shadcn/ui's Calendar over native date inputs, bound with th:field; a date or a range of dates.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/calendar/

**Example: Calendar.** A date in a field; th:field binds the date input the calendar draws over.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{dueDate}">
  <sl:field-label>Due date</sl:field-label>
  <sl:calendar/>
</sl:field>
```

### Drawn over date inputs

A calendar's value lives in real `<input type="date">` elements. The server renders them, and the `slCalendar` Alpine
component hides them and draws the month in their place. So `th:field` binds a calendar as it binds any input, and
the form submits ISO dates (`2026-10-23`). Without JavaScript, the date inputs show as they are and still work.

```html
<form th:object="${task}" th:action="@{/tasks}" method="post">
  <sl:field th:field="*{dueDate}">
    <sl:field-label>Due date</sl:field-label>
    <sl:calendar/>
    <sl:field-error/>
  </sl:field>
</form>
```

Without content, `<sl:calendar>` renders one date input itself. That input takes every attribute that is not a prop:
`th:field`, `name`, `required`, `min`, `max`, `disabled`, `hx-*`. `class` is the exception: it stays on the calendar.

#### LocalDate fields

A date input only takes ISO dates, so the field has to be formatted as one. Do one of these:

- Annotate the field with `@DateTimeFormat(iso = DateTimeFormat.ISO.DATE)`.
- Set `spring.mvc.format.date=iso` for the whole application.

Without either, Spring formats a `LocalDate` for the locale (`10/23/26`), which a date input shows as empty.

### Range

For a range, give the calendar two date inputs as content: the start, then the end. Each input binds its own field.
The calendar's own attributes then stay on the calendar, so give it an `aria-label` that names the range, and give
each input a name of its own.

**Example: Range.** Two date inputs, the start and the end, make a range. Two months show side by side.

```html
<sl:calendar aria-label="Stay" number-of-months="2">
  <sl:input type="date" th:field="*{checkIn}" aria-label="Check-in"/>
  <sl:input type="date" th:field="*{checkOut}" aria-label="Check-out"/>
</sl:calendar>
```

The first pick sets the start. The second sets the end; if it comes before the start, the two swap. While only the
start is picked, the band follows the pointer. A pick on a complete range starts a new one.

`number-of-months` shows several months side by side; on a narrow screen they stack.

### Month and year selects

`caption-layout="dropdown"` replaces the month's name with a select for the month and one for the year. This helps
for dates far from today, such as a date of birth. `dropdown-months` and `dropdown-years` show only one of the two.

The years run from `start-month` to `end-month`. By default those are the months of the inputs' `min` and `max`, or
else 100 years back and 10 years ahead. Both also stop the previous and next buttons.

**Example: Month and year selects.** caption-layout="dropdown" for dates far from today, such as a date of birth.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{birthDate}">
  <sl:field-label>Date of birth</sl:field-label>
  <sl:calendar caption-layout="dropdown" start-month="1926-01" end-month="2026-12"/>
</sl:field>
```

### Disabled dates

The inputs' `min` and `max` disable the days outside them. `disabled-dates` disables more, comma-separated:

- a date: `2026-12-25`;
- a range: `2026-12-24..2026-12-31`;
- a day of the week: `sat`, `sun`.

From a list in the model, join it: `th:disabled-dates="${#strings.listJoin(booked, ',')}"`. A `LocalDate` prints as
an ISO date.

**Example: Disabled dates.** Weekends and the booked days cannot be picked; neither can the days outside min and max.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{meetingDate}">
  <sl:field-label>Meeting</sl:field-label>
  <sl:calendar min="2026-10-05" max="2026-11-27" disabled-dates="sat,sun,2026-10-14..2026-10-16,2026-10-28"/>
</sl:field>
```

A disabled day still takes the focus, so the arrow keys move across it, but it cannot be picked. Only the calendar
knows about `disabled-dates`; a date input does not. So check the date on the server too, as you would any input.

### Week numbers and the first day of the week

The page's locale (Spring's `LocaleResolver`) decides the first day of the week and how weeks are numbered. In most
of Europe weeks start on Monday and are ISO weeks; in the United States they start on Sunday, and the week of
1 January is week 1. `week-starts-on` overrides the first day: 0 is Sunday, 1 Monday, up to 6. `show-week-number`
adds a column with the week numbers.

The names of the months and days come from the browser (`Intl`), in the page's language.

**Example: Week numbers.** The week numbers, and Monday as the first day of the week, as the locale counts them unless week-starts-on says otherwise.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{sprintStart}">
  <sl:field-label>Sprint start</sl:field-label>
  <sl:calendar show-week-number week-starts-on="1"/>
</sl:field>
```

### In a form

- **`th:field`**, on the calendar or on its [field](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md), binds the input. In a field, the input also takes the
  field's `id` (the label points at it) and `aria-describedby`. The calendar takes the label of its input as its own
  name.
- **Errors.** A field with errors marks the input `aria-invalid="true"`, and the field's error describes the calendar.
- **Events.** A pick fires `input` and `change` on the input, so `hx-trigger="change"` and your own listeners work as
  on a date input. The month and year selects fire nothing outside the calendar.
- **A value set by a script** shows once the script fires `change` on the input, as it should for any input.
- **Form reset** puts the inputs back to their rendered values, and the calendar shows them.
- **A click on the label**, `autofocus` or the browser's validation focus the input; the calendar passes the focus on
  to the day that takes it.

**Example: With errors.** In a field with errors, the input is aria-invalid and the calendar is described by the message.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{deliveryDate}">
  <sl:field-label>Delivery</sl:field-label>
  <sl:calendar default-month="2026-10"/>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

### Disabled and read-only

`disabled` on the input, or on a `<sl:field-set>` around it, disables the calendar: no day takes the focus.
`readonly` keeps the navigation but stops the picking.

**Example: Disabled.**

```html
<sl:calendar name="closedDate" aria-label="Closed on" value="2026-10-23" disabled/>
```

### Keyboard

The previous and next buttons are tab stops, and the month is one more. Within the month:

| Key | Moves the focus to |
| --- | --- |
| Arrow left, arrow right | The previous, next day (swapped on a right-to-left page) |
| Arrow up, arrow down | The same day a week earlier, later |
| Home, End | The first, last day of the week |
| Page Up, Page Down | The same day a month earlier, later (or the month's last day when it is shorter) |
| Shift + Page Up, Shift + Page Down | The same day a year earlier, later |
| Enter, Space | Picks the day |

When the focus moves past the months that show, the calendar shows the next ones. A change of month is announced.

### With htmx

- Back restores the calendar as htmx saved it, and `slCalendar` draws it again.
- `hx-trigger="change"` on the calendar's input (or on the form) sends the picked date.

### Left out

These react-day-picker props, which shadcn's Calendar passes on, have no counterpart, on purpose:

- **`mode`.** The inputs decide: one is a date, two are a range. `mode="multiple"` has no counterpart yet.
- **`selected` and `onSelect`.** The inputs are the value: `th:field` renders it, and the input's `change` event
  reports a new one.
- **`disabled` with matchers.** `disabled` disables the whole calendar, as on any input; the days go in
  `disabled-dates`.
- **`timeZone`.** A date input holds a date without a time, so there is no time zone to get wrong.
- **`buttonVariant`, `classNames`, `components` and `formatters`.** The skins draw the calendar; the locale formats it.

For a button that opens the calendar in a popover, see the [date picker](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/date-picker.md).

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:calendar>`: A month calendar to pick a date or a range of dates, drawn over native date inputs: the inputs are its value, so `th:field` binds it and the form submits ISO dates (`2026-10-23`). Without content, it renders one date input itself, which takes every attribute that is not a prop except `class` (`th:field`, `name`, `required`, `min`, `max`), and in a `sl:field` the field's `th:field`, id and `aria-describedby`. For a range, give it two `sl:input type="date"`s as content, the start and the end; its attributes then go to the calendar. `slCalendar` hides the inputs and draws the month; without JavaScript the inputs show as they are.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `caption-layout` | `label`, `dropdown`, `dropdown-months`, `dropdown-years` | `label` | The month's caption: its name, or selects for the month and year, the month, or the year. |
| `number-of-months` | *number* | `1` | How many months show side by side (stacked on a narrow screen). |
| `show-outside-days` | *boolean* | `true` | Show the days of the previous and next month that fill the first and last week. |
| `show-week-number` | *boolean* | — | Show the week numbers, counted as the page's locale counts them (ISO weeks in most of Europe). |
| `week-starts-on` | *number* | — | The first day of the week: 0 is Sunday, 1 Monday, up to 6. By default the page's locale decides. |
| `default-month` | *string* | — | The month shown first, as `2026-10`. By default the month of the chosen date (or of the start of the range), or else the current month. |
| `start-month` | *string* | — | The first month the calendar navigates to, as `2026-01`; also the first year of the year select. By default the month of the inputs' `min`, or else 100 years ago. |
| `end-month` | *string* | — | The last month the calendar navigates to, as `2026-12`; also the last year of the year select. By default the month of the inputs' `max`, or else 10 years from now. |
| `disabled-dates` | *string* | — | Days that cannot be picked, comma-separated: a date (`2026-12-25`), a range (`2026-12-24..2026-12-31`) or a day of the week (`sat`, `sun`). The inputs' `min` and `max` disable the days outside them too. Only the calendar knows: check the date on the server as well. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:caption-layout="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

### JavaScript

`slCalendar` sits on the calendar: `<div class="calendar" x-data="slCalendar">`.

- **Without Alpine the date inputs show**, and the form works with them.
- **With it**, the inputs stay in the form, hidden, and still submit, reset and validate. `slCalendar` adds a
  `.calendar-months` element with the navigation and a `<table role="grid">` per month after them.
- **It needs no Alpine plugin.** If you load Alpine yourself (`shadleaf.assets.alpine=external`), plain Alpine is
  enough.
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:calendar>`**: an element has one `x-data`. Listen for `change` on the input
  instead.

---

## Date Picker

> A button showing the chosen date that opens a calendar in a popover; a date or a range, bound with th:field.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/date-picker/

**Example: Date picker.** A date in a field. The button shows "Pick a date" until a date is chosen; a pick closes the popover.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{pickupDate}">
  <sl:field-label>Pickup date</sl:field-label>
  <sl:date-picker/>
</sl:field>
```

### A popover with a calendar

shadcn/ui has no date picker component. Its docs build one from a [popover](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/popover.md), a button and a
[calendar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/calendar.md), plus some React state that updates the button's text and closes the popover after a pick.
`<sl:date-picker>` is that composition as one component, with the glue in the `slDatePicker` Alpine component.

The value lives in the calendar's date inputs, so `th:field` binds a date picker as it binds a calendar, and the form
submits ISO dates (`2026-10-23`). The [LocalDate fields](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/calendar.md#localdate-fields) note applies here too.

```html
<form th:object="${booking}" th:action="@{/bookings}" method="post">
  <sl:field th:field="*{pickupDate}">
    <sl:field-label>Pickup date</sl:field-label>
    <sl:date-picker/>
    <sl:field-error/>
  </sl:field>
</form>
```

Every attribute that is not a prop goes to the calendar inside. `class` is the exception: it stays on the date picker.
So the calendar's props work on the date picker (`caption-layout`, `number-of-months`, `disabled-dates`, ...), and so
