do its input's attributes (`th:field`, `name`, `required`, `min`, `max`, `disabled`, `hx-*`).

### The button's text

The button shows the chosen date, or `placeholder` ("Pick a date") while there is none.

- **On the server**, the date is formatted in the page's locale (Spring's `LocaleResolver`). That is what shows before
  JavaScript runs.
- **In the browser**, `slDatePicker` formats it again with `Intl`, for the same locale, when it starts and after every
  change. Java and `Intl` both use the CLDR's patterns, so the two texts agree.

`format` picks the style, as `Intl.DateTimeFormat`'s `dateStyle` and Java's `FormatStyle`:

| `format` | en-US | nl-BE |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `short` | 10/23/26 | 23/10/2026 |
| `medium` | Oct 23, 2026 | 23 okt 2026 |
| `long` (default) | October 23, 2026 | 23 oktober 2026 |
| `full` | Friday, October 23, 2026 | vrijdag 23 oktober 2026 |

**Example: Format.** format is Intl's dateStyle (Java's FormatStyle on the server); long is the default.

```html
<sl:field-group>
  <sl:field th:field="*{reminderShort}">
    <sl:field-label>Short</sl:field-label>
    <sl:date-picker format="short"/>
  </sl:field>
  <sl:field th:field="*{reminderFull}">
    <sl:field-label>Full</sl:field-label>
    <sl:date-picker format="full"/>
  </sl:field>
</sl:field-group>
```

**Example: With a date.** The server formats the bound date in the page's locale; the browser formats it the same way after a pick.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{appointmentDate}">
  <sl:field-label>Appointment</sl:field-label>
  <sl:date-picker/>
</sl:field>
```

### Range

For a range, give the date picker two date inputs as content, the start and then the end, and an `aria-label` that
names the range. The popover closes after the second pick. The button shows both ends: `Oct 12, 2026 – Oct 21, 2026`
with `format="medium"` (the separator is the message `sl.date-picker.range`, see [Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md)).

**Example: Range.** Two date inputs, the start and the end, make a range; the popover closes after the second pick.

```html
<sl:date-picker aria-label="Trip" format="medium" number-of-months="2">
  <sl:input type="date" th:field="*{tripStart}" aria-label="Departure"/>
  <sl:input type="date" th:field="*{tripEnd}" aria-label="Return"/>
</sl:date-picker>
```

### Date of birth

The calendar's month and year selects (`caption-layout="dropdown"`) help with dates far from today.

**Example: Date of birth.** The calendar's props pass through, such as caption-layout="dropdown" for a date far from today.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{dateOfBirth}">
  <sl:field-label>Date of birth</sl:field-label>
  <sl:date-picker format="medium" placeholder="Select date" caption-layout="dropdown" start-month="1926-01"
                  end-month="2026-12" max="2026-10-23"/>
</sl:field>
```

### In a form

- **The label.** In a [field](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md), the label points at the date input inside the popover, as for a calendar.
  The button takes the label's text and the date it shows as its name ("Pickup date, October 23, 2026"), and a click
  on the label focuses the button.
- **Errors.** A field with errors marks the input and the button `aria-invalid="true"`, and the field's error
  describes both.
- **Validation.** The browser can only point at a control it can show. When a `required` date is empty on submit,
  the popover opens, so the browser's message points at the calendar.
- **Events.** A pick fires `input` and `change` on the input, as on a [calendar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/calendar.md#in-a-form).
- **A value set by a script** shows on the button once the script fires `change` on the input.
- **Form reset** puts the button's text back too.

**Example: With errors.** The field's error marks the button aria-invalid and describes it. A required empty date opens the popover on submit.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{returnDate}">
  <sl:field-label>Return date</sl:field-label>
  <sl:date-picker required default-month="2026-10"/>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

### Disabled

`disabled` on the input, or on a `<sl:field-set>` around it, disables the button.

**Example: Disabled.**

```html
<sl:date-picker name="archivedOn" aria-label="Archived on" value="2026-10-23" disabled/>
```

### Keyboard

| Key | On | Does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Enter, Space | The button | Opens the popover, with the focus on the chosen day, or else today |
| Arrows, Home, End, Page Up, Page Down | A day | Move the focus, as in the [calendar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/calendar.md#keyboard) |
| Enter, Space | A day | Picks it, and closes the popover once the date (or both ends of the range) is chosen |
| Escape | The popover | Closes it, and the focus goes back to the button |
| Tab | The popover's last stop | Closes it, as the focus leaves it |

The popover is a non-modal dialog, as [sl:popover](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/popover.md) is. The focus is not trapped in it.

### With htmx

- Back restores the date picker as htmx saved it, and `slDatePicker` formats the date again.
- `hx-trigger="change"` on the date picker's input (`<sl:date-picker hx-post="..." hx-trigger="change">`) or on the
  form sends the picked date.

### Left out

- **The input you type into** (shadcn's "Input" demo), **the time** and **natural language** variants are not here
  yet. For a time, put an `<sl:input type="time">` next to the date picker.
- **Presets** (a select of "Today", "Tomorrow", ...) are not here either.

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:date-picker>`: A button showing the chosen date that opens a `sl:calendar` in a popover: shadcn/ui's date picker, made of its popover and calendar, as one component. Its value lives in the calendar's date inputs, so `th:field` binds it and the form submits ISO dates. Every attribute that is not a prop goes to the `sl:calendar` inside, except `class`, which stays on the date picker: the calendar's props (`caption-layout`, `number-of-months`, `disabled-dates`, ...) and, for a single date, the attributes of its input (`th:field`, `name`, `required`, `min`, `max`). In a `sl:field`, the input takes the field's `th:field`, id and `aria-describedby`, and the button is named by the field's label. For a range, give it two `sl:input type="date"`s as content, the start and the end, and an `aria-label`. A pick closes the popover; for a range the second pick does. Needs JavaScript (`slDatePicker`).

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `placeholder` | *string* | — | The button's text while no date is chosen. By default "Pick a date". |
| `format` | `short`, `medium`, `long`, `full` | `long` | How the button shows the date, in the page's locale: as `Intl.DateTimeFormat`'s `dateStyle` and Java's `FormatStyle` (`10/23/26`, `Oct 23, 2026`, `October 23, 2026`, `Friday, October 23, 2026`). |
| `popover-id` | *string* | — | The popover's id, which the button points at with `popovertarget`. By default a generated one (`sl-date-picker-` and eight random characters). |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:placeholder="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

### JavaScript

`slDatePicker` sits on the date picker: `<div class="date-picker" x-data="slDatePicker">`. Its popover has
`slPopover` and its calendar `slCalendar`.

- **It needs JavaScript.** Without Alpine, the button still opens the popover (`popovertarget`), but it holds the bare
  date inputs, and the button's text does not follow them.
- **It needs no Alpine plugin.** If you load Alpine yourself (`shadleaf.assets.alpine=external`), plain Alpine is
  enough.
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:date-picker>`**: an element has one `x-data`. Listen for `change` on the input
  instead.

---

## File Upload

> A drop zone with a Browse button and the list of the files picked, around a file input bound with th:field.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/file-upload/

**Example: File upload.** Drop files on it or browse. Each file shows as an attachment with a remove button; a file of another type or larger than max-size shows as an error.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{uploadAttachments}">
  <sl:field-label>Attachments</sl:field-label>
  <sl:file-upload multiple accept="image/png,image/jpeg,application/pdf" max-size="10MB">
    <sl:slot name="description">PNG, JPG, PDF up to 10 MB</sl:slot>
  </sl:file-upload>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

### A file input with a drop zone

shadcn/ui has no file upload component, so this one is Shadleaf's own. It looks like shadcn's file upload card: an
outlined [empty state](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/empty.md) with an icon, a title, a description and a Browse button.

Underneath is a plain `<input type="file">`, visually hidden. It holds the files and the form submits it, so it binds
with `th:field` as any other input does, to a `MultipartFile`, or with `multiple` to a `List<MultipartFile>`. The form
needs `enctype="multipart/form-data"`:

```html
<form th:object="${upload}" th:action="@{/people/{id}/files(id=${person.id})}" method="post"
      enctype="multipart/form-data">
  <sl:field th:field="*{files}">
    <sl:field-label>Attachments</sl:field-label>
    <sl:file-upload multiple accept="image/png,image/jpeg,application/pdf" max-size="10MB">
      <sl:slot name="description">PNG, JPG, PDF up to 10 MB</sl:slot>
    </sl:file-upload>
    <sl:field-error/>
  </sl:field>
  <sl:button type="submit">Upload</sl:button>
</form>
```

```java
public class UploadForm {
  @NotEmpty
  private List<MultipartFile> files = new ArrayList<>();
  // getter and setter
}
```

Every attribute that is not a prop goes to the input: `th:field`, `name`, `id`, `required`, `disabled`, `aria-*`,
`hx-*`. `class` stays on the component. In an [`<sl:field>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/field.md) the input takes the field's binding, its id
(which the field's label points at), `aria-describedby` and `aria-invalid`, and a field error colours the drop zone's
border.

Spring Boot limits uploads to 1 MB a file and 10 MB a request by default. Raise
`spring.servlet.multipart.max-file-size` and `max-request-size` to match `max-size`.

### What the script adds

With JavaScript (the `slFileUpload` Alpine component), the component does more:

- **Drop.** Files dropped anywhere on the component are added. The drop zone takes the ring colour while files are
  dragged over it.
- **A list.** Each file shows below the drop zone as an [`<sl:attachment>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/attachment.md) in its `idle` state, with
  its name, type and size and a button that removes it. The list is in the order the files were added.
- **Adding more.** With `multiple`, picking or dropping files again adds them to the ones there; the browser would
  replace them. A file that is there already is not added twice. Without `multiple`, a new file replaces the old one.
- **Checks.** A file that does not match `accept`, or is larger than `max-size`, is not added. It shows as an
  attachment in its `error` state, with the reason, until it is dismissed or files are added again. The browser's
  picker applies `accept` by itself, but a drop does not, and the picker lets one choose any file.
- **Announcements.** Added, removed and rejected files are announced once per change, in one polite live region: "Added
  report.pdf and photo.png."

Everything still goes through the input: the script puts the files back with a `DataTransfer` (`input.files =
dt.files`), so the form submits exactly what the list shows, and a drop dispatches `change` as a pick does, so
`hx-trigger="change"` on the component works for both.

**Example: In a card.** As shadcn's file upload card.

```html
<sl:card>
  <sl:card-header>
    <sl:card-title>File Upload</sl:card-title>
    <sl:card-description>Drag and drop or browse</sl:card-description>
  </sl:card-header>
  <sl:card-content>
    <sl:file-upload th:field="*{uploadCardFiles}" multiple max-size="10MB">
      <sl:slot name="description">PNG, JPG, PDF up to 10MB</sl:slot>
    </sl:file-upload>
  </sl:card-content>
</sl:card>
```

The server checks the files anyway: `accept` and `max-size` only spare the user a round trip. A browser without
JavaScript, or a request not made through the page, can send anything.

### One file

Without `multiple`, the title and button say so ("Upload a file", "Browse"). The slots `media`, `title` and
`description` replace the icon and the texts, and `browse-label` the button's text.

**Example: One file.** Without multiple, a new file replaces the one picked before. The slots and browse-label change the icon, title and button.

```html
<sl:file-upload th:field="*{uploadAvatar}" accept="image/*" max-size="2MB" browse-label="Choose a photo">
  <sl:slot name="media"><sl:icon name="image"/></sl:slot>
  <sl:slot name="title">Profile photo</sl:slot>
  <sl:slot name="description">An image of up to 2 MB</sl:slot>
</sl:file-upload>
```

### Image previews

With `previews`, a picked or dropped image shows a thumbnail in place of the file icon. It stays through the upload,
dimmed while the file is sent, processed, or failed, as an [attachment's image](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/attachment.md#states) is.

**Example: Image previews.** With previews, a picked image shows a thumbnail instead of the file icon, from a blob URL; other files keep theirs. A Content-Security-Policy must allow blob in img-src.

```html
<sl:file-upload th:field="*{uploadPhotos}" multiple accept="image/*,.pdf" max-size="10MB" previews>
  <sl:slot name="media"><sl:icon name="images"/></sl:slot>
  <sl:slot name="description">Images and PDFs up to 10 MB</sl:slot>
</sl:file-upload>
```

- **Which files.** Images the browser can show (`image/*`), up to 20 MB: decoding a larger photo takes much memory and
  time. A file the browser cannot decode (HEIC, outside Safari) keeps its icon, and no broken image shows: the
  thumbnail takes the icon's place only once it has loaded. SVG keeps its icon too. Shown in an `<img>` an SVG runs
  no script, but its `blob:` URL has the page's origin, and opened from it ("Open image in new tab") it would.
- **The image.** Its `alt` is empty: the file's name is right beside it.
- **The URLs.** Each thumbnail is a `blob:` URL (`URL.createObjectURL`), made in the browser: the file is not sent
  for it. It is revoked when the file leaves the list (removed, replaced, the next change after an upload), and when
  the component is swapped out. A page htmx saves in its history gets the icons back, so it holds no URL that no
  longer works.

#### Content-Security-Policy

A policy without `img-src`, or with `img-src 'self'`, blocks `blob:` images, so add it:

```text
img-src 'self' blob:
```

Without it the files keep their icons and the upload works as before. The browser reports the blocked image in the
console; after that, the page makes no more previews. See [Content-Security-Policy](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/csp.md).

### Grid

With `layout="grid"`, the picked files show as cards in columns that fill the width, instead of small attachments one
below the other. With `previews`, a photo then shows as a large square thumbnail: big enough to judge, say, the
photos of an inspection before they are sent with the rest of the form.

**Example: Grid.** With layout grid, the picked files show as cards in columns that fill the width, images with a large thumbnail, other files with their icon, rejected ones as errors.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{uploadInspectionPhotos}">
  <sl:field-label>Photos of defects</sl:field-label>
  <sl:file-upload multiple accept="image/*,.pdf" max-size="10MB" layout="grid" previews>
    <sl:slot name="media"><sl:icon name="images"/></sl:slot>
    <sl:slot name="description">Images and PDFs up to 10 MB</sl:slot>
  </sl:file-upload>
</sl:field>
```

```html
<sl:file-upload th:field="*{photos}" multiple accept="image/*,.pdf" max-size="10MB" layout="grid" previews>
  <sl:slot name="description">Images and PDFs up to 10 MB</sl:slot>
</sl:file-upload>
```

- **The cards.** Each file is a vertical [`<sl:attachment>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/attachment.md#image) at the default size, filling
  its cell; the grid is the one of [`<sl:attachment-group layout="grid">`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/attachment.md#grid), so both look
  alike. Other files show their icon in the same square, and a rejected file shows as an error card with the reason.
- **The buttons.** The remove or dismiss button sits in the card's corner, and is hidden while the files are sent.
- **The order.** The cards are in the order of the files in the input, as in the list, so the focus order, the focus
  after a remove and the announcements are the same.

Without JavaScript nothing changes: there is no list.

### Errors

A second submit renders the form again with the binding's errors, as for any field. The input is marked
`aria-invalid` and described by the error. A browser never keeps the files of a file input across a page load, so the
user picks them again.

**Example: With errors.** The server checks the files anyway. A field error marks the input aria-invalid and the drop zone's border destructive.

```html
<sl:field th:field="*{uploadContract}">
  <sl:field-label>Signed contract</sl:field-label>
  <sl:file-upload accept=".pdf">
    <sl:slot name="description">A PDF</sl:slot>
  </sl:file-upload>
  <sl:field-error/>
</sl:field>
```

### Disabled

A disabled input fades the Browse button, and the component takes no files.

**Example: Disabled.** A disabled input fades the Browse button, and the component takes no files.

```html
<sl:file-upload th:field="*{uploadArchive}" disabled>
  <sl:slot name="description">Uploads are closed for this project.</sl:slot>
</sl:file-upload>
```

### Accessibility

- The input is the only tab stop. Space or Enter opens the file picker, as on any file input; the Browse button is
  its `<label>` (`<sl:button as="label">`), so a click opens it too, and the input's focus ring is drawn on the button.
- The input's name is the field's label and the Browse button's text; it is described by the drop zone's description
  and the field's description and error.
- Each remove button is named after its file: "Remove report.pdf". After a remove, the focus moves to the next
  file's button, or back to the input.
- Dropping is a pointer gesture; the keyboard path is the input.
- An upload is announced once when it starts ("Uploading 3 files."), and again when it fails, is cancelled, or ends
  while the component is still there. The percentages are not announced; each uploading or processing attachment is
  `aria-busy`. After a Cancel, the focus goes back to the input.
- Its texts and announcements are messages (`sl.file-upload.*`); your own message files can reword them or add a
  language, as [Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md) explains.

### Without JavaScript

The Browse button still opens the picker and the form submits the files. There is no drop and no list: the browser
shows nothing of the picked files but what the hidden input would.

### htmx

The component can be swapped in and out. The browser never restores a file input's files, and htmx's history restore
renders the page's markup again: the list is drawn from the input's files whenever the component starts, so going back
shows an empty upload, never files that would not be sent. A page saved in the history while uploading comes back
as an upload that is not sending anything.

### Upload progress

When the form is sent with htmx (`hx-post` on the form, or a boosted form), the component shows how far the upload
is, with no attribute of its own. It takes part in every htmx request whose body carries its files, so the form needs
`enctype="multipart/form-data"` or `hx-encoding="multipart/form-data"`. The files go through the
[attachment's states](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/attachment.md#states):

1. **Uploading.** Each file shows the share of its bytes sent: "Uploading · 64%".
2. **Processing**, once the whole request is sent and the server is still working on it.
3. **The outcome.** On success the server's answer is swapped in, as for any htmx request. It usually replaces the
   component, with an empty form and what was uploaded, so the answer is what tells the user it worked. A
   [toast](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toast.md) or the focus on the new files does that well. If the component stays, its files show as
   "Uploaded" and leave the input, so a second submit does not send them again. On a failed request (an error status,
   no connection, a timeout), the files show "Upload failed" and stay in the input, so the user can submit again.

**Example: States.** idle draws a dashed border, uploading and processing shimmer the title, error turns destructive. The description says the state too.

```html
<sl:attachment state="idle" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="clock"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>selected-file.pdf</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>Ready to upload</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove selected-file.pdf"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
<sl:attachment state="uploading" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:spinner aria-hidden="true"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>design-system.zip</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>Uploading · 64%</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Cancel upload of design-system.zip"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
<sl:attachment state="processing" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-text"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>market-research.pdf</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>Processing document</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove market-research.pdf"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
<sl:attachment state="error" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-warning"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>financial-model.xlsx</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>Upload failed. Try again.</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Retry upload of financial-model.xlsx"><sl:icon name="refresh-cw"/></sl:attachment-action>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove financial-model.xlsx"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
<sl:attachment state="done" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="check"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>uploaded-report.pdf</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>Uploaded · 1.8 MB</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove uploaded-report.pdf"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
```

The browser reports the progress of the whole request, not of each file. The files go into the request one after the
other, in the list's order, so each one's share is worked out from where its bytes are in the request. The figure is
off by the few bytes of the form's other fields, which hardly shows.

While the files are sent, they cannot be added or removed: the input is disabled and the remove buttons are hidden. A
**Cancel upload** button takes the Browse button's place. It aborts the request (`htmx:abort`), and the files are back
as they were, ready to send again. All files go in one request, so cancelling stops them all.

- **htmx 2** sends with an `XMLHttpRequest`, and the component follows its upload.
- **htmx 4** sends with `fetch()`, which reports no upload progress. For a request that carries the files, the
  component gives htmx a `fetch` of its own (`ctx.fetch`), which sends with an `XMLHttpRequest`. Everything else
  (swapping, `HX-*` headers, history, `htmx:abort`) is still htmx's. htmx 4 aborts a request after 60 seconds by
  default; for large uploads, raise its `defaultTimeout` or set `hx-config` on the form. htmx 4 also swaps an error
  answer in, where htmx 2 leaves the page alone: if that answer replaces the component, it shows what went wrong
  instead of the failed files.

Without htmx, a plain POST, nothing of this happens: the browser shows its own progress, if any.

Spring Boot answers a request larger than `spring.servlet.multipart.max-request-size` with an error, which shows as a
failed upload: set the limits to what the form may send.

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:file-upload>`: A drop zone to pick files with, and the list of the files picked. Shadleaf's own: shadcn/ui has none. A visually hidden `&lt;input type="file"&gt;` holds the files and is submitted with the form, so put `th:field` on the component (a `MultipartFile`, or with `multiple` a `List&lt;MultipartFile&gt;`) and give the form `enctype="multipart/form-data"`. Every attribute that is not a prop goes to the input (`th:field`, `name`, `id`, `required`, `disabled`, `aria-*`, `hx-*`); `class` is appended to the component. In a `sl:field`, the input takes the field's `th:field`, id and `aria-describedby`. With JavaScript, files can be dropped too, each picked file shows as an `sl:attachment` with a remove button, and a file that does not match `accept` or is larger than `max-size` shows as an error. When the form is sent with htmx (2 or 4), each file shows its upload progress, then that the server is processing it, and an error if the request fails; a Cancel button stops the upload. With `previews`, a picked image shows a thumbnail. Without JavaScript, the Browse button still picks files. The server checks the files anyway.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `multiple` | *boolean* | — | Take more than one file. Picking or dropping files adds them to the ones already there. |
| `accept` | *string* | — | The file types to take, as the input's `accept`: extensions (`.pdf`), MIME types (`application/pdf`) or `image/*`, separated by commas. A dropped file of another type is not added. |
| `max-size` | *string* | — | The largest file to take, as Spring Boot writes sizes: `10MB`, `500KB`. A larger file is not added. |
| `level` | *number* | — | Makes the title a heading of this level (`role="heading"`). |
| `previews` | *boolean* | — | Show a thumbnail of each picked image (up to 20 MB, no SVG) instead of the file icon. The thumbnail is a `blob:` URL: a Content-Security-Policy must allow it in `img-src`, or the icon stays. |
| `layout` | `list`, `grid` | `list` | `list` shows the picked files one below the other, as small attachments; `grid` as cards in columns that fill the width, the grid of `sl:attachment-group layout="grid"`: with `previews`, for photos one wants to see before sending them. |
| `browse-label` | *string* | — | The text of the Browse button. Defaults to "Browse files", or "Browse" without `multiple`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:multiple="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Dialog

> A modal window over the page, on the native dialog element.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog/

**Example: Dialog.** A form in a dialog. The form wraps the dialog, so the submit button in its footer submits it.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="edit-profile" command="show-modal">Edit profile</sl:button>
<form method="post" action="/profile">
  <sl:dialog id="edit-profile">
    <sl:dialog-header>
      <sl:dialog-title>Edit profile</sl:dialog-title>
      <sl:dialog-description>
        Make changes to your profile here. Click save when you're done.
      </sl:dialog-description>
    </sl:dialog-header>
    <sl:field-group>
      <sl:field for="profile-name">
        <sl:field-label>Name</sl:field-label>
        <sl:input name="name" value="Pedro Duarte"/>
      </sl:field>
      <sl:field for="profile-username">
        <sl:field-label>Username</sl:field-label>
        <sl:input name="username" value="@peduarte"/>
      </sl:field>
    </sl:field-group>
    <sl:dialog-footer>
      <sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="edit-profile" command="close">Cancel</sl:button>
      <sl:button type="submit">Save changes</sl:button>
    </sl:dialog-footer>
  </sl:dialog>
</form>
```

### Built from parts

A dialog is `<sl:dialog>` plus the parts you need, as in shadcn/ui: `<sl:dialog-header>` (holding
`<sl:dialog-title>` and `<sl:dialog-description>`), your own content and `<sl:dialog-footer>`. shadcn's `Dialog`,
`DialogContent`, `DialogOverlay` and `DialogPortal` are all `<sl:dialog>`: it renders the browser's own `<dialog>`
element, which is the panel, and its `::backdrop` is the overlay.

The browser does the hard parts. A dialog opened as a modal is drawn above everything else, the page behind it is inert
(no clicks, no focus, hidden from screen readers), focus moves to the first control in it, Escape closes it, and closing
returns focus to the button that opened it. A click outside closes it too. The page behind it does not scroll.

The title names the dialog and the description describes it (`aria-labelledby` and `aria-describedby`, with ids
derived from the dialog's `id`: `edit-profile-title`). A dialog without a title needs an `aria-label`.

### Opening and closing

Every dialog has an `id`, and the buttons that open and close it point at it with HTML's
[invoker commands](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Invoker_Commands_API): `commandfor` names the
dialog and `command` says what to do. No JavaScript of yours is involved.

```html
<sl:button commandfor="edit-profile" command="show-modal">Edit profile</sl:button>

<sl:dialog id="edit-profile">
  ...
  <sl:dialog-footer>
    <sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="edit-profile" command="close">Cancel</sl:button>
    <sl:button type="submit">Save changes</sl:button>
  </sl:dialog-footer>
</sl:dialog>
```

The close button in the corner does the same; `show-close-button="false"` leaves it out. `<sl:dialog-footer show-close-button>`
adds an outline "Close" button after the footer's own actions.

**Example: Close button in the footer.** No close button in the corner; the footer has one instead.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="share-link" command="show-modal">Share</sl:button>
<sl:dialog id="share-link" show-close-button="false">
  <sl:dialog-header>
    <sl:dialog-title>Share link</sl:dialog-title>
    <sl:dialog-description>Anyone who has this link will be able to view this.</sl:dialog-description>
  </sl:dialog-header>
  <sl:input aria-label="Link" value="https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/installation" readonly/>
  <sl:dialog-footer show-close-button/>
</sl:dialog>
```

A dialog per row of a table takes its id from an expression, and so do the buttons:

```html
<tr th:each="order : ${orders}">
  <td>
    <sl:button variant="outline" th:commandfor="|delete-${order.id}|" command="show-modal">Delete</sl:button>
    <sl:dialog th:id="|delete-${order.id}|">...</sl:dialog>
  </td>
</tr>
```

Invoker commands work in every current browser. For older ones (Safari before 26.2), and for a click outside where
the browser lacks `closedby`, the dialog's small Alpine component stands in, so both still work with Alpine on the page.
To react when a dialog closes, listen for the `close` event of the `<dialog>` element.

### A form in a dialog

Put the form around the dialog, as shadcn's example does, so the submit button in the footer submits it. After a full
page post, the page renders again and the dialog is closed. To show the form's errors in the dialog again, render it
with `open`, below.

### Rendered by the server: htmx

With htmx the dialog can come from the server when it is needed: a
button fetches it into an empty element on the page, and `open` shows it as a modal as soon as it is there.

```html
<!-- In the layout -->
<div id="modal-root"></div>

<!-- The button -->
<sl:button hx:get="@{/members/{id}/edit(id=${member.id})}" hx-target="#modal-root">Edit</sl:button>
```

The fragment the controller returns has the dialog, and inside it a form that swaps only itself. The submit button
sits in the footer, outside the form, and points at it with `form`:

```html
<sl:dialog th:fragment="edit-dialog" id="edit-member" open>
  <sl:dialog-header>
    <sl:dialog-title>Edit member</sl:dialog-title>
  </sl:dialog-header>
  <form th:fragment="edit-form" id="edit-member-form" th:object="${member}"
        hx:post="@{/members/{id}(id=${member.id})}" hx-target="this" hx-swap="outerHTML">
    <sl:form-errors autofocus/>
    <sl:field th:field="*{name}">
      <sl:field-label>Name</sl:field-label>
      <sl:input/>
      <sl:field-error/>
    </sl:field>
  </form>
  <sl:dialog-footer>
    <sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="edit-member" command="close">Cancel</sl:button>
    <sl:button type="submit" form="edit-member-form">Save</sl:button>
  </sl:dialog-footer>
</sl:dialog>
```

With errors, the controller returns the form alone: the dialog stays open and focus moves to the first field with an
error (see [form errors](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/form-errors.md)). When the save succeeds, it closes the dialog with an `HX-Trigger`
response header, swaps nothing where the form is, and updates the member's row out of band:

```java
@HxRequest
@PostMapping("/members/{id}")
public String update(@PathVariable long id, @Valid @ModelAttribute("member") EditMemberForm form,
    BindingResult bindingResult, HtmxResponse htmxResponse, Model model) {
  if (bindingResult.hasErrors()) {
    return "members :: edit-form";
  }
  model.addAttribute("member", memberService.update(id, form));
  htmxResponse.addTrigger("sl-dialog-close");
  htmxResponse.setReswap(HtmxReswap.none());
  return "members :: member-row-oob";  // the row, with hx-swap-oob="true"
}
```

htmx fires `sl-dialog-close` on the form that made the request; the event bubbles up to the dialog, which closes, and
focus returns to the button that fetched it. When the response then replaces that button, as the new row does here,
give the button an id (`th:id="|edit-member-${member.id}|"`): htmx moves focus to the element with the same id after a
swap. Without one, focus falls back to the top of the page. Use `HX-Trigger`, not `HX-Trigger-After-Swap` or `-After-Settle`: those
fire after the swap, when the element that made the request may be gone.

`open` needs Alpine (on by default: see [Alpine](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/alpine.md)); the browser has no attribute that opens a
dialog as a modal. A dialog removed from the page while it is open, by any swap, takes its backdrop and the page's
inertness with it. Before htmx 2 saves a page in its history cache (`hx-push-url`, `hx-boost`), open dialogs close, so
the back button never brings one back; htmx 4 keeps no cache, and loads the page again.

### Loaded into the dialog: a body

The dialog above appears only once the server has answered, and a slow answer leaves the user waiting with nothing to
show for the click. `<sl:dialog-body>` turns this around: the dialog is in the page, closed, and its body is what htmx
loads. A request that targets the body opens the dialog at once, and a spinner covers the body until the answer is
swapped in.

```html
<!-- The button, in each row -->
<sl:button hx:get="@{/members/{id}/edit(id=${member.id})}" hx-target="#edit-member-body">Edit</sl:button>

<!-- The dialog, once on the page -->
<sl:dialog id="edit-member">
  <sl:dialog-header>
    <sl:dialog-title>Edit member</sl:dialog-title>
  </sl:dialog-header>
  <sl:dialog-body/>
  <sl:dialog-footer>
    <sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="edit-member" command="close">Cancel</sl:button>
    <sl:button type="submit" form="edit-member-form">Save</sl:button>
  </sl:dialog-footer>
</sl:dialog>
```

The body's content has the id `<dialog id>-body`; the controller returns only what goes in it, here the form of the
previous section (`members :: edit-form`). Once it is in, the focus moves to its first field (or to one with
`autofocus`), as it would have had the form been there when the dialog opened. Saving works as before: with errors the
form swaps itself, and `HX-Trigger: sl-dialog-close` closes the dialog.

**Example: Loading.** While an htmx request into sl:dialog-body runs, a spinner covers it. Here the body keeps data-loading, which the script sets and removes.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="dialog-loading" command="show-modal">Edit member</sl:button>
<sl:dialog id="dialog-loading">
  <sl:dialog-header>
    <sl:dialog-title>Edit member</sl:dialog-title>
    <sl:dialog-description>Changes are saved when you click Save.</sl:dialog-description>
  </sl:dialog-header>
  <sl:dialog-body data-loading="true"/>
  <sl:dialog-footer show-close-button/>
</sl:dialog>
```

What the body does with a request that targets it (or an element inside it):

- **A request from outside opens the dialog**, as a modal, at once; focus returns to the button when it closes. A
  request from inside a closed dialog, such as content loaded with `hx-trigger="load"`, opens nothing.
- **The spinner shows after a moment** (200 ms), so a quick answer does not flicker. The body takes no clicks while
  the request runs, and a second request into it is not sent: clicking Save twice saves once. The content has
  `aria-busy="true"` meanwhile.
- **Closing the dialog aborts a request still running**, so a slow answer never fills a dialog the user has left.
- **An empty body is emptied again** once the dialog has closed, so the next member's dialog never shows the previous
  member while it loads. Content you put in the body in the page stays.
- **The header and the footer stay in view.** The body scrolls by itself when its content is taller than the screen.

#### When a request fails

When the request that opened the dialog fails (an error status, no connection, a timeout), the dialog closes and the
error is shown as a [toast](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toast.md). Without an `<sl:toaster>` on the page, or with `load-error="inline"`, the dialog
stays open and the error is shown in it, above the content. A failed request from inside the dialog, such as a save,
always shows the error in the dialog, so nothing the user typed is lost; the next request hides it.

The message is "Something went wrong. Please try again." (`sl.dialog.error` in your `messages.properties`; see [Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md)). The
`error` slot replaces it for one dialog:

```html
<sl:dialog-body>
  <sl:slot name="error">The member could not be loaded.</sl:slot>
</sl:dialog-body>
```

Validation errors are no failure: return the form with its errors and a success status, as above.

The body works with htmx 2 and htmx 4. htmx 4 swaps an error answer in by default; the body stops that for the
requests into it, so the error shows above the content, as with htmx 2. An `hx-status` attribute on the element that
sends the request, or an `HX-Reswap` header, still decides otherwise. See [htmx 4](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/htmx.md#htmx-4).

### Width

The skin decides the dialog's width (at most `28rem` in vega). Give the dialog a class and set its width in your own
stylesheet, loaded after the library's: the library's rules sit in a cascade layer, so yours win.

```html
<sl:dialog id="edit-member" class="dialog-wide">...</sl:dialog>
```

```css
.dialog-wide {
  max-width: min(42rem, calc(100% - 2rem));
}
```

If you compile Tailwind yourself (the `embedded` bundle), a utility class does the same: `class="sm:max-w-2xl"`.

### Attributes

#### sl:dialog

Attributes of `<sl:dialog>`: A modal dialog on the native `dialog` element: the page behind it is inert, Escape and a click outside close it, and focus returns to where it came from. Build it from `sl:dialog-header` (holding `sl:dialog-title` and `sl:dialog-description`), your content and `sl:dialog-footer`. A button opens it with `commandfor` set to the dialog's `id` and `command="show-modal"`, and closes it with `command="close"`; an htmx response closes it with the `sl-dialog-close` event.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | **required** | The dialog's id, which the buttons that open and close it point at with `commandfor`. The title and description ids derive from it. |
| `open` | *boolean* | — | Open the dialog as soon as it is on the page, for a dialog the server renders in answer to a request (an htmx swap, or a page shown after a redirect). Needs Alpine. |
| `show-close-button` | *boolean* | `true` | A close button (an x) in the corner. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:dialog-header

Attributes of `<sl:dialog-header>`: The top of a `sl:dialog`: a `sl:dialog-title` and an optional `sl:dialog-description` below it.

`sl:dialog-header` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:dialog-title

Attributes of `<sl:dialog-title>`: The title of a `sl:dialog`, inside `sl:dialog-header`: a heading that names the dialog (`aria-labelledby`). Its id derives from the dialog's (`edit-profile-title`).

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` | `1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5`, `6` | `2` | Heading level (`role="heading"` and `aria-level`). A dialog starts an outline of its own, so the default, 2, rarely needs changing. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:level="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:dialog-description

Attributes of `<sl:dialog-description>`: Supporting text below a `sl:dialog-title`, which describes the dialog (`aria-describedby`). Its id derives from the dialog's (`edit-profile-description`).

`sl:dialog-description` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:dialog-body

Attributes of `<sl:dialog-body>`: The content of a `sl:dialog` between its header and footer, which scrolls by itself so the header and footer stay in view. Its content has the id `&lt;id&gt;-body` (`edit-body`), the target for htmx: a request from outside that targets it opens the dialog at once and shows a spinner until the answer is swapped in; closing aborts a request still running. Left empty, it is emptied again when the dialog closes, so it never shows the previous content. When a request fails, the error shows in the dialog, or, for the request that opened it, as a toast (see `load-error`). Needs Alpine.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `load-error` | `toast`, `inline` | `toast` | What happens when the request that opened the dialog fails: `toast` closes it and shows the error as a toast (shown in the dialog when the page has no `sl:toaster`); `inline` keeps it open with the error in it. A failed request from inside (a form) always shows the error in the dialog, so nothing typed is lost. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:load-error="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:dialog-footer

Attributes of `<sl:dialog-footer>`: The bottom of a `sl:dialog`, for its actions: side by side at the end on a wide screen, stacked with the first one at the bottom on a narrow one.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `show-close-button` | *boolean* | — | An outline "Close" button after the other actions. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:show-close-button="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

### JavaScript

A small Alpine component, `slDialog`, adds what the browser's `<dialog>` cannot do: `<sl:dialog>` renders as
`<dialog class="dialog" x-data="slDialog">`.

- **Most of it works without Alpine.** Opening and closing with `commandfor`, Escape, a click outside and focus
  returning to the button are the browser's. Alpine is needed for `open`, for the `sl-dialog-close` event, for a
  body that htmx loads, and for invoker commands and a click outside in browsers that lack them. Alpine is included by default; see
  [Alpine](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/alpine.md).
- **It needs no Alpine plugin.** If you load Alpine yourself (`shadleaf.assets.alpine=external`), plain Alpine is
  enough.
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:dialog>`.** An element has one `x-data`, and attributes on `<sl:dialog>` end up on
  the `<dialog>`, so yours would replace `slDialog`. Put it on an element inside the dialog.

| Event | On | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sl-dialog-close` | the dialog, or anything inside it (it bubbles) | Closes the dialog. |
| `close` | the `<dialog>` (the browser's own) | Fired when it closes, however that happened. |

---

## Alert Dialog

> A modal dialog that interrupts the user with something important and waits for an answer.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/alert-dialog/

**Example: Alert dialog.** A question the user has to answer. Cancel has the focus when it opens.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="delete-account" command="show-modal">Delete account</sl:button>
<sl:alert-dialog id="delete-account">
  <sl:alert-dialog-header>
    <sl:alert-dialog-title>Are you absolutely sure?</sl:alert-dialog-title>
    <sl:alert-dialog-description>
      This action cannot be undone. This will permanently delete your account and remove your data from our
      servers.
    </sl:alert-dialog-description>
  </sl:alert-dialog-header>
  <sl:alert-dialog-footer>
    <sl:alert-dialog-cancel/>
    <sl:button>Continue</sl:button>
  </sl:alert-dialog-footer>
</sl:alert-dialog>
```

### Built from parts

An alert dialog asks a question the user has to answer before going on, such as "Delete this account?". It is
`<sl:alert-dialog>` plus its parts, as in shadcn/ui: `<sl:alert-dialog-header>` (holding an optional
`<sl:alert-dialog-media>`, `<sl:alert-dialog-title>` and `<sl:alert-dialog-description>`) and
`<sl:alert-dialog-footer>` with `<sl:alert-dialog-cancel>` and the action, a plain `<sl:button>`.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="delete-account" command="show-modal">Delete account</sl:button>

<sl:alert-dialog id="delete-account">
  <sl:alert-dialog-header>
    <sl:alert-dialog-title>Are you absolutely sure?</sl:alert-dialog-title>
    <sl:alert-dialog-description>This action cannot be undone.</sl:alert-dialog-description>
  </sl:alert-dialog-header>
  <sl:alert-dialog-footer>
    <sl:alert-dialog-cancel/>
    <sl:button>Continue</sl:button>
  </sl:alert-dialog-footer>
</sl:alert-dialog>
```

It works like a [dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md), with the differences an alert dialog needs:

- It has `role="alertdialog"`, so a screen reader announces it as an alert and reads the description with the title.
- A click outside does not close it: the user has to choose. Escape still does, as in Radix, and counts as cancel.
- It has no close button in the corner.
- `<sl:alert-dialog-cancel>` takes the focus when it opens, so pressing Enter straight away never confirms by accident.
  It closes the dialog (`commandfor` and `command="close"` are set for you) and says "Cancel" unless you give it text
  of its own (the default comes from the message `sl.alert-dialog.cancel`, see [Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md)). It takes the button's `variant` (default
  `outline`) and `size`, and passes any other attribute on to the button.

The action has no part of its own: what it does depends on the page. A `type="submit"` button in a form, a button
with `hx-delete`, or one with `commandfor` and `command="close"` when closing is all it has to do.

### Small, and with media

`size="sm"` makes it narrower, centres the header and puts the two buttons side by side in equal columns.
`<sl:alert-dialog-media>` shows an icon above the title.

**Example: Small, with media.** size="sm" centres the header and puts the two buttons side by side; the media shows an icon above the title.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="allow-accessory" command="show-modal">Connect accessory</sl:button>
<sl:alert-dialog id="allow-accessory" size="sm">
  <sl:alert-dialog-header>
    <sl:alert-dialog-media><sl:icon name="bluetooth"/></sl:alert-dialog-media>
    <sl:alert-dialog-title>Allow accessory to connect?</sl:alert-dialog-title>
    <sl:alert-dialog-description>
      Do you want to allow the USB accessory to connect to this device?
    </sl:alert-dialog-description>
  </sl:alert-dialog-header>
  <sl:alert-dialog-footer>
    <sl:alert-dialog-cancel>Don't allow</sl:alert-dialog-cancel>
    <sl:button>Allow</sl:button>
  </sl:alert-dialog-footer>
</sl:alert-dialog>
```

In a default-size dialog on a wide screen, the media sits beside the title and description.

**Example: Media.** In a default-size dialog on a wide screen, the media sits beside the title and description.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="share-project" command="show-modal">Share project</sl:button>
<sl:alert-dialog id="share-project">
  <sl:alert-dialog-header>
    <sl:alert-dialog-media><sl:icon name="circle-fading-plus"/></sl:alert-dialog-media>
    <sl:alert-dialog-title>Share this project?</sl:alert-dialog-title>
    <sl:alert-dialog-description>
      Anyone with the link will be able to view and edit this project.
    </sl:alert-dialog-description>
  </sl:alert-dialog-header>
  <sl:alert-dialog-footer>
    <sl:alert-dialog-cancel/>
    <sl:button>Share</sl:button>
  </sl:alert-dialog-footer>
</sl:alert-dialog>
```

### Destructive

For an action that deletes something, give the action `variant="destructive"`, and the media too.

**Example: Destructive.** A destructive action, with a destructive media square.

```html
<sl:button variant="destructive" commandfor="delete-chat" command="show-modal">Delete chat</sl:button>
<sl:alert-dialog id="delete-chat" size="sm">
  <sl:alert-dialog-header>
    <sl:alert-dialog-media variant="destructive"><sl:icon name="trash-2"/></sl:alert-dialog-media>
    <sl:alert-dialog-title>Delete chat?</sl:alert-dialog-title>
    <sl:alert-dialog-description>
      This will permanently delete this chat conversation. View <a href="#">Settings</a> to delete any
      memories saved during this chat.
    </sl:alert-dialog-description>
  </sl:alert-dialog-header>
  <sl:alert-dialog-footer>
    <sl:alert-dialog-cancel/>
    <sl:button variant="destructive">Delete</sl:button>
  </sl:alert-dialog-footer>
</sl:alert-dialog>
```

### Confirming with htmx

The alert dialog can come from the server, as the [dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md#rendered-by-the-server-htmx) does: a Delete
button in a row fetches it into an empty element on the page, and `open` shows it as soon as it is there.

```html
<!-- In the layout -->
<div id="modal-root"></div>

<!-- In the row -->
<sl:button variant="outline" hx:get="@{/members/{id}/delete(id=${member.id})}" hx-target="#modal-root">
  Delete
</sl:button>
```

The fragment has the question, and the destructive button sends the request:

```html
<sl:alert-dialog th:fragment="delete-dialog" id="delete-member" size="sm" open>
  <sl:alert-dialog-header>
    <sl:alert-dialog-media variant="destructive"><sl:icon name="trash-2"/></sl:alert-dialog-media>
    <sl:alert-dialog-title th:text="|Delete ${member.name}?|">Delete Ada Lovelace?</sl:alert-dialog-title>
    <sl:alert-dialog-description>They lose access to the team at once.</sl:alert-dialog-description>
  </sl:alert-dialog-header>
  <sl:alert-dialog-footer>
    <sl:alert-dialog-cancel/>
    <sl:button variant="destructive" hx:delete="@{/members/{id}(id=${member.id})}"
               th:hx-target="|#member-${member.id}|" hx-swap="outerHTML">Delete</sl:button>
  </sl:alert-dialog-footer>
</sl:alert-dialog>
```

The controller deletes, closes the dialog with the `sl-dialog-close` event (the same one that closes a dialog), says
what happened in a [toast](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toast.md) and answers with nothing for the row, which removes it:

```java
@HxRequest
@DeleteMapping("/members/{id}")
public String delete(@PathVariable long id, HtmxRequest htmxRequest, HtmxResponse htmxResponse, Model model) {
  List<Member> rows = memberService.list(sortOf(htmxRequest.getCurrentUrl()));  // as the page shows them
  Member member = memberService.delete(id);
  htmxResponse.addTrigger("sl-dialog-close");
  htmxResponse.addTrigger("sl-toast", Toast.success("%s was deleted".formatted(member.name())));
  Member next = neighbour(rows, member);  // the next row, the previous one after the last, or null
  if (next == null) {
    return "members :: invite-button-focus";
  }
  model.addAttribute("member", next);
  return "members :: member-row-focus";  // the next row, out of band, its button focused
}
```

Closing returns focus to the Delete button that opened the dialog, but the swap then removes that button with its
row, and focus would fall back to the top of the page. The toast cannot take it instead: it goes away after a few
seconds, and the focus would fall with it. So the response moves the focus to what comes next: the next row's button,
re-rendered out of band with `autofocus`, which htmx focuses after the swap. After the last row that is the row before
it, and once the table is empty, the button that adds a member. The toast is announced, so a screen reader user hears
what happened and is where they can go on.

```html
<th:block th:fragment="member-row-focus" th:with="oob=true, focus=true">
  <tr th:replace="~{members :: member-row}"></tr>
</th:block>

<tr th:fragment="member-row" th:id="|member-${member.id}|"
    th:attr="hx-swap-oob=${oob != null and oob ? 'true' : null}">
  <td th:text="${member.name}">Ada Lovelace</td>
  <td>
    <sl:button variant="outline" hx:get="@{/members/{id}/delete(id=${member.id})}" hx-target="#modal-root"
               th:autofocus="${focus != null and focus}">Delete</sl:button>
  </td>
</tr>
```

"Next" is the next row as the page shows it: htmx sends the page's address in the `HX-Current-URL` header, so the
controller can read the sort order from it, as sample-01's `/dialog` page does.

Without JavaScript the same buttons need a page of their own for the question, or a form per row with
`<sl:alert-dialog>` in the page and a `type="submit"` action.

### Attributes

#### sl:alert-dialog

Attributes of `<sl:alert-dialog>`: A modal dialog that interrupts the user with something important and waits for an answer, such as a confirmation before deleting. Built on the native `dialog` element with `role="alertdialog"`: a click outside does not close it, Escape does. Build it from `sl:alert-dialog-header` (with an optional `sl:alert-dialog-media`, `sl:alert-dialog-title` and `sl:alert-dialog-description`) and `sl:alert-dialog-footer` holding `sl:alert-dialog-cancel`, which takes the focus when the dialog opens, and the action, a `sl:button`. A button opens it with `commandfor` set to its `id` and `command="show-modal"`; an htmx response closes it with the `sl-dialog-close` event.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | **required** | The dialog's id, which the button that opens it points at with `commandfor`. The title and description ids derive from it. |
| `size` | `default`, `sm` | `default` | Size of the dialog. `sm` is narrower, with the header centred and the footer's two buttons side by side. |
| `open` | *boolean* | — | Open the dialog as soon as it is on the page, for a dialog the server renders in answer to a request (an htmx swap). Needs Alpine. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:alert-dialog-header

Attributes of `<sl:alert-dialog-header>`: The top of a `sl:alert-dialog`: an optional `sl:alert-dialog-media`, a `sl:alert-dialog-title` and an optional `sl:alert-dialog-description`. Centred on a narrow screen and in a small dialog.

`sl:alert-dialog-header` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:alert-dialog-media

Attributes of `<sl:alert-dialog-media>`: An icon (or an image) at the start of a `sl:alert-dialog-header`, on a muted square: above the title, or beside title and description in a wide dialog.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `destructive` | `default` | Colour of the square and its icon: `destructive` for a dialog that confirms deleting something. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:alert-dialog-title

Attributes of `<sl:alert-dialog-title>`: The title of a `sl:alert-dialog`, inside `sl:alert-dialog-header`: a heading that names the dialog (`aria-labelledby`). Its id derives from the dialog's (`delete-account-title`).

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` | `1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5`, `6` | `2` | Heading level (`role="heading"` and `aria-level`). A dialog starts an outline of its own, so the default, 2, rarely needs changing. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:level="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:alert-dialog-description

Attributes of `<sl:alert-dialog-description>`: Supporting text below a `sl:alert-dialog-title`, which describes the dialog (`aria-describedby`): what happens, and whether it can be undone. Its id derives from the dialog's (`delete-account-description`).

`sl:alert-dialog-description` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:alert-dialog-footer

Attributes of `<sl:alert-dialog-footer>`: The bottom of a `sl:alert-dialog`, for `sl:alert-dialog-cancel` and the action: side by side at the end on a wide screen, stacked with the first one at the bottom on a narrow one, and two equal columns in a small dialog.

`sl:alert-dialog-footer` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:alert-dialog-cancel

Attributes of `<sl:alert-dialog-cancel>`: The button that closes a `sl:alert-dialog` without doing anything, in its `sl:alert-dialog-footer`. It takes the focus when the dialog opens, so Enter never confirms by accident. Its text defaults to "Cancel" (`sl.alert-dialog.cancel`); content of its own replaces it. Any other attribute passes through to the `sl:button`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline`, `ghost`, `destructive`, `secondary`, `link` | `outline` | Visual style of the button, as on `sl:button`. |
| `size` | `xs`, `sm`, `default`, `lg` | `default` | Size of the button, as on `sl:button`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

### JavaScript

An alert dialog uses the dialog's Alpine component, `slDialog`; see [Dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md#javascript).

- **Most of it works without Alpine.** Opening from a button, closing with Escape or Cancel, and focus on Cancel are
  the browser's. Alpine is needed for `open` and the `sl-dialog-close` event.
- **It needs no Alpine plugin.**
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:alert-dialog>`**: it would replace `slDialog`. Put it on an element inside.

---

## Sheet

> A dialog that slides in from an edge of the screen.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sheet/

**Example: Sheet.** A form in a sheet. The form wraps the sheet, so the submit button in its footer submits it.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="sheet-profile" command="show-modal">Open</sl:button>
<form method="post" action="/profile">
  <sl:sheet id="sheet-profile">
    <sl:sheet-header>
      <sl:sheet-title>Edit profile</sl:sheet-title>
      <sl:sheet-description>
        Make changes to your profile here. Click save when you're done.
      </sl:sheet-description>
    </sl:sheet-header>
    <sl:field-group>
      <sl:field for="sheet-profile-name">
        <sl:field-label>Name</sl:field-label>
        <sl:input name="name" value="Pedro Duarte"/>
      </sl:field>
      <sl:field for="sheet-profile-username">
        <sl:field-label>Username</sl:field-label>
        <sl:input name="username" value="@peduarte"/>
      </sl:field>
    </sl:field-group>
    <sl:sheet-footer show-close-button>
      <sl:button type="submit">Save changes</sl:button>
    </sl:sheet-footer>
  </sl:sheet>
</form>
```

### Built from parts

A sheet is a [dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md) on an edge of the screen, for content that complements the page: filters,
settings, the details of a row. It is `<sl:sheet>` plus the parts you need, as in shadcn/ui: `<sl:sheet-header>`
(holding `<sl:sheet-title>` and `<sl:sheet-description>`), your own content and `<sl:sheet-footer>`.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="edit-profile" command="show-modal">Open</sl:button>

<form method="post" th:action="@{/profile}">
  <sl:sheet id="edit-profile">
    <sl:sheet-header>
      <sl:sheet-title>Edit profile</sl:sheet-title>
      <sl:sheet-description>Make changes to your profile here.</sl:sheet-description>
    </sl:sheet-header>
    <sl:field-group>
      ...
    </sl:field-group>
    <sl:sheet-footer show-close-button>
      <sl:button type="submit">Save changes</sl:button>
    </sl:sheet-footer>
  </sl:sheet>
</form>
```

Everything the dialog page says holds for a sheet: it is the browser's `<dialog>` element, buttons open and close it
with `commandfor` and `command`, the page behind it is inert and does not scroll, Escape and a click outside close it,
focus returns to the button that opened it, and `open` shows one the server renders (an htmx swap). The title and
description name and describe it, with ids derived from its `id`.

The content between the header and the footer gets the sheet's inline padding, so it lines up with them. The footer
stays at the bottom of a sheet on the side of the screen, however little is above it. Content taller than the screen
scrolls inside the sheet; put it in `<sl:sheet-body>` to scroll only the content, with the header and the footer in
view.

**Example: Body.** sl:sheet-body scrolls by itself, so the header and the footer stay in view.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="sheet-member" command="show-modal">Open</sl:button>
<sl:sheet id="sheet-member">
  <sl:sheet-header>
    <sl:sheet-title>Ada Lovelace</sl:sheet-title>
    <sl:sheet-description>Member since 1843.</sl:sheet-description>
  </sl:sheet-header>
  <sl:sheet-body>
    <sl:field-group>
      <sl:field for="sheet-member-name">
        <sl:field-label>Name</sl:field-label>
        <sl:input name="name" value="Ada Lovelace"/>
      </sl:field>
      <sl:field for="sheet-member-email">
        <sl:field-label>Email</sl:field-label>
        <sl:input name="email" type="email" value="ada@example.com"/>
      </sl:field>
      <sl:field for="sheet-member-notes">
        <sl:field-label>Notes</sl:field-label>
        <sl:textarea name="notes" rows="12">Wrote the first program for the Analytical Engine.</sl:textarea>
      </sl:field>
    </sl:field-group>
  </sl:sheet-body>
  <sl:sheet-footer show-close-button>
    <sl:button type="submit">Save changes</sl:button>
  </sl:sheet-footer>
</sl:sheet>
```

### Side

`side` is the edge it slides in from: `top`, `right` (the default), `bottom` or `left`. A sheet on the top or bottom
is as wide as the screen and as tall as its content; one on the right or left is as tall as the screen and three
quarters of its width, up to a maximum.

**Example: Side.** side="top", "right" (the default), "bottom" or "left".

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="sheet-top" command="show-modal">Top</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="sheet-right" command="show-modal">Right</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="sheet-bottom" command="show-modal">Bottom</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="sheet-left" command="show-modal">Left</sl:button>
<sl:sheet id="sheet-top" side="top">
  <sl:sheet-header>
    <sl:sheet-title>From the top</sl:sheet-title>
    <sl:sheet-description>A sheet on the top edge, as wide as the screen.</sl:sheet-description>
  </sl:sheet-header>
  <sl:sheet-footer show-close-button/>
</sl:sheet>
<sl:sheet id="sheet-right" side="right">
  <sl:sheet-header>
    <sl:sheet-title>From the right</sl:sheet-title>
    <sl:sheet-description>The default: the end of the line, which is the left on a right-to-left page.</sl:sheet-description>
  </sl:sheet-header>
  <sl:sheet-footer show-close-button/>
</sl:sheet>
<sl:sheet id="sheet-bottom" side="bottom">
  <sl:sheet-header>
    <sl:sheet-title>From the bottom</sl:sheet-title>
    <sl:sheet-description>A sheet on the bottom edge, as wide as the screen.</sl:sheet-description>
  </sl:sheet-header>
  <sl:sheet-footer show-close-button/>
</sl:sheet>
<sl:sheet id="sheet-left" side="left">
  <sl:sheet-header>
    <sl:sheet-title>From the left</sl:sheet-title>
    <sl:sheet-description>The start of the line, which is the right on a right-to-left page.</sl:sheet-description>
  </sl:sheet-header>
  <sl:sheet-footer show-close-button/>
</sl:sheet>
```

`right` and `left` are the end and the start of a line, as everywhere in Shadleaf: on a right-to-left page
(`dir="rtl"`) a `right` sheet opens on the left and slides in from there.

The sheet fades in and slides a short distance from its edge. With reduced motion (`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`)
it only fades.

### Close buttons

The close button in the corner is on by default; `show-close-button="false"` leaves it out. `<sl:sheet-footer
show-close-button>` adds an outline "Close" button after the footer's own actions.

**Example: No close button in the corner.** show-close-button="false" leaves the x out; the footer's close button remains.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="sheet-notifications" command="show-modal">Notifications</sl:button>
<sl:sheet id="sheet-notifications" show-close-button="false">
  <sl:sheet-header>
    <sl:sheet-title>Notifications</sl:sheet-title>
    <sl:sheet-description>You have no unread messages.</sl:sheet-description>
  </sl:sheet-header>
  <sl:sheet-footer show-close-button/>
</sl:sheet>
```

### Loaded into the sheet: a body

`<sl:sheet-body>` is what htmx loads, as the [dialog's body](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md#loaded-into-the-dialog-a-body). You put the sheet
in the page once, closed. A request that targets its body (`#<sheet id>-body`) opens it at once and shows a spinner
until the answer is swapped in:

1. **It opens** as soon as the request is sent, with the focus in it.
2. **A spinner covers the body** while the request runs. It shows only after a moment (200 ms), so a quick answer does
   not flicker, and the body takes no clicks meanwhile.
3. **The answer is swapped in**, and the focus moves to its first field, if it has one.

Closing the sheet while the request still runs aborts it. A body that was empty in the page is emptied again when the
sheet closes, so it never shows the previous row while the next one loads.

#### Show the details of a row

1. Add the sheet to the page, with an empty `<sl:sheet-body>`. The body's content gets the id `<id>-body`, here
   `member-details-body`.

   ```html
   <sl:sheet id="member-details">
     <sl:sheet-header>
       <sl:sheet-title>Member details</sl:sheet-title>
     </sl:sheet-header>
     <sl:sheet-body/>
     <sl:sheet-footer show-close-button/>
   </sl:sheet>
   ```

2. Write a fragment with what goes in the body.

   ```html
   <div th:fragment="member-details">
     <dl>
       <dt>Name</dt>
       <dd th:text="${member.name}">Ada Lovelace</dd>
       <dt>Email</dt>
       <dd th:text="${member.email}">ada@example.com</dd>
     </dl>
   </div>
   ```

3. Write a controller method that returns the fragment.

   ```java
   @HxRequest
   @GetMapping("/members/{id}")
   public String details(@PathVariable long id, Model model) {
     model.addAttribute("member", memberService.get(id));
     return "members :: member-details";
   }
   ```

4. Add a button that asks for it with `hx:get`, with `hx-target` set to the sheet's body.

   ```html
   <sl:button variant="outline"
              hx:get="@{/members/{id}(id=${member.id})}"
              hx-target="#member-details-body">Details</sl:button>
   ```

A click on the button opens the sheet and loads the member. While the answer takes its time, the sheet shows the
spinner:

**Example: Loading.** While an htmx request into the body runs, a spinner covers it. Here the body keeps data-loading, which the script sets and removes.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" commandfor="sheet-loading" command="show-modal">Open</sl:button>
<sl:sheet id="sheet-loading">
  <sl:sheet-header>
    <sl:sheet-title>Member details</sl:sheet-title>
  </sl:sheet-header>
  <sl:sheet-body data-loading="true"/>
  <sl:sheet-footer show-close-button/>
</sl:sheet>
```

#### When loading fails

When the request fails (an error status, no connection, a timeout), the sheet closes again and a
[toast](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toast.md) shows "Something went wrong. Please try again." The `error` slot replaces that message:

```html
<sl:sheet-body>
  <sl:slot name="error">The member could not be loaded.</sl:slot>
</sl:sheet-body>
```

The toast needs an `<sl:toaster>` on the page (in the layout). Without one, or with `load-error="inline"`, the sheet
stays open and shows the message in it instead:

```html
<sl:sheet-body load-error="inline">
  <sl:slot name="error">The member could not be loaded.</sl:slot>
</sl:sheet-body>
```

#### A form in the sheet

Put the actions in `<sl:sheet-footer>`: it stays at the bottom while the body scrolls. The footer is part of the sheet
in the page and the form is what htmx loads, so the submit button points at the form with `form`:

```html
<sl:sheet id="member-edit">
  <sl:sheet-header>
    <sl:sheet-title>Edit member</sl:sheet-title>
  </sl:sheet-header>
  <sl:sheet-body/>
  <sl:sheet-footer show-close-button>
    <sl:button type="submit" form="member-edit-form">Save</sl:button>
  </sl:sheet-footer>
</sl:sheet>

<sl:button variant="outline"
           hx:get="@{/members/{id}/edit(id=${member.id})}"
           hx-target="#member-edit-body">Edit</sl:button>
```

The form swaps only itself, so the sheet stays open while it has errors:

```html
<form th:fragment="edit-form" id="member-edit-form" th:object="${memberForm}"
      hx:post="@{/members/{id}(id=${member.id})}" hx-target="this" hx-swap="outerHTML">
  <sl:form-errors autofocus/>
  <sl:field th:field="*{name}">
    <sl:field-label>Name</sl:field-label>
    <sl:input/>
    <sl:field-error/>
  </sl:field>
</form>
```

The spinner covers the form while it is being saved too, and a second click on Save sends nothing. With errors, the
controller returns the form again; when the save succeeds, it closes the sheet with the `HX-Trigger` response header
and updates the member's row out of band:

```java
@HxRequest
@PostMapping("/members/{id}")
public String update(@PathVariable long id, @Valid @ModelAttribute("memberForm") MemberForm memberForm,
    BindingResult bindingResult, HtmxResponse htmxResponse, Model model) {
  if (bindingResult.hasErrors()) {
    return "members :: edit-form";
  }
  model.addAttribute("member", memberService.update(id, memberForm));
  htmxResponse.addTrigger("sl-dialog-close");
  htmxResponse.setReswap(HtmxReswap.none());
  return "members :: member-row-oob";  // the row, with hx-swap-oob="true"
}
```

htmx fires `sl-dialog-close` on the form, inside the sheet, which closes; the focus returns to the Edit button. Give
that button an id if the row is replaced, so htmx puts the focus back on the new one (see
[Dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md#rendered-by-the-server-htmx)). A failed save (an error status) shows the error in the sheet and
keeps what was typed.

### Width

A sheet on the right or left is three quarters of the screen wide, up to a maximum the skin sets (`24rem` in vega).
For a wider one, give it a class and set the maximum in your own stylesheet, loaded after the library's: the library's
rules sit in a cascade layer, so yours win.

```html
<sl:sheet id="member-edit" class="sheet-wide">...</sl:sheet>
```

```css
.sheet-wide {
  max-width: 40rem;
}
```

If you compile Tailwind yourself (the `embedded` bundle), a utility class does the same: `class="sm:max-w-2xl"`.

### Attributes

#### sl:sheet

Attributes of `<sl:sheet>`: A dialog that slides in from an edge of the screen, for content that complements the page: filters, settings, details. Built on the native `dialog` element, like `sl:dialog`: the page behind it is inert, Escape and a click outside close it, and focus returns to where it came from. Build it from `sl:sheet-header` (holding `sl:sheet-title` and `sl:sheet-description`), your content and `sl:sheet-footer`. A button opens it with `commandfor` set to the sheet's `id` and `command="show-modal"`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | **required** | The sheet's id, which the buttons that open and close it point at with `commandfor`. The title and description ids derive from it. |
| `side` | `top`, `right`, `bottom`, `left` | `right` | The edge it slides in from. `right` and `left` are the end and the start of a line: on a right-to-left page a `right` sheet opens on the left. |
| `open` | *boolean* | — | Open the sheet as soon as it is on the page, for a sheet the server renders in answer to a request (an htmx swap). Needs Alpine. |
| `show-close-button` | *boolean* | `true` | A close button (an x) in the corner. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sheet-header

Attributes of `<sl:sheet-header>`: The top of a `sl:sheet`: a `sl:sheet-title` and an optional `sl:sheet-description` below it.

`sl:sheet-header` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:sheet-title

Attributes of `<sl:sheet-title>`: The title of a `sl:sheet`, inside `sl:sheet-header`: a heading that names the sheet (`aria-labelledby`). Its id derives from the sheet's (`filters-title`).

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` | `1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5`, `6` | `2` | Heading level (`role="heading"` and `aria-level`). A sheet starts an outline of its own, so the default, 2, rarely needs changing. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:level="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sheet-description

Attributes of `<sl:sheet-description>`: Supporting text below a `sl:sheet-title`, which describes the sheet (`aria-describedby`). Its id derives from the sheet's (`filters-description`).

`sl:sheet-description` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:sheet-body

Attributes of `<sl:sheet-body>`: The content of a `sl:sheet` between its header and footer, which scrolls by itself so the header and footer stay in view. Its content has the id `&lt;id&gt;-body` (`edit-body`), the target for htmx: a request from outside that targets it opens the sheet at once and shows a spinner until the answer is swapped in; closing aborts a request still running. Left empty, it is emptied again when the sheet closes, so it never shows the previous content. When a request fails, the error shows in the sheet, or, for the request that opened it, as a toast (see `load-error`). Needs Alpine.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `load-error` | `toast`, `inline` | `toast` | What happens when the request that opened the sheet fails: `toast` closes it and shows the error as a toast (shown in the sheet when the page has no `sl:toaster`); `inline` keeps it open with the error in it. A failed request from inside (a form) always shows the error in the sheet, so nothing typed is lost. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:load-error="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sheet-footer

Attributes of `<sl:sheet-footer>`: The bottom of a `sl:sheet`, for its actions, stacked. It stays at the bottom of a sheet on the side of the screen however little content there is above it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `show-close-button` | *boolean* | — | An outline "Close" button after the other actions. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:show-close-button="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

### JavaScript

A sheet uses the dialog's Alpine component, `slDialog`; see [Dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md#javascript).

- **Most of it works without Alpine.** Opening from a button, Escape, a click outside and the close buttons are the
  browser's. Alpine is needed for `open`, the `sl-dialog-close` event, which closes a sheet as it closes a dialog, and
  a body that htmx loads.
- **It needs no Alpine plugin.**
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:sheet>`**: it would replace `slDialog`. Put it on an element inside.

---

## Popover

> Rich content in a panel next to the button that opens it, on the browser's Popover API.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/popover/

**Example: Popover.** A small form next to its trigger. Focus moves to the first field when it opens.

```html
<sl:popover id="dimensions">
  <sl:popover-trigger variant="outline">Open popover</sl:popover-trigger>
  <sl:popover-content>
    <sl:popover-header>
      <sl:popover-title>Dimensions</sl:popover-title>
      <sl:popover-description>Set the dimensions for the layer.</sl:popover-description>
    </sl:popover-header>
    <sl:field-group>
      <sl:field orientation="horizontal" for="dimensions-width">
        <sl:field-label>Width</sl:field-label>
        <sl:input value="100%"/>
      </sl:field>
      <sl:field orientation="horizontal" for="dimensions-height">
        <sl:field-label>Height</sl:field-label>
        <sl:input value="25px"/>
      </sl:field>
    </sl:field-group>
  </sl:popover-content>
</sl:popover>
```

### Built from parts

A popover is `<sl:popover>` with a `<sl:popover-trigger>` and a `<sl:popover-content>`, as in shadcn/ui. The
content can hold a `<sl:popover-header>` with `<sl:popover-title>` and `<sl:popover-description>`, and anything
else: text, a small form, links. `<sl:popover>` renders no element of its own: it gives the parts their ids.

```html
<sl:popover id="dimensions">
  <sl:popover-trigger variant="outline">Open popover</sl:popover-trigger>
```
