```html
  <sl:popover-content>
    <sl:popover-header>
      <sl:popover-title>Dimensions</sl:popover-title>
      <sl:popover-description>Set the dimensions for the layer.</sl:popover-description>
    </sl:popover-header>
    ...
  </sl:popover-content>
</sl:popover>
```

The panel is a [popover](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Popover_API): it is drawn above everything
else, Escape or a click outside closes it, and closing returns focus to the trigger. When it opens, focus moves to
its first control (or to an element with `autofocus`), or to the panel when it has none; moving the focus out of it,
with Tab past its last control for example, closes it. The trigger is a `<sl:button>` with `popovertarget` set to the
popover's `id`; any other button can open it the same way.

The panel is a non-modal dialog (`role="dialog"`): the rest of the page stays usable. The title names it and the
description describes it (`aria-labelledby` and `aria-describedby`, with ids derived from the popover's `id`:
`dimensions-title`). A popover without a title needs an `aria-label` on `<sl:popover-content>`. `level` on the title
makes it a heading.

### Position

The panel opens below its trigger, centred on it. `side="top|right|bottom|left"` and `align="start|center|end"` on
`<sl:popover-content>` change that, and it flips to the other side when there is no room. `right` and `left` are the
end and the start of a line, so they swap on a right-to-left page.

**Example: Sides.** side puts it on a side of its trigger; it flips when there is no room there.

```html
<sl:popover id="popover-top">
  <sl:popover-trigger variant="outline">Top</sl:popover-trigger>
  <sl:popover-content side="top" aria-label="Top">Opens above its trigger.</sl:popover-content>
</sl:popover>
<sl:popover id="popover-right">
  <sl:popover-trigger variant="outline">Right</sl:popover-trigger>
  <sl:popover-content side="right" aria-label="Right">Opens after its trigger.</sl:popover-content>
</sl:popover>
<sl:popover id="popover-bottom">
  <sl:popover-trigger variant="outline">Bottom</sl:popover-trigger>
  <sl:popover-content aria-label="Bottom">Opens below its trigger.</sl:popover-content>
</sl:popover>
<sl:popover id="popover-left">
  <sl:popover-trigger variant="outline">Left</sl:popover-trigger>
  <sl:popover-content side="left" aria-label="Left">Opens before its trigger.</sl:popover-content>
</sl:popover>
```

It is placed with [CSS anchor positioning](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_anchor_positioning),
which every current browser supports (Chrome and Edge 133, Safari 26, Firefox 147). In older ones the popover's small
Alpine component places it from the trigger's position instead.

### A form in a popover, with htmx

A form in the panel can post with htmx and swap only what it changes. An `HX-Trigger: sl-popover-close` response
header closes the popover after a valid save, and focus returns to the trigger:

```html
<h2 id="team-name" th:text="${team.name}">Design</h2>
<sl:popover id="rename-team">
  <sl:popover-trigger variant="outline" size="sm">Rename</sl:popover-trigger>
  <sl:popover-content>
    <sl:popover-header><sl:popover-title>Rename team</sl:popover-title></sl:popover-header>
    <form th:replace="~{team :: rename-form}"></form>
  </sl:popover-content>
</sl:popover>
```

```java
@HxRequest
@PostMapping("/team/name")
public String rename(@Valid @ModelAttribute("renameForm") RenameForm form, BindingResult bindingResult,
    HtmxResponse htmxResponse, Model model) {
  if (bindingResult.hasErrors()) {
    return "team :: rename-form";  // the form swaps itself: the popover stays open
  }
  model.addAttribute("team", teamService.rename(form.getName()));
  htmxResponse.addTrigger("sl-popover-close");
  return "team :: team-name-oob";  // the heading, with hx-swap-oob="true"
}
```

htmx fires `sl-popover-close` on the form; it bubbles up to the popover, which closes. A popover nested in another
closes only itself. Back never brings a popover back open: whether it is open is not part of the markup htmx saves in
its history cache.

### Left out

- **`PopoverAnchor`**: the popover is placed against its trigger.
- **`PopoverPortal`**: the top layer does what the portal is for.
- **State in the browser** (`open`/`onOpenChange`): the trigger opens it; the `sl-popover-close` event closes it.
- **`modal`**: a popover never makes the rest of the page inert. Use a [dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md) for that.

### Attributes

#### sl:popover

Attributes of `<sl:popover>`: Rich content in a panel that opens from a button, on the browser's Popover API: it sits above the page next to its trigger, and Escape, a click outside or moving the focus out of it closes it. Holds a `sl:popover-trigger` and a `sl:popover-content`. Renders no element of its own.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | **required** | The popover's id, which the trigger points at with `popovertarget`. The title and description ids derive from it. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:popover-trigger

Attributes of `<sl:popover-trigger>`: The button that opens and closes a `sl:popover`: a `sl:button` with `popovertarget` set to the popover. Any other attribute passes through to the `sl:button`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline`, `ghost`, `destructive`, `secondary`, `link` | `default` | Visual style of the button, as on `sl:button`. |
| `size` | `xs`, `sm`, `default`, `lg`, `icon`, `icon-xs`, `icon-sm`, `icon-lg` | `default` | Size of the button, as on `sl:button`. The `icon` sizes need an `aria-label`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:popover-content

Attributes of `<sl:popover-content>`: The panel of a `sl:popover`, next to its trigger in the top layer. It is a non-modal dialog: name it with a `sl:popover-title` (in a `sl:popover-header`) or an `aria-label`. When it opens, focus moves to its first control. It flips to the other side when there is no room.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `side` | `top`, `right`, `bottom`, `left` | `bottom` | The side of the trigger it opens on. `right` and `left` are the end and the start of a line: on a right-to-left page a `right` popover opens on the left. |
| `align` | `start`, `center`, `end` | `center` | How it lines up with the trigger along that side. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:side="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:popover-header

Attributes of `<sl:popover-header>`: The top of a `sl:popover-content`, holding `sl:popover-title` and `sl:popover-description`.

`sl:popover-header` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:popover-title

Attributes of `<sl:popover-title>`: The title of a `sl:popover`, inside `sl:popover-header`, which names the popover (`aria-labelledby`). Its id derives from the popover's (`dimensions-title`).

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` | `1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5`, `6` | — | Heading level. With it the title is a heading (`role="heading"` and `aria-level`); without it, plain text, as in shadcn/ui. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:level="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:popover-description

Attributes of `<sl:popover-description>`: Supporting text below a `sl:popover-title`, which describes the popover (`aria-describedby`). Its id derives from the popover's (`dimensions-description`).

`sl:popover-description` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

### JavaScript

A small Alpine component, `slPopover`, adds what the browser's popover lacks: `<sl:popover-content>` renders as
`<div class="popover-content" role="dialog" popover x-data="slPopover">`.

- **The basics work without Alpine.** The trigger opens and closes the popover, and Escape and a click outside close
  it. Alpine is needed for moving the focus into it when it opens, closing it when the focus leaves, the
  `sl-popover-close` event, `aria-expanded` on the trigger, and placing it in browsers without CSS anchor positioning.
  Alpine is included by default; see [Alpine](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/alpine.md).
- **It needs no Alpine plugin.** If you load Alpine yourself (`shadleaf.assets.alpine=external`), plain Alpine is
  enough.
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:popover-content>`.** An element has one `x-data`, and attributes on it end up on
  the panel, so yours would replace `slPopover`. Put it on an element inside the panel.

| Event | On | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sl-popover-close` | the popover, or anything inside it (it bubbles) | Closes the popover. |
| `toggle` | the popover (the browser's own) | Fired when it opens or closes, however that happened. |

---

## Dropdown Menu

> A menu of actions or links that opens from a button, on the browser's Popover API.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dropdown-menu/

**Example: Dropdown menu.** Labels, groups, separators and shortcuts; links and buttons; a disabled item.

```html
<sl:dropdown-menu id="account-menu">
  <sl:dropdown-menu-trigger variant="outline">Open</sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>
  <sl:dropdown-menu-content>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-label>My Account</sl:dropdown-menu-label>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-group>
      <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/profile">
        Profile <sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>⇧⌘P</sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>
      </sl:dropdown-menu-item>
      <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/billing">
        Billing <sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>⌘B</sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>
      </sl:dropdown-menu-item>
      <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/settings">
        Settings <sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>⌘S</sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>
      </sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    </sl:dropdown-menu-group>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-separator/>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-group>
      <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/team">Team</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
      <sl:dropdown-menu-item>
        New Team <sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>⌘T</sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>
      </sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    </sl:dropdown-menu-group>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-separator/>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="https://github.com/wimdeblauwe/shadleaf">GitHub</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/support">Support</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item disabled>API</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-separator/>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item type="submit" form="logout-form">
      Log out <sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>⇧⌘Q</sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>
    </sl:dropdown-menu-item>
  </sl:dropdown-menu-content>
</sl:dropdown-menu>
<form id="logout-form" method="post" action="/logout"></form>
```

### Built from parts

A dropdown menu is `<sl:dropdown-menu>` with a `<sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>` and a `<sl:dropdown-menu-content>`
holding the items, as in shadcn/ui. `<sl:dropdown-menu>` renders no element of its own: it gives the parts their ids.

```html
<sl:dropdown-menu id="account-menu">
  <sl:dropdown-menu-trigger variant="outline">Open</sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>
  <sl:dropdown-menu-content>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-label>My Account</sl:dropdown-menu-label>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-group>
      <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" th:href="@{/profile}">
        Profile <sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>⇧⌘P</sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>
      </sl:dropdown-menu-item>
      <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" th:href="@{/settings}">Settings</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    </sl:dropdown-menu-group>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-separator/>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item type="submit" form="logout-form">Log out</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
  </sl:dropdown-menu-content>
</sl:dropdown-menu>
```

The menu is a [popover](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Popover_API), so the browser does the hard
parts: the menu is drawn above everything else (no `z-index` to manage, no portal), Escape or a click outside closes
it, and closing returns focus to the trigger. The trigger is a `<sl:button>` with `popovertarget` set to the menu's
`id`; it takes the button's `variant` and `size`, its `icon-start` and `icon-end` slots, and any other attribute passes
through. Any other button can open the menu the same way: `<sl:button popovertarget="account-menu">`.

The menu is labelled by its trigger. A menu opened by a button of your own needs an `aria-label` on
`<sl:dropdown-menu-content>`.

### Opened from the sidebar

`as` makes the trigger a sidebar part instead of a button: shadcn's `<DropdownMenuTrigger asChild>` around a
`SidebarMenuButton` or a `SidebarMenuAction`.

| `as` | Renders | For |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `button` (default) | `<sl:button>` | Any menu |
| `sidebar-menu-button` | `<sl:sidebar-menu-button as="button">`, with its `variant`, `size`, `tooltip` and `icon-start`/`icon-end` slots | A team or version switcher in the sidebar header, a user menu in its footer |
| `sidebar-menu-action` | `<sl:sidebar-menu-action>`, with `show-on-hover` | "More" beside a project's link |

```html
<sl:sidebar-menu-item>
  <sl:dropdown-menu id="team-switcher">
    <sl:dropdown-menu-trigger as="sidebar-menu-button" size="lg" tooltip="Acme Inc.">
      <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="gallery-vertical-end"/></sl:slot>
      <strong>Acme Inc.</strong>
      <small>Enterprise</small>
      <sl:slot name="icon-end"><sl:icon name="chevrons-up-down"/></sl:slot>
    </sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-content side="right">...</sl:dropdown-menu-content>
  </sl:dropdown-menu>
</sl:sidebar-menu-item>
```

The trigger is the same element the part renders on its own, with the menu's `popovertarget`, `aria-haspopup` and
id, so it looks and lines up like the entries around it. The [Sidebar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md#menus-in-the-sidebar) page shows
the team switcher, the version switcher and the "More" menu, and where each opens: beside the sidebar on a desktop
(`side="right"`), below its trigger in the phone panel and in the header layout's row.

### Items: links and buttons

An item is a button, or with `as="a"` a link. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through, so an item does
anything a button or link does, and the menu closes once it is chosen, with focus back on the trigger:

| Item | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `<sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" th:href="@{/orders/{id}(id=${order.id})}">` | Navigates. |
| `<sl:dropdown-menu-item hx:post="@{/orders/{id}/duplicate(id=${order.id})}">` | Sends an htmx request. |
| `<sl:dropdown-menu-item type="submit" form="logout-form">` | Submits a form (a POST logout). |
| `<sl:dropdown-menu-item commandfor="rename-dialog" command="show-modal">` | Opens a [dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md); closing it returns focus to the menu's trigger. |

`variant="destructive"` is for an item that deletes something. `disabled` disables an item: the arrow keys skip it, a
disabled button cannot be clicked (htmx included), and a disabled link loses its `href`. `inset` indents an item (or a
label) to line up with items that start with an icon.

**Example: Icons and a destructive item.** An icon at the start of an item; variant="destructive" for one that deletes.

```html
<sl:dropdown-menu id="file-menu">
  <sl:dropdown-menu-trigger variant="outline">File</sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>
  <sl:dropdown-menu-content>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item><sl:icon name="file"/>New file</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item><sl:icon name="folder"/>New folder</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item><sl:icon name="share"/>Share</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-separator/>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item variant="destructive"><sl:icon name="trash-2"/>Delete</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
  </sl:dropdown-menu-content>
</sl:dropdown-menu>
```

`<sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>` shows a keyboard shortcut at the end of an item. It only shows it: your application
handles the keys.

### Checkbox and radio items

A checkbox item shows an on/off state with a check mark (`role="menuitemcheckbox"`), a radio item one choice out of a
few (`role="menuitemradio"`, inside `<sl:dropdown-menu-radio-group>`). The state comes from the server: `checked`
marks it, and choosing an item does what its attributes say, usually a link to the same page with another parameter.
The page that comes back shows the new state.

```html
<sl:dropdown-menu-radio-group aria-label="Sort by">
  <sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item as="a" th:href="@{/orders(sort='name')}" th:checked="${sort == 'name'}">
    Name
  </sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item>
  <sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item as="a" th:href="@{/orders(sort='date')}" th:checked="${sort == 'date'}">
    Date
  </sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item>
</sl:dropdown-menu-radio-group>
```

Give such an item its text as content (`[[${option.label}]]`), not with `th:text`: that attribute passes through to
the item and would replace its check mark too.

With an `hx-post` instead of a link, return the menu again (or the part of the page that holds it), so the check mark
follows the server. Nothing changes the check mark in the browser: it always shows what the server knows.

**Example: Checkbox items.** Settings that are on or off. The server renders the state; choosing an item sends a request (here a link) and the page that comes back shows the new state.

```html
<sl:dropdown-menu id="view-menu">
  <sl:dropdown-menu-trigger variant="outline">View</sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>
  <sl:dropdown-menu-content>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-label>Appearance</sl:dropdown-menu-label>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-checkbox-item as="a" href="?status-bar=false" checked>Status Bar</sl:dropdown-menu-checkbox-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-checkbox-item as="a" href="?activity-bar=true" disabled>Activity Bar</sl:dropdown-menu-checkbox-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-checkbox-item as="a" href="?panel=true">Panel</sl:dropdown-menu-checkbox-item>
  </sl:dropdown-menu-content>
</sl:dropdown-menu>
```

**Example: Radio items.** One choice out of a few, such as where a panel goes or how a list is sorted.

```html
<sl:dropdown-menu id="position-menu">
  <sl:dropdown-menu-trigger variant="outline">Panel position</sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>
  <sl:dropdown-menu-content>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-label>Panel Position</sl:dropdown-menu-label>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-separator/>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-radio-group aria-label="Panel position">
      <sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item as="a" href="?position=top">Top</sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item>
      <sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item as="a" href="?position=bottom" checked>Bottom</sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item>
      <sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item as="a" href="?position=right">Right</sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item>
    </sl:dropdown-menu-radio-group>
  </sl:dropdown-menu-content>
</sl:dropdown-menu>
```

### Keyboard

| Key | On the trigger | In the menu |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Enter, Space | Opens the menu on its first item | Chooses the item (Space works on links too) |
| Arrow down | Opens the menu on its first item | Next item |
| Arrow up | Opens the menu on its last item | Previous item |
| Home, End | | First, last item |
| A letter | | The next item whose text starts with it; typing quickly matches a prefix |
| Escape | | Closes the menu; focus returns to the trigger (also inside another popover, such as the sidebar's phone panel, where the browser itself does not return it) |
| Tab | | Closes the menu and moves on |

Disabled items, labels and separators are skipped. The item under the pointer takes the focus, so pointer and
keyboard highlight items the same way; only keyboard focus draws the focus ring.

### Position

The menu opens below its trigger, lined up with its start. `side="top|right|bottom|left"` and
`align="start|center|end"` on `<sl:dropdown-menu-content>` change that, and the menu flips to the other side when there
is no room. `right` and `left` are the end and the start of a line, so they swap on a right-to-left page. Inside the
sidebar's phone panel and its header row a `right` or `left` menu opens below its trigger instead (see
[Sidebar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md#menus-in-the-sidebar)).

It is placed with [CSS anchor positioning](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_anchor_positioning),
which every current browser supports (Chrome and Edge 133, Safari 26, Firefox 147). In older ones the menu's small
Alpine component places it from the trigger's position instead.

### Row actions

A menu per row of a table takes its id from an expression; the parts derive theirs from it.

**Example: Row actions.** An icon button opens the actions of a table row; align="end" lines the menu up with its end.

```html
<sl:dropdown-menu id="order-1042-actions">
  <sl:dropdown-menu-trigger variant="ghost" size="icon-sm" aria-label="Actions for order 1042">
    <sl:icon name="ellipsis"/>
  </sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>
  <sl:dropdown-menu-content align="end">
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/orders/1042">View order</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item hx-post="/orders/1042/duplicate">Duplicate</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-separator/>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item variant="destructive" hx-delete="/orders/1042">Delete</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
  </sl:dropdown-menu-content>
</sl:dropdown-menu>
```

```html
<tr th:each="order : ${orders}" th:id="|order-${order.id}|">
  <td th:text="${order.number}">1042</td>
  <td>
    <sl:dropdown-menu th:id="|order-${order.id}-actions|">
      <sl:dropdown-menu-trigger variant="ghost" size="icon-sm" th:aria-label="|Actions for order ${order.number}|">
        <sl:icon name="ellipsis"/>
      </sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>
      <sl:dropdown-menu-content align="end">
        <sl:dropdown-menu-item hx:get="@{/orders/{id}/edit(id=${order.id})}" hx-target="#modal-root">
          Edit
        </sl:dropdown-menu-item>
        <sl:dropdown-menu-separator/>
        <sl:dropdown-menu-item variant="destructive" hx:get="@{/orders/{id}/delete(id=${order.id})}"
                               hx-target="#modal-root">Delete</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
      </sl:dropdown-menu-content>
    </sl:dropdown-menu>
  </td>
</tr>
```

The trigger's id is the menu's id plus `-trigger` (`order-1042-actions-trigger`). When a response replaces the row,
htmx puts the focus back on the element with the same id in the new row, so the user does not lose their place. An item
that fetches a [dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md#rendered-by-the-server-htmx) closes the menu first, so the dialog returns the
focus to the trigger when it closes.

### With htmx

- A swap that replaces an open menu leaves nothing behind: the new menu starts closed and works like the old one.
- Back never brings a menu back open: whether a popover is open is not part of the markup htmx saves in its history
  cache, and the restored menu starts again.
- An `hx-*` item sends its request and the menu closes; an `hx-target` can be anything, the menu's own row included.

### Left out

These shadcn/Radix parts and props have no counterpart, on purpose:

- **Sub-menus** (`DropdownMenuSub`, `-SubTrigger`, `-SubContent`). They need a lot more JavaScript (hover intent,
  a pointer grace area towards the sub-menu, arrow right and left) and are hard to use on a touch screen and with a
  screen reader. A server-rendered menu is short: flatten it with labels and groups, or open a dialog.
- **`DropdownMenuPortal`**: the top layer does what the portal is for.
- **State in the browser** (`open`/`onOpenChange`, `checked`/`onCheckedChange`, `value`/`onValueChange`, `onSelect`):
  the menu is opened by its trigger, an item is chosen by what its attributes say, and checked states come from the
  server.
- **`modal`**: the menu never makes the rest of the page inert; a click outside closes it.
- **`loop`**: arrow keys stop at the first and last item, Radix's default.

### Attributes

#### sl:dropdown-menu

Attributes of `<sl:dropdown-menu>`: A menu of actions or links that opens from a button, on the browser's Popover API: it sits above the page, and Escape or a click outside closes it. Holds a `sl:dropdown-menu-trigger` and a `sl:dropdown-menu-content` with the items. Renders no element of its own. Needs Alpine for the arrow keys, typeahead and closing after an item is chosen.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | **required** | The menu's id, which the trigger points at with `popovertarget`. Any other button can open it that way too. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:dropdown-menu-trigger

Attributes of `<sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>`: What opens and closes a `sl:dropdown-menu`: by default a `sl:button`, or with `as` a sidebar part (shadcn's `&lt;DropdownMenuTrigger asChild&gt;`). It renders that part with `popovertarget` set to the menu, which it names. Arrow down opens the menu on its first item, arrow up on its last. Its `icon-start` and `icon-end` slots go to the part's; any other attribute passes through to it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `as` | `button`, `sidebar-menu-button`, `sidebar-menu-action` | `button` | The part to render: a `sl:button`, a `sl:sidebar-menu-button` (a team switcher or user menu in a `sl:sidebar-menu-item`), or a `sl:sidebar-menu-action` (a "More" beside a menu button, which needs an `aria-label`). |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline`, `ghost`, `destructive`, `secondary`, `link` | `default` | Visual style, as on `sl:button`, or `default` and `outline` as on `sl:sidebar-menu-button`. Not for a sidebar menu action. |
| `size` | `xs`, `sm`, `default`, `lg`, `icon`, `icon-xs`, `icon-sm`, `icon-lg` | `default` | Size, as on `sl:button` (the `icon` sizes need an `aria-label`), or `sm`, `default` and `lg` as on `sl:sidebar-menu-button`. Not for a sidebar menu action. |
| `tooltip` | *string* | — | With `as="sidebar-menu-button"`: the label shown beside it while the sidebar is collapsed to icons, as on `sl:sidebar-menu-button`. Never shown while the menu is open. |
| `show-on-hover` | *boolean* | — | With `as="sidebar-menu-action"`: from 768&nbsp;px, show it only while the pointer is over its item, the focus is in it, or the menu is open. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:as="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:dropdown-menu-content

Attributes of `<sl:dropdown-menu-content>`: The menu itself, inside `sl:dropdown-menu`: a popover next to its trigger, in the top layer, holding the items, labels, groups and separators. It flips to the other side when there is no room.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `side` | `top`, `right`, `bottom`, `left` | `bottom` | The side of the trigger it opens on. `right` and `left` are the end and the start of a line: on a right-to-left page a `right` menu opens on the left. |
| `align` | `start`, `center`, `end` | `start` | How it lines up with the trigger along that side: `start` lines up the start edges. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:side="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:dropdown-menu-item

Attributes of `<sl:dropdown-menu-item>`: An action in a `sl:dropdown-menu-content`: a button, or with `as="a"` a link. Every attribute that is not a prop (`hx-*`, `href`, `commandfor`, `form`) passes through, and the menu closes once the item is chosen.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `as` | `button`, `a` | `button` | Element to render: a `button`, or an anchor (`a`) for navigation. |
| `variant` | `default`, `destructive` | `default` | `destructive` for an item that deletes something. |
| `type` | `button`, `submit`, `reset` | `button` | Submit behaviour of a button item. Defaults to `button`; `submit` submits the form it is in (or names with `form`). |
| `inset` | *boolean* | — | Indent the item to line up with items that start with an icon. |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | Disable the item. The arrow keys skip it; a disabled link loses its `href`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:as="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:dropdown-menu-checkbox-item

Attributes of `<sl:dropdown-menu-checkbox-item>`: An item in a `sl:dropdown-menu-content` that shows an on/off state with a check mark (`role="menuitemcheckbox"`), such as a filter or a view setting. The state comes from the server: choosing the item does what its attributes say (a link to `?archived=true`, an `hx-post`), and the page or fragment that comes back shows the new state. Give it its text as content (`[[${...}]]`), not with `th:text`, which would replace the check mark too. Otherwise as `sl:dropdown-menu-item`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `checked` | *boolean* | — | Whether the setting is on (`aria-checked`). |
| `as` | `button`, `a` | `button` | Element to render: a `button`, or an anchor (`a`). |
| `type` | `button`, `submit`, `reset` | `button` | Submit behaviour of a button item. |
| `inset` | *boolean* | — | Indent the item to line up with items that start with an icon. |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | Disable the item. The arrow keys skip it. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:checked="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:dropdown-menu-radio-group

Attributes of `<sl:dropdown-menu-radio-group>`: A set of `sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item`s in a menu, one of which is chosen (`role="group"`). Name it with `aria-label` when a label above it does not make the choice clear.

`sl:dropdown-menu-radio-group` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item

Attributes of `<sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item>`: One of a set of choices in a `sl:dropdown-menu-radio-group`, with a check mark on the chosen one (`role="menuitemradio"`), such as the sort order of a list. The server marks the chosen one `checked`: each item is a link (`?sort=name`) or a request, and the page or fragment that comes back shows the new choice. Give it its text as content (`[[${...}]]`), not with `th:text`, which would replace the check mark too. Otherwise as `sl:dropdown-menu-item`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `checked` | *boolean* | — | Whether this is the chosen item (`aria-checked`). |
| `as` | `button`, `a` | `button` | Element to render: a `button`, or an anchor (`a`). |
| `type` | `button`, `submit`, `reset` | `button` | Submit behaviour of a button item. |
| `inset` | *boolean* | — | Indent the item to line up with items that start with an icon. |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | Disable the item. The arrow keys skip it. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:checked="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:dropdown-menu-label

Attributes of `<sl:dropdown-menu-label>`: A heading for the items below it in a `sl:dropdown-menu-content`. It cannot be chosen, and the arrow keys pass it by.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `inset` | *boolean* | — | Indent the label to line up with items that start with an icon. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:inset="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:dropdown-menu-group

Attributes of `<sl:dropdown-menu-group>`: Items that belong together in a `sl:dropdown-menu-content` (`role="group"`), usually between separators or under a label.

`sl:dropdown-menu-group` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:dropdown-menu-separator

Attributes of `<sl:dropdown-menu-separator>`: A line between groups of items in a `sl:dropdown-menu-content` (`role="separator"`).

`sl:dropdown-menu-separator` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut

Attributes of `<sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut>`: A keyboard shortcut at the end of a `sl:dropdown-menu-item`, such as `⌘S`. It only shows the shortcut; the application handles the keys. Typeahead ignores it.

`sl:dropdown-menu-shortcut` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

### JavaScript

A small Alpine component, `slDropdownMenu`, adds the menu's keyboard and behaviour: `<sl:dropdown-menu-content>`
renders as `<div class="dropdown-menu-content" role="menu" popover x-data="slDropdownMenu">`.

- **The basics work without Alpine.** The trigger opens and closes the menu, Escape and a click outside close it, and
  items work with the mouse and with Tab. Alpine is needed for the arrow keys and typeahead, for closing the menu after
  an item is chosen, for `aria-expanded` on the trigger, and for placing the menu in browsers without CSS anchor
  positioning. Alpine is included by default; see [Alpine](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/alpine.md).
- **It needs no Alpine plugin.** If you load Alpine yourself (`shadleaf.assets.alpine=external`), plain Alpine is
  enough.
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:dropdown-menu-content>`.** An element has one `x-data`, and attributes on it end up
  on the menu, so yours would replace `slDropdownMenu`. Put it on an element inside the menu.

---

## Tooltip

> A short text that shows while the pointer rests on an element or the keyboard focuses it.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/tooltip/

**Example: Tooltip.** Shows while the pointer rests on the button, and at once when the keyboard focuses it.

```html
<sl:tooltip>
  <sl:button variant="outline">Hover</sl:button>
  <sl:tooltip-content>Add to library</sl:tooltip-content>
</sl:tooltip>
```

### Usage

`<sl:tooltip>` wraps the element it describes, the trigger, and a `<sl:tooltip-content>` after it. The trigger is
the first element inside: a `<sl:button>`, a link, anything that takes focus.

```html
<sl:tooltip>
  <sl:button variant="outline">Hover</sl:button>
  <sl:tooltip-content>Add to library</sl:tooltip-content>
</sl:tooltip>
```

The tooltip shows when the pointer rests on the trigger for a moment (`delay`, 300 ms by default), and at once when
the keyboard focuses it or when another tooltip hid a moment ago. It stays while the pointer moves onto it, so it can
be read when zoomed in, and hides when the pointer leaves both, when the trigger loses focus or is pressed, and on
Escape. Escape then does nothing else, so a dialog or menu around the trigger stays open. Showing a tooltip never
closes an open menu or popover either, so an item in a dropdown menu can have one. One tooltip shows at a time, and
touch screens never show one.

`<sl:tooltip>` itself has no box, so the trigger keeps its place in the layout, in a toolbar or in running text.

### Describes, does not name

The tooltip describes its trigger (`aria-describedby`, added to any description the trigger already has), so a screen
reader reads it after the trigger's name. It does not replace the name: an icon-only button keeps its `aria-label`.

**Example: With a keyboard shortcut.** An icon button keeps its aria-label as its name; the tooltip describes it, shortcut included.

```html
<sl:tooltip>
  <sl:button variant="outline" size="icon" aria-label="Save"><sl:icon name="save"/></sl:button>
  <sl:tooltip-content>
    Save changes <sl:kbd-group><sl:kbd>Ctrl</sl:kbd><sl:kbd>S</sl:kbd></sl:kbd-group>
  </sl:tooltip-content>
</sl:tooltip>
```

#### When it repeats the name

A tooltip that only shows an icon button's name (the same words as its `aria-label`) would make a screen reader say
them twice: "Edit, button, Edit". `mode="label"` says the tooltip repeats the name: it is hidden from assistive
technology (`aria-hidden` on the content) and describes nothing. It still shows on hover and keyboard focus.

```html
<sl:tooltip mode="label">
  <sl:button variant="outline" size="icon" aria-label="Edit"><sl:icon name="pencil"/></sl:button>
  <sl:tooltip-content>Edit</sl:tooltip-content>
</sl:tooltip>
```

**Example: Repeating the name.** mode="label" is for a tooltip that says what the trigger's name already says, such as an icon button's aria-label: it is hidden from assistive technology and describes nothing, so a screen reader does not hear the name twice.

```html
<sl:tooltip mode="label">
  <sl:button variant="outline" size="icon" aria-label="Edit"><sl:icon name="pencil"/></sl:button>
  <sl:tooltip-content>Edit</sl:tooltip-content>
</sl:tooltip>
<sl:tooltip mode="label">
  <sl:button variant="outline" size="icon" aria-label="Delete"><sl:icon name="trash"/></sl:button>
  <sl:tooltip-content>Delete</sl:tooltip-content>
</sl:tooltip>
```

The [sidebar's](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md#collapsed-to-icons) menu buttons use this for their labels while it is collapsed to icons.

Keep the text short and plain. A tooltip cannot hold links or buttons (it hides when the pointer or focus leaves), and
nobody on a touch screen sees it, so never put information in it that is not available another way. For richer
content that the user can interact with, use a [popover](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/popover.md).

A disabled button fires no pointer events and cannot take focus, so its tooltip never shows. Wrap it in an element
that takes focus (`<span tabindex="0">`) when the tooltip explains why it is disabled, or better, say why next to it.

### Position

The tooltip shows above its trigger, centred on it. `side="top|right|bottom|left"` and `align="start|center|end"` on
`<sl:tooltip-content>` change that, and it flips to the other side when there is no room, its arrow with it. `right`
and `left` are the end and the start of a line.

**Example: Sides.** side puts it on a side of its trigger; it flips when there is no room there.

```html
<sl:tooltip>
  <sl:button variant="outline">Top</sl:button>
  <sl:tooltip-content>Shown above</sl:tooltip-content>
</sl:tooltip>
<sl:tooltip>
  <sl:button variant="outline">Right</sl:button>
  <sl:tooltip-content side="right">Shown after</sl:tooltip-content>
</sl:tooltip>
<sl:tooltip>
  <sl:button variant="outline">Bottom</sl:button>
  <sl:tooltip-content side="bottom">Shown below</sl:tooltip-content>
</sl:tooltip>
<sl:tooltip>
  <sl:button variant="outline">Left</sl:button>
  <sl:tooltip-content side="left">Shown before</sl:tooltip-content>
</sl:tooltip>
```

It is placed with [CSS anchor positioning](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/CSS_anchor_positioning),
which every current browser supports (Chrome and Edge 133, Safari 26, Firefox 147). In older ones the tooltip's small
Alpine component places it from the trigger's position instead.

### Attributes

#### sl:tooltip

Attributes of `<sl:tooltip>`: A short text that shows while the pointer rests on an element or the keyboard focuses it, on the browser's Popover API. The first element inside is the trigger, any focusable element (a `sl:button`, a link); the `sl:tooltip-content` after it describes it (`aria-describedby`). Escape hides it. Needs Alpine.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `mode` | `description`, `label` | `description` | What the text is to a screen reader: `description` adds it to the trigger as a description; `label` is for a text that repeats the trigger's name, such as an icon button's `aria-label`: it is hidden from assistive technology and describes nothing, so the name is not said twice. |
| `delay` | *number* | `300` | How long the pointer rests on the trigger before the tooltip shows, in milliseconds. Keyboard focus shows it at once, and so does moving to another trigger right after a tooltip hid. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:mode="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:tooltip-content

Attributes of `<sl:tooltip-content>`: The text of a `sl:tooltip` (`role="tooltip"`), after its trigger. Keep it short and plain: it cannot hold links or buttons, and touch screens never show it. It flips to the other side when there is no room. In a tooltip with `mode="label"` it is hidden from assistive technology (`aria-hidden`).

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `side` | `top`, `right`, `bottom`, `left` | `top` | The side of the trigger it shows on. `right` and `left` are the end and the start of a line. |
| `align` | `start`, `center`, `end` | `center` | How it lines up with the trigger along that side. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:side="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

### JavaScript

The tooltip is shown and hidden by a small Alpine component, `slTooltip`: `<sl:tooltip>` renders as
`<span class="tooltip" x-data="slTooltip">`.

- **It needs Alpine.** Browsers cannot show a popup on hover by themselves yet, so without Alpine the trigger still
  works but no tooltip shows. Alpine is included by default; see [Alpine](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/alpine.md).
- **It needs no Alpine plugin.** If you load Alpine yourself (`shadleaf.assets.alpine=external`), plain Alpine is
  enough.
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:tooltip>`.** An element has one `x-data`, and attributes on `<sl:tooltip>` end up
  on that span, so yours would replace `slTooltip` and the tooltip would stop working. Put it on the trigger, or on an
  element around `<sl:tooltip>`.

---

## Alert

> A callout for a message the user should notice.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/alert/

**Example: Alert.**

```html
<sl:alert>
  <sl:icon name="circle-check"/>
  <sl:alert-title>Order placed</sl:alert-title>
  <sl:alert-description>We will email you when it ships.</sl:alert-description>
</sl:alert>
```

### Built from parts

`<sl:alert>` holds an optional [`<sl:icon>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/icon.md), then `<sl:alert-title>` and `<sl:alert-description>`, as in
shadcn/ui. The icon takes the alert's colour and sits beside both. Use either part on its own.

**Example: Title only, no icon.**

```html
<sl:alert>
  <sl:alert-title>Your trial ends in 3 days.</sl:alert-title>
</sl:alert>
```

### Destructive

**Example: Destructive.**

```html
<sl:alert variant="destructive">
  <sl:icon name="circle-alert"/>
  <sl:alert-title>Payment failed</sl:alert-title>
  <sl:alert-description>
    <p>Your card was declined. Check the details and try again:</p>
    <ul>
      <li>the expiry date</li>
      <li>the billing address</li>
    </ul>
  </sl:alert-description>
</sl:alert>
```

### Announcing an alert

An alert is not announced by itself. Most alerts are part of the page when it loads, and a screen reader reads them in
order with everything else; announcing them too would interrupt the user for no reason.

When an alert appears later, for example in the response to an htmx request, add `role="alert"`. A screen reader then
reads it as soon as it is inserted. Like any other attribute, `role` passes through to the rendered element. An
alert that takes the focus when it appears (`tabindex="-1"` and `autofocus`, which htmx honours in swapped content) is
read out because of the focus, and needs no role; see [Forms with htmx](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/htmx.md).

**Example: Announced.** role="alert" makes a screen reader read the alert as soon as it appears.

```html
<sl:alert variant="destructive" role="alert">
  <sl:icon name="circle-alert"/>
  <sl:alert-title>The order could not be saved</sl:alert-title>
  <sl:alert-description>Someone else changed it. Reload the page to see their changes.</sl:alert-description>
</sl:alert>
```

For a message that is not urgent, `role="status"` is announced when the user is idle instead of at once.

### Attributes

#### sl:alert

Attributes of `<sl:alert>`: A callout for a message the user should notice: an optional `sl:icon`, then `sl:alert-title` and `sl:alert-description`. It is not announced by itself; add `role="alert"` when it appears after the page loaded, such as in an htmx response.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `destructive` | `default` | Visual style of the alert. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:alert-title

Attributes of `<sl:alert-title>`: The title of an alert, inside `sl:alert`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` | `1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5`, `6` | — | Heading level. With it the title is a heading (`role="heading"` and `aria-level`) that screen reader users can jump to; without it, plain text, as in shadcn/ui. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:level="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:alert-description

Attributes of `<sl:alert-description>`: The body of an alert, inside `sl:alert`: text, paragraphs or a list.

`sl:alert-description` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Toast

> A short message in a corner of the page that closes itself, sent by the server; shadcn/ui's Sonner.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/toast/

**Example: Toast.** The button sends an sl-toast event; the toaster shows it, and a screen reader announces it.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" x-data
           x-on:click="$dispatch('sl-toast', {title: 'Event has been created', description: 'Monday, January 3rd at 6:00pm'})">
  Show toast
</sl:button>

<sl:toaster id="toaster-default"/>
```

### One toaster, toasts from the server

A toast says what just happened, such as "Member deleted", and goes away by itself. shadcn/ui uses
[Sonner](https://sonner.emilkowal.ski/) for it, with a `<Toaster />` in the layout and `toast("...")` calls in the
browser. In Shadleaf the layout has one `<sl:toaster>`, and the toasts come from the server, in one of three ways:

| Where the message comes from | How it gets to the toaster |
| --- | --- |
| An htmx request | The response header `HX-Trigger` sends the `sl-toast` event with the toast as its detail. |
| A full page load, e.g. after a redirect | The page renders `<sl:toast>`s in the toaster, from a flash attribute. |
| An htmx response that renders markup anyway | An out-of-band swap puts `<sl:toast>`s into the toaster. |

Put the toaster at the end of the layout's `<body>`, and let it render the toasts of a flash attribute:

```html
<body>
  ...
  <sl:toaster>
    <sl:toast th:each="toast : ${toasts}" th:title="${toast.title}" th:description="${toast.description}"
              th:variant="${toast.variant}" th:duration="${toast.duration}"/>
  </sl:toaster>
</body>
```

`Toast` (`io.github.wimdeblauwe.shadleaf.toast.Toast`) is what a controller writes for either path: a title, an
optional description, a variant and an optional duration.

```java
Toast.success("Member deleted")
Toast.error("Could not reach the mail server").withDescription("Your message was saved as a draft.")
Toast.of("Copied").withDuration(Duration.ofSeconds(2))
Toast.warning("Your session ends in 5 minutes").untilClosed()
```

#### From an htmx response: `HX-Trigger`

This is what the toast is for: a controller answering an htmx request says what happened, whatever the response
swaps. With [htmx-spring-boot](https://github.com/wimdeblauwe/htmx-spring-boot):

```java
@HxRequest
@DeleteMapping("/members/{id}")
public String delete(@PathVariable long id, HtmxResponse htmxResponse, Model model) {
  Member member = memberService.delete(id);
  htmxResponse.addTrigger("sl-toast", Toast.success("%s was deleted".formatted(member.name())));
  return "members :: deleted";
}
```

The response gets `HX-Trigger: {"sl-toast": {"title": "Ada Lovelace was deleted", "description": null,
"variant": "success", "duration": null}}`; htmx dispatches the event when the response arrives, and the toaster shows
it. Several toasts in one response are a list: `htmxResponse.addTrigger("sl-toast", List.of(first, second))`.

- **The text is only ever text.** The toaster puts the title and description in with `textContent`, so a name such as
  `<b>Ada</b>` shows as typed and can never inject markup.
- **Use `HX-Trigger`**, not `HX-Trigger-After-Swap` or `-After-Settle`: those are dispatched on the element that sent
  the request, and when the swap removed it (a deleted row), the event reaches no one.
- **Not with a redirect.** An `HX-Redirect` or `HX-Location` loads another page, and the toast with it is gone. Put
  it in a flash attribute instead (next section).

#### After a redirect: a flash attribute

A plain form post that redirects (post/redirect/get) puts the toast in a flash attribute, which the toaster in the
layout renders on the page the redirect loads:

```java
@PostMapping("/contact")
public String send(@Valid ContactForm form, BindingResult bindingResult, RedirectAttributes redirectAttributes) {
  if (bindingResult.hasErrors()) {
    return "contact";
  }
  contactService.send(form);
  redirectAttributes.addFlashAttribute("toasts", List.of(
      Toast.success("Message sent").withDescription("We answer within one working day.")));
  return "redirect:/contact";
}
```

The page the redirect loads then renders this toast in the layout's toaster (the `th:each` above), as below. Only
here in the docs does it stay (`duration="0"`), so you can look at it; in your application it closes after the
toaster's duration like any other.

**Example: Rendered by the server.** A toast the page renders, such as a flash attribute after a redirect. duration="0" keeps this one here.

```html
<sl:toaster id="toaster-server">
  <sl:toast variant="success" title="Message sent" description="We answer within one working day."
            duration="0"/>
</sl:toaster>
```

A screen reader announces such a toast a moment after the page loads (see [Announcement](#announcement)), but that
moment is also when it starts reading the new page, and not every screen reader lets one interrupt the other. When
the outcome is the whole point of the page, say it in the page too: in the title, or in an
[alert](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/alert.md).

#### Out of band

A response that renders markup anyway can put a toast into the toaster itself, whose id is `toaster` unless you give it
another. Wrap the toasts in an element whose children a `beforeend` swap appends (an `ol` suits `li` toasts):

```html
<th:block th:fragment="invited">
  <form th:replace="~{members :: invite-form}"></form>
  <ol hx-swap-oob="beforeend:#toaster">
    <sl:toast variant="success" th:title="|Invitation sent to ${member.name}|"
              description="They get an email with a link to join the team."/>
  </ol>
</th:block>
```

A toast rendered this way can hold markup of its own, such as a link: content in `<sl:toast>` replaces the
description. Remember that the toast goes away (see [No action button](#no-action-button)).

#### From your own script

The toaster listens for the event on the document, so your own Alpine markup (or any script) can send one too:

```html
<sl:button x-data x-on:click="$dispatch('sl-toast', {title: 'Link copied'})">Copy link</sl:button>
```

The detail is a toast (`{title, description, variant, duration}`), a list of them, or a string, the title.

### Variants

The variant only changes the icon, as in shadcn: none, a check (`success`), an i (`info`), a triangle (`warning`) and
an x (`error`), whose icon takes the destructive colour. A screen reader announces an `error` at once, interrupting
what it was reading; the others when it is done. Give a toast a title that says what happened on its own ("Could not
save"), since the icon is decoration and is not read out.

**Example: Variants.** The variant sets the icon; a screen reader announces an error at once.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" x-data x-on:click="$dispatch('sl-toast', {title: 'Event has been created'})">
  Default
</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="outline" x-data
           x-on:click="$dispatch('sl-toast', {title: 'Event has been created', variant: 'success'})">
  Success
</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="outline" x-data
           x-on:click="$dispatch('sl-toast', {title: 'Be at the area 10 minutes before the event time', variant: 'info'})">
  Info
</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="outline" x-data
           x-on:click="$dispatch('sl-toast', {title: 'Event start time cannot be earlier than 8am', variant: 'warning'})">
  Warning
</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="outline" x-data
           x-on:click="$dispatch('sl-toast', {title: 'Event has not been created', variant: 'error'})">
  Error
</sl:button>

<sl:toaster id="toaster-variants"/>
```

An icon of your own replaces the variant's:

```html
<sl:toast title="Invitation sent">
  <sl:slot name="icon"><sl:icon name="mail"/></sl:slot>
</sl:toast>
```

### Timing, pausing and closing

A toast closes after 5 seconds (`duration` on the toaster, or on one toast; `0` keeps it until it is closed). The
time stops while the pointer is on the toasts, while one of them has the focus and while the page is in another tab,
and at least a second is left afterwards, so nobody loses a toast while reading it. Choose a longer duration, or `0`,
for a toast with a long description.

- **The close button** closes one toast. It needs Alpine, so it only shows once Alpine runs; its label comes from the
  message `sl.toast.close` (see [Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md)).
- **Alt+T** moves the focus to the newest toast, as in Sonner, from anywhere on the page; the toaster's name,
  "Notifications (Alt+T)" (message `sl.toaster.label`), tells screen reader users so. **Tab** then reaches the close
  buttons.
- **Escape** closes the toast that has the focus, and nothing else: an open dialog around it stays open.
- When the toast with the focus closes, the focus moves to the next toast, and after the last one back to where it was
  before.

### Several at once

Newer toasts stack in front of older ones, as Sonner's do; hovering or focusing them spreads them out. At most three
show (`visible-toasts`); the older ones wait, with their time stopped, and show again when a newer one closes.

**Example: Several at once.** Newer toasts stack in front; hover or focus them to spread them out, which also stops their time.

```html
<sl:button variant="outline" x-data
           x-on:click="$dispatch('sl-toast', [{title: 'Invitation sent to Ada'}, {title: 'Invitation sent to Grace'}, {title: 'Invitation sent to Alan', description: 'They get an email with a link to join the team.'}])">
  Invite three members
</sl:button>

<sl:toaster id="toaster-stack"/>
```

Where the user prefers reduced motion, toasts only fade in and out; they do not slide.

### Position

`position` puts the toasts in a corner or at the middle of the top or bottom edge: `top-left`, `top-center`,
`top-right`, `bottom-left`, `bottom-center` or `bottom-right` (the default, as Sonner's). Right and left are the end
and the start of a line, so they swap on a right-to-left page. On a narrow screen the toasts take the full width.

```html
<sl:toaster position="top-center"/>
```

### Above a modal dialog

A modal dialog makes the rest of the page inert: it can be seen but not clicked, focused or read by a screen reader,
and the dialog is drawn above everything else. The toasts are a popover (in the top layer, above everything), and while
a modal dialog is open they move into it, so a toast about something done in a dialog that stays open shows above it,
can be closed and is announced. When the dialog closes they move back. A response that closes the dialog and shows a
toast (`HX-Trigger: {"sl-dialog-close": null, "sl-toast": {...}}`) works whichever event comes first.

### Announcement

The toasts themselves are not a live region, so a screen reader does not read them twice. The toaster has two empty
live regions from the start, one polite (`role="status"`) and one assertive (`role="alert"`, for errors), and writes
each toast's title and description into one of them when it arrives. A live region has to be on the page before its
content changes for a screen reader to notice, which is why they are in the toaster's markup rather than added with
the toast; a toast rendered with the page is written into them a moment after it loads. The messages are removed from
the live regions after a few seconds, so reading the page later finds each toast once, in the "Notifications" region,
while it shows. The region is only a landmark while it holds toasts.

### Focus after the thing is gone

A toast must not take the focus: it goes away, and the focus would fall to the top of the page with it. When an action
removes the element that had the focus, such as a Delete button in a table row, move the focus to what comes next and
let the toast say what happened. The [alert dialog's htmx example](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/alert-dialog.md#confirming-with-htmx) moves it to
the next row.

### No action button

Sonner's toast can hold an action button ("Undo"). Shadleaf's has none: a toast closes by itself, and whatever it offers
is gone with it, for someone who reads slowly, uses a screen reader or looked away. WCAG asks that a time limit on
something the user has to do can be turned off or extended. Put the action in the page (an Undo in the row's place, a
link in the confirmation), and use the toast to say what happened. If a toast you render out of band holds a link, the
same must be reachable elsewhere, or give it `duration="0"`.

### Left out

- **`toast()` in the browser.** The server sends toasts; a script can dispatch `sl-toast` (see above).
- **Action and cancel buttons**, and **promise and loading toasts**: see above; a loading state belongs on the button
  that started the request.
- **Swipe to dismiss.** The close button, Escape and the time do it.
- **Rich colours** (`richColors`), **`expand`** (always spread out), **`invert`**, **`offset`** and **`gap`** as
  props: the skins set the look, and the CSS custom properties `--toaster-offset` and `--toast-gap` on
  `.toaster-viewport` the spacing.
- **Updating a toast by id.**

### Without JavaScript

Without Alpine a toast rendered with the page still shows, in its corner, but it has no close button and does not go
away; events and out-of-band toasts need htmx or a script anyway.

### Attributes

#### sl:toaster

Attributes of `<sl:toaster>`: Where toasts show: short messages in a corner of the page that close themselves, such as "Member deleted". Put one at the end of the layout's `body`. Toasts get there three ways: as `sl:toast`s inside it, which the server renders (e.g. from a flash attribute after a redirect); from an `sl-toast` event, which an htmx response sends with the `HX-Trigger` header; or swapped in out of band. A screen reader announces each toast, and toasts show above an open modal dialog and can be used there. Needs Alpine.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | `toaster` | The toaster's id, which an out-of-band swap targets (`hx-swap-oob="beforeend:#toaster"`). |
| `position` | `top-left`, `top-center`, `top-right`, `bottom-left`, `bottom-center`, `bottom-right` | `bottom-right` | The corner or edge the toasts show in. `right` and `left` are the end and the start of a line. |
| `duration` | *number* | `5000` | How long a toast shows, in milliseconds, unless it sets its own. The time stops while the pointer is on the toasts, while one of them has the focus and while the page is hidden. `0` keeps toasts until they are closed. |
| `visible-toasts` | *number* | `3` | How many toasts show at once, the newest ones. Older ones wait, with their time stopped, and show again when a newer one closes. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:toast

Attributes of `<sl:toast>`: One toast in a `sl:toaster`: a title, an optional description and a close button. The server renders it for a message that has to show when the page loads, such as a flash attribute after a redirect, or in an out-of-band swap. Content of its own replaces the description. A toast goes away by itself, so it must never hold the only way to do something.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | *string* | **required** | What happened, in a few words: "Member deleted". |
| `description` | *string* | — | More about it, under the title. |
| `variant` | `default`, `success`, `info`, `warning`, `error` | `default` | The kind of message, shown by its icon: none, a check, an i, a warning triangle or an x. A screen reader announces an `error` at once, interrupting what it reads; the others when it is done. |
| `duration` | *number* | — | How long this toast shows, in milliseconds, instead of the toaster's `duration`. `0` keeps it until it is closed. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:title="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

### JavaScript

A small Alpine component, `slToaster`, is on `<sl:toaster>`: it renders as
`<div class="toaster" x-data="slToaster">`.

- **It needs no Alpine plugin.** If you load Alpine yourself (`shadleaf.assets.alpine=external`), plain Alpine is
  enough. See [Alpine](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/alpine.md).
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:toaster>`.** An element has one `x-data`.
- **One toaster per page.** A second one works, but the first takes every event.
- **htmx history:** the toaster removes its toasts before htmx saves the page in its history cache, so going back never
  shows an old toast again.

| Event | On | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sl-toast` | anything (it bubbles to the document), or the document | Shows the toast in its detail: `{title, description, variant, duration}`, a list of them, or a string. |

---

## Skeleton

> A placeholder that pulses while content loads.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/skeleton/

**Example: Skeleton.**

```html
<sl:skeleton/>
<sl:skeleton/>
```

### Shapes and sizes

A skeleton is a line of text by default: the full width of its container and one line high. `shape="circle"` stands in
for an avatar. Both are starting points: give a skeleton the size of what it replaces with a class of your own, for
example `class="h-32 w-64"` when your application compiles Tailwind. Your class wins over the skin's size.

**Example: In a card.** The card that is loading says so with aria-busy; the skeletons are hidden from assistive technology.

```html
<sl:card aria-busy="true">
  <sl:card-header>
    <sl:skeleton shape="circle"/>
  </sl:card-header>
  <sl:card-content>
    <sl:skeleton/>
    <sl:skeleton/>
  </sl:card-content>
</sl:card>
```

### While it loads

A skeleton is only a picture of content to come, so it is hidden from assistive technology (`aria-hidden="true"`).
Put `aria-busy="true"` on the region that is loading, and remove it when the content arrives, for example when htmx
swaps it in. The pulse stops for users who ask for reduced motion.

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:skeleton>`: A placeholder that pulses while content loads. It is hidden from assistive technology; mark the region that is loading with `aria-busy="true"` instead. Give it the size of what it stands in for with a class of your own.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `shape` | `line`, `circle` | `line` | A line of text (full width, one line high) or a circle (an avatar). |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:shape="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Spinner

> A spinning icon that says something is loading.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/spinner/

**Example: Spinner.**

```html
<sl:spinner/>
```

### What it says

A spinner is a status (`role="status"`) named "Loading". Give it an `aria-label` of its own to say what is loading;
the default is the message `sl.spinner.label`, which you can reword or [translate](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md) in your `messages.properties`.

**Example: Saying what loads.** An aria-label replaces the default name, "Loading".

```html
<sl:spinner aria-label="Loading invoices"/>
```

For a button that is busy, use [`<sl:button loading>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/button.md) instead: it shows the spinner, disables the button
and announces the busy state. To show a skeleton of the content to come, use [`<sl:skeleton>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/skeleton.md).

### Sizes

A spinner is 16 pixels, or the size of an icon in the component around it, such as
[`<sl:empty-media variant="icon">`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/empty.md#while-it-loads). `size` sets the width and height in pixels.

**Example: Sizes.**

```html
<sl:spinner/>
<sl:spinner size="24"/>
<sl:spinner size="32"/>
```

### Reduced motion

For users who ask for reduced motion the spinner turns slower instead of standing still: a frozen arc reads as a
broken icon.

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:spinner>`: A spinning icon that says something is loading: a status (`role="status"`) named "Loading" (the message `sl.spinner.label`; an `aria-label` of its own replaces it). It turns slower, not still, when the user asks for reduced motion. For a button that is busy, use `sl:button loading` instead.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `size` | *number* | — | Width and height in pixels. Without it the spinner is 16 pixels, or sized by the surrounding component (as an icon in `sl:empty-media`). |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:size="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Empty

> What a list, a table, a search or a page shows when there is nothing to show yet.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/empty/

**Example: Empty.**

```html
<sl:empty>
  <sl:empty-header>
    <sl:empty-media variant="icon"><sl:icon name="folder-code"/></sl:empty-media>
    <sl:empty-title level="3">No projects yet</sl:empty-title>
    <sl:empty-description>You haven't created any projects yet. Get started by creating your first
      project.</sl:empty-description>
  </sl:empty-header>
  <sl:empty-content>
    <sl:button as="a" href="/projects/new">Create project</sl:button>
  </sl:empty-content>
  <sl:button as="a" href="/docs/projects" variant="link" size="sm">
    Learn more
    <sl:slot name="icon-end"><sl:icon name="arrow-up-right"/></sl:slot>
  </sl:button>
</sl:empty>
```

### Built from parts

An empty state is `<sl:empty>` plus the parts you need, as in shadcn/ui: `<sl:empty-header>` (holding
`<sl:empty-media>`, `<sl:empty-title>` and `<sl:empty-description>`) and `<sl:empty-content>` for what to do next, such
as a button, a search field or a link. Everything is centred, and the empty state grows to fill a flex container, so
it sits in the middle of the space it is given.

`<sl:empty-media variant="icon">` puts an icon on a muted square. The default media shows its content as it is, for an
avatar or an image.

**Example: With an avatar.** The default media shows its content as it is.

```html
<sl:empty>
  <sl:empty-header>
    <sl:empty-media>
      <sl:avatar size="lg" label="Ada Lovelace">
        <sl:avatar-image th:src="@{/users/{id}/photo(id=${user.id})}"/>
        <sl:avatar-fallback>AL</sl:avatar-fallback>
      </sl:avatar>
    </sl:empty-media>
    <sl:empty-title level="3">User offline</sl:empty-title>
    <sl:empty-description>This user is currently offline. You can leave a message to notify them or try
      again later.</sl:empty-description>
  </sl:empty-header>
  <sl:empty-content>
    <sl:button size="sm">Leave message</sl:button>
  </sl:empty-content>
</sl:empty>
```

### A title that is a heading

`<sl:empty-title>` is plain text unless you give it a `level`. With one, it is announced as a heading of that level.
Pick the level that fits the page: an empty state under an `<h2>` gets `level="3"`.

### Outline

`border` draws a dashed line around the empty state, to mark out the area. It works without utility classes, so also
with the standalone stylesheet.

**Example: Outline.** border draws a dashed line around the empty state.

```html
<sl:empty border>
  <sl:empty-header>
    <sl:empty-media variant="icon"><sl:icon name="cloud"/></sl:empty-media>
    <sl:empty-title level="3">Cloud storage is empty</sl:empty-title>
    <sl:empty-description>Upload files to your cloud storage to access them anywhere.</sl:empty-description>
  </sl:empty-header>
  <sl:empty-content>
    <sl:button variant="outline" size="sm">Upload files</sl:button>
  </sl:empty-content>
</sl:empty>
```

### In a card

**Example: In a card.**

```html
<sl:card>
  <sl:card-header>
    <sl:card-title level="3">Projects</sl:card-title>
    <sl:card-description>Everything your team is working on</sl:card-description>
  </sl:card-header>
  <sl:card-content>
    <sl:empty>
      <sl:empty-header>
        <sl:empty-media variant="icon"><sl:icon name="folder"/></sl:empty-media>
        <sl:empty-title level="4">No projects yet</sl:empty-title>
        <sl:empty-description>Create a project to start working with your team.</sl:empty-description>
      </sl:empty-header>
      <sl:empty-content>
        <sl:button as="a" href="/projects/new">Create project</sl:button>
      </sl:empty-content>
    </sl:empty>
  </sl:card-content>
</sl:card>
```

### In an empty table

Put the empty state in [`<sl:table-empty>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/table.md), which spans every column: it replaces the row's
"No results." text.

**Example: In an empty table.** An sl:empty as the content of sl:table-empty replaces its "No results." text.

```html
<sl:table>
  <thead>
    <tr>
      <th>Name</th>
      <th>Email</th>
      <th>Role</th>
    </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
    <tr th:each="person : ${people}">
      <td th:text="${person.name}">Ada Lovelace</td>
      <td th:text="${person.email}">ada@example.com</td>
      <td th:text="${person.role}">Owner</td>
    </tr>
    <sl:table-empty colspan="3" th:if="${people.empty}">
      <sl:empty>
        <sl:empty-header>
          <sl:empty-media variant="icon"><sl:icon name="users"/></sl:empty-media>
          <sl:empty-title level="3">No people yet</sl:empty-title>
          <sl:empty-description>Invite your team to collaborate on this project.</sl:empty-description>
        </sl:empty-header>
        <sl:empty-content>
          <sl:button as="a" href="/people/invite" size="sm">
            <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="plus"/></sl:slot>
            Invite people
          </sl:button>
        </sl:empty-content>
      </sl:empty>
    </sl:table-empty>
  </tbody>
</sl:table>
```

### With a search field

A link in a description is underlined. An `<sl:empty-description>` can also go in the content, below the field.

**Example: With a search field.** Links in the description are underlined.

```html
<sl:empty>
  <sl:empty-header>
    <sl:empty-title level="3">404 - Not found</sl:empty-title>
    <sl:empty-description>The page you're looking for doesn't exist. Try searching for what you need
      below.</sl:empty-description>
  </sl:empty-header>
  <sl:empty-content>
    <form action="/search" role="search" aria-label="Pages">
      <sl:input-group>
        <sl:input-group-input type="search" name="pageQuery" placeholder="Try searching for pages..."
                              aria-label="Search pages"/>
        <sl:input-group-addon><sl:icon name="search"/></sl:input-group-addon>
      </sl:input-group>
    </form>
    <sl:empty-description>Need help? <a href="/support">Contact support</a></sl:empty-description>
  </sl:empty-content>
</sl:empty>
```

### While it loads

An [`<sl:spinner>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/spinner.md) takes the icon's place in the media while something loads.

**Example: With a spinner.** While something loads, an sl:spinner takes the icon's place.

```html
<sl:empty>
  <sl:empty-header>
    <sl:empty-media variant="icon"><sl:spinner/></sl:empty-media>
    <sl:empty-title level="3">Processing your request</sl:empty-title>
    <sl:empty-description>Please wait while we process your request. Do not refresh the
      page.</sl:empty-description>
  </sl:empty-header>
  <sl:empty-content>
    <sl:button variant="outline" size="sm">Cancel</sl:button>
  </sl:empty-content>
</sl:empty>
```

### Attributes

#### sl:empty

Attributes of `<sl:empty>`: An empty state: what a list, a table, a search or a page shows when there is nothing to show yet. Holds an `sl:empty-header` (media, title and description) and an optional `sl:empty-content` with what to do next, centred in the space it is given.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `border` | *boolean* | — | A dashed line around the empty state, to mark out the area (shadcn's `border border-dashed` classes). |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:border="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:empty-header

Attributes of `<sl:empty-header>`: The top of an `sl:empty`: an optional `sl:empty-media`, an `sl:empty-title` and an `sl:empty-description`, stacked and centred.

`sl:empty-header` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:empty-media

Attributes of `<sl:empty-media>`: An icon, an avatar or an image above the title of an `sl:empty`, inside `sl:empty-header`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `icon` | `default` | `icon` puts an icon on a muted square; `default` shows the content as it is, e.g. an avatar. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:empty-title

Attributes of `<sl:empty-title>`: The title of an `sl:empty`, inside `sl:empty-header`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` | `1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5`, `6` | — | Heading level. With it the title is a heading (`role="heading"` and `aria-level`) that screen reader users can jump to; without it, plain text. Pick the level that fits the page's outline. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:level="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:empty-description

Attributes of `<sl:empty-description>`: The text below the title of an `sl:empty`. Links in it are underlined.

`sl:empty-description` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:empty-content

Attributes of `<sl:empty-content>`: What to do next in an `sl:empty`, below its header: a button, a search field, a link.

`sl:empty-content` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Table

> A table in shadcn/ui's look, whose column headers sort it on the server.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/table/

**Example: Table.**

```html
<sl:table>
  <caption>A list of your recent invoices.</caption>
  <thead>
    <tr>
      <th>Invoice</th>
      <th>Status</th>
      <th>Method</th>
      <sl:table-head align="end">Amount</sl:table-head>
    </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
    <tr>
      <td>INV001</td>
      <td><sl:badge variant="secondary">Paid</sl:badge></td>
      <td>Credit card</td>
      <td data-align="end">€250.00</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>INV002</td>
      <td><sl:badge variant="outline">Pending</sl:badge></td>
      <td>Bank transfer</td>
      <td data-align="end">€150.00</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>INV003</td>
      <td><sl:badge variant="destructive">Overdue</sl:badge></td>
      <td>Credit card</td>
      <td data-align="end">€350.00</td>
    </tr>
  </tbody>
  <tfoot>
    <tr>
      <td colspan="3">Total</td>
      <td data-align="end">€750.00</td>
    </tr>
  </tfoot>
</sl:table>
```

### Plain rows and cells

`<sl:table>` styles the plain table elements you write inside it: `caption`, `thead`, `tbody`, `tfoot`, `tr`, `th` and
`td`. There is no component per row or cell, as there is in shadcn/ui, so a row is the `th:each` you already write:

```html
<sl:table>
  <thead>
    <tr><th>Name</th><th>Email</th></tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
    <tr th:each="person : ${people}">
      <td th:text="${person.name}">Ada Lovelace</td>
      <td th:text="${person.email}">ada@example.com</td>
    </tr>
  </tbody>
</sl:table>
```

Attributes on `<sl:table>` go to the `table`; it sits in a container that scrolls sideways when the table is wider
than the page. A table nested in a cell keeps its own look, so put it in an `<sl:table>` of its own.

### Alignment

`<sl:table-head align="end">` puts a header's label at the end of its cell. The cells below take the same with
`data-align="end"` (or `"center"`), which works without utility classes, so also with the standalone stylesheet. Use
it for amounts and other numbers, so their digits line up.

### Wide tables

A table wider than the page scrolls sideways inside its container. A keyboard user can scroll it too: Chromium and
Firefox make the container a tab stop of its own when nothing in it can take the focus, and the arrow keys then scroll
it; when a sort link, a checkbox or a row's menu button in it takes the focus, the browser scrolls it into view. The
container has no `tabindex` of its own, which would add a tab stop to every table that fits.

WebKit, checked with Playwright's build of it, does neither for a container of plain text: there a wide table with
nothing to focus in it cannot be reached with the keyboard, and with Option+Tab (Safari's *Press Tab to highlight each
item*) its links can, as above. axe reports such a table as
`scrollable-region-focusable`. Keep a table that is only text from growing wider than a phone, or give its rows
something to focus.

#### Pin the first column

When the first column names the row, it should not scroll away. `sticky-first-column` pins the first cell of every row
(`th` and `td`, in `thead`, `tbody` and `tfoot`) to the start edge with `position: sticky`, so it also works in
right-to-left pages. No JavaScript is involved:

**Example: Pinned first column.** A wide table whose first column stays in view while the rest scrolls sideways.

```html
<sl:table sticky-first-column>
  <caption>Sales per month.</caption>
  <thead>
    <tr>
      <th>Person</th>
      <th data-align="end">Jan</th>
      <th data-align="end">Feb</th>
      <th data-align="end">Mar</th>
      <th data-align="end">Apr</th>
      <th data-align="end">May</th>
      <th data-align="end">Jun</th>
      <th data-align="end">Jul</th>
      <th data-align="end">Aug</th>
      <th data-align="end">Sep</th>
      <th data-align="end">Oct</th>
      <th data-align="end">Nov</th>
      <th data-align="end">Dec</th>
    </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
    <tr>
      <td>Ada Lovelace</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1200</td>
```
