```html
      <td data-align="end">€1237</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1274</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1311</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1348</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1385</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1422</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1459</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1496</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1533</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1570</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1607</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>Grace Hopper</td>
      <td data-align="end">€900</td>
      <td data-align="end">€953</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1006</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1059</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1112</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1165</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1218</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1271</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1324</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1377</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1430</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1483</td>
    </tr>
    <tr>
      <td>Alan Turing</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1500</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1479</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1458</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1437</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1416</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1395</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1374</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1353</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1332</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1311</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1290</td>
      <td data-align="end">€1269</td>
    </tr>
  </tbody>
  <tfoot>
    <tr>
      <td>Total</td>
      <td data-align="end">€3600</td>
      <td data-align="end">€3669</td>
      <td data-align="end">€3738</td>
      <td data-align="end">€3807</td>
      <td data-align="end">€3876</td>
      <td data-align="end">€3945</td>
      <td data-align="end">€4014</td>
      <td data-align="end">€4083</td>
      <td data-align="end">€4152</td>
      <td data-align="end">€4221</td>
      <td data-align="end">€4290</td>
      <td data-align="end">€4359</td>
    </tr>
  </tfoot>
</sl:table>
```

The pinned cells have the page's `--background` so the scrolled cells do not show through; when the table sits on
something else, such as a card, set `--sl-table-bg` on the table (`style` is not allowed under a strict CSP, so do it
in your stylesheet: `.my-table { --sl-table-bg: var(--card); }`). Row hover, selected and footer tints carry over to the
pinned cells. A line on the pinned column's end edge appears once the table has scrolled; it is driven by a CSS
scroll-driven animation (Chromium and Safari 26; Firefox where `animation-timeline` is enabled), and where that is
missing the line is always drawn. In forced colours the line is a system-coloured border, and on paper nothing is
pinned. The example is plain text, so what [Wide tables](#wide-tables) says about WebKit and the keyboard applies to it.

### Sorting on the server

The rows of a Spring application live on the server, which sends one page of them. So a sortable header is a link that
asks the server for the page in another order, not a script that reorders the rows in the browser:

**Example: Sorted on the server.** Sorted by name, in a request for /people?q=a&sort=name,asc. Each link keeps q.

```html
<sl:table th:page="${people}">
  <thead>
    <tr>
      <sl:table-head sort="name">Name</sl:table-head>
      <sl:table-head sort="email">Email</sl:table-head>
      <th data-align="end">Orders</th>
    </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
    <tr th:each="person : ${people}">
      <td th:text="${person.name}">Ada Lovelace</td>
      <td th:text="${person.email}">ada@example.com</td>
      <td data-align="end" th:text="${person.orders}">3</td>
    </tr>
  </tbody>
</sl:table>
```

`<sl:table-head sort="name">` links to the same page with `sort=name,asc`, or `sort=name,desc` when the table is
sorted by name ascending already. The link keeps every other parameter in the address (a search, the page size) and
drops the page number, so a new order starts on the first page. The sorted column gets `aria-sort`, which screen
readers announce, and an arrow; the others show both chevrons. A click on the sorted column reverses it; there is no
unsorted state, because the default order is the controller's.

The controller takes a `Pageable`, which Spring Data fills from those parameters, and puts the page in the model:

```java
@GetMapping("/people")
String people(@PageableDefault(sort = "name") Pageable pageable, Model model) {
  model.addAttribute("people", personRepository.findAll(pageable));
  return "people";
}
```

```html
<sl:table th:page="${people}">
  <thead>
    <tr>
      <sl:table-head sort="name">Name</sl:table-head>
      <sl:table-head sort="email">Email</sl:table-head>
    </tr>
  </thead>
  ...
</sl:table>
```

`th:page` takes the `Page` (or `Slice`), whose `Sort` tells the headers how the rows are sorted, the default from
`@PageableDefault` included. Only the first sort key is shown. `spring-data-commons` is all it needs, not a Spring Data
module: rows that do not come from a repository can be wrapped in a
`new PageImpl<>(rows, pageable, total)`.

Without `th:page` the headers read the order from the request's `sort` parameter, so a table sorts without Spring Data
too; the controller then reads that parameter itself.

#### Parameter names

The links use the parameter names your `Pageable` reads. Shadleaf takes them from Spring Boot's properties:
`spring.data.web.sort.sort-parameter`, and for the page and size links
`spring.data.web.pageable.page-parameter`, `size-parameter`, `prefix`, `qualifier-delimiter` and
`one-indexed-parameters`. If you configure Spring Data's resolvers in code instead, declare a `PagingParameters` bean
with the same values.

#### Two tables on one page

Two sortable tables on one page need their own parameters. Give the controller's second `Pageable` a `@Qualifier`, and
the table the same `qualifier`:

```java
@GetMapping("/team")
String team(Pageable pageable, @Qualifier("members") Pageable members, Model model) { ... }
```

```html
<sl:table th:page="${members}" qualifier="members"> ... </sl:table>
```

Its headers then link to `members_sort=email,asc` and leave the other table's `sort` and `page` alone.

#### Limit what can be sorted

The sort parameter is part of the address, so anyone can edit it. Spring Data passes an unknown property on to the
repository, which fails with an error page (`?sort=password` is an error at best). So the controller checks the order
against the columns the table offers before it queries, and falls back to the default order when it asks for anything
else:

```java
@Controller
class PeopleController {

  // The properties of the table's <sl:table-head sort="..."> columns.
  private static final Set<String> SORTABLE = Set.of("name", "email", "orders");

  private final PersonRepository personRepository;

  PeopleController(PersonRepository personRepository) {
    this.personRepository = personRepository;
  }

  @GetMapping("/people")
  String people(@PageableDefault(sort = "name") Pageable pageable, Model model) {
    model.addAttribute("people", personRepository.findAll(sortable(pageable)));
    return "people";
  }

  /** The pageable as requested, or sorted by name when it names a property the table does not offer. */
  private static Pageable sortable(Pageable pageable) {
    boolean allowed = pageable.getSort().stream().allMatch(order -> SORTABLE.contains(order.getProperty()));
    return allowed
        ? pageable
        : PageRequest.of(pageable.getPageNumber(), pageable.getPageSize(), Sort.by("name"));
  }
}
```

The page the repository returns carries the order it was sorted by, so the table shows the fallback correctly too.

#### A stable order across pages

Rows with the same value in the sorted column (two people with 59 orders) come back from the database in no particular
order, and not necessarily the same one for every page. Once the table has [pages](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/pagination.md), such a row can
show up on two pages, or on none. Add a unique key the reader does not see, such as the id, as the last sort key before
you query:

```java
@GetMapping("/people")
String people(@PageableDefault(sort = "name") Pageable pageable, Model model) {
  Pageable stable = PageRequest.of(pageable.getPageNumber(), pageable.getPageSize(),
      sortable(pageable).getSort().and(Sort.by("id")));
  model.addAttribute("people", personRepository.findAll(stable));
  return "people";
}
```

The headers show only the first sort key, and the links are built from the request rather than from the page's
`Sort`, so the id never shows up in the address. Sample-01's `/people` page does this, and its test follows Next from
the first page to the last and finds every person exactly once.

#### Sorting an enum column

An enum stored as a string (`@Enumerated(EnumType.STRING)`) sorts alphabetically: Admin, Guest, Member, Owner. To sort
roles by rank, sort the database by the rank:

- **Store the rank.** An `AttributeConverter` writes the enum as a number, so `sort=role` sorts by rank and the header
  needs nothing else. Sample-01 does this:

  ```java
  public enum Role {
    OWNER(1), ADMIN(2), MEMBER(3), GUEST(4);
    ...
  }

  @Converter
  class RoleConverter implements AttributeConverter<Role, Integer> {
    public Integer convertToDatabaseColumn(Role role) { return role == null ? null : role.getRank(); }
    public Role convertToEntityAttribute(Integer rank) { return rank == null ? null : Role.ofRank(rank); }
  }
  ```

  ```java
  @Convert(converter = RoleConverter.class)
  private Role role;
  ```

  An explicit rank, unlike `EnumType.ORDINAL`, survives reordering the enum's constants.
- **Sort by an expression.** Keep the string column and order by a `CASE` expression in a query of your own
  (`order by case p.role when 'OWNER' then 1 when 'ADMIN' then 2 ... end`), or with `JpaSort.unsafe(...)`. The
  controller then maps the `role` property of the requested `Sort` to that expression, and the `Page` it gets back
  carries the expression, not `role`: build the page the template gets with the requested `Pageable`
  (`new PageImpl<>(page.getContent(), pageable, page.getTotalElements())`) so the header still shows the order.

#### Focus after an htmx swap

Each sort link has an id (`sort-name`, or `members-sort-name` with a qualifier), so when htmx replaces the table it
gives the focus back to the link that was clicked. [Tables with htmx](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/tables-htmx.md) puts it together.

### Search and filters

A search or filter above the table is a `GET` form the controller reads (`?q=ada`). Put
[`<sl:query-params except="q"/>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/query-params.md) in it, so sending it keeps the table's order and page size and
starts on the first page; the sort and page links keep `q` by themselves. To search as the user types, see
[Tables with htmx](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/tables-htmx.md).

### Nothing found

`<sl:table-empty>` is the row to show when there are no rows: one cell across the table's `colspan` columns, saying
"No results." (the message `sl.table.empty`, which your `messages.properties` can [reword or translate](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md)). Content of its own replaces
the text.

**Example: Nothing found.**

```html
<sl:table>
  <thead>
    <tr>
      <th>Name</th>
      <th>Email</th>
      <th>Role</th>
    </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
    <tr th:each="person : ${people}">
      <td th:text="${person.name}">Ada Lovelace</td>
      <td th:text="${person.email}">ada@example.com</td>
      <td th:text="${person.role}">Owner</td>
    </tr>
    <sl:table-empty colspan="3" th:if="${people.empty}"/>
  </tbody>
</sl:table>
```

```html
<sl:table-empty colspan="3" th:if="${people.empty}">Nobody matches your search.</sl:table-empty>
```

### Actions per row

Put a [dropdown menu](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dropdown-menu.md) in the last cell, as shadcn's data table does. Give each menu an id built
from its row, so every trigger has a stable id of its own (`people-12-actions-trigger`), and name the trigger after the
row: a screen reader user tabbing through the table hears "Actions for Ada Lovelace, menu button". The header cell
above the menus needs a name too, read out with each cell: `<span class="sl-sr-only">Actions</span>`.

**Example: Actions per row.** A menu in the last cell of each row, named after its row. In an application its items post or link (see Tables with htmx); here they do nothing.

```html
<sl:table>
  <thead>
    <tr>
      <th>Name</th>
      <th>Role</th>
      <th data-align="end"><span class="sl-sr-only">Actions</span></th>
    </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
    <tr th:each="person : ${people}">
      <td th:text="${person.name}">Ada Lovelace</td>
      <td><sl:badge variant="secondary" th:text="${person.role.label}">Owner</sl:badge></td>
      <td data-align="end">
        <!--/* A role="menu" holds menu items only, so the form goes around the menu. */-->
        <form method="post" th:action="${selfUrl}">
          <input type="hidden" name="person" th:value="${person.id}">
          <sl:dropdown-menu th:id="|people-${person.id}-actions|">
            <sl:dropdown-menu-trigger variant="ghost" size="icon-sm"
                                      th:aria-label="|Actions for ${person.name}|">
              <sl:icon name="ellipsis"/>
            </sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>
            <sl:dropdown-menu-content align="end">
              <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" th:href="@{/people/{id}(id=${person.id})}">
                <sl:icon name="eye"/>View
              </sl:dropdown-menu-item>
              <sl:dropdown-menu-separator/>
              <sl:dropdown-menu-label>Change role</sl:dropdown-menu-label>
              <sl:dropdown-menu-radio-group aria-label="Change role">
                <sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item th:each="role : ${roles}" type="submit" name="role"
                                             th:value="${role}" th:checked="${person.role == role}">
                  [[${role.label}]]
                </sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item>
              </sl:dropdown-menu-radio-group>
              <sl:dropdown-menu-separator/>
              <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" variant="destructive"
                                     th:href="@{/people/{id}/delete(id=${person.id})}">
                <sl:icon name="trash-2"/>Delete
              </sl:dropdown-menu-item>
            </sl:dropdown-menu-content>
          </sl:dropdown-menu>
        </form>
      </td>
    </tr>
  </tbody>
</sl:table>
```

An item that changes something is a submit button of a `post` form. A `role="menu"` may only hold menu items, so the
form goes around the menu: a hidden input names the row, and each item sends its own `name` and `value` (`role=ADMIN`).
View is a link; Delete is a link to a page that asks first. [Tables with htmx](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/tables-htmx.md#actions-per-row)
turns the same menu into htmx requests, with an alert dialog for Delete, and keeps the focus on the row's menu button.

A menu per row is markup per row: 50 rows with a badge and the menu above render in about 9 ms and 222 KB of HTML,
5 KB once compressed (see [the cost](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/tables-htmx.md#the-cost-of-a-menu-per-row)).

### Selecting rows

For a bulk action (delete, change a role), give each row a checkbox of your own and put
`<sl:table-select-all/>` in the header cell above them. Select-all checks or unchecks every row on the page, and is
checked when they all are, indeterminate (a minus) when some are. `<sl:table-selection-count>` says how many are
selected; place it where it fits, e.g. at the start of the footer, as shadcn's data table does.

**Example: Selecting rows.** The rows are plain checkboxes named ids; one is selected, so select-all starts indeterminate.

```html
<sl:table id="people-selection">
  <thead>
    <tr>
      <th><sl:table-select-all/></th>
      <th>Name</th>
      <th>Email</th>
    </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
    <tr th:each="person : ${people}">
      <td><sl:checkbox name="ids" th:value="${person.id}"
                       th:aria-label="#{sl.table.select-row(${person.name})}"/></td>
      <td th:text="${person.name}">Ada Lovelace</td>
      <td th:text="${person.email}">ada@example.com</td>
    </tr>
  </tbody>
</sl:table>
<sl:table-selection-count for="people-selection"/>
```

```html
<form id="people-bulk" method="post" th:action="${selfUrl}">
  <sl:table id="people-table" th:page="${people}">
    <thead>
      <tr>
        <th><sl:table-select-all/></th>
        <sl:table-head sort="name">Name</sl:table-head>
        <sl:table-head sort="email">Email</sl:table-head>
      </tr>
    </thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr th:each="person : ${people}">
        <td><sl:checkbox name="ids" th:value="${person.id}"
                         th:aria-label="#{sl.table.select-row(${person.name})}"/></td>
        <td th:text="${person.name}">Ada Lovelace</td>
        <td th:text="${person.email}">ada@example.com</td>
      </tr>
    </tbody>
  </sl:table>
  <sl:button type="submit" variant="destructive">Delete selected</sl:button>
</form>
<div class="table-footer">
  <sl:table-selection-count for="people-table"/>
  <sl:pagination th:page="${people}"/>
</div>
```

- **The rows are yours.** Plain `sl:checkbox`es (`name="ids"`, the row's id as value) submit with the form, also
  without JavaScript; the controller gets `@RequestParam List<Long> ids`. The table can sit inside the form, or the
  checkboxes can name a form elsewhere with `form="..."`.
- **Which checkboxes are rows.** Select-all works on the checkboxes in the body cells of the table it is in (not of a
  table nested in a cell), leaving out disabled ones, which would not be submitted. When the body has other
  checkboxes too, `name="ids"` on select-all narrows it to those.
- **The selected look** comes from CSS: a row whose first cell holds a checked checkbox gets the muted background
  (shadcn's `data-state="selected"`), with or without JavaScript. Put the selection in the first column.
- **Names.** Every row checkbox needs an accessible name, telling the rows apart. `aria-label` with the message
  `sl.table.select-row` (`Select {0}`) as above, or point at the cell that names the row:

  ```html
  <td><sl:checkbox name="ids" th:value="${person.id}"
                   th:aria-labelledby="|person-${person.id}-name|"/></td>
  <td th:id="|person-${person.id}-name|" th:text="${person.name}">Ada Lovelace</td>
  ```

  Select-all is named "Select all rows on this page" (`sl.table.select-all`); an `aria-label` of yours replaces it.
- **The count** reads "2 of 20 selected" (`sl.table.selection.count`; keep `{0}` and `{1}` as they are, the browser
  fills them in; see [Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md)). Its `table` prop is the table's `id`, which `sl:table` puts on the `table`. It is not a live region:
  a row's checkbox announces its own state, so the count is only announced after a click on select-all, which
  changes rows the user did not touch.

#### Without JavaScript

Select-all and the count need Alpine, so they keep their place but stay hidden until it starts (`x-cloak`). Without
JavaScript the user checks the rows one by one, and the form submits them as before.

#### The bulk action

Post the form to the page's own URL, query string included, so the action knows the search, order and page to come
back to. The controller below puts that URL in the model; after the action it redirects back there with a toast
(post/redirect/get):

```java
@PostMapping("/people")
public String delete(@RequestParam(name = "ids", required = false) List<Long> ids,
    HttpServletRequest request, RedirectAttributes redirectAttributes) {
  repository.deleteAllByIdInBatch(ids == null ? List.of() : ids);
  redirectAttributes.addFlashAttribute("toasts", List.of(Toast.success("People deleted")));
  String query = request.getQueryString();
  return "redirect:/people" + (query == null ? "" : "?" + query);
}
```

Ask before a destructive action with an [alert dialog](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/alert-dialog.md) inside the form: its destructive button is a
submit button. With htmx, answer with the table alone; [Tables with htmx](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/tables-htmx.md#selection-and-a-bulk-action)
shows how, and sample-01's `/people-multiselect` page does both.

#### Selection across pages

Select-all selects the rows on the page, and a sort, another page or a new search (with htmx too) starts with nothing
selected: the selection lives in the checkboxes, and those are replaced. Keeping it across pages is the server's
business. Two ways:

- **Keep the ids on the server**, e.g. in the session: each page posts its selection (or a checkbox posts on change
  with htmx), and the page renders `checked` on the rows that were selected before. Select-all and the count then
  start from those.
- **Select everything that matches** instead of ids: after select-all, offer "Select all 270 people", which posts the
  search itself (`q`) for the action to apply to every match.

Sample-01 keeps the selection per page.

#### JavaScript

`<sl:table-select-all>` renders `x-data="slTableSelection"` and `<sl:table-selection-count>`
`x-data="slTableSelectionCount"`. They need no Alpine plugin. They follow the rows' `change` events (Space or a click),
a form reset, and rows being added or replaced (an htmx swap). Changing the rows from select-all fires `input` and
`change` on every row it changes, as a click would.

| Event | On | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sl-table-selection-change` | the `table`, bubbling | `{selected, total}`, after every change, and when select-all starts |

A bulk action can listen to it, for instance to show how many people it would delete:

```html
<div x-data="{selected: 0}" @sl-table-selection-change.document="selected = $event.detail.selected">...</div>
```

A checkbox of your own can be indeterminate too: set its `indeterminate` property, or render
[`<sl:checkbox indeterminate>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/checkbox.md#indeterminate).

### Left out

shadcn's data table runs TanStack Table in the browser. These parts of it are not in Shadleaf: sorting and filtering
in the browser, column visibility, resizing, reordering and pinning other than the first column (see above), drag handles, expandable rows, editable cells,
virtual scrolling, selecting a range of rows with Shift and a click, and a selection that lasts across pages (see
above).

### Attributes

#### sl:table

Attributes of `<sl:table>`: A table in shadcn/ui's look. Write the plain table elements inside it (`caption`, `thead`, `tbody`, `tfoot`, `tr`, `th`, `td`), with `th:each` on a row; there is no component per row or cell. Use `sl:table-head` for a header cell that sorts or aligns, and `sl:table-empty` for the row that says nothing was found. The table scrolls sideways inside its own container when it is wider than the page. Attributes go to the `table`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `th:page` | *object* | — | The Spring Data `Page` or `Slice` the table shows. Its `Sort` tells every `sl:table-head` inside how the rows are sorted now. Without it, the headers read the order from the request's sort parameter. |
| `qualifier` | *string* | — | The `@Qualifier` of the controller's `Pageable`, for a second table on the page: its sort links use `members_sort` instead of `sort`. |
| `sticky-first-column` | *boolean* | — | Pin the first cell of every row to the start edge, so it stays in view when a wide table scrolls sideways. It gets the page's `--background` (set `--sl-table-bg` on the table when it sits on something else, such as `var(--card)`), and an edge line appears once the table has scrolled. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:page="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:table-head

Attributes of `<sl:table-head>`: A header cell (`th`) of a `sl:table`. With `sort` its label is a link that sorts the table by that property on the server: ascending, or descending when it is sorted ascending already. The link keeps the request's other parameters (filters, page size) and goes back to the first page. The sorted column gets `aria-sort` and an arrow. A plain `th` works too, for a header that does neither.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `sort` | *string* | — | The property to sort by, as the controller's `Sort` names it (`name`, `address.city`). Only the first key of the current order is shown. |
| `align` | `start`, `center`, `end` | `start` | Where the label sits in the cell. Put the same `data-align` on the column's `td`s: `end` for amounts. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:sort="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:table-empty

Attributes of `<sl:table-empty>`: The row a `sl:table` shows when there are no rows to show: one cell across every column saying "No results." (the message `sl.table.empty`). Content of its own replaces the text. Render it with `th:if`, e.g. `th:if="${people.empty}"`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `colspan` | *number* | **required** | The number of columns the cell spans: all of the table's. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:colspan="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:table-select-all

Attributes of `<sl:table-select-all>`: A checkbox for a header cell of a `sl:table` that selects every row on the page. The rows are the application's own checkboxes (a `sl:checkbox` with `name="ids"` and `th:value="${person.id}"`) in the cells of the table's body; they submit without JavaScript. This one is checked when every row is, indeterminate when some are, and checks or unchecks them all when clicked; it is named "Select all rows on this page" (the message `sl.table.select-all`) unless it gets an `aria-label`. It needs JavaScript, so it stays hidden until Alpine starts. It fires `sl-table-selection-change` on the table (`{selected, total}`), which `sl:table-selection-count` shows. It submits nothing itself (no `name`); other attributes go to its `input`, `class` to its wrapper.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `name` | *string* | — | The `name` of the row checkboxes, when the body has checkboxes of another kind too. Without it, every checkbox in a body cell is a row. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:name="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:table-selection-count

Attributes of `<sl:table-selection-count>`: How many rows of a `sl:table` are selected: "2 of 20 selected" (the message `sl.table.selection.count`), kept up to date in the browser as rows are checked, as rows are replaced (an htmx swap) and after a form reset. Place it anywhere, e.g. at the start of the table's footer as shadcn/ui's data table does. It is not a live region: a click on `sl:table-select-all` announces the count, a row's checkbox announces its own state. Empty and hidden until Alpine starts.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `for` | *string* | **required** | The `id` of the table whose rows it counts (`sl:table` puts its `id` on the `table`). |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:for="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Pagination

> Links to the other pages of a list, built from the Spring Data Page the controller returns.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/pagination/

**Example: From a page.** Page 5 of 27, in a request for /people?sort=name,asc&page=4. Each link keeps the sort.

```html
<sl:pagination th:page="${people}"/>
```

### From a page

Give `<sl:pagination>` the Spring Data `Page` the controller put in the model, and nothing else:

```html
<sl:pagination th:page="${people}"/>
```

It renders Previous, the first and the last page, the current page with one page on each side, an ellipsis for the
pages it leaves out, and Next. A gap of a single page shows that page instead of an ellipsis, which would take the same
room. `siblings` sets how many pages show on each side of the current one:

**Example: More pages around the current one.**

```html
<sl:pagination th:page="${orders}" siblings="2" qualifier="orders" aria-label="Order pages"/>
```

Each link is the request that rendered the page with another page number: every other parameter in the address (the
sort, the page size, a search) is kept as it was. The controller needs nothing new, as the `Pageable` that sorts the
[table](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/table.md) also reads the page:

```java
@GetMapping("/people")
String people(@PageableDefault(size = 20, sort = "name") Pageable pageable, Model model) {
  model.addAttribute("people", personRepository.findAll(pageable));
  return "people";
}
```

Sort the rows by a unique key last, or a row can show up on two pages or on none: see
[a stable order across pages](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/table.md#a-stable-order-across-pages).

The page shown now is an outline button with `aria-current="page"`. On the first page Previous is disabled, and on the
last page Next, the way a disabled link button is: no `href`, `aria-disabled="true"`, out of the tab order. An empty
result still has one page, the one saying so.

**Example: On the first page.** Previous is disabled. A gap of one page shows that page rather than an ellipsis.

```html
<sl:pagination th:page="${team}" qualifier="team" aria-label="Team pages"/>
```

#### A Slice

A `Slice` knows whether there is a next page, not how many pages there are, so it gets Previous and Next only. It
saves the count query on a large table:

**Example: A Slice.** A Slice does not know how many pages there are, so it gets Previous and Next only.

```html
<sl:pagination th:page="${activity}" qualifier="activity" aria-label="Activity pages"/>
```

A list read a `Slice` at a time can also grow instead: [Load more](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/tables-htmx.md#load-more) appends the
next slice's rows with htmx, and is a link to the next page without it.

#### Without Spring Data

Without a `Page`, pass the page shown now (counting from 1) and the number of pages:

**Example: Without Spring Data.**

```html
<sl:pagination th:current="${results.page}" th:total="${results.pages}" qualifier="results"
               aria-label="Result pages"/>
```

The links ask for the page with the same parameter Spring Data would read, zero-based unless
`spring.data.web.pageable.one-indexed-parameters` is set (see [parameter names](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/table.md#parameter-names)); the
controller reads it itself. `sl:pagination-summary` and `sl:pagination-size` need a `Page` or `Slice`: without Spring
Data, write the summary with `#messages` and the sizes as links of your own.

#### Two lists on one page

The `qualifier` of the controller's second `Pageable` (`@Qualifier("members")`) gives a pagination its own parameter
(`members_page`) and its links their own ids (`members-page-2`), as it does for the
[table's headers](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/table.md#two-tables-on-one-page).

### Written by hand

With content of its own, `<sl:pagination>` is the `nav` around a pagination of parts, as in shadcn/ui:

**Example: Written by hand.** The parts, as in shadcn/ui's example.

```html
<sl:pagination aria-label="Invoice pages">
  <sl:pagination-content>
    <sl:pagination-item>
      <sl:pagination-previous href="#"/>
    </sl:pagination-item>
    <sl:pagination-item>
      <sl:pagination-link href="#">1</sl:pagination-link>
    </sl:pagination-item>
    <sl:pagination-item>
      <sl:pagination-link href="#" active>2</sl:pagination-link>
    </sl:pagination-item>
    <sl:pagination-item>
      <sl:pagination-link href="#">3</sl:pagination-link>
    </sl:pagination-item>
    <sl:pagination-item>
      <sl:pagination-ellipsis/>
    </sl:pagination-item>
    <sl:pagination-item>
      <sl:pagination-next href="#"/>
    </sl:pagination-item>
  </sl:pagination-content>
</sl:pagination>
```

`sl:pagination-link` takes `active` for the page shown now and `disabled` for nowhere to go; `sl:pagination-previous`
and `sl:pagination-next` take `disabled`, and content of their own replaces their text.

### Summary

`<sl:pagination-summary>` says which rows the page shows, or with `format="page"` which page it is:

**Example: Summary.**

```html
<sl:pagination-summary th:page="${people}"/>
```

**Example: Summary as the page number.**

```html
<sl:pagination-summary th:page="${people}" format="page"/>
```

A `Slice` has no total, so it reads "11–20" or "Page 2". The words are messages, so your `messages.properties` rewords
or [translates](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md) them; the numbers are formatted for the locale ("1,001–1,010 of 12,345").

### Rows per page

`<sl:pagination-size>` is a label and a [dropdown menu](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dropdown-menu.md) of sizes, as in shadcn/ui's data table:

**Example: Rows per page.** In a request for /people?sort=name,asc&page=3&size=20. Each size keeps the sort and starts on the first page.

```html
<sl:pagination-size th:page="${people}"/>
```

Each size is a link to the same request with that size, every other parameter kept except the page number, so the list
starts on its first page. It works without JavaScript: the menu is a popover. `sizes="10,20,50"` is the default; a size
the page has that is not in the list is added to it, so the menu always shows a checked one. Limit what the controller
accepts with `spring.data.web.pageable.max-page-size`.

### A table footer

Summary, size and pagination are separate, so you lay out the footer. shadcn/ui's data table puts the summary at the
start and the controls at the end:

```html
<div class="table-footer">
  <sl:pagination-summary th:page="${people}"/>
  <div class="table-footer-controls">
    <sl:pagination-size th:page="${people}"/>
    <sl:pagination th:page="${people}"/>
  </div>
</div>
```

```css
.table-footer { display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; align-items: center; justify-content: space-between; gap: 1rem; }
.table-footer-controls { display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; align-items: center; gap: 1.5rem; }
/* The pagination centres itself across the full width by default. */
.table-footer .pagination { inline-size: auto; margin: 0; }
```

Sample-01's `/people` page does this.

### Focus after an htmx swap

Every link has an id: `page-previous`, `page-next` and `page-<number>` (prefixed with the qualifier). When htmx replaces
the list and its pagination, it gives the focus back to the element with the id that had it, so after Next the focus is
on the new Next. The size menu's button is `page-size-trigger`: the menu gives the focus back to it before the chosen
link is followed. [Tables with htmx](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/tables-htmx.md) shows the whole page.

### Accessibility

- The `nav` is named "Pagination" (`sl.pagination.label`); an `aria-label` of your own replaces it, which two
  paginations on one page need.
- Previous and Next are named "Go to previous page" and "Go to next page". On a narrow screen only their chevron shows.
- The ellipsis reads "More pages", where shadcn/ui hides it from screen readers: a list item that says nothing is
  harder to follow.
- The size menu's button reads "Rows per page" followed by the size.

### Messages

[Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md) lists the languages Shadleaf ships them in, and explains how to add one.

| Key | English |
| --- | --- |
| `sl.pagination.label` | Pagination |
| `sl.pagination.previous`, `sl.pagination.previous.label` | Previous, Go to previous page |
| `sl.pagination.next`, `sl.pagination.next.label` | Next, Go to next page |
| `sl.pagination.more` | More pages |
| `sl.pagination.summary` | `{0}–{1} of {2}` |
| `sl.pagination.summary.slice` | `{0}–{1}` |
| `sl.pagination.summary.empty` | 0 of 0 |
| `sl.pagination.summary.page`, `sl.pagination.summary.page.slice` | `Page {0} of {1}`, `Page {0}` |
| `sl.pagination-size.label` | Rows per page |

### Left out

First and last page buttons (shadcn's dashboard has them; the first and the last page are always among the numbers),
a field to type a page number, and paging in the browser.

### Attributes

#### sl:pagination

Attributes of `<sl:pagination>`: Links to the other pages of a list, in a `nav`. Given the Spring Data `Page` it shows (`th:page="${people}"`) and no content of its own, it renders them all: Previous, the first and the last page, the current page with `siblings` on each side, an ellipsis for the pages left out, and Next. Every link keeps the request's other parameters (sort, size, filters) and has a stable id, so htmx can give the focus back after a swap. A `Slice`, which does not know how many pages there are, gets Previous and Next only. Without Spring Data, pass `current` and `total` instead. With content of its own it is the frame for a hand-written pagination of `sl:pagination-content`, `-item`, `-link`, `-previous`, `-next` and `-ellipsis`, as in shadcn/ui.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `th:page` | *object* | — | The Spring Data `Page` or `Slice` shown now. |
| `current` | *number* | — | Without a `page`: the page shown now, counting from 1. |
| `total` | *number* | — | Without a `page`: the number of pages. |
| `siblings` | *number* | `1` | How many pages to show on each side of the current one. |
| `qualifier` | *string* | — | The `@Qualifier` of the controller's `Pageable`, for a second list on the page: its links use `members_page` instead of `page`, and their ids start with `members-`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:page="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:pagination-content

Attributes of `<sl:pagination-content>`: The list (`ul`) of a hand-written `sl:pagination`, holding `sl:pagination-item`s.

`sl:pagination-content` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:pagination-item

Attributes of `<sl:pagination-item>`: One entry (`li`) of a `sl:pagination-content`: holds a link, Previous, Next or an ellipsis.

`sl:pagination-item` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:pagination-link

Attributes of `<sl:pagination-link>`: A link to one page, drawn as a ghost button, or an outline one for the page shown now (`active`). Give it an `href` or `th:href`, and the page number as content.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `active` | *boolean* | — | Whether it is the page shown now: `aria-current="page"` and the outline look. |
| `size` | `xs`, `sm`, `default`, `lg`, `icon`, `icon-xs`, `icon-sm`, `icon-lg` | `icon` | Size of the link, as on `sl:button`: square by default, for a number. |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | There is nowhere to go: the link loses its `href`, gets `aria-disabled` and leaves the tab order, as a disabled `sl:button as="a"`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:active="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:pagination-previous

Attributes of `<sl:pagination-previous>`: The link to the previous page: a `sl:pagination-link` with a chevron and the text "Previous" (the message `sl.pagination.previous`), which content of its own replaces. Its name is "Go to previous page" (`sl.pagination.previous.label`); an `aria-label` replaces it. On a narrow screen only the chevron shows.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | There is no previous page: the link loses its `href`, gets `aria-disabled` and leaves the tab order. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:disabled="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:pagination-next

Attributes of `<sl:pagination-next>`: The link to the next page: a `sl:pagination-link` with a chevron and the text "Next" (the message `sl.pagination.next`), which content of its own replaces. Its name is "Go to next page" (`sl.pagination.next.label`); an `aria-label` replaces it. On a narrow screen only the chevron shows.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | There is no next page: the link loses its `href`, gets `aria-disabled` and leaves the tab order. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:disabled="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:pagination-ellipsis

Attributes of `<sl:pagination-ellipsis>`: Stands for the pages a `sl:pagination` leaves out: an ellipsis, read as "More pages" (the message `sl.pagination.more`).

`sl:pagination-ellipsis` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:pagination-summary

Attributes of `<sl:pagination-summary>`: Where the page is in the results, as text: "11–20 of 270", or with `format="page"` "Page 2 of 27". Takes the Spring Data `Page` shown now (`th:page`); for a `Slice`, which does not know the total, "11–20" or "Page 2". The words are messages (`sl.pagination.summary*`), so an application rewords or translates them; numbers are formatted for the locale.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `th:page` | *object* | — | The Spring Data `Page` or `Slice` shown now. |
| `format` | `range`, `page` | `range` | `range`: the rows shown and the total. `page`: the page number and the number of pages. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:page="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:pagination-size

Attributes of `<sl:pagination-size>`: "Rows per page" and a menu of page sizes, as in shadcn/ui's data table. The sizes are `sl:dropdown-menu-radio-item` links: each asks for the same request with that size, every other parameter kept except the page number, so the list starts on the first page. It works without JavaScript (the menu is a popover). Takes the Spring Data `Page` shown now, whose size is the checked one; a size the page has but `sizes` does not list is added. The label is the message `sl.pagination-size.label`. Attributes go to the wrapper.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `th:page` | *object* | — | The Spring Data `Page` or `Slice` shown now. |
| `sizes` | *string* | `10,20,50` | The sizes to choose from, separated by commas. |
| `qualifier` | *string* | — | The `@Qualifier` of the controller's `Pageable`, for a second list on the page: its links use `members_size` instead of `size`. |
| `id` | *string* | — | The menu's id; its button gets `-trigger` added, its label `-label`. `page-size` when not set, prefixed with the qualifier (`members-page-size`). |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:page="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Query params

> Hidden inputs that keep a table's order, page size and filters when a search form is sent.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/query-params/

**Example: Query params.** In a request for /people?q=ada&sort=email,desc&size=50&page=2. Sending the form asks for /people?q=…&sort=email,desc&size=50: the order and the size stay, the page starts again at the first.

```html
<form action="/people" method="get">
  <sl:label for="query-params-q">Search people</sl:label>
  <sl:input type="search" id="query-params-q" name="q" th:value="${param.q}" placeholder="Name or email"/>
  <sl:query-params except="q"/>
</form>
```

### Searching a sorted table

A search above a [table](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/table.md) is a `GET` form: sending it asks for the same page with `?q=...`, which the
controller filters on. A browser sends only the form's own fields, so on its own the form would forget the table's
order and page size. `<sl:query-params>` copies the current request's other parameters into the form as hidden inputs:

```html
<form th:action="@{/people}" method="get" role="search">
  <sl:input type="search" name="q" th:value="${param.q}" aria-label="Search people"/>
  <sl:query-params except="q"/>
</form>
```

In a request for `/people?q=ada&sort=email,desc&size=50&page=2`, that renders:

```html
<form action="/people" method="get" role="search">
  <input class="input" type="search" name="q" value="ada" aria-label="Search people">
  <input type="hidden" name="sort" value="email,desc">
  <input type="hidden" name="size" value="50">
</form>
```

and searching for "grace" asks for `/people?q=grace&sort=email,desc&size=50`.

- `except` names the form's own fields, separated by commas (`except="q,status"`). Without it, `q` would be sent twice:
  the field's new value and the hidden copy of the old one.
- The page number is always left out, so a new search starts on the first page. It is the page parameter your
  `Pageable` reads (`spring.data.web.pageable.*`, see [parameter names](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/table.md#parameter-names)); with
  `qualifier="members"` it is `members_page`, for the [second table](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/table.md#two-tables-on-one-page) on a page.
- A repeated parameter stays repeated, in its order: `?sort=name,desc&sort=email` gives two `sort` inputs, as Spring
  Data's `Sort` reads them.
- Values are decoded once (`?q=ada%20l%C3%A9` gives `value="ada lé"`) and escaped like any other attribute value, so a
  parameter cannot add markup.

The controller needs nothing for it: the `Pageable` reads `sort` and `size` as it does for the table's links.

```java
@GetMapping("/people")
String people(@RequestParam(required = false) String q, @PageableDefault(sort = "name") Pageable pageable,
    Model model) {
  Page<Person> people = q == null || q.isBlank()
      ? personRepository.findAll(pageable)
      : personRepository.findByNameContainingIgnoreCaseOrEmailContainingIgnoreCase(q, q, pageable);
  model.addAttribute("people", people);
  return "people";
}
```

The links of the table's headers, its [pagination](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/pagination.md) and the rows-per-page menu keep `q` in the same
way, as they are built from the request too.

### Outside the form

Other attributes go to every hidden input. With `form`, the inputs belong to a form elsewhere on the page. The
[live search with htmx](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/tables-htmx.md) uses this to put them next to the table, so they are swapped with it
and follow the order of the table they came with:

```html
<form id="people-search" ...>...</form>
<div id="people-results">
  <sl:query-params except="q" form="people-search"/>
  <sl:table th:page="${people}">...</sl:table>
</div>
```

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:query-params>`: Hidden inputs that copy the current request's query parameters into a `GET` form, so sending a search keeps the table's order, page size and other filters. Name the form's own fields in `except`, or they are sent twice. The page number is always left out, so a new search starts on the first page. Values are decoded once and escaped like any other attribute; a repeated parameter (several `sort` values) stays repeated. Renders no element of its own. Other attributes go to every input: `form="search"` ties them to a form elsewhere on the page.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `except` | *string* | — | The parameters not to copy, separated by commas: the fields the form has itself (`q`, `q,status`). |
| `qualifier` | *string* | — | The `@Qualifier` of the controller's `Pageable`, for a second table on the page: its page parameter (`members_page`) is the one left out. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:except="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Month view

> A month rendered on the server, with content of your own per day and a link per day; no picking.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/month-view/

**Example: Month view.** Each day shows its events from the model and links to the day's agenda.

```html
<sl:month-view day-href="/agenda/{date}">
  <sl:slot name="day">
    <sl:badge th:each="event : ${events[slDay.date]}" variant="secondary">[[${event}]]</sl:badge>
  </sl:slot>
</sl:month-view>
```

### Content per day

`<sl:month-view>` shows a month to look at, not to pick from: an agenda, a booking overview, orders per day. The server
renders it whole, so it needs no JavaScript. For a date in a form, use the [calendar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/calendar.md) or the
[date picker](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/date-picker.md).

The `day` slot is rendered once for every day, and sees that day as `${slDay}`:

| Property | |
| --- | --- |
| `date` | The day as a `LocalDate`, to look it up in your model: `${events[slDay.date]}` |
| `iso` | The same as an ISO string: `2026-10-23` |
| `number` | The day of the month, which the cell shows |
| `label` | The whole date in the page's locale (`Friday, October 23, 2026`), which screen readers get |
| `outside` | Whether the day belongs to the previous or next month |
| `today` | Whether it is today |

Put the data in the model as a map from `LocalDate` to what a day shows, and look the day up in the slot:

```java
@GetMapping("/agenda")
String agenda(Model model) {
  model.addAttribute("events", agenda.eventsByDay()); // Map<LocalDate, List<String>>
  return "agenda";
}
```

```html
<sl:month-view day-href="/agenda/{date}">
  <sl:slot name="day">
    <sl:badge th:each="event : ${events[slDay.date]}" variant="secondary">[[${event}]]</sl:badge>
  </sl:slot>
</sl:month-view>
```

The seven days share the width. Content wider than its day is cut off, so keep it short: a badge, a count, a word.

### Links to a day

`day-href` makes every day a link. `{date}` in it is replaced by the day's ISO date: `/agenda/{date}` links to
`/agenda/2026-10-23`, `?date={date}` to the same page with a parameter. The result is a Thymeleaf link, so a path that
starts with `/` gets the context path.

The link holds the day's number, its whole date for screen readers, and the slot's content. So a screen reader reads
"Friday, October 23, 2026, Demo". Put nothing interactive in the slot then: a link cannot hold a button or another
link. Today's day is marked `aria-current="date"`.

Without `day-href`, the days are no links.

### The month shown

The previous and next buttons are links to the same page with `?month=2026-09`; every other parameter of the request
is kept, so a filter (`?team=ops`) stays. Without a `month` attribute, `<sl:month-view>` reads that parameter itself,
so the controller needs nothing for it. A value that is no month falls back to the current month.

To read it in the controller, take a `YearMonth` (Spring converts `2026-10`) and pass it on:

```java
@GetMapping("/agenda")
String agenda(@RequestParam(required = false) YearMonth month, Model model) {
  YearMonth shown = month != null ? month : YearMonth.now();
  model.addAttribute("month", shown);
  model.addAttribute("events", agenda.eventsByDay(shown));
  return "agenda";
}
```

```html
<sl:month-view th:month="${month}">...</sl:month-view>
```

`month` takes a `YearMonth`, a `LocalDate` in the month, or the text `2026-10`. `month-param` renames the parameter,
for a second month view on the page.

Today is marked; by default that is the server's date. If your users are in another time zone, put their date in the
model (`LocalDate.now(zone)`) and pass it: `th:today="${today}"`.

### Size of the navigation

`nav-size` sets the size of the previous and next links, with the sizes of an icon [`<sl:button>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/button.md#sizes)
(`icon-xs`, `icon-sm`, `icon`, `icon-lg`), so they can match the buttons elsewhere on the page. Without it they have the
size of the skin's calendar. On a touch screen the links have a hit area of at least 44 by 44 pixels whatever their
size, as the icon buttons.

**Example: Size of the navigation.** The previous and next links take the size of an icon button, here icon-lg, to match the buttons around the month. On a touch screen their hit area is at least 44 by 44 pixels whatever they look like.

```html
<sl:month-view nav-size="icon-lg"/>
```

### Week numbers and the first day of the week

As for the [calendar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/calendar.md#week-numbers-and-the-first-day-of-the-week), the page's locale decides the first day
of the week and how weeks are numbered, and the names of the month and the days. `week-starts-on` overrides the first
day: 0 is Sunday, 1 Monday, up to 6. `show-week-number` adds a column with the week numbers.
`show-outside-days="false"` leaves the days of the previous and next month empty.

**Example: Counts and week numbers.** A count per day, no links. Monday first, with the week numbers; the days of the other months left out.

```html
<sl:month-view show-outside-days="false" show-week-number week-starts-on="1">
  <sl:slot name="day">
    <span th:if="${orders[slDay.date]}">[[${orders[slDay.date]}]] orders</span>
  </sl:slot>
</sl:month-view>
```

### Without content

**Example: Without content.** Without a day slot or day-href, the month only shows its dates.

```html
<sl:month-view/>
```

### With htmx

The month links are plain links, so `hx-boost` on the page, or `hx-get` with `hx-target` and `hx-select` on a
container around the month view, swaps the next month in. The controller renders the same page; nothing in the month
view needs JavaScript.

### Accessibility

- The month is a `<table>`, named by its caption (`October 2026`). The column headers carry the whole name of the day
  (`Monday`), the week numbers are row headers.
- Each day says its whole date, then its content.
- It is no ARIA grid: the arrow keys do not move between days. Tab goes through the previous and next links, then
  through the days that are links.

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:month-view>`: A month to look at, rendered on the server: each day shows content of its own (a badge, a short text, a count) from the `day` slot, which is rendered once per day and sees the day as `${slDay}` (`date`, a `LocalDate`, `iso`, `number`, `label`, `outside`, `today`), and can link to a page for that day. Nothing is picked: for a date in a form, use `sl:calendar`. The previous and next month are links to the same page with `?month=2026-09`, every other parameter kept, so it needs no JavaScript and works with htmx.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `month` | *string* | — | The month shown, as `2026-10` (a `YearMonth`) or a date in it. By default the month the request asks for in `month-param`, or else the current month. |
| `month-param` | *string* | `month` | The request parameter the links to the previous and next month set, and that gives the month when `month` is not set. |
| `day-href` | *string* | — | Makes every day a link: `{date}` is replaced by the day's ISO date (`/agenda/{date}`, `?date={date}`), and the result is a Thymeleaf link, so a path starting with `/` gets the context path. The `day` slot is then inside the link: put nothing interactive in it. |
| `today` | *string* | — | Today, as `2026-10-23` (a `LocalDate`), marked as the current date. By default the server's date; pass it in the user's time zone when that differs. |
| `show-outside-days` | *boolean* | `true` | Show the days of the previous and next month that fill the first and last week. |
| `show-week-number` | *boolean* | — | Show the week numbers, counted as the page's locale counts them (ISO weeks in most of Europe). |
| `nav-size` | `default`, `icon-xs`, `icon-sm`, `icon`, `icon-lg` | `default` | Size of the previous and next month links, the sizes of an icon `sl:button`, to match the buttons elsewhere on the page. By default the skin's calendar size. On a touch screen the link is always at least 44 by 44 pixels to hit, whatever it looks like. |
| `week-starts-on` | *number* | — | The first day of the week: 0 is Sunday, 1 Monday, up to 6. By default the page's locale decides. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:month="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Chart

> shadcn/ui's Chart, Recharts drawing bars, lines and areas from rows the controller puts in the model; labels and colours on the series, named Intl formats.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/chart/

**Example: Bar chart.** Two series side by side, in a card as shadcn shows it. The months are YearMonths: month-short writes the ticks (Jan), month the tooltip's label (January).

```html
<sl:card>
  <sl:card-header>
    <sl:card-title level="3">Bar Chart - Multiple</sl:card-title>
    <sl:card-description>January - June 2024</sl:card-description>
  </sl:card-header>
  <sl:card-content>
    <sl:chart th:data="${visitors}" label="Visitors, January to June 2024">
      <sl:chart-grid/>
      <sl:chart-x-axis key="month" format="month-short" tick-margin="10"/>
      <sl:chart-tooltip indicator="dashed" cursor="false" label-format="month"/>
      <sl:chart-bar key="desktop" label="Desktop" radius="4"/>
      <sl:chart-bar key="mobile" label="Mobile" radius="4"/>
    </sl:chart>
  </sl:card-content>
  <sl:card-footer>
    <p>Showing total visitors for the last 6 months</p>
  </sl:card-footer>
</sl:card>
```

### Built from parts

A chart is shadcn's: [Recharts](https://recharts.github.io/) draws it in the browser, with shadcn's look. The template
says what to draw, the controller gives the rows:

```html
<sl:chart th:data="${visitors}" th:label="#{visitors.title}">
  <sl:chart-grid/>
  <sl:chart-x-axis key="month" format="month-short"/>
  <sl:chart-tooltip/>
  <sl:chart-bar key="desktop" th:label="#{visitors.desktop}"/>
  <sl:chart-bar key="mobile" th:label="#{visitors.mobile}"/>
</sl:chart>
```

```java
record MonthlyVisitors(YearMonth month, long desktop, long mobile) {}

@GetMapping("/visitors")
String visitors(Model model) {
  model.addAttribute("visitors", repository.visitorsPerMonth()); // List<MonthlyVisitors>
  return "visitors";
}
```

- **`th:data`** takes a list of rows: records, objects or maps, or a Spring Data `Page`. They go into the page as
  JSON, written by Jackson.
- **The series parts** (`sl:chart-bar`, `sl:chart-line`, `sl:chart-area`) name the property they draw in `key`, and
  carry the series' `label` and `color`: what shadcn's `ChartConfig` holds. The chart draws bars for bars only, lines
  for lines only, areas for areas only, and a composed chart for a mix.
- **The other parts** add a grid, the axes, a tooltip and a legend. Every part is optional; their order is the order
  Recharts draws them in.
- **`label`** names the chart for a screen reader. The chart takes the focus, and the arrow keys move the tooltip from
  one category to the next.

### Colours

A series without a `color` takes the chart tokens in turn: the first `var(--chart-1)`, the second `var(--chart-2)`,
up to `--chart-5`, then again from the first. A theme that redefines them recolours every chart. `color` takes any
CSS colour, and `color-dark` a different one for the dark theme:

```html
<sl:chart-line key="revenue" color="oklch(0.55 0.2 260)" color-dark="oklch(0.75 0.15 260)"/>
```

The colour becomes the CSS property `--color-<key>` on the chart, which is why a key is checked to be a CSS
identifier: letters, digits, `_` and `-`, not starting with a digit or `-`. A key that is not fails the render.

### Stacked bars and a legend

**Example: Stacked bars and a legend.** The same stack id stacks the series; the radius rounds the bottom of the first and the top of the last.

```html
<sl:chart th:data="${visitors}" label="Visitors, January to June 2024">
  <sl:chart-grid/>
  <sl:chart-x-axis key="month" format="month-short" tick-margin="10"/>
  <sl:chart-tooltip hide-label/>
  <sl:chart-legend/>
  <sl:chart-bar key="desktop" label="Desktop" stack="a" radius="0,0,4,4"/>
  <sl:chart-bar key="mobile" label="Mobile" stack="a" radius="4,4,0,0"/>
</sl:chart>
```

The legend lists the series by label, in the order of the series parts, like the tooltip.

### Line chart

**Example: Line chart.**

```html
<sl:chart th:data="${visitors}" label="Visitors, January to June 2024" margin="12">
  <sl:chart-grid/>
  <sl:chart-x-axis key="month" format="month-short"/>
  <sl:chart-tooltip cursor="false" label-format="month"/>
  <sl:chart-line key="desktop" label="Desktop"/>
  <sl:chart-line key="mobile" label="Mobile"/>
</sl:chart>
```

`margin` leaves room left and right of the plot, so the first and last tick labels fit. `curve` sets how a line runs
between the points (`monotone`, `natural`, `linear`, `step`), `dots` puts a dot on each.

**Example: Actual and forecast.** A dashed series for a forecast, target or budget. The rows without a number (null) leave a gap, so the actual line stops at the last month with figures; join names the series the forecast continues, so it starts there without a number of its own, and the tooltip lists only the actual value for that month. The legend and the tooltip show the forecast dashed too.

```html
<sl:chart th:data="${sales}" label="Cumulative sales, January to December 2024" margin="12">
  <sl:chart-grid/>
  <sl:chart-x-axis key="month" format="month-short"/>
  <sl:chart-tooltip cursor="false" label-format="month"/>
  <sl:chart-legend/>
  <sl:chart-line key="actual" label="Actual" color="var(--chart-1)"/>
  <sl:chart-line key="forecast" label="Forecast" color="var(--chart-1)" dashed join="actual"/>
</sl:chart>
```

`dashed` draws a series dashed (on `sl:chart-area` only its line, the fill stays), for a forecast or a target next to actual figures. The
legend and the tooltip show it dashed as well. A row without a number (`null`) leaves a gap, so an actual line stops
at the last month with figures while the dashed one carries on. `join` (on `sl:chart-line` and `sl:chart-area`) names the series a dashed line continues
(`join="actual"`): the line starts at that series' last number, without a duplicate in your rows, and the tooltip does
not list the forecast for that month.

### Area chart

**Example: Area chart.** The tooltip's line indicator, nested beside the label when there is one series.

```html
<sl:chart th:data="${visitors}" label="Desktop visitors, January to June 2024" margin="12">
  <sl:chart-grid/>
  <sl:chart-x-axis key="month" format="month-short"/>
  <sl:chart-tooltip indicator="line" cursor="false" label-format="month"/>
  <sl:chart-area key="desktop" label="Desktop"/>
</sl:chart>
```

**Example: Stacked areas.**

```html
<sl:chart th:data="${visitors}" label="Visitors, January to June 2024" margin="12">
  <sl:chart-grid/>
  <sl:chart-x-axis key="month" format="month-short"/>
  <sl:chart-tooltip cursor="false" label-format="month"/>
  <sl:chart-area key="mobile" label="Mobile" stack="a" color="var(--chart-2)"/>
  <sl:chart-area key="desktop" label="Desktop" stack="a" color="var(--chart-1)"/>
</sl:chart>
```

**Example: Area with a forecast.** The same on areas: the forecast's line is dashed, its fill stays, and join starts it at the last actual number.

```html
<sl:chart th:data="${sales}" label="Cumulative sales, January to December 2024" margin="12">
  <sl:chart-grid/>
  <sl:chart-x-axis key="month" format="month-short"/>
  <sl:chart-tooltip indicator="line" cursor="false" label-format="month"/>
  <sl:chart-legend/>
  <sl:chart-area key="actual" label="Actual" color="var(--chart-1)"/>
  <sl:chart-area key="forecast" label="Forecast" color="var(--chart-1)" fill-opacity="0.15" dashed join="actual"/>
</sl:chart>
```

On an area, `dashed` dashes the line and the fill stays; a lower `fill-opacity` on the forecast sets it apart.

### Tooltip

The tooltip shows the hovered category and every series' label and value. `indicator` is the mark before each label:
`dot` (the default), `line` or `dashed`. `hide-label` leaves out the category, `hide-indicator` the marks, and
`cursor="false"` the highlight of the hovered category.

### Formats

Instead of shadcn's `tickFormatter`, `formatter` and `labelFormatter` functions, the parts take a named format,
written with `Intl` in the page's language (`<html lang>`, from Spring's locale):

| Format | Writes | For |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `number` | 1,234.5 | numbers; the tooltip's values by default |
| `integer` | 1,235 | |
| `compact` | 1.2K | a value axis |
| `percent` | 25% for 0.25 | |
| `currency` | €1,234.50, with `currency="EUR"` | |
| `day` | Apr 1 | dates |
| `month-short` | Apr | |
| `month` | April | |
| `month-year` | Apr 2024 | |
| `date` | Apr 1, 2024 | |
| `time` | 10:00 AM | |

`format` on an axis writes its ticks, `label-format` and `value-format` on the tooltip its label and values. A format
is an enum: a typo fails the render. A value of another kind (a date format on a name) is shown as it is.

**Example: Formats.** Days as LocalDates (day on the axis, date in the tooltip), a value axis in compact numbers and the values in euros, all in the page's language.

```html
<sl:chart th:data="${revenue}" label="Revenue per day, 1 to 10 April 2024">
  <sl:chart-grid/>
  <sl:chart-x-axis key="day" format="day"/>
  <sl:chart-y-axis format="compact"/>
  <sl:chart-tooltip label-format="date" value-format="currency" currency="EUR"/>
  <sl:chart-bar key="revenue" label="Revenue" radius="4"/>
</sl:chart>
```

For a label no format writes, format it in Java and give the tooltip that property in `label-key`: it then shows that
property of the hovered row as its label.

#### Dates and time zones

The dates are read by their Java type, as Jackson writes them, never by the browser's date parser (which reads
`2024-04-01` as midnight UTC, the evening before in America):

- **`LocalDate`** (`2024-04-01`) and **`YearMonth`** (`2024-04`) are calendar dates: shown as they are, in every time
  zone.
- **`LocalDateTime`** (`2024-04-01T10:00:00`) is a time on the clock: shown as written.
- **`Instant`**, **`OffsetDateTime`** and **`ZonedDateTime`** (`2024-04-01T08:00:00Z`) are moments: shown in the
  chart's time zone. That is the request's (Spring's `LocaleContextHolder`, which a `LocaleResolver` that knows the
  user's zone sets; the server's otherwise), or the one `time-zone` names.

**Example: Instants in a time zone.** Instants (written with Z) are shown in the chart's time zone: the request's, or time-zone. The first point is 06:00 UTC, so 08:00 in Brussels.

```html
<sl:chart th:data="${requests}" label="Requests per hour, 1 April 2024" time-zone="Europe/Brussels" margin="12">
  <sl:chart-grid/>
  <sl:chart-x-axis key="hour" format="time"/>
  <sl:chart-tooltip label-format="time" value-format="integer"/>
  <sl:chart-line key="requests" label="Requests" dots/>
</sl:chart>
```

### Series from the model

For series known only at run time, put `th:each` on a series part:

**Example: Series from the model.** th:each on a series part, for series known at run time. Each takes the next chart colour.

```html
<sl:chart th:data="${browsers}" label="Visitors per browser, January to June 2024">
  <sl:chart-grid/>
  <sl:chart-x-axis key="month" format="month-short"/>
  <sl:chart-tooltip label-format="month"/>
  <sl:chart-legend/>
  <sl:chart-line th:each="browser : ${series}" th:key="${browser.key}" th:label="${browser.label}"/>
</sl:chart>
```

### Size

A chart is as wide as its container and 16:9, as shadcn's. For a fixed height, give it one in your CSS, with
`aspect-ratio: auto` (shadcn's `aspect-auto h-[250px]`):

```css
.visitors-chart { aspect-ratio: auto; height: 250px; }
```

### Right to left

On a `dir="rtl"` page the category axis runs from right to left and the value axis stands on the right; the arrow
keys follow what you see.

### With htmx

- A chart that htmx swaps in draws itself; one it swaps out is cleaned up.
- Before htmx saves a page for Back, the charts are put back to their skeletons, so the saved page holds none of the
  inline styles Recharts sets (a strict Content-Security-Policy would report them when the page comes back). Back
  draws them again.

### Left out

These shadcn and Recharts features have no counterpart yet: pie, radar and radial charts (pie charts are planned),
horizontal bars, gradients, custom tooltip and legend content, icons in the config, reference lines and brushes.
For anything else, `sl-chart` is a custom element: an application can write its own.

### Attributes

#### sl:chart

Attributes of `<sl:chart>`: A chart drawn by Recharts in the browser, as shadcn's: bars, lines and areas over a category or time axis, with a grid, axes, a tooltip and a legend. Pass the rows in `th:data` and put the parts inside, in the order Recharts draws them: `sl:chart-grid`, `sl:chart-x-axis`, `sl:chart-y-axis`, `sl:chart-tooltip`, `sl:chart-legend`, then one `sl:chart-bar`, `sl:chart-line` or `sl:chart-area` per series (only bars give a bar chart, a mix a composed chart). Needs JavaScript: without it the chart's skeleton stays. The chart is 16:9; for a fixed height, give it one in your CSS with `aspect-ratio: auto`. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through to the element.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `th:data` | *object* | **required** | The rows: a list (or a Spring Data `Page`) of objects, records or maps, one per category or point in time. Each row holds the category (`month`) and a number per series (`desktop`, `mobile`). Serialised with Jackson: dates as ISO strings, which the named date formats read by their Java type. |
| `label` | *string* | **required** | The chart's accessible name, such as its title: what a screen reader announces when the chart takes the focus. |
| `margin` | *number* | — | Space in pixels left and right of the plot, so the first and last tick labels fit (shadcn's line and area charts use 12). |
| `time-zone` | *string* | — | The time zone (an IANA id such as `Europe/Brussels`) instants in the data are shown in: `Instant`, `OffsetDateTime` and `ZonedDateTime`. Defaults to the request's (Spring's `LocaleContextHolder`). A `LocalDate` or `LocalDateTime` is shown as written, in any zone. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:data="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:chart-grid

Attributes of `<sl:chart-grid>`: The grid lines behind a `sl:chart`'s plot. Draws no element of its own on the page: the chart reads it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `vertical` | *boolean* | — | Vertical lines too, one per category. |
| `horizontal` | *boolean* | `true` | Horizontal lines, one per value tick. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:vertical="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:chart-x-axis

Attributes of `<sl:chart-x-axis>`: The category or time axis of a `sl:chart`, along the bottom: its ticks are the values of `key` in the rows, without tick or axis lines (as shadcn's). Under `dir="rtl"` it runs from right to left. Draws no element of its own on the page: the chart reads it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `key` | *string* | **required** | The property of the rows that holds the category (`month`) or the date. |
| `format` | `number`, `integer`, `compact`, `percent`, `currency`, `day`, `month-short`, `month`, `month-year`, `date`, `time` | — | How the ticks are written, with `Intl` in the page's language. Numbers: `number`, `integer`, `compact` (1.2K), `percent` (0.25 is 25%), `currency` (with `currency`). Dates: `day` (Apr 1), `month-short` (Apr), `month` (April), `month-year` (Apr 2024), `date`, `time`. Without one, the values as they are. |
| `currency` | *string* | — | The ISO 4217 code (`EUR`) for `format="currency"`. |
| `tick-margin` | *number* | `8` | Space in pixels between the ticks and the plot. |
| `min-tick-gap` | *number* | `5` | The least space in pixels between two ticks; Recharts leaves out ticks that would come closer, as on a long time axis. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:key="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:chart-y-axis

Attributes of `<sl:chart-y-axis>`: The value axis of a `sl:chart`, at the start side (the left, or the right under `dir="rtl"`), without tick or axis lines. shadcn's charts often leave it out and show the values in the tooltip. Draws no element of its own on the page: the chart reads it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `format` | `number`, `integer`, `compact`, `percent`, `currency`, `day`, `month-short`, `month`, `month-year`, `date`, `time` | — | How the ticks are written, as on `sl:chart-x-axis`: `compact` (1.2K) and `currency` suit a value axis. |
| `currency` | *string* | — | The ISO 4217 code (`EUR`) for `format="currency"`. |
| `tick-margin` | *number* | `8` | Space in pixels between the ticks and the plot. |
| `tick-count` | *number* | `5` | How many ticks Recharts aims for. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:format="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:chart-tooltip

Attributes of `<sl:chart-tooltip>`: The tooltip of a `sl:chart`: on hover, or with the arrow keys once the chart has the focus, it shows the category and every series' label and value. Draws no element of its own on the page: the chart reads it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `indicator` | `dot`, `line`, `dashed` | `dot` | The mark before each series' label, in its colour. |
| `cursor` | *boolean* | `true` | Highlight the hovered category (a band behind bars, a line over lines and areas). |
| `hide-label` | *boolean* | — | Leave out the label at the top (the category). |
| `hide-indicator` | *boolean* | — | Leave out the marks before the series' labels. |
| `label-key` | *string* | — | The property of the rows to take the label from instead of the axis category: a series key gives that series' label, any other property its value in the hovered row, such as a date the server formatted. |
| `label-format` | `number`, `integer`, `compact`, `percent`, `currency`, `day`, `month-short`, `month`, `month-year`, `date`, `time` | — | How the label is written, as the ticks of `sl:chart-x-axis` (`month`, `date`, ...). Without one, as it is. |
| `value-format` | `number`, `integer`, `compact`, `percent`, `currency`, `day`, `month-short`, `month`, `month-year`, `date`, `time` | `number` | How the values are written: `number` in the page's language, or `currency`, `percent`, ... |
| `currency` | *string* | — | The ISO 4217 code (`EUR`) for a `currency` format. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:indicator="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:chart-legend

Attributes of `<sl:chart-legend>`: The legend of a `sl:chart`: every series' label with its colour. Draws no element of its own on the page: the chart reads it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `vertical-align` | `bottom`, `top` | `bottom` | Below the plot, or above it. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:vertical-align="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:chart-bar

Attributes of `<sl:chart-bar>`: One series of a `sl:chart`, drawn as bars: one per row. Draws no element of its own on the page: the chart reads it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `key` | *string* | **required** | The property of the rows that holds this series' numbers (`desktop`): letters, digits, `_` and `-`, as it also names the colour property `--color-&lt;key&gt;`. |
| `label` | *string* | — | The series' name in the tooltip and the legend, typically a message (`th:label="#{visitors.desktop}"`). Defaults to the key. |
| `color` | *string* | — | Its colour, any CSS colour (`var(--chart-3)`, `#2563eb`). Defaults to the chart tokens in turn: the first series `var(--chart-1)`, the second `var(--chart-2)`, and so on (after the fifth, again from the first). |
| `color-dark` | *string* | — | A different colour in the dark theme; `color` is then the light one (the two go into `light-dark()`). |
| `radius` | *string* | — | The bars' corner radius in pixels: `4` for every corner, or four numbers (top-left, top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left), as `4,4,0,0` for the top bar of a stack. |
| `stack` | *string* | — | Stack this series on the others with the same `stack` id (any name, such as `a`) instead of drawing them side by side. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:key="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:chart-line

Attributes of `<sl:chart-line>`: One series of a `sl:chart`, drawn as a line through the rows. Draws no element of its own on the page: the chart reads it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `key` | *string* | **required** | The property of the rows that holds this series' numbers (`desktop`): letters, digits, `_` and `-`, as it also names the colour property `--color-&lt;key&gt;`. |
| `label` | *string* | — | The series' name in the tooltip and the legend, typically a message (`th:label="#{visitors.desktop}"`). Defaults to the key. |
| `color` | *string* | — | Its colour, any CSS colour (`var(--chart-3)`, `#2563eb`). Defaults to the chart tokens in turn: the first series `var(--chart-1)`, the second `var(--chart-2)`, and so on (after the fifth, again from the first). |
| `color-dark` | *string* | — | A different colour in the dark theme; `color` is then the light one (the two go into `light-dark()`). |
| `curve` | `monotone`, `natural`, `linear`, `step`, `step-before`, `step-after` | `monotone` | How the line runs between the points: smooth (`monotone`, `natural`), straight (`linear`) or in steps. |
| `dots` | *boolean* | — | A dot on every point. |
| `dashed` | *boolean* | — | Draw the line dashed, as a forecast or target next to actual figures. The legend and the tooltip show the series dashed as well. A row without a number (`null`) leaves a gap, so an actual line can stop where a dashed one takes over (see `join`). |
| `join` | *string* | — | The `key` of the series this dashed line continues (`actual`). The chart draws the dashed line from that series' last number to its own first one, so the rows need no duplicated value, and the tooltip does not list this series at that last row. Needs `dashed`. |
| `stroke-width` | *number* | `2` | The line's width in pixels. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:key="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:chart-area

Attributes of `<sl:chart-area>`: One series of a `sl:chart`, drawn as a filled area under a line through the rows. Draws no element of its own on the page: the chart reads it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
