| `default-state` | `expanded`, `collapsed` | `expanded` | The state before the user chose one (no cookie yet). |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:state="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sidebar

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar>`: The application's navigation, inside `sl:sidebar-provider`. One element serves both widths: from 768&nbsp;px up it is a column beside the page, which the trigger collapses and expands; below that it is a panel over the page, which the trigger opens. It is a `nav` named "Main" (`sl.sidebar.label`; an `aria-label` replaces it) and a popover: the browser opens the panel in the top layer and closes it on Escape or a click outside. On a phone, Alpine moves the focus into the open panel and makes the rest of the page inert, and closes it when a link in it is followed. It holds `sl:sidebar-header`, `sl:sidebar-content` (groups of menus) and `sl:sidebar-footer`. With `placement="header"` it goes inside `sl:site-header` instead, where from 768&nbsp;px it is a row of links; on a phone it is the same panel.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | `sidebar` | The id the trigger points at (`sl:sidebar-trigger`'s `for`). |
| `side` | `left`, `right` | `left` | The edge it sits on, as on `sl:sheet`: `left` is the start of a line and `right` the end, so they swap on a right-to-left page. |
| `placement` | `sidebar`, `header` | `sidebar` | Where it sits on a desktop: `sidebar` is a column beside the page; `header` a row of links inside `sl:site-header` (the header layout), which never collapses: `collapsible`, the collapsed state and the trigger do not apply there (the trigger is hidden), and `side` only picks the phone panel's edge. Collapsible items and the parts marked `panel-only` are left out of the row; the phone panel is the same for both. |
| `variant` | `sidebar`, `floating`, `inset` | `sidebar` | How it looks on a desktop: `sidebar` is a column along the edge with a border; `floating` a panel with a margin all round; `inset` lays the sidebar's ground around the page, which (`sl:sidebar-inset`) becomes a card on it. The phone panel looks the same for all three. In the header placement only `inset` counts: the page becomes a card below the header. |
| `collapsible` | `offcanvas`, `icon`, `none` | `offcanvas` | How it collapses on a desktop: `offcanvas` moves it out of view entirely; `icon` narrows it to a strip of its menu buttons' icons (labels, badges, actions and sub-menus hidden; a menu button's `tooltip` shows its label); `none` never collapses it, and the trigger is hidden on a desktop. The phone panel is the same for all three. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sidebar-trigger

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-trigger>`: The icon button that collapses and expands the `sl:sidebar` on a desktop and opens its panel on a phone. Named "Toggle sidebar" (`sl.sidebar.trigger`; an `aria-label` replaces it); content of its own replaces the icon. Without JavaScript it is hidden on a desktop, where it could not do anything, and still opens the panel on a phone. Any other attribute passes through to the `sl:button`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `for` | *string* | `sidebar` | The `id` of the sidebar it toggles. |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline`, `ghost`, `destructive`, `secondary`, `link` | `ghost` | Visual style of the button, as on `sl:button`. |
| `size` | `icon`, `icon-xs`, `icon-sm`, `icon-lg` | `icon-sm` | Size of the button: one of `sl:button`'s icon sizes. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:for="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sidebar-inset

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-inset>`: The page's main content beside the `sl:sidebar`: the `main` element, which takes the rest of the width. Put the page header with the `sl:sidebar-trigger` at its top. It is the target of a `sl:skip-link`: `tabindex="-1"` lets the link move the focus to it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | `main` | The element's id, which a `sl:skip-link` points at (`main` is its default too). |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sidebar-header

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-header>`: The top of the `sl:sidebar`: the application or team, above the `sl:sidebar-content`. It does not scroll with the content.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `panel-only` | *boolean* | — | Show it only in the phone panel: from 768&nbsp;px it is left out (`display: none`), so a header row or a sidebar stays short. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:panel-only="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sidebar-content

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-content>`: The part of the `sl:sidebar` that scrolls, between `sl:sidebar-header` and `sl:sidebar-footer`; it holds the `sl:sidebar-group`s. A plain `div`: the sidebar itself is the navigation landmark.

`sl:sidebar-content` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:sidebar-footer

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-footer>`: The bottom of the `sl:sidebar`: settings, help or the signed-in user, below the `sl:sidebar-content`. It does not scroll with the content.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `panel-only` | *boolean* | — | Show it only in the phone panel: from 768&nbsp;px it is left out (`display: none`), so a header row or a sidebar stays short. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:panel-only="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sidebar-separator

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-separator>`: A line between parts of the `sl:sidebar`, in the sidebar's border colour. Decorative: screen readers skip it.

`sl:sidebar-separator` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:sidebar-group

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-group>`: A section of the `sl:sidebar`'s links: an optional `sl:sidebar-group-label` and `sl:sidebar-group-action`, then `sl:sidebar-group-content` with a `sl:sidebar-menu`. With a label it is a group named by that label (`role="group"`, `aria-labelledby`), so a screen reader says "Platform, group" when the focus moves into it; without one it is a plain `div`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | — | The group's id. Its label gets `&lt;id&gt;-label`; without an id, each render generates one (`sl-sidebar-group-` and eight random characters). |
| `panel-only` | *boolean* | — | Show it only in the phone panel: from 768&nbsp;px it is left out (`display: none`), so a header row or a sidebar stays short. |
| `collapsible` | *boolean* | — | Make it a section that opens and closes, on the browser's `details` element: its `sl:sidebar-group-label` (which must come first) becomes the `summary`, with a chevron at the end. Collapsed to icons, and in the header row, its links show whether it is open or not. |
| `open` | *boolean* | — | Render a collapsible group open, for instance when it holds the current page: `th:open="${#slNav.current('/build')}"`. |
| `icon-hidden` | *boolean* | — | Leave the group out while the sidebar is collapsed to icons (`collapsible="icon"`), as for a group whose links have no icons of their own. Expanded, and in the phone panel, it shows as usual. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sidebar-group-label

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-group-label>`: The name of a `sl:sidebar-group`, in small muted text above its menu. The group is labelled by it, so it gets an id from the group (`&lt;group id&gt;-label`); give the group an `id` rather than the label. In a `collapsible` group it is the `summary` that opens and closes it, with a chevron at the end, and must be the group's first part.

`sl:sidebar-group-label` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:sidebar-group-action

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-group-action>`: An icon button at the end of a `sl:sidebar-group`'s label, such as "Add project". It holds only an icon, so it needs an `aria-label`. Every other attribute passes through (`hx-*`, `commandfor`, `href`).

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `as` | `button`, `a` | `button` | Element to render: a `button`, or an anchor (`a`) to a page. |
| `type` | `button`, `submit`, `reset` | `button` | Submit behaviour of the button; defaults to `button`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:as="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended. An `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` is required; rendering fails without one.

#### sl:sidebar-group-content

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-group-content>`: The content of a `sl:sidebar-group`, below its label: usually one `sl:sidebar-menu`.

`sl:sidebar-group-content` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:sidebar-menu

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-menu>`: A list of links (or buttons) in the `sl:sidebar`: a `ul` of `sl:sidebar-menu-item`s, so a screen reader says how many there are.

`sl:sidebar-menu` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:sidebar-menu-item

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-menu-item>`: One entry of a `sl:sidebar-menu`: an `li` holding a `sl:sidebar-menu-button`, and optionally a `sl:sidebar-menu-badge` or a `sl:sidebar-menu-action` beside it. With a badge, the button is described by it (`aria-describedby`), so a screen reader says "Inbox, link, 12". With `collapsible` it is a group of links that opens and closes: the menu button is what the user clicks, followed by a `sl:sidebar-menu-sub`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | — | The item's id. Its badge gets `&lt;id&gt;-badge`; without an id, each render generates one (`sl-sidebar-menu-item-` and eight random characters). |
| `collapsible` | *boolean* | — | Make it a group that opens and closes, on the browser's `details` element (no JavaScript needed): its `sl:sidebar-menu-button` becomes the `summary`, with a chevron, and its `sl:sidebar-menu-sub` the content. Collapsed to icons, a click on the button expands the sidebar and opens the group. It takes no badge or action: the browser would hide them with the sub-menu. |
| `open` | *boolean* | — | Render a collapsible item open, for instance when it holds the current page: `th:open="${#slNav.current('/settings')}"`. |
| `panel-only` | *boolean* | — | Show it only in the phone panel: from 768&nbsp;px it is left out (`display: none`), so a header row or a sidebar stays short. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sidebar-menu-button

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-menu-button>`: A link (or button) in a `sl:sidebar-menu-item`: an icon in the `icon-start` slot, then the label. Mark the current page with `active`, for instance `th:active="${#slNav.current('/people')}"`. The label is cut off with an ellipsis when it does not fit; for two lines, put each in an element of its own (`&lt;strong&gt;`, `&lt;small&gt;`). Every attribute that is not a prop passes through (`href`, `hx-*`); `class` is appended to `sidebar-menu-button`. In a `collapsible` `sl:sidebar-menu-item` it is the item's `summary`, which opens and closes the group, with a chevron at the end; `as`, `type` and `disabled` do not apply there, and it takes no `href`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `as` | `a`, `button` | `a` | Element to render: an anchor (`a`) to a page, or a `button` for an action. |
| `active` | *boolean* | — | The current one: the link gets `aria-current="page"`, a button `aria-current="true"`, and both the active look. |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline` | `default` | Visual style: `outline` adds a background and a border. |
| `size` | `default`, `sm`, `lg` | `default` | Height: `sm` for a dense list, `lg` for an entry with two lines, such as the application's name in the `sl:sidebar-header`. |
| `type` | `button`, `submit`, `reset` | `button` | Submit behaviour of a button; defaults to `button`. |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | Disable it. A disabled link loses its `href` and leaves the tab order. |
| `tooltip` | *string* | — | The label to show beside the button while the sidebar is collapsed to icons (`collapsible="icon"`), on hover and on keyboard focus; usually the same words as the label. Not shown while the sidebar is expanded or on a phone. It repeats the button's name, so it is hidden from assistive technology and describes nothing (`sl:tooltip`'s `mode="label"`). |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:as="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sidebar-menu-action

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-menu-action>`: An icon button at the end of a `sl:sidebar-menu-item`, beside its `sl:sidebar-menu-button`: a second action for that entry, such as "More" or "Remove from favourites". It holds only an icon, so it needs an `aria-label` that names the entry too ("More for Design Engineering"). Every other attribute passes through.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `as` | `button`, `a` | `button` | Element to render: a `button`, or an anchor (`a`) to a page. |
| `type` | `button`, `submit`, `reset` | `button` | Submit behaviour of the button; defaults to `button`. |
| `show-on-hover` | *boolean* | — | From 768&nbsp;px, show it only while the pointer is over the item, the focus is in it, or its menu is open (`aria-expanded="true"`). Always shown on a phone. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:as="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended. An `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` is required; rendering fails without one.

#### sl:sidebar-menu-badge

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-menu-badge>`: A count at the end of a `sl:sidebar-menu-button`, inside the same `sl:sidebar-menu-item`. The button is described by it, so a screen reader says the count after the link's name ("Inbox, link, 12"); the badge gets its id from the item (`&lt;item id&gt;-badge`). Clicks go through it to the button.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` | *string* | — | What a screen reader says instead of the visible text, such as "12 unread messages". The visible count is then hidden from it. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:label="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:sidebar-menu-sub

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-menu-sub>`: A list of links below a `sl:sidebar-menu-button`, indented along a line: a `ul` of `sl:sidebar-menu-sub-item`s. In a `collapsible` `sl:sidebar-menu-item` it is what opens and closes. With `collapsible` of its own it opens and closes by a chevron beside the menu button, which stays a link to a page of its own. Hidden while the sidebar is collapsed to icons.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `collapsible` | *boolean* | — | Open and close it with a chevron at the end of the menu button (a `summary`, drawn as a `sl:sidebar-menu-action`), so the menu button stays a link. The chevron holds only an icon, so it needs an `aria-label` that names the sub-menu ("Playground's pages"); every other attribute goes to the `ul`. |
| `open` | *boolean* | — | Render a collapsible sub-menu open, for instance when it holds the current page: `th:open="${#slNav.current('/playground')}"`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:collapsible="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended. An `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` is required when `collapsible` is `true`; rendering fails without one.

#### sl:sidebar-menu-sub-item

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-menu-sub-item>`: One entry of a `sl:sidebar-menu-sub`: an `li` holding a `sl:sidebar-menu-sub-button`.

`sl:sidebar-menu-sub-item` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:sidebar-menu-sub-button

Attributes of `<sl:sidebar-menu-sub-button>`: A link (or button) in a `sl:sidebar-menu-sub-item`. Mark the current page with `active`, for instance `th:active="${#slNav.current('/settings/billing')}"`. An optional icon goes in the `icon-start` slot; the label is cut off with an ellipsis when it does not fit. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through (`href`, `hx-*`); `class` is appended to `sidebar-menu-sub-button`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `as` | `a`, `button` | `a` | Element to render: an anchor (`a`) to a page, or a `button` for an action. |
| `active` | *boolean* | — | The current one: the link gets `aria-current="page"`, a button `aria-current="true"`, and both the active look. |
| `size` | `sm`, `md` | `md` | Text size: `sm` for a denser list. |
| `type` | `button`, `submit`, `reset` | `button` | Submit behaviour of a button; defaults to `button`. |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | Disable it. A disabled link loses its `href` and leaves the tab order. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:as="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

### JavaScript

A small Alpine component, `slSidebar`, does the work: `<sl:sidebar-provider>` renders as
`<div class="sidebar-provider" x-data="slSidebar" data-state="expanded">`.

- **It needs Alpine**, and no Alpine plugin. It writes the cookie itself (a plain cookie, not `$persist`, which would
  store JSON in the browser where the server cannot read it). Alpine is included by default; see
  [Alpine](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/alpine.md).
- **On a desktop it takes `popovertarget` off the trigger** and sets `aria-expanded` from the state instead: browsers
  report a `popovertarget` button as collapsed while its popover is closed, which the sidebar on a desktop always is.
  Below 768 px it puts it back. It finds the trigger by its `aria-controls`.
- **Collapsed to icons, it expands the sidebar** when a collapsible group's icon is clicked, and opens the group.
  Each menu button's tooltip is a second small Alpine component, `slSidebarMenuTooltip`, on the tooltip itself; it
  shares `slTooltip`'s code.
- **It fires `sl-sidebar-toggle`** on the provider (it bubbles) after the user collapses or expands the sidebar and when
  the panel opens or closes: `detail` is `{state: 'expanded' | 'collapsed', open, mobile}`.
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:sidebar-provider>`**: it would replace `slSidebar`. Put it on an element inside.

---

## Site Header

> A header bar across the top of the page, or at the top of the sidebar layout's page.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/site-header/

**Example: Site header.** A header bar: its content in a row from the start, the end slot at the end. Across the top of a page it has the page's ground and a border below.

```html
<sl:site-header>
  <sl:button as="a" href="/" variant="ghost">
    <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="gallery-vertical-end"/></sl:slot>
    Acme Inc.
  </sl:button>
  <sl:slot name="end">
    <sl:button variant="ghost" size="icon-sm" aria-label="Search"><sl:icon name="search"/></sl:button>
    <sl:button variant="outline" size="sm">Sign in</sl:button>
  </sl:slot>
</sl:site-header>
```

### Usage

`<sl:site-header>` is a `header` that lays out its content in a row. Whatever you put in it starts at the start of the
line; the `end` slot goes to the end:

```html
<sl:site-header>
  <sl:button as="a" th:href="@{/}" variant="ghost">Acme Inc.</sl:button>
  <sl:slot name="end">
    <sl:theme-toggle/>
  </sl:slot>
</sl:site-header>
```

Across the top of the page it is the page's banner landmark, on the page's colour with a border below. It has no
`sticky` option yet; the header scrolls with the page.

### In the header layout

With a [`<sl:sidebar placement="header">`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md#in-the-header) in it, it is the header layout's header: from
768 px the sidebar is a row of links in it, and on a phone a menu button (`<sl:sidebar-trigger>`) opens the same
navigation as a panel. It then goes first in `<sl:sidebar-provider>`, above `<sl:sidebar-inset>`. With
`variant="inset"` on the sidebar the header lies on the sidebar's colour without a border, and the page is a card below
it.

**Example: Header layout.** placement="header" puts the same navigation in a sl:site-header: from 768 px a row of links (the current page marked as in the sidebar), with the application's name before it and search, the theme toggle and the signed-in user's menu at the end. Favorites, Settings and Help are panel-only, so the row stays short; the panel's own header with the name is panel-only too. "More" beside Documents opens a dropdown menu below it. variant="inset" makes the page a card below the header. Narrower, the menu button opens the same nav as the panel: see the next example.

```html
<sl:skip-link/>
<sl:sidebar-provider>
  <sl:site-header>
    <sl:sidebar-trigger aria-label="Menu"><sl:icon name="menu"/></sl:sidebar-trigger>
    <sl:button as="a" href="/" variant="ghost">
      <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="gallery-vertical-end"/></sl:slot>
      Acme Inc.
    </sl:button>
    <sl:sidebar placement="header" variant="inset">
      <sl:sidebar-header panel-only>
        <sl:sidebar-menu>
          <sl:sidebar-menu-item>
            <sl:sidebar-menu-button href="/" size="lg">
              <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="gallery-vertical-end"/></sl:slot>
              <strong>Acme Inc.</strong>
              <small>Enterprise</small>
            </sl:sidebar-menu-button>
          </sl:sidebar-menu-item>
        </sl:sidebar-menu>
      </sl:sidebar-header>
      <sl:sidebar-content>
        <sl:sidebar-group>
          <sl:sidebar-group-content>
            <sl:sidebar-menu>
              <sl:sidebar-menu-item>
                <sl:sidebar-menu-button href="/" th:active="${#slNav.current('/')}">
                  <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="house"/></sl:slot>
                  Home
                </sl:sidebar-menu-button>
              </sl:sidebar-menu-item>
              <sl:sidebar-menu-item id="header-inbox">
                <sl:sidebar-menu-button href="/inbox" th:active="${#slNav.current('/inbox')}">
                  <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="inbox"/></sl:slot>
                  Inbox
                </sl:sidebar-menu-button>
                <sl:sidebar-menu-badge label="12 unread">12</sl:sidebar-menu-badge>
              </sl:sidebar-menu-item>
              <sl:sidebar-menu-item>
                <sl:sidebar-menu-button href="/calendar" th:active="${#slNav.current('/calendar')}">
                  <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="calendar"/></sl:slot>
                  Calendar
                </sl:sidebar-menu-button>
              </sl:sidebar-menu-item>
              <sl:sidebar-menu-item>
                <sl:sidebar-menu-button href="/documents" th:active="${#slNav.current('/documents')}">
                  <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="file-text"/></sl:slot>
                  Documents
                </sl:sidebar-menu-button>
                <sl:dropdown-menu id="header-documents-more">
                  <sl:dropdown-menu-trigger as="sidebar-menu-action" aria-label="More for Documents">
                    <sl:icon name="ellipsis"/>
                  </sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>
                  <sl:dropdown-menu-content side="right">
                    <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/documents/new">New document</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
                    <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/documents/shared">Shared with me</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
                  </sl:dropdown-menu-content>
                </sl:dropdown-menu>
              </sl:sidebar-menu-item>
            </sl:sidebar-menu>
          </sl:sidebar-group-content>
        </sl:sidebar-group>
        <sl:sidebar-group id="favorites" panel-only>
          <sl:sidebar-group-label>Favorites</sl:sidebar-group-label>
          <sl:sidebar-group-content>
            <sl:sidebar-menu>
              <sl:sidebar-menu-item>
                <sl:sidebar-menu-button href="/projects/marketing">
                  <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="globe"/></sl:slot>
                  Marketing site
                </sl:sidebar-menu-button>
              </sl:sidebar-menu-item>
              <sl:sidebar-menu-item>
                <sl:sidebar-menu-button href="/projects/android">
                  <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="smartphone"/></sl:slot>
                  Android app
                </sl:sidebar-menu-button>
              </sl:sidebar-menu-item>
            </sl:sidebar-menu>
          </sl:sidebar-group-content>
        </sl:sidebar-group>
      </sl:sidebar-content>
      <sl:sidebar-footer panel-only>
        <sl:sidebar-menu>
          <sl:sidebar-menu-item>
            <sl:sidebar-menu-button href="/settings" size="sm" th:active="${#slNav.current('/settings')}">
              <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="settings"/></sl:slot>
              Settings
            </sl:sidebar-menu-button>
          </sl:sidebar-menu-item>
          <sl:sidebar-menu-item>
            <sl:sidebar-menu-button href="/help" size="sm">
              <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="life-buoy"/></sl:slot>
              Help
            </sl:sidebar-menu-button>
          </sl:sidebar-menu-item>
        </sl:sidebar-menu>
      </sl:sidebar-footer>
    </sl:sidebar>
    <sl:slot name="end">
      <sl:button variant="ghost" size="icon-sm" aria-label="Search"><sl:icon name="search"/></sl:button>
      <sl:theme-toggle/>
      <sl:user-menu placement="header" id="header-user-menu">
        <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/account"><sl:icon name="badge-check"/>Account</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
      </sl:user-menu>
    </sl:slot>
  </sl:site-header>
  <sl:sidebar-inset>
    <h1>Home</h1>
    <p>The page's content.</p>
  </sl:sidebar-inset>
</sl:sidebar-provider>
```

The [Navigation in the header](https://shadleaf.dev/current/layouts/header.md) layout has the whole layout to copy.

### As the page's header

At the top of `<sl:sidebar-inset>`, in the sidebar layout, it is the page's header (shadcn's sidebar-07): the trigger,
a vertical [separator](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/separator.md), the [breadcrumb](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/breadcrumb.md), and the theme toggle at the end. Inside the
`main` it is no landmark, and it has no border or colour of its own. The separator is a short line in the middle of the
row there. The [Collapses to icons](https://shadleaf.dev/current/layouts/collapses-to-icons.md) layout uses it this way.

```html
<sl:sidebar-inset>
  <sl:site-header>
    <sl:sidebar-trigger/>
    <sl:separator orientation="vertical"/>
    <sl:breadcrumb>...</sl:breadcrumb>
    <sl:slot name="end"><sl:theme-toggle/></sl:slot>
  </sl:site-header>
  ...
</sl:sidebar-inset>
```

### Height

The skins make it 3.5rem high (`--site-header-height`, set on `.site-header`): a minimum, so a breadcrumb that wraps on
a narrow screen makes it taller.

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:site-header>`: A header bar: a `header` laying out its content in a row. Across the top of the page (inside `sl:sidebar-provider`, before `sl:sidebar-inset`) it is the header layout's: a `sl:sidebar-trigger` that opens the menu on a phone, the application's name, the `sl:sidebar` with `placement="header"` as a row of links, and the `end` slot. At the top of `sl:sidebar-inset` it is the page's header in the sidebar layout: the trigger, a vertical `sl:separator` and a `sl:breadcrumb`. The `end` slot (search, the theme toggle, the signed-in user) goes to the end of the row.

`sl:site-header` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## User Menu

> The signed-in user with a menu that signs out, or a Sign in link, for the sidebar's footer or the site header.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/user-menu/

**Example: User menu.** In the sidebar's footer, the signed-in user as a large menu button: picture, name and email. It opens a menu beside the sidebar (below the button in the phone panel) with the user, the application's items and Sign out, which posts to #slUser.logoutUrl with the CSRF token. Collapsed to icons, the picture stays, with the name as a tooltip.

```html
<sl:sidebar-provider>
  <sl:sidebar collapsible="none">
    <sl:sidebar-content>
      <p>The application's navigation.</p>
    </sl:sidebar-content>
    <sl:sidebar-footer>
      <sl:user-menu>
        <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/account"><sl:icon name="badge-check"/>Account</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
        <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/billing"><sl:icon name="credit-card"/>Billing</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
        <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/notifications"><sl:icon name="bell"/>Notifications</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
      </sl:user-menu>
    </sl:sidebar-footer>
  </sl:sidebar>
  <sl:sidebar-inset>
    <p>The page's content.</p>
  </sl:sidebar-inset>
</sl:sidebar-provider>
```

### Usage

Put it in the [sidebar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md)'s footer:

```html
<sl:sidebar-footer>
  <sl:user-menu>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" th:href="@{/account}"><sl:icon name="badge-check"/>Account</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" th:href="@{/settings}"><sl:icon name="settings"/>Settings</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
  </sl:user-menu>
</sl:sidebar-footer>
```

It shows who is signed in, as Spring Security knows them: the user's picture, name and email, in a large
[menu button](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md#menu-buttons) (shadcn's `NavUser`). The button opens a [dropdown menu](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dropdown-menu.md)
with the user at the top, then the items you put inside `<sl:user-menu>`, then **Sign out**. Without items there is
nothing between the user and Sign out but a separator.

The user comes from `#slUser` (see [Spring Security](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/spring-security.md#the-signed-in-user)), so no
controller adds anything to the model, and the menu works on every page, error pages included. The second line is the
email, or the username when there is no email (GitHub often sends none) and it is not the name already (a form
login's user is named by the username), or nothing.

### In the site header

`placement="header"` makes it a round button with the picture alone, for the `end` slot of a
[site header](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/site-header.md), as in the header layout:

**Example: In the site header.** placement="header": a round button with the picture, named "Account: " and the user's name, at the end of a sl:site-header. The menu opens below it, lined up with its end.

```html
<sl:site-header>
  <sl:button as="a" href="/" variant="ghost">
    <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="gallery-vertical-end"/></sl:slot>
    Acme Inc.
  </sl:button>
  <sl:slot name="end">
    <sl:user-menu placement="header" id="user-menu-header">
      <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/account"><sl:icon name="badge-check"/>Account</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
      <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/settings"><sl:icon name="settings"/>Settings</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    </sl:user-menu>
  </sl:slot>
</sl:site-header>
```

```html
<sl:site-header>
  ...
  <sl:slot name="end">
    <sl:theme-toggle/>
    <sl:user-menu placement="header">
      <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" th:href="@{/account}"><sl:icon name="badge-check"/>Account</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    </sl:user-menu>
  </sl:slot>
</sl:site-header>
```

The button has no text, so it is named "Account: " and the user's name: a screen reader says "Account: shadcn, menu
button". The menu opens below it, lined up with its end.

### The picture

The picture goes through an [avatar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/avatar.md): while it loads, when the user has none and when it fails to load,
the initials show instead (from the name, or from the email's local part), and the user icon when there are no
initials either. A picture that is gone, as an identity provider's often are after a while, never shows a broken
image.

**Example: Without a picture.** A user without a picture, or whose picture fails to load, gets the initials (here from a GitHub sign-in: no email, so the username is the second line).

```html
<sl:site-header>
  <sl:slot name="end">
    <sl:user-menu placement="header" id="user-menu-initials"/>
  </sl:slot>
</sl:site-header>
```

### Nobody signed in

For an anonymous visitor the menu is a **Sign in** link instead: a menu button with the log-in icon in the sidebar, an
outline button in the header. It goes to `#slUser.loginUrl`: `/login`, the authorization endpoint of your one OAuth2
client (`/oauth2/authorization/github`), or what `shadleaf.security.login-url` says.

**Example: Nobody signed in.** An anonymous visitor gets a Sign in link to #slUser.loginUrl instead: /login, the one OAuth2 client's authorization endpoint, or shadleaf.security.login-url. In the sidebar a menu button, in the header an outline button.

```html
<sl:site-header>
  <sl:button as="a" href="/" variant="ghost">
    <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="gallery-vertical-end"/></sl:slot>
    Acme Inc.
  </sl:button>
  <sl:slot name="end">
    <sl:user-menu placement="header"/>
  </sl:slot>
</sl:site-header>
```

So one layout serves the sign-in page, a public page and the application. Without Spring Security on the classpath
nobody is ever signed in, and the menu is always the link.

### Signing out

**Sign out** is a POST, with Spring Security's CSRF token, to `#slUser.logoutUrl`: `/logout`, or what
`shadleaf.security.logout-url` says. The menu holds only menu items, so the form is not inside it: `<sl:user-menu>`
renders it after the menu, hidden, with the id `user-menu-sign-out`, and Sign out is a submit button in the menu that
names it (`form="user-menu-sign-out"`).

```html
<button type="submit" class="dropdown-menu-item user-menu-sign-out" role="menuitem"
        form="user-menu-sign-out">Sign out</button>
...
<form id="user-menu-sign-out" method="post" action="/logout" hx-boost="false" hidden>
  <input type="hidden" name="_csrf" value="...">
</form>
```

The form's `th:action` is what adds the token, as on every form of yours; nothing to configure. Spring Security's
default `/logout` then signs the user out and redirects to `/login?logout`. With OpenID Connect, sign out at the
provider too with Spring Security's `OidcClientInitiatedLogoutSuccessHandler`: see
[Signing out at Keycloak](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/oauth2.md#signing-out-at-keycloak).

`hx-boost="false"` keeps a [boosted](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/htmx.md) page from sending it with htmx: signing out should load a
whole page, with a new CSRF token. The Sign in link has it too, as signing in usually redirects to an identity
provider, which an htmx request cannot follow.

### Where it opens

In the sidebar, the menu opens beside it (`side="right"`), its end lined up with the button's: from the footer, it
grows upwards. Collapsed to icons the button is the picture, with the name as a [tooltip](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md#collapsed-to-icons);
the tooltip and the menu are never shown together. In the phone panel there is no room beside the button, so the menu
opens above or below it, as every [menu in the sidebar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md#menus-in-the-sidebar) does. `side` and `align`
change it:

```html
<sl:user-menu side="top" align="start"/>
```

### Labels

The labels are messages, which your `messages.properties` can reword or translate (see
[Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md)):

```properties
sl.user-menu.trigger=Account: {0}
sl.user-menu.sign-in=Sign in
sl.user-menu.sign-out=Sign out
```

`{0}` is the user's name. An `aria-label` on `<sl:user-menu placement="header">` replaces the header button's name for one
menu. In the sidebar the button is named by the name and email it shows, and the Sign in link by its text, so an
`aria-label` is left out there.

### A different user menu

`<sl:user-menu>` covers shadcn's `NavUser`. A menu laid out differently (another trigger, items above the user, a
second group) is a [dropdown menu](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dropdown-menu.md) of your own that reads `#slUser`:

```html
<sl:dropdown-menu id="account-menu" th:if="${#slUser.signedIn}">
  <sl:dropdown-menu-trigger variant="ghost">[[${#slUser.current.name}]]</sl:dropdown-menu-trigger>
  <sl:dropdown-menu-content align="end">
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" th:href="@{/account}">Account</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-separator/>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item type="submit" form="sign-out">Sign out</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
  </sl:dropdown-menu-content>
</sl:dropdown-menu>
<form id="sign-out" method="post" th:action="@{${#slUser.logoutUrl}}" hx-boost="false" hidden></form>
<sl:button th:unless="${#slUser.signedIn}" as="a" th:href="@{${#slUser.loginUrl}}" hx-boost="false">Sign in</sl:button>
```

Keep the form outside `role="menu"`, and keep `th:action`: it adds the CSRF token.

A team or version switcher in the sidebar's header is built the same way, from a dropdown menu: see
[Menus in the sidebar](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md#menus-in-the-sidebar).

### Without JavaScript

The menu is a popover, opened by the browser, so it opens and Sign out signs out without JavaScript. The arrow keys,
typeahead and closing after an item need Alpine, as in every [dropdown menu](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dropdown-menu.md). Without JavaScript the
picture lies over the initials and covers them once loaded.

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:user-menu>`: The signed-in user (`#slUser.current`) with a menu that signs out, or a Sign in link for an anonymous visitor. In the sidebar's footer a large menu button with the user's picture, name and email (or username, when it is not the name); in the site header a button with the picture alone. Both open a `sl:dropdown-menu`: the user's name and email, the items in its content (Account, Settings), and Sign out, which posts to `#slUser.logoutUrl` (`shadleaf.security.logout-url`) from a form of its own, so `th:action` adds Spring Security's CSRF token. The picture goes through `sl:avatar`: while it loads, without one and when it fails to load, the initials show. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through to the button (or the Sign in link), except an `aria-label`, which only names the header's button: in the sidebar the button's name is the text it shows, and Sign in is named by its text.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `placement` | `sidebar`, `header` | `sidebar` | `sidebar`: a `sl:sidebar-menu` with one large menu button, for the `sl:sidebar-footer` (shadcn's NavUser). `header`: a round button with the picture, for the `end` slot of `sl:site-header`, named "Account: " and the user's name. |
| `id` | *string* | `user-menu` | The menu's id. Its button gets the id with `-trigger` added, the sign-out form with `-sign-out`. Two user menus on a page need different ids. |
| `side` | `top`, `right`, `bottom`, `left` | — | The side of the button the menu opens on, as on `sl:dropdown-menu-content`. By default `right` in the sidebar (below the button in the phone panel) and `bottom` in the header. |
| `align` | `start`, `center`, `end` | `end` | How the menu lines up with the button along that side, as on `sl:dropdown-menu-content`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:placement="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Breadcrumb

> The trail of pages above the one shown now, with a menu for the levels a long trail leaves out.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/breadcrumb/

**Example: Breadcrumb.**

```html
<sl:breadcrumb>
  <sl:breadcrumb-list>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link href="/">Home</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link href="/components">Components</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-page>Breadcrumb</sl:breadcrumb-page></sl:breadcrumb-item>
  </sl:breadcrumb-list>
</sl:breadcrumb>
```

### Built from parts

A breadcrumb is shadcn/ui's parts: a `nav` holding an ordered list of levels, with a separator between them. The last
level is the page shown now.

```html
<sl:breadcrumb>
  <sl:breadcrumb-list>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link th:href="@{/}">Home</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link th:href="@{/components}">Components</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-page>Breadcrumb</sl:breadcrumb-page></sl:breadcrumb-item>
  </sl:breadcrumb-list>
</sl:breadcrumb>
```

`<sl:breadcrumb-link>` is a link: give it an `href` or `th:href`. `<sl:breadcrumb-page>` is plain text with
`aria-current="page"`. The list wraps onto a second line when there is no room, as on a phone.

In an [application shell](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md) the breadcrumb goes in the page header, after the sidebar's trigger and a
vertical [separator](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/separator.md).

### A level without a page

A level that has no page of its own, such as a group in the sidebar, is plain text in its item:

**Example: A level without a page.** A level that has no page of its own is plain text in its item.

```html
<sl:breadcrumb aria-label="Breadcrumb with a plain level">
  <sl:breadcrumb-list>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link href="/">Home</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item>Settings</sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-page>Notifications</sl:breadcrumb-page></sl:breadcrumb-item>
  </sl:breadcrumb-list>
</sl:breadcrumb>
```

### From a list

There is no mode that renders the levels from a list: write them with `th:each`, and the page shown now after them.
`th:block` keeps item and separator together:

```html
<sl:breadcrumb>
  <sl:breadcrumb-list>
    <th:block th:each="level : ${levels}">
      <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link th:href="${level.url}">[[${level.name}]]</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
      <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    </th:block>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-page th:text="${title}">Title</sl:breadcrumb-page></sl:breadcrumb-item>
  </sl:breadcrumb-list>
</sl:breadcrumb>
```

### Collapsed levels

A long trail can leave levels out. An empty `<sl:breadcrumb-ellipsis>` stands for them; a screen reader says "More
levels".

**Example: Collapsed levels.** An ellipsis without content stands for the levels left out; a screen reader says "More levels".

```html
<sl:breadcrumb aria-label="Breadcrumb with collapsed levels">
  <sl:breadcrumb-list>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link href="/">Home</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-ellipsis/></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link href="/components">Components</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-page>Breadcrumb</sl:breadcrumb-page></sl:breadcrumb-item>
  </sl:breadcrumb-list>
</sl:breadcrumb>
```

#### With a menu

Give the ellipsis the levels it stands for, as [dropdown menu](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dropdown-menu.md) items, and it becomes a ghost icon
button that opens a menu of them, as in shadcn's example:

**Example: With a menu.** An ellipsis with the hidden levels as its content is a button that opens a menu of them. Arrow keys move through the menu, Escape closes it.

```html
<sl:breadcrumb aria-label="Breadcrumb with a menu">
  <sl:breadcrumb-list>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link href="/">Home</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item>
      <sl:breadcrumb-ellipsis>
        <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/docs">Documentation</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
        <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/themes">Themes</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
        <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/github">GitHub</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
      </sl:breadcrumb-ellipsis>
    </sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link href="/components">Components</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator/>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-page>Breadcrumb</sl:breadcrumb-page></sl:breadcrumb-item>
  </sl:breadcrumb-list>
</sl:breadcrumb>
```

```html
<sl:breadcrumb-item>
  <sl:breadcrumb-ellipsis>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/docs">Documentation</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
    <sl:dropdown-menu-item as="a" href="/themes">Themes</sl:dropdown-menu-item>
  </sl:breadcrumb-ellipsis>
</sl:breadcrumb-item>
```

The button is named "Show more levels"; an `aria-label` replaces it, and any other attribute goes to the button too.
The menu's id is `breadcrumb-more` (the button's `breadcrumb-more-trigger`): give a second breadcrumb with a menu on the
same page another `id`. The menu behaves as every [dropdown menu](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dropdown-menu.md): arrow keys, Home, End and
typeahead move through the levels, Enter follows one, Escape closes it and gives the focus back to the button.

### Separator

The separator is a chevron that points to the next level: to the left on a right-to-left page (`dir="rtl"`). Content
of its own replaces it, such as another icon; that content is not turned around.

**Example: Another separator.** Content of its own replaces the chevron.

```html
<sl:breadcrumb aria-label="Breadcrumb with slashes">
  <sl:breadcrumb-list>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link href="/">Home</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator><sl:icon name="slash"/></sl:breadcrumb-separator>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-link href="/components">Components</sl:breadcrumb-link></sl:breadcrumb-item>
    <sl:breadcrumb-separator><sl:icon name="slash"/></sl:breadcrumb-separator>
    <sl:breadcrumb-item><sl:breadcrumb-page>Breadcrumb</sl:breadcrumb-page></sl:breadcrumb-item>
  </sl:breadcrumb-list>
</sl:breadcrumb>
```

### Accessibility

- The `nav` is named "Breadcrumb" (`sl.breadcrumb.label`); an `aria-label` of your own replaces it, which two
  breadcrumbs on one page need.
- The separators are list items hidden from assistive technology, so a screen reader counts only the levels.
- The page shown now has `aria-current="page"` and is not a link. shadcn's adds `role="link"` and
  `aria-disabled="true"`, which a screen reader announces as a link that goes nowhere.
- The empty ellipsis reads "More levels", where shadcn/ui hides it from screen readers.
- In Windows' high-contrast mode a focused link gets an outline, as the focus ring (a shadow) is gone there.

### Messages

[Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md) lists the languages Shadleaf ships them in, and explains how to add one.

| Key | English |
| --- | --- |
| `sl.breadcrumb.label` | Breadcrumb |
| `sl.breadcrumb.more` | More levels |
| `sl.breadcrumb.more.trigger` | Show more levels |

### Left out

A breadcrumb built from the request's path or from the sidebar's menu, and structured data (`BreadcrumbList`) for
search engines.

### Attributes

#### sl:breadcrumb

Attributes of `<sl:breadcrumb>`: The trail of pages above the one shown now, in a `nav` named "Breadcrumb" (the message `sl.breadcrumb.label`; an `aria-label` replaces it). Holds a `sl:breadcrumb-list` with `sl:breadcrumb-item`s, each a `sl:breadcrumb-link` or, last, the `sl:breadcrumb-page`, with a `sl:breadcrumb-separator` between them.

`sl:breadcrumb` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:breadcrumb-list

Attributes of `<sl:breadcrumb-list>`: The ordered list of a `sl:breadcrumb`: its items and the separators between them, on one line that wraps when there is no room.

`sl:breadcrumb-list` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:breadcrumb-item

Attributes of `<sl:breadcrumb-item>`: One level of a `sl:breadcrumb-list`: a `sl:breadcrumb-link`, the `sl:breadcrumb-page`, a `sl:breadcrumb-ellipsis`, or plain text for a level that has no page of its own.

`sl:breadcrumb-item` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:breadcrumb-link

Attributes of `<sl:breadcrumb-link>`: A link to a level above the page shown now. Give it an `href` or `th:href`. A level without a page of its own is plain text in its `sl:breadcrumb-item` instead.

`sl:breadcrumb-link` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:breadcrumb-page

Attributes of `<sl:breadcrumb-page>`: The page shown now, last in the trail: text in the foreground colour, marked `aria-current="page"`. Not a link.

`sl:breadcrumb-page` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:breadcrumb-separator

Attributes of `<sl:breadcrumb-separator>`: The mark between two items of a `sl:breadcrumb-list`: a chevron that points the other way on a right-to-left page. Content of its own (another icon, a slash) replaces it; that content is not turned around. Decorative: screen readers skip it, so the list holds only the levels.

`sl:breadcrumb-separator` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:breadcrumb-ellipsis

Attributes of `<sl:breadcrumb-ellipsis>`: Stands for the levels a long trail leaves out. Without content it is an ellipsis, read as "More levels" (the message `sl.breadcrumb.more`). With content, the `sl:dropdown-menu-item`s of those levels (`as="a"`), it is a ghost icon button named "Show more levels" (`sl.breadcrumb.more.trigger`; an `aria-label` replaces it) that opens a `sl:dropdown-menu` of them; any other attribute then goes to the button.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | — | With content: the menu's id, `breadcrumb-more` when not set (the button's is `&lt;id&gt;-trigger`); give a second breadcrumb on the page another one. Without content: the ellipsis's own id. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

### JavaScript

Only the ellipsis with a menu needs any: it is a [dropdown menu](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dropdown-menu.md#javascript), which needs Alpine and
no plugin. Without it the button still opens the menu (the browser's popover), without the arrow keys.

---

## Skip Link

> A link past the navigation to the page's content, for keyboard users.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/skip-link/

**Example: Skip link.** Hidden until it has the focus: click into the frame and press Tab. It skips to the element with id="main" (here the paragraph below), which tabindex="-1" lets take the focus.

```html
<sl:skip-link/>
<p id="main" tabindex="-1">The page's content.</p>
```

### Usage

A sidebar or a header puts many links before the content. A skip link lets a keyboard user jump past them
([WCAG 2.4.1](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/bypass-blocks)). Put it first in the body:

```html
<body>
  <sl:skip-link/>
  <sl:sidebar-provider>
    <sl:sidebar>...</sl:sidebar>
    <sl:sidebar-inset>...</sl:sidebar-inset>
  </sl:sidebar-provider>
</body>
```

It is hidden until it has the focus, which the first Tab on the page gives it, and then shows at the top of the page.
Following it moves the focus to the page's `main`, and the next Tab goes on from there.

### The target

The link goes to the element with `id="main"`; `for` points it at another id. [`<sl:sidebar-inset>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/sidebar.md) is
`<main id="main" tabindex="-1">`, so with a sidebar there is nothing to set up.

The target needs `tabindex="-1"` when it is no control of its own, as a `main` is. Without it, Chromium and Firefox
only move the place the next Tab starts from: the focus itself goes to the page, and a screen reader stays where it
was. `tabindex="-1"` lets the target take the focus without making it a tab stop. A target of your own:

```html
<sl:skip-link for="content"/>
...
<main id="content" tabindex="-1">...</main>
```

The focused `main` draws no focus ring: it is no control, and the page scrolling to it shows where the focus went.

### Text

The text is "Skip to main content" (the message `sl.skip-link`, see [Translating](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/translating.md)). Content of its own replaces it:

```html
<sl:skip-link for="results">Skip to the results</sl:skip-link>
```

### htmx

With `hx-boost` on the body the skip link still works: htmx leaves links to a place on the same page alone, so
following it makes no request.

### Messages

| Key | English |
| --- | --- |
| `sl.skip-link` | Skip to main content |

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:skip-link>`: A link past the navigation to the page's content, for keyboard users: put it first in the body. Hidden until it has the focus (the first Tab on the page), then drawn at the top of the page. Its text is "Skip to main content" (the message `sl.skip-link`), which content of its own replaces. The target should be able to take the focus: `sl:sidebar-inset` is `id="main"` with `tabindex="-1"`, so the default target works with it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `for` | *string* | `main` | The `id` of the element it skips to, usually the `main`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:for="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Tabs

> Panels of content shown one at a time, chosen from a row of tabs.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/tabs/

**Example: Tabs.** The arrow keys move between the tabs; Tab moves on to the panel.

```html
<sl:tabs id="tabs-demo" active="account">
  <sl:tabs-list>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="account">Account</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="password">Password</sl:tabs-trigger>
  </sl:tabs-list>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="account">
    <sl:card>
      <sl:card-header>
        <sl:card-title level="2">Account</sl:card-title>
        <sl:card-description>Make changes to your account here. Click save when you're done.</sl:card-description>
      </sl:card-header>
      <sl:card-content>
        <sl:field-group>
          <sl:field for="tabs-demo-name">
            <sl:field-label>Name</sl:field-label>
            <sl:input value="Pedro Duarte"/>
          </sl:field>
          <sl:field for="tabs-demo-username">
            <sl:field-label>Username</sl:field-label>
            <sl:input value="@peduarte"/>
          </sl:field>
        </sl:field-group>
      </sl:card-content>
      <sl:card-footer><sl:button>Save changes</sl:button></sl:card-footer>
    </sl:card>
  </sl:tabs-content>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="password">
    <sl:card>
      <sl:card-header>
        <sl:card-title level="2">Password</sl:card-title>
        <sl:card-description>Change your password here. After saving, you'll be logged out.</sl:card-description>
      </sl:card-header>
      <sl:card-content>
        <sl:field-group>
          <sl:field for="tabs-demo-current">
            <sl:field-label>Current password</sl:field-label>
            <sl:input type="password"/>
          </sl:field>
          <sl:field for="tabs-demo-new">
            <sl:field-label>New password</sl:field-label>
            <sl:input type="password"/>
          </sl:field>
        </sl:field-group>
      </sl:card-content>
      <sl:card-footer><sl:button>Save password</sl:button></sl:card-footer>
    </sl:card>
  </sl:tabs-content>
</sl:tabs>
```

### Built from parts

Tabs are `<sl:tabs>` with an `<sl:tabs-list>` of `<sl:tabs-trigger>`s and an `<sl:tabs-content>` per tab, as in
shadcn/ui. A trigger and its panel share a `tab`, and `active` on `<sl:tabs>` names the one that shows:

```html
<sl:tabs id="settings" active="account">
  <sl:tabs-list aria-label="Settings">
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="account">Account</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="password">Password</sl:tabs-trigger>
  </sl:tabs-list>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="account">…</sl:tabs-content>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="password">…</sl:tabs-content>
</sl:tabs>
```

shadcn/ui calls these `value` and `defaultValue`; `value` is taken in Thymeleaf (`th:value` is Spring's form value). The
tabs need an `id`: the triggers and panels get ids derived from it and their `tab` (`settings-account-trigger`,
`settings-account-content`), which link each tab to its panel (`aria-controls`, `aria-labelledby`). So a `tab` is
letters, digits and dashes.

**The server renders the active tab's panel and hides the others** (`hidden`), so the page is right before any script
runs. The Alpine component `slTabs` then switches panels in the browser and adds the keyboard of the
[ARIA tabs pattern](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/tabs/). An `aria-label` on the list names it, which helps
when a page has more than one.

### Tabs in the address

A trigger with an `href` is a link. Without JavaScript (or before Alpine starts) it loads the page with that tab
active, so every panel is reachable; with Alpine the tab switches in place, and the page's address becomes the link's
(`history.replaceState`), so a reload or a shared link opens the same tab. A click with Ctrl, Cmd or Shift still opens
the link in a new tab or window. The controller reads the tab from the request:

```html
<sl:tabs id="settings" th:active="${tab}">
  <sl:tabs-list aria-label="Settings">
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="account" th:href="@{/settings(tab=account)}">Account</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="password" th:href="@{/settings(tab=password)}">Password</sl:tabs-trigger>
  </sl:tabs-list>
  …
</sl:tabs>
```

```java
@GetMapping("/settings")
public String settings(@RequestParam(defaultValue = "account") String tab, Model model) {
  model.addAttribute("tab", TABS.contains(tab) ? tab : "account");
  return "settings";
}
```

Triggers without an `href` are buttons, which need Alpine: without it only the active panel can be seen. Use links for
tabs that must work without JavaScript.

**Example: Links.** Triggers with an href load the page with that tab active when there is no JavaScript; with it, the tab switches in place and the address follows.

```html
<sl:tabs id="tabs-links" active="week" activation-mode="manual">
  <sl:tabs-list aria-label="Period">
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="day" href="?period=day">Day</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="week" href="?period=week">Week</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="month" href="?period=month">Month</sl:tabs-trigger>
  </sl:tabs-list>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="day">14 orders today.</sl:tabs-content>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="week">86 orders this week.</sl:tabs-content>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="month">312 orders this month.</sl:tabs-content>
</sl:tabs>
```

### Keyboard

Tab moves into the list on the active tab, and out of it to the active panel (which takes the focus, as in Radix, so a
panel without controls can be scrolled and read). In the list:

| Key | Does |
| --- | --- |
| Left and Right arrow | The previous and next tab, wrapping around; they swap on a right-to-left page. Up and Down with `orientation="vertical"`. |
| Home, End | The first and last tab. |
| Enter, Space | Shows the focused tab's panel (for `activation-mode="manual"`). |

Disabled tabs are skipped. With `activation-mode="automatic"` (the default) a tab shows its panel as soon as the
arrow keys reach it. `manual` only moves the focus, and Enter or Space shows the panel: for panels that take a request
to show, such as ones loaded with htmx, so that arrowing past a tab does not load it.

### Line

`variant="line"` on the list draws no track, and a line under the active tab:

**Example: Line.**

```html
<sl:tabs id="tabs-line" active="overview">
  <sl:tabs-list variant="line" aria-label="Report">
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="overview">Overview</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="analytics">Analytics</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="reports">Reports</sl:tabs-trigger>
  </sl:tabs-list>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="overview">Sales are up 12% on last month.</sl:tabs-content>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="analytics">1,204 visitors this week.</sl:tabs-content>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="reports">Three reports are ready to download.</sl:tabs-content>
</sl:tabs>
```

### Vertical

`orientation="vertical"` puts the tabs in a column next to the panels:

**Example: Vertical.** orientation="vertical" puts the tabs in a column; the up and down arrow keys move between them.

```html
<sl:tabs id="tabs-vertical" active="general" orientation="vertical">
  <sl:tabs-list aria-label="Preferences">
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="general">General</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="appearance">Appearance</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="advanced">Advanced</sl:tabs-trigger>
  </sl:tabs-list>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="general">Language, time zone and start page.</sl:tabs-content>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="appearance">Theme and density.</sl:tabs-content>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="advanced">Developer options.</sl:tabs-content>
</sl:tabs>
```

### Icons and disabled tabs

**Example: Icons and a disabled tab.** The arrow keys skip a disabled tab.

```html
<sl:tabs id="tabs-icons" active="preview">
  <sl:tabs-list aria-label="View">
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="preview"><sl:icon name="app-window"/> Preview</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="code"><sl:icon name="code"/> Code</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="history" disabled><sl:icon name="history"/> History</sl:tabs-trigger>
  </sl:tabs-list>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="preview">The rendered page.</sl:tabs-content>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="code">The template.</sl:tabs-content>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="history">Earlier versions.</sl:tabs-content>
</sl:tabs>
```

A disabled button tab gets `disabled`; a disabled link tab loses its `href` and gets `aria-disabled`.

### Find in page

Once Alpine runs, the inactive panels are `hidden="until-found"` instead of `hidden`: the browser's find-in-page, and
a link to an element inside a panel (`#billing-address`), find their text, and the tab of the panel with the match
becomes active (the `beforematch` event). This works in Chrome and Edge 102, Firefox 148 and Safari 26.2 (Safari and
Firefox 139 to 147 do not scroll to the match yet). The server renders plain `hidden`: without Alpine a match would
show the panel under a tab that stays inactive.

### With htmx

**A panel that loads on first view.** Each time a panel is shown, `slTabs` fires `sl-tabs-show` on it, so htmx can
fetch its content the first time:

```html
<sl:tabs id="settings" th:active="${tab}" activation-mode="manual">
  <sl:tabs-list aria-label="Settings">
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="account" th:href="@{/settings(tab=account)}">Account</sl:tabs-trigger>
    <sl:tabs-trigger tab="sessions" th:href="@{/settings(tab=sessions)}">Sessions</sl:tabs-trigger>
  </sl:tabs-list>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="account" th:insert="~{settings :: account}"/>
  <sl:tabs-content tab="sessions" th:hx-get="${tab != 'sessions'} ? @{/settings/sessions}" hx-trigger="sl-tabs-show once">
    <th:block th:if="${tab == 'sessions'}" th:insert="~{settings :: sessions}"/>
  </sl:tabs-content>
</sl:tabs>
```

The server renders the active panel's content itself: `sl-tabs-show` is only fired when a panel is shown in the
browser, not for the one that is active when the page loads. The event does not bubble, so a panel's `hx-trigger`
never reacts to the tabs nested in it. `activation-mode="manual"` keeps the arrow keys from loading every panel on
the way.

**A swap that replaces the tabs** shows the tab the new markup has active: send the `active` the user had (for
example with `hx-vals` or `hx-include`) if it should stay.

**History.** htmx saves the page's markup as it is when it navigates away, with the tab the user chose and any panel
content loaded since; back restores exactly that. With link triggers the address has the tab too.

### Left out

- **`value`/`onValueChange` and controlled state**: the server renders the active tab; the `sl-tabs-show` event
  reports a switch.
- **`dir`**: the arrow keys follow the page's direction (`dir` on an ancestor or the page).
- **`loop`**: the arrow keys always wrap around, as Radix's do by default.
- **`forceMount`**: every panel is always in the page; the inactive ones are hidden.

### Attributes

#### sl:tabs

Attributes of `<sl:tabs>`: Panels of content shown one at a time, chosen from a row of tabs. Holds a `sl:tabs-list` of `sl:tabs-trigger`s and a `sl:tabs-content` per tab. The server renders the active tab's panel and hides the others, so the page is right before any script runs; Alpine adds switching in the browser and the keyboard (the ARIA tabs pattern). Give the triggers an `href` and the tabs work without JavaScript too.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | *string* | **required** | The id of the tabs. The ids of the triggers and panels derive from it (`settings-account-trigger`, `settings-account-content`). |
| `active` | *string* | **required** | The `tab` of the trigger and panel that are active when the page renders, e.g. from a request parameter: `th:active="${tab}"`. |
| `orientation` | `horizontal`, `vertical` | `horizontal` | `vertical` puts the tabs in a column next to the panels, and the up and down arrow keys move between them. |
| `activation-mode` | `automatic`, `manual` | `automatic` | `automatic`: a tab shows its panel as soon as the arrow keys reach it. `manual`: the arrow keys only move the focus, and Enter or Space shows the panel; for panels that are slow to show, such as ones loaded with htmx. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:id="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:tabs-list

Attributes of `<sl:tabs-list>`: The row (or column) of `sl:tabs-trigger`s of a `sl:tabs`: a `tablist`. An `aria-label` names it when the page has more than one.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `line` | `default` | `default`: the tabs sit on a muted track and the active one is raised. `line`: no track, a line under the active tab. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:tabs-trigger

Attributes of `<sl:tabs-trigger>`: A tab in a `sl:tabs-list`, which shows the `sl:tabs-content` with the same `tab`. A button, or with an `href` a link to the page with this tab active: without JavaScript the link loads it, with Alpine the tab switches in place and the address follows.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tab` | *string* | **required** | Which tab this is: the `tab` of its `sl:tabs-content`, and the value `sl:tabs`' `active` names. Part of the ids, so letters, digits and dashes. |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | The tab cannot be chosen; the arrow keys skip it. A disabled link loses its `href`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:tab="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:tabs-content

Attributes of `<sl:tabs-content>`: The panel of one tab of a `sl:tabs`, labelled by its trigger. Only the active tab's panel shows; the others are `hidden`. It takes the focus with Tab after the tabs (as in Radix), so a panel without controls can be read and scrolled.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tab` | *string* | **required** | Which tab's panel this is: the `tab` of its `sl:tabs-trigger`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:tab="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

### JavaScript

A small Alpine component, `slTabs`, is on `<sl:tabs>`: it renders as
`<div class="tabs" x-data="slTabs">`.

- **Without Alpine** the active panel shows, and link triggers load the page with another tab active. Button triggers
  need Alpine. Alpine is included by default; see [Alpine](https://shadleaf.dev/current/guides/alpine.md).
- **It needs no Alpine plugin.** If you load Alpine yourself (`shadleaf.assets.alpine=external`), plain Alpine is
  enough.
- **Keep your own `x-data` off `<sl:tabs>`.** An element has one `x-data`; put yours on an element inside.

| Event | On | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sl-tabs-show` | a panel (it does not bubble) | Fired when the panel is shown in the browser: a click, the keyboard, or find-in-page. |

---

## Accordion

> A stack of sections, each with a heading that shows or hides its content, on the browser's details element.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/accordion/

**Example: Accordion.** One section open at a time. Opening another closes it; the open one closes on a click.

```html
<sl:accordion name="accordion-demo">
  <sl:accordion-item open>
    <sl:accordion-trigger>Is it accessible?</sl:accordion-trigger>
    <sl:accordion-content>Yes. Each section is a details element, which the browser opens and closes, from the
      keyboard too.</sl:accordion-content>
  </sl:accordion-item>
  <sl:accordion-item>
    <sl:accordion-trigger>Is it styled?</sl:accordion-trigger>
    <sl:accordion-content>Yes. It comes with default styles that match the other components.</sl:accordion-content>
  </sl:accordion-item>
  <sl:accordion-item>
    <sl:accordion-trigger>Is it animated?</sl:accordion-trigger>
    <sl:accordion-content>Yes, where the browser can animate a height to auto, and only when the user has not
      asked for reduced motion.</sl:accordion-content>
  </sl:accordion-item>
</sl:accordion>
```

### Built from parts

An accordion is `<sl:accordion>` holding `<sl:accordion-item>`s, each with an `<sl:accordion-trigger>` and an
`<sl:accordion-content>`, as in shadcn/ui:

```html
<sl:accordion>
  <sl:accordion-item open>
    <sl:accordion-trigger>Is it accessible?</sl:accordion-trigger>
    <sl:accordion-content>Yes. It adheres to the WAI-ARIA design pattern.</sl:accordion-content>
  </sl:accordion-item>
  <sl:accordion-item>
    <sl:accordion-trigger>Is it styled?</sl:accordion-trigger>
    <sl:accordion-content>Yes.</sl:accordion-content>
  </sl:accordion-item>
</sl:accordion>
```

Each item is a [`<details>`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/details) element and
its trigger is the `<summary>`, so **the accordion needs no JavaScript**: the browser opens and closes the sections,
from the keyboard too (Tab, then Enter or Space), and a screen reader announces each trigger as expanded or collapsed.
The trigger must be the item's first child.

### One or several open

`type="single"` (the default) keeps one section open: opening another closes it. The items share a `details` `name`,
which the browser uses to close the others. The open section closes on a click too, so a single accordion is always
what shadcn/ui calls `collapsible`. The name must be unique on the page; without `name` each render generates one
(`sl-accordion-` and eight random characters), so an accordion that htmx swaps in never joins one that is already there.
Set `name` yourself when you want stable markup, but not on an accordion that htmx replaces: see below.

`type="multiple"` opens and closes every section on its own:

**Example: Multiple.** type="multiple" opens and closes each section on its own.

```html
<sl:accordion type="multiple">
  <sl:accordion-item open>
    <sl:accordion-trigger>How do I reset my password?</sl:accordion-trigger>
    <sl:accordion-content>Choose "Forgot password" on the sign-in page and follow the link we email
      you.</sl:accordion-content>
  </sl:accordion-item>
  <sl:accordion-item open>
    <sl:accordion-trigger>Can I change my username?</sl:accordion-trigger>
    <sl:accordion-content>Yes, once every 30 days, from your profile settings.</sl:accordion-content>
  </sl:accordion-item>
</sl:accordion>
```

### Open from the server

`open` on an item renders it open, where shadcn/ui takes `defaultValue`. The server decides, for example from a
request parameter or a fragment of the URL it rendered a link to:

```html
<sl:accordion>
  <sl:accordion-item th:each="question : ${faq}" th:open="${question.id == openQuestion}" th:id="${question.id}">
    <sl:accordion-trigger>[[${question.title}]]</sl:accordion-trigger>
    <sl:accordion-content th:utext="${question.answerHtml}">Answer</sl:accordion-content>
  </sl:accordion-item>
</sl:accordion>
```

In a single accordion only the first open item stays open. Write the trigger's text with `[[${...}]]`, not `th:text`:
`th:text` replaces the trigger's content, heading and chevron included.

### Headings

shadcn/ui puts each trigger in an `h3`. A `<summary>` must be the first child of its `<details>`, so it cannot sit in
a heading; the heading goes inside it instead. The trigger renders
`<summary><span role="heading" aria-level="3">…</span><svg …/></summary>`: the summary keeps its own role, a
disclosure with its expanded state, and screen reader users can still jump from question to question by heading.
`level` sets the level (3 by default); pick the one that fits the page's outline. For content that is not a list of
headed sections, use a [collapsible](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/collapsible.md).

### Find in page and links

The content of a closed section is still in the page. When the browser's find-in-page, or a link to an element inside
it (`/faq#refunds`), finds a match there, the browser opens the section. This works in Chrome and Edge 97, Firefox 148
and Safari 26.2 (where Safari and Firefox 139 to 147 do not scroll to the match yet); older browsers do not search
closed sections.

### Animation

The section's height animates open and closed where the browser can animate a height to `auto`
([`interpolate-size`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/interpolate-size), Chrome and Edge 129). The
height is that of `::details-content`, the box around everything but the summary (Chrome 131, Safari 18.4, Firefox
143). Other browsers open and close the section at once, and so does every browser when the user asks for reduced
motion. The chevron turns in both cases (not under reduced motion).

### In a card

**Example: In a card.**

```html
<sl:card>
  <sl:card-header>
    <sl:card-title level="2">Subscription &amp; billing</sl:card-title>
    <sl:card-description>Common questions about your account, plans and payments</sl:card-description>
  </sl:card-header>
  <sl:card-content>
    <sl:accordion name="accordion-billing">
      <sl:accordion-item open>
        <sl:accordion-trigger>What subscription plans do you offer?</sl:accordion-trigger>
        <sl:accordion-content>
          <p>Three: Starter, Professional and Enterprise, each with more storage and support.</p>
          <p><a href="/plans">Annual billing is available</a> with a 20% discount.</p>
        </sl:accordion-content>
      </sl:accordion-item>
      <sl:accordion-item>
        <sl:accordion-trigger>How does billing work?</sl:accordion-trigger>
        <sl:accordion-content>At the start of each billing cycle, by card or bank transfer.</sl:accordion-content>
      </sl:accordion-item>
      <sl:accordion-item>
        <sl:accordion-trigger>How do I cancel?</sl:accordion-trigger>
        <sl:accordion-content>From your account settings, at any time.</sl:accordion-content>
      </sl:accordion-item>
    </sl:accordion>
  </sl:card-content>
</sl:card>
```

### Disabled

`disabled` on an item keeps it closed (or open, with `open`): its trigger fades, leaves the tab order and ignores the
pointer. A closed disabled item renders no content, so find-in-page cannot open it either. shadcn/ui does the same by
not rendering closed content at all.

**Example: Disabled.** A disabled section cannot be opened, and renders no content while it is closed.

```html
<sl:accordion name="accordion-disabled">
  <sl:accordion-item>
    <sl:accordion-trigger>Can I access my account history?</sl:accordion-trigger>
    <sl:accordion-content>Yes, in the Account history section of your dashboard.</sl:accordion-content>
  </sl:accordion-item>
  <sl:accordion-item disabled>
    <sl:accordion-trigger>Premium feature information</sl:accordion-trigger>
    <sl:accordion-content>Upgrade your plan to see this.</sl:accordion-content>
  </sl:accordion-item>
  <sl:accordion-item>
    <sl:accordion-trigger>How do I update my email address?</sl:accordion-trigger>
    <sl:accordion-content>In your account settings; we send a link to the new address.</sl:accordion-content>
  </sl:accordion-item>
</sl:accordion>
```

### With htmx

An accordion is plain markup, so a swap that replaces it shows it as the server rendered it, and htmx's history cache
keeps the sections the user opened (the `open` attribute is in the markup it saves).

Leave `name` out of a single accordion that a swap replaces. htmx inserts the new markup before it removes the old,
and the browser closes an open `details` that joins a group which already has one open: with the same name on both,
the section the server rendered open arrives closed. The generated name is new on every render, so it never meets
the old one.

### Left out

- **Arrow keys between the triggers.** Radix moves the focus with the up and down arrows, Home and End; the ARIA
  pattern makes these optional. A `details` accordion has no script, so Tab moves between the triggers.
- **A single accordion that cannot be closed** (shadcn/ui's single without `collapsible`): the browser always lets the
  open `details` close.
- **`value`, `onValueChange` and controlled state**: the server renders the open items with `open`; the browser's
  `toggle` event reports changes.
- **`dir` and `orientation`**: the accordion follows the page's direction, and is vertical.
- **`role="region"` on the content.** Radix gives every panel one; the ARIA pattern advises against it where more than
  about six panels can be open at once, as landmarks pile up. The content is inside the `details`, which already
  groups it with its trigger.

### Attributes

#### sl:accordion

Attributes of `<sl:accordion>`: A stack of sections, each with a heading that shows or hides its content. Built on the browser's `details` element, so it needs no JavaScript: the keyboard, the open state, and find-in-page opening a section that holds a match all come from the browser.
