Holds `sl:accordion-item`s, each with a `sl:accordion-trigger` and a `sl:accordion-content`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `type` | `single`, `multiple` | `single` | `single`: opening a section closes the one that was open (the items share a `details` `name`). `multiple`: sections open and close on their own. |
| `name` | *string* | — | The group name the items of a `single` accordion share. Must be unique on the page. Without it, each render generates one (`sl-accordion-` and eight random characters). |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:type="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:accordion-item

Attributes of `<sl:accordion-item>`: One section of a `sl:accordion`: a `details` element holding a `sl:accordion-trigger` (first) and a `sl:accordion-content`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `open` | *boolean* | — | Render the section open. In a `single` accordion only the first open section stays open. |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | The section cannot be opened or closed: its trigger is out of the tab order and ignores the pointer, and a closed disabled section renders no content, so find-in-page cannot open it. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:open="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:accordion-trigger

Attributes of `<sl:accordion-trigger>`: The heading of a `sl:accordion-item`, which opens and closes it: the `details` element's `summary`, so it must be the item's first child. Its text is a heading inside the `summary`, and a chevron follows it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` | `1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5`, `6` | `3` | Heading level of the text (`role="heading"` and `aria-level`), as shadcn/ui's trigger sits in an `h3`. Pick the level that fits the page's outline. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:level="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:accordion-content

Attributes of `<sl:accordion-content>`: The content of a `sl:accordion-item`, shown while it is open. Find-in-page searches it while it is closed, and opens the section on a match.

`sl:accordion-content` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Collapsible

> Content that a button shows and hides, on the browser's details element.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/collapsible/

**Example: Collapsible.**

```html
<sl:collapsible>
  <sl:collapsible-trigger>@peduarte starred 3 repositories <sl:icon name="chevrons-up-down"/></sl:collapsible-trigger>
  <sl:collapsible-content>
    <p>@radix-ui/primitives</p>
    <p>@radix-ui/colors</p>
    <p>@stitches/react</p>
  </sl:collapsible-content>
</sl:collapsible>
```

### Built from parts

A collapsible is `<sl:collapsible>` with an `<sl:collapsible-trigger>` and an `<sl:collapsible-content>`:

```html
<sl:collapsible>
  <sl:collapsible-trigger>@peduarte starred 3 repositories <sl:icon name="chevrons-up-down"/></sl:collapsible-trigger>
  <sl:collapsible-content>
    <p>@radix-ui/primitives</p>
    <p>@radix-ui/colors</p>
  </sl:collapsible-content>
</sl:collapsible>
```

It is a [`<details>`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Reference/Elements/details) element with the
trigger as its `<summary>`, so it needs no JavaScript: the browser opens and closes it, from the keyboard too, and
tells a screen reader whether it is expanded. Two consequences for porting a shadcn/ui example:

- **The trigger comes first**, and the content after it, both inside `<sl:collapsible>`. shadcn/ui's examples can put
  the trigger anywhere in the collapsible, even after the content; a `summary` must be the first child.
- **Everything else goes in the trigger or the content.** Text next to the trigger (shadcn's "@peduarte starred 3
  repositories" heading) can be part of the trigger.

`open` renders it open. As with the [accordion](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/accordion.md), find-in-page and a link to an element inside open a
closed collapsible on a match (Chrome and Edge 97, Firefox 148, Safari 26.2). It does not animate, as shadcn/ui's does
not.

### Drawn as a button

The trigger is plain text by default. `variant` or `size` draws it as a [button](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/button.md), with the button's
variants and sizes, where shadcn/ui writes `<CollapsibleTrigger asChild><Button …>`. An icon-only trigger
(`size="icon"`) needs an `aria-label`.

**Example: Drawn as a button.** variant and size draw the trigger as a button, as shadcn/ui's asChild on a Button does.

```html
<sl:collapsible open>
  <sl:collapsible-trigger variant="outline" size="sm"><sl:icon name="sliders-horizontal"/> Filters</sl:collapsible-trigger>
  <sl:collapsible-content>
    <sl:field orientation="horizontal" for="collapsible-in-stock">
      <sl:checkbox checked/>
      <sl:field-label>In stock only</sl:field-label>
    </sl:field>
  </sl:collapsible-content>
</sl:collapsible>
```

### Disabled

`disabled` keeps it closed (or open, with `open`): the trigger fades, leaves the tab order and ignores the pointer, and
a closed disabled collapsible renders no content, so find-in-page cannot open it.

**Example: Disabled.**

```html
<sl:collapsible disabled>
  <sl:collapsible-trigger variant="ghost" size="sm">Order history</sl:collapsible-trigger>
  <sl:collapsible-content>Your past orders.</sl:collapsible-content>
</sl:collapsible>
```

### Left out

- **`asChild` on the trigger**: `variant` and `size` draw it as a button; the element stays a `summary`.
- **`open`/`onOpenChange` state**: `open` renders it open; the browser's `toggle` event reports changes.
- **A trigger outside the collapsible**, or after the content: a `details` element has its `summary` first.
- **Styling the trigger's icon by state** (shadcn's `group-data-[state=open]:rotate-90`): the bundle has no utility
  classes. In your own CSS, `.collapsible[open] > .collapsible-trigger svg { rotate: 90deg; }` does it.

### Attributes

#### sl:collapsible

Attributes of `<sl:collapsible>`: Content that a button shows and hides. Built on the browser's `details` element, so it needs no JavaScript. Holds a `sl:collapsible-trigger` (first) and a `sl:collapsible-content`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `open` | *boolean* | — | Render it open. |
| `disabled` | *boolean* | — | It cannot be opened or closed: its trigger is out of the tab order and ignores the pointer, and closed it renders no content, so find-in-page cannot open it. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:open="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:collapsible-trigger

Attributes of `<sl:collapsible-trigger>`: What the user clicks to open and close a `sl:collapsible`: its `details` element's `summary`, so it must come first. Plain text by default; `variant` or `size` draws it as a `sl:button`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline`, `ghost`, `destructive`, `secondary`, `link` | — | Draw the trigger as a button of this variant, as on `sl:button`. |
| `size` | `xs`, `sm`, `default`, `lg`, `icon`, `icon-xs`, `icon-sm`, `icon-lg` | — | Draw the trigger as a button of this size, as on `sl:button`. The `icon` sizes need an `aria-label`. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended. An `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` is required when `size` is `icon` or `icon-xs` or `icon-sm` or `icon-lg`; rendering fails without one.

#### sl:collapsible-content

Attributes of `<sl:collapsible-content>`: The content of a `sl:collapsible`, shown while it is open. Find-in-page searches it while it is closed, and opens it on a match.

`sl:collapsible-content` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Card

> A container for related content and actions, built from its parts.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/card/

**Example: Card.**

```html
<sl:card>
  <sl:card-header>
    <sl:card-title level="3">Order #1042</sl:card-title>
    <sl:card-description>Placed on 12 September</sl:card-description>
    <sl:card-action><sl:badge variant="secondary">Shipped</sl:badge></sl:card-action>
  </sl:card-header>
  <sl:card-content>
    <p>Three items, delivered to the Antwerp warehouse.</p>
  </sl:card-content>
  <sl:card-footer>
    <sl:button as="a" href="/orders/1042" variant="outline">View order</sl:button>
  </sl:card-footer>
</sl:card>
```

### Built from parts

A card is `<sl:card>` plus the parts you need, as in shadcn/ui: `<sl:card-header>` (holding `<sl:card-title>`,
`<sl:card-description>` and `<sl:card-action>`), `<sl:card-content>` and `<sl:card-footer>`. Use any of them, in any
order. Each part is its own component, so it has its own attributes and IDE completion, and passes every other
attribute through.

A card takes the width of its container; set it from your layout.

**Example: Content only.**

```html
<sl:card>
  <sl:card-content><p>A card needs none of its parts but one.</p></sl:card-content>
</sl:card>
```

### A title that is a heading

`<sl:card-title>` is plain text unless you give it a `level`. With one, it is announced as a heading of that level, so
screen reader users can jump between cards. Pick the level that fits the page: a card under an `<h2>` gets
`level="3"`.

### Small

`size="sm"` tightens the spacing inside the card and between its parts.

**Example: Small.**

```html
<sl:card size="sm">
  <sl:card-header>
    <sl:card-title level="3">Late orders</sl:card-title>
    <sl:card-description>Past their delivery date</sl:card-description>
  </sl:card-header>
  <sl:card-content><p>3</p></sl:card-content>
</sl:card>
```

### Dividing lines

`border` on the header or the footer draws a line between it and the content, with matching spacing. It works without
utility classes, so also with the standalone stylesheet.

**Example: Dividing lines.**

```html
<sl:card>
  <sl:card-header border>
    <sl:card-title level="3">Notifications</sl:card-title>
    <sl:card-description>What we email you about</sl:card-description>
  </sl:card-header>
  <sl:card-content><p>Order updates and weekly summaries.</p></sl:card-content>
  <sl:card-footer border>
    <sl:button>Save</sl:button>
    <sl:button variant="ghost">Cancel</sl:button>
  </sl:card-footer>
</sl:card>
```

### Attributes

#### sl:card

Attributes of `<sl:card>`: A container for related content and actions. Build it from its parts, in any order and number: `sl:card-header` (holding `sl:card-title`, `sl:card-description` and `sl:card-action`), `sl:card-content` and `sl:card-footer`. An `img` as the first or last child runs to the card's edges.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `size` | `default`, `sm` | `default` | Spacing inside the card and between its parts. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:size="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:card-header

Attributes of `<sl:card-header>`: The top of a card: a `sl:card-title`, an optional `sl:card-description` below it, and an optional `sl:card-action` beside both.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `border` | *boolean* | — | A dividing line below the header, with matching spacing. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:border="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:card-title

Attributes of `<sl:card-title>`: The title of a card, inside `sl:card-header`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `level` | `1`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5`, `6` | — | Heading level. With it the title is a heading (`role="heading"` and `aria-level`) that screen reader users can jump to; without it, plain text. Pick the level that fits the page's outline. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:level="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:card-description

Attributes of `<sl:card-description>`: Supporting text below a `sl:card-title`, inside `sl:card-header`.

`sl:card-description` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:card-action

Attributes of `<sl:card-action>`: An action in the top end corner of a card, such as a button or a badge, inside `sl:card-header`.

`sl:card-action` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:card-content

Attributes of `<sl:card-content>`: The body of a card.

`sl:card-content` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:card-footer

Attributes of `<sl:card-footer>`: The bottom of a card, for actions or a summary.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `border` | *boolean* | — | A dividing line above the footer, with matching spacing. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:border="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Attachment

> A file or an image attached to a message, a form or a record, with its name, its size or state, and actions.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/attachment/

**Example: Attachment.**

```html
<sl:attachment>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-text"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>sales-dashboard.pdf</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>PDF · 2.4 MB</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove sales-dashboard.pdf"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
```

### Built from parts

An attachment is `<sl:attachment>` plus the parts you need, as in shadcn/ui: `<sl:attachment-media>` (an icon or an
image), `<sl:attachment-content>` (holding `<sl:attachment-title>` and `<sl:attachment-description>`),
`<sl:attachment-actions>` with one or more `<sl:attachment-action>`s, and an optional `<sl:attachment-trigger>` that
makes the whole card clickable. `<sl:attachment-group>` lays several out in a row or a grid.

The attachment only shows a file; it uploads nothing. Use it for files that are already on the server, such as the
documents on a customer's page, and for the list of a file upload, where your page or your script sets each one's
`state`.

An `<sl:attachment-action>` is an [`<sl:button>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/button.md), ghost and an extra small icon by default. It holds
only an icon, so give it an `aria-label` that names the file: "Remove report.pdf", not just "Remove", because a page
can show many.

A title or description that does not fit is cut off with an ellipsis. Without `full-width` an attachment is as wide
as its content; with it, it fills its container, for a list of attachments.

### Image

`<sl:attachment-media variant="image">` crops an `<img>` to fill the media's square. `orientation="vertical"` puts the
media above the content. The title already names the file, so the image can have an empty `alt`.

**Example: Image.** variant="image" crops the image to the media's square; orientation="vertical" puts it above the content. The trigger opens the file.

```html
<sl:attachment-group>
  <sl:attachment orientation="vertical" th:each="photo : ${photos}">
    <sl:attachment-media variant="image">
      <img th:src="@{/files/{id}/thumbnail(id=${photo.id})}" alt="" loading="lazy">
    </sl:attachment-media>
    <sl:attachment-content>
      <sl:attachment-title>[[${photo.name}]]</sl:attachment-title>
      <sl:attachment-description>[[${photo.meta}]]</sl:attachment-description>
    </sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-actions>
      <sl:attachment-action th:aria-label="|Remove ${photo.name}|"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
    </sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-trigger as="a" th:href="@{/files/{id}(id=${photo.id})}" target="_blank"
                           th:aria-label="|Open ${photo.name}|"/>
  </sl:attachment>
</sl:attachment-group>
```

The image is your own `<img>`, so it works under a strict Content Security Policy as long as it comes from your own
server: point `th:src` at a thumbnail URL, such as `@{/files/{id}/thumbnail(id=${file.id})}`. A `data:` URL would need
`data:` in `img-src`, which a strict policy leaves out.

### States

`state` says where a file is in its upload:

- `idle`: picked, not sent yet. A dashed border.
- `uploading` and `processing`: the title shimmers (not for users who ask for reduced motion) and an image is dimmed.
  The attachment is marked busy (`aria-busy="true"`).
- `error`: a destructive border, media and description.
- `done`, the default: the file is there.

**Example: States.** idle draws a dashed border, uploading and processing shimmer the title, error turns destructive. The description says the state too.

```html
<sl:attachment state="idle" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="clock"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>selected-file.pdf</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>Ready to upload</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove selected-file.pdf"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
<sl:attachment state="uploading" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:spinner aria-hidden="true"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>design-system.zip</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>Uploading · 64%</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Cancel upload of design-system.zip"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
<sl:attachment state="processing" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-text"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>market-research.pdf</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>Processing document</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove market-research.pdf"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
<sl:attachment state="error" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-warning"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>financial-model.xlsx</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>Upload failed. Try again.</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Retry upload of financial-model.xlsx"><sl:icon name="refresh-cw"/></sl:attachment-action>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove financial-model.xlsx"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
<sl:attachment state="done" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="check"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>uploaded-report.pdf</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>Uploaded · 1.8 MB</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove uploaded-report.pdf"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
```

The state is not announced by itself. Say it in the description as well ("Uploading · 64%", "Upload failed. Try
again."), so it is not told by colour alone and a screen reader reads it with the file. The attachment is no live
region and has no `role="alert"`: a page that renders a failed upload would announce it on every load. When a script
changes the state, it should announce the change itself, once, in one polite live region for the whole list.

An [`<sl:spinner>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/spinner.md) in the media shows that a file is uploading. The description already says so: give
the spinner `aria-hidden="true"`, so "Loading" is not read too. There is no progress bar; show the percentage in the
description.

### Sizes

`size` is `default`, `sm` or `xs`.

**Example: Sizes.**

```html
<sl:attachment full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-text"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>Default attachment</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>PDF · 2.4 MB</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
</sl:attachment>
<sl:attachment size="sm" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-text"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>Small attachment</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>PDF · 2.4 MB</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
</sl:attachment>
<sl:attachment size="xs" full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-text"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>Extra small attachment</sl:attachment-title>
  </sl:attachment-content>
</sl:attachment>
```

### Group

`<sl:attachment-group>` lays attachments out in a row that scrolls sideways when they do not fit, snapping to each
attachment, its edges fading out. The scrollbar is hidden, as in shadcn: the fade shows there is more.

**Example: Group.** A row that scrolls sideways when the attachments do not fit, snapping to each one. Tab reaches the ones off screen through their actions.

```html
<sl:attachment-group>
  <sl:attachment>
    <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-text"/></sl:attachment-media>
    <sl:attachment-content>
      <sl:attachment-title>briefing-notes.pdf</sl:attachment-title>
      <sl:attachment-description>PDF · 1.4 MB</sl:attachment-description>
    </sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-actions>
      <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove briefing-notes.pdf"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
    </sl:attachment-actions>
  </sl:attachment>
  <sl:attachment>
    <sl:attachment-media variant="image">
      <img th:src="@{/files/{id}/thumbnail(id=${photo.id})}" alt="" loading="lazy">
    </sl:attachment-media>
    <sl:attachment-content>
      <sl:attachment-title>dunes.png</sl:attachment-title>
      <sl:attachment-description>PNG · 820 KB</sl:attachment-description>
    </sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-actions>
      <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove dunes.png"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
    </sl:attachment-actions>
  </sl:attachment>
  <sl:attachment>
    <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="table"/></sl:attachment-media>
    <sl:attachment-content>
      <sl:attachment-title>customers.csv</sl:attachment-title>
      <sl:attachment-description>CSV · 18 KB</sl:attachment-description>
    </sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-actions>
      <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove customers.csv"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
    </sl:attachment-actions>
  </sl:attachment>
  <sl:attachment>
    <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-code"/></sl:attachment-media>
    <sl:attachment-content>
      <sl:attachment-title>renderer.tsx</sl:attachment-title>
      <sl:attachment-description>TSX · 12 KB</sl:attachment-description>
    </sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-actions>
      <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove renderer.tsx"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
    </sl:attachment-actions>
  </sl:attachment>
</sl:attachment-group>
```

Keyboard users reach attachments off screen by tabbing to their actions or triggers. A row of attachments without any
has nothing to tab to: give the group a `label`. It is then a named group (`role="group"`) that takes the focus, and
the arrow keys scroll it.

**Example: Group without actions.** Attachments without actions or a trigger give the keyboard nothing to tab to. label makes the group a named group that takes the focus, so the arrow keys scroll it.

```html
<sl:attachment-group label="Reference photos">
  <sl:attachment orientation="vertical" th:each="photo : ${photos}">
    <sl:attachment-media variant="image">
      <img th:src="@{/files/{id}/thumbnail(id=${photo.id})}" th:alt="${photo.description}" loading="lazy">
    </sl:attachment-media>
  </sl:attachment>
</sl:attachment-group>
```

### Grid

`layout="grid"` wraps the attachments instead, in as many columns as fit the width, for a gallery of photos. It does
not scroll, so nothing is hidden off screen and the edges do not fade. Give each attachment `orientation="vertical"`
and `full-width`, so it fills its cell: the image is cropped to a square and the actions sit in its corner. The width
of a cell and the gap between them come from the skin.

**Example: Grid.** layout="grid" wraps the attachments in columns that fill the width, for a gallery of photos. Each vertical attachment with full-width fills its cell.

```html
<sl:attachment-group layout="grid" label="Inspection photos">
  <sl:attachment orientation="vertical" full-width th:each="photo : ${photos}">
    <sl:attachment-media variant="image">
      <img th:src="@{/files/{id}/thumbnail(id=${photo.id})}" alt="" loading="lazy">
    </sl:attachment-media>
    <sl:attachment-content>
      <sl:attachment-title>[[${photo.name}]]</sl:attachment-title>
      <sl:attachment-description>[[${photo.meta}]]</sl:attachment-description>
    </sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-actions>
      <sl:attachment-action th:aria-label="|Remove ${photo.name}|"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
    </sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-trigger as="a" th:href="@{/files/{id}(id=${photo.id})}" target="_blank"
                           th:aria-label="|Open ${photo.name}|"/>
  </sl:attachment>
</sl:attachment-group>
```

A grid's `label` still makes it a named group, but the group takes no focus: there is nothing to scroll. Tab goes
from one attachment's actions and trigger to the next.

### Trigger

`<sl:attachment-trigger>` covers the whole card, behind its actions, so a click anywhere but on an action activates
it. It is a button, to open an [`<sl:dialog>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/dialog.md) with `commandfor` and `command="show-modal"`, or with
`as="a"` a link that opens the file. It has no text of its own: give it an `aria-label` that says what it does. Put
it last in the attachment, so Tab reaches the actions first; its focus ring is drawn around the whole card.

**Example: Trigger.** The trigger covers the card and opens a dialog with commandfor; the actions above it stay clickable.

```html
<sl:attachment full-width>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-search"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>research-summary.pdf</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>Open preview dialog</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Copy link to research-summary.pdf"><sl:icon name="copy"/></sl:attachment-action>
    <sl:attachment-action aria-label="Remove research-summary.pdf"><sl:icon name="x"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
  <sl:attachment-trigger commandfor="research-summary-preview" command="show-modal"
                         aria-label="Preview research-summary.pdf"/>
</sl:attachment>
<sl:dialog id="research-summary-preview">
  <sl:dialog-header>
    <sl:dialog-title>research-summary.pdf</sl:dialog-title>
    <sl:dialog-description>The attachment trigger fills the card and opens the dialog, while the actions stay
      independently clickable above it.</sl:dialog-description>
  </sl:dialog-header>
</sl:dialog>
```

### Download

An action can be a link (`as="a"`) and take any of the button's variants and sizes, such as a download button in a
message thread.

On a page with `hx-boost`, add `hx-boost="false"` to a download link: htmx boosts every link to your own server
without a `target`, `download` or not, and would put the file's bytes into the page instead of saving it. A trigger
that opens the file in a new tab (`target="_blank"`) is left alone.

**Example: Download.** An action can be a link (as="a") in another variant and size, as in a message thread.

```html
<sl:attachment>
  <sl:attachment-media><sl:icon name="file-text"/></sl:attachment-media>
  <sl:attachment-content>
    <sl:attachment-title>sales-dashboard.pdf</sl:attachment-title>
    <sl:attachment-description>PDF · 2.4 MB</sl:attachment-description>
  </sl:attachment-content>
  <sl:attachment-actions>
    <sl:attachment-action as="a" href="/files/sales-dashboard.pdf" download variant="secondary" size="icon-sm"
                          aria-label="Download sales-dashboard.pdf"><sl:icon name="download"/></sl:attachment-action>
  </sl:attachment-actions>
</sl:attachment>
```

### Without content

An attachment can be only its media, as an image in a chat message. Then the image's `alt` names it.

**Example: Without content.** An attachment can be only its media, as an image in a chat message. The image's alt is its name then.

```html
<sl:attachment orientation="vertical">
  <sl:attachment-media variant="image">
    <img th:src="@{/files/{id}/thumbnail(id=${photo.id})}" alt="A mountain lake">
  </sl:attachment-media>
</sl:attachment>
```

### Attributes

#### sl:attachment

Attributes of `<sl:attachment>`: A file or an image attached to something: a message, a form, a record. Holds an `sl:attachment-media`, an `sl:attachment-content` (title and description), optional `sl:attachment-actions` and an optional `sl:attachment-trigger` that makes the whole card open a link or a dialog. Purely presentational: the server renders its state. Say the state in the description too ("Uploading · 64%", "Upload failed"), so it is not told by colour alone.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `state` | `idle`, `uploading`, `processing`, `error`, `done` | `done` | Where the file is in its upload: `idle` (picked, not sent yet) draws a dashed border, `uploading` and `processing` shimmer the title and mark the attachment busy (`aria-busy`), `error` turns it destructive. `done` is a file that is there. |
| `size` | `default`, `sm`, `xs` | `default` | Size of the attachment. |
| `orientation` | `horizontal`, `vertical` | `horizontal` | `vertical` puts the media above the content, for an image. |
| `full-width` | *boolean* | — | Fill the width of the container, for a list of attachments (shadcn's `w-full` class). Without it the attachment is as wide as its content. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:state="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:attachment-media

Attributes of `<sl:attachment-media>`: The icon or image of an `sl:attachment`: an icon (or an `sl:spinner` while it uploads) on a muted square, or an `&lt;img&gt;` that fills it.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `icon`, `image` | `icon` | `icon` centres an icon on a muted square; `image` crops an `&lt;img&gt;` to fill the square, dimmed while the attachment uploads, is processed or failed. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:attachment-content

Attributes of `<sl:attachment-content>`: The text of an `sl:attachment`: its `sl:attachment-title` and `sl:attachment-description`. Takes the space the media and the actions leave.

`sl:attachment-content` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:attachment-title

Attributes of `<sl:attachment-title>`: The name of an attachment, usually its file name, in `sl:attachment-content`. Cut off with an ellipsis when it does not fit; shimmers while the attachment uploads or is processed, unless the user asks for reduced motion.

`sl:attachment-title` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:attachment-description

Attributes of `<sl:attachment-description>`: What else to know about an attachment, below its title: the file type and size, or its state ("Uploading · 64%", "Upload failed. Try again."). Cut off with an ellipsis when it does not fit.

`sl:attachment-description` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:attachment-actions

Attributes of `<sl:attachment-actions>`: The `sl:attachment-action`s of an attachment, at its end; in a vertical attachment, over the top corner of the media. They sit above an `sl:attachment-trigger`, so they stay clickable.

`sl:attachment-actions` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:attachment-action

Attributes of `<sl:attachment-action>`: A button in `sl:attachment-actions`, to remove, retry or download the file: a `sl:button`, ghost and an extra small icon by default. Give it an `aria-label` that names the file ("Remove report.pdf"). Any other attribute passes through to the `sl:button` (`href`, `type`, `disabled`, `hx-*`).

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `outline`, `ghost`, `destructive`, `secondary`, `link` | `ghost` | Visual style of the button, as on `sl:button`. |
| `size` | `xs`, `sm`, `default`, `lg`, `icon`, `icon-xs`, `icon-sm`, `icon-lg` | `icon-xs` | Size of the button, as on `sl:button`. The `icon` sizes are square, for a button that holds only an icon, and need an `aria-label`. |
| `as` | `button`, `a` | `button` | Element to render: a `button`, or an anchor (`a`) to download or open the file. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended. An `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` is required when `size` is `icon` or `icon-xs` or `icon-sm` or `icon-lg`; rendering fails without one.

#### sl:attachment-trigger

Attributes of `<sl:attachment-trigger>`: Makes a whole `sl:attachment` clickable: a button (to open an `sl:dialog` with `commandfor` and `command="show-modal"`) or, with `as="a"`, a link that covers the card behind its actions. It has no text of its own, so it needs an `aria-label` that says what it does ("Open report.pdf"). Put it last in the attachment.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `as` | `button`, `a` | `button` | Element to render: a `button`, or an anchor (`a`) to open the file. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:as="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended. An `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` is required; rendering fails without one.

#### sl:attachment-group

Attributes of `<sl:attachment-group>`: A row of `sl:attachment`s that scrolls sideways when they do not fit, snapping to each attachment, its edges fading out, or a grid that wraps them. Keyboard users reach attachments off screen by tabbing to their actions or triggers; for a row of attachments without any, give the group a `label`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `layout` | `row`, `grid` | `row` | `row` scrolls sideways; `grid` wraps the attachments in columns that fill the width, for a gallery of photos: vertical attachments with `full-width`, each filling its cell. |
| `label` | *string* | — | Accessible name of the group. With it the group is a named group (`role="group"`). A row then takes keyboard focus, so the arrow keys scroll it: for a row of attachments that have no actions or trigger. A grid does not scroll, so it takes no focus. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:layout="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Badge

> A small label for a status, a count or a category.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/badge/

**Example: Badge.**

```html
<sl:badge>New</sl:badge>
```

### Variants

The same variants as the [button](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/button.md).

**Example: Variants.**

```html
<sl:badge>Default</sl:badge>
<sl:badge variant="secondary">Secondary</sl:badge>
<sl:badge variant="destructive">Destructive</sl:badge>
<sl:badge variant="outline">Outline</sl:badge>
<sl:badge variant="ghost">Ghost</sl:badge>
<sl:badge variant="link">Link</sl:badge>
```

### With an icon

Put an [`<sl:icon>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/icon.md) in the `icon-start` or `icon-end` slot. It takes the badge's colour and a 12 pixel size.

**Example: With an icon.**

```html
<sl:badge variant="secondary">
  <sl:slot name="icon-start"><sl:icon name="badge-check"/></sl:slot>
  Verified
</sl:badge>
<sl:badge variant="outline">
  Details
  <sl:slot name="icon-end"><sl:icon name="arrow-right"/></sl:slot>
</sl:badge>
```

### As a link

`as="a"` renders an anchor, for a badge that filters a list or opens a detail page. Only a linked badge reacts to hover
and shows a focus ring; a plain badge is not interactive, so it does not pretend to be.

**Example: As a link.** Only a linked badge changes on hover and takes keyboard focus.

```html
<sl:badge as="a" href="/orders?status=open">Open</sl:badge>
<sl:badge as="a" href="/orders?status=late" variant="destructive">Late</sl:badge>
<sl:badge as="a" href="/orders" variant="outline">
  All orders
  <sl:slot name="icon-end"><sl:icon name="arrow-up-right"/></sl:slot>
</sl:badge>
```

A badge is text, so a screen reader reads it where it stands. When a bare number or a single word would not make sense
out of context, write the context into the text, or next to the badge.

### Props from expressions

**Example: Props from an expression.**

```html
<sl:badge th:variant="${order.late ? 'destructive' : 'secondary'}"
          th:text="${order.status}">Open</sl:badge>
```

### Everything else passes through

**Example: Pass-through attributes.** Anything that is not a prop lands on the rendered element; class is appended.

```html
<sl:badge variant="outline" class="app-wide" id="order-status" data-testid="status">Shipped</sl:badge>
```

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:badge>`: A small label for a status, a count or a category; with `as="a"`, a link that looks the same. Every attribute that is not a prop passes through; `class` is appended to `badge`.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `default`, `secondary`, `destructive`, `outline`, `ghost`, `link` | `default` | Visual style of the badge. |
| `as` | `span`, `a` | `span` | Element to render: a `span`, or an anchor (`a`) for a badge that links somewhere. Only a linked badge reacts to hover. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:variant="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Avatar

> A picture of a person, with initials when there is no picture.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/avatar/

**Example: Avatar.**

```html
<sl:avatar label="shadcn">
  <sl:avatar-image th:src="@{/users/{id}/photo(id=${user.id})}"/>
  <sl:avatar-fallback>CN</sl:avatar-fallback>
</sl:avatar>
```

### Image and fallback

Write both parts, as in shadcn/ui: `<sl:avatar-image>` with the picture's `src` or `th:src`, and
`<sl:avatar-fallback>` with what to show without one, usually initials or an [`<sl:icon>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/icon.md). The avatar shows
the fallback when the image has no `src` or an empty one. A `th:src` whose value is null renders an empty `src`, so a
user without a photo needs no `th:if`:

**Example: Fallback.** A user without a photo gets their initials; th:src with a null value renders an empty src, which shows the fallback.

```html
<sl:avatar label="Jane Doe">
  <sl:avatar-image th:src="${user.photoUrl}"/>
  <sl:avatar-fallback>JD</sl:avatar-fallback>
</sl:avatar>
<sl:avatar label="Unknown user">
  <sl:avatar-fallback><sl:icon name="user"/></sl:avatar-fallback>
</sl:avatar>
```

Both parts are always rendered, and the stylesheet decides which one shows. The hidden one is also hidden from
assistive technology. The image lies over the fallback at the same size, so nothing moves when one replaces the other.

### A photo that fails to load

A photo URL that fails to load shows the fallback too: a 404, a URL your Content-Security-Policy's `img-src` blocks, a
file that is no image, or no network. So does a photo that is still loading, as in shadcn/ui; the picture replaces the
fallback once it is there.

**Example: Broken image.** A photo URL that fails to load (a 404, a URL the Content-Security-Policy blocks, offline) shows the fallback, as does one that is still loading.

```html
<sl:avatar label="Grace Hopper">
  <sl:avatar-image th:src="@{/users/{id}/photo(id=${user.id})}"/>
  <sl:avatar-fallback>GH</sl:avatar-fallback>
</sl:avatar>
```

Shadleaf's script marks each avatar image with `data-status="loaded"` when its picture has loaded, or
`data-status="error"`, and the stylesheet only shows an image that is marked loaded. So a broken image never shows, not
even for a moment before the script runs. The script also marks images htmx swaps in, and an image whose `src` changes
later (htmx swapping the attribute, or a script of yours): the fallback shows while the new picture loads.

Your own CSS can use the mark, for instance to fade the picture in:

```css
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference) {
  .avatar-image[data-status='loaded'] {
    animation: avatar-fade-in 150ms;
  }
}

@keyframes avatar-fade-in {
  from { opacity: 0; }
}
```

#### Without the script

- **Without JavaScript**, nothing marks the images, so they lie over the fallback as plain images: a picture that loads
  covers it. One that fails shows the fallback through it in Firefox, which draws nothing for a broken image with an
  empty `alt`; Chromium draws its broken-image icon over it.
- **With JavaScript but without Shadleaf's script** (your policy blocks it, say), every avatar shows its fallback.

The script is part of the one in the `assets` fragment, whatever `shadleaf.assets.alpine` is: it is not an Alpine
component, and works without Alpine on the page. One listener on the document handles every avatar, so a long list of
them costs nothing extra.

### Sizes

**Example: Sizes.**

```html
<sl:avatar size="sm" label="Jane Doe"><sl:avatar-fallback>JD</sl:avatar-fallback></sl:avatar>
<sl:avatar label="Jane Doe"><sl:avatar-fallback>JD</sl:avatar-fallback></sl:avatar>
<sl:avatar size="lg" label="Jane Doe"><sl:avatar-fallback>JD</sl:avatar-fallback></sl:avatar>
```

### Naming the avatar

An avatar usually sits next to the person's name, so it is decorative by default and hidden from assistive technology.
When there is no name next to it, give it a `label`: it is then announced as an image with that name, whichever part
shows. `aria-labelledby`, pointing at a name elsewhere on the page, works too.

**Example: Next to a name.** Without a label the avatar is decorative; the name beside it is what a screen reader reads.

```html
<sl:avatar><sl:avatar-fallback>JD</sl:avatar-fallback></sl:avatar>
<span>Jane Doe</span>
```

### Left out

- **A delay before the fallback shows** (Radix's `delayMs`): the fallback shows at once while a photo loads. A photo
  from the browser's cache is there before the first paint, so it does not flash.
- **A loading status event** (Radix's `onLoadingStatusChange`): read `data-status` on the image instead.

### Attributes

#### sl:avatar

Attributes of `<sl:avatar>`: A picture of a person: an `sl:avatar-image`, and an `sl:avatar-fallback` (initials) shown instead while the image has no `src`, is loading or fails to load. Decorative unless it has a `label`, as an avatar usually sits next to the person's name.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `size` | `sm`, `default`, `lg` | `default` | Size of the avatar. |
| `label` | *string* | — | The person's name, for an avatar with no name next to it. It is then announced as an image with that name; without it the avatar is hidden from assistive technology. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:size="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

#### sl:avatar-image

Attributes of `<sl:avatar-image>`: The picture inside `sl:avatar`. Pass `src` or `th:src`; when it is empty or null, while the picture loads, and when it fails to load (marked by Shadleaf's script), the image is hidden and the `sl:avatar-fallback` shows instead. The avatar's `label` names it, so the image itself has an empty `alt`.

`sl:avatar-image` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:avatar-fallback

Attributes of `<sl:avatar-fallback>`: What `sl:avatar` shows when its image has no `src`, while it loads and when it fails to load: usually the person's initials, or an `sl:icon`.

`sl:avatar-fallback` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Icon

> Inline SVG icons from lucide, or your own.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/icon/

`<sl:icon>` inlines the SVG of a [lucide](https://lucide.dev) icon into the page: no sprite, no extra request, and it
works inside htmx swaps. It draws in `currentColor`, so it takes the colour of the text around it. An unknown name
fails with the closest known ones.

**Example: Icon.**

```html
<sl:icon name="leaf"/>
<sl:icon name="trash"/>
<sl:icon name="settings"/>
```

### Size and stroke width

**Example: Sizes.**

```html
<sl:icon name="leaf" size="16"/>
<sl:icon name="leaf"/>
<sl:icon name="leaf" size="32"/>
```
**Example: Stroke width.**

```html
<sl:icon name="leaf" stroke-width="1"/>
<sl:icon name="leaf"/>
<sl:icon name="leaf" stroke-width="3"/>
```

### Decorative or meaningful

Without a `label` an icon is decorative: it renders `aria-hidden="true"`, which is right next to text or inside a
labelled button. With a `label` it is announced as an image with that name.

**Example: A meaningful icon.** With a label the icon is announced as an image; without one it is hidden from assistive technology.

```html
<sl:icon name="circle-alert" label="Warning"/>
```

### Your own icons

Register an `IconSource` bean. Sources are asked in `@Order`, before the bundled lucide icons, so your icons can also
replace lucide's:

```java
@Bean
IconSource applicationIcons() {
  // reads src/main/resources/icons/<name>.svg
  return IconSource.classpathDirectory("icons/");
}
```

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:icon>`: Inlines the SVG of an icon: the bundled lucide icons, or the application's own through an `IconSource` bean. An unknown name fails with the closest known ones.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `name` | *string* | — | Name of the icon, e.g. `trash`. Lucide's names and aliases, plus any icons the application registers. |
| `size` | *number* | — | Width and height in pixels. Without it the icon is 24 pixels, or sized by the surrounding component (16 pixels inside a button). |
| `stroke-width` | *number* | — | Stroke width, overriding the icon's own (2 for lucide). |
| `label` | *string* | — | Accessible name for an icon that carries meaning on its own. Without it the icon is decorative and hidden from assistive technology. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:name="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.

---

## Kbd

> A key on the keyboard, or a combination of keys.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/kbd/

**Example: Kbd.**

```html
<sl:kbd>Esc</sl:kbd>
<sl:kbd>Enter</sl:kbd>
<sl:kbd>/</sl:kbd>
```

`<sl:kbd>` renders HTML's own `<kbd>` element, so it fits in running text.

**Example: In a sentence.**

```html
<p>Press <sl:kbd>/</sl:kbd> to search.</p>
```

### Key combinations

Put the keys pressed together in `<sl:kbd-group>`. It is an outer `<kbd>`, which is how HTML marks up a combination.
Text between the keys, such as `+`, is up to you.

**Example: A key combination.**

```html
<sl:kbd-group>
  <sl:kbd>Ctrl</sl:kbd>
  <sl:kbd>Shift</sl:kbd>
  <sl:kbd>P</sl:kbd>
</sl:kbd-group>
<sl:kbd-group><sl:kbd>Ctrl</sl:kbd>+<sl:kbd>K</sl:kbd></sl:kbd-group>
```

### Icons

A key can hold an [`<sl:icon>`](https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/icon.md). Give the icon a `label`, as a screen reader has no text to read otherwise.
An `aria-label` on the key itself would not do: `<kbd>` has no role that takes a name.

Lucide has icons for the usual keys, so you never need to type a symbol: `command`, `option`, `arrow-big-up` (Shift),
`corner-down-left` (Enter), `delete` (Backspace), `arrow-up` / `arrow-down` / `arrow-left` / `arrow-right`, and
`space`.

**Example: With an icon.** An icon on its own has no text, so give the key a name.

```html
<sl:kbd-group>
  <sl:kbd><sl:icon name="command" label="Command"/></sl:kbd>
  <sl:kbd>K</sl:kbd>
</sl:kbd-group>
```

### In a button

A key can sit next to a button's label, to show its shortcut. Hide the key from screen readers with
`aria-hidden="true"`, or it becomes part of the button's name ("Accept Enter"), and name the shortcut in
`aria-keyshortcuts` on the button instead. An icon in a hidden key needs no `label`. `aria-keyshortcuts` only
announces the shortcut: listening for the key is up to your own script.

**Example: In a button.** The shortcut goes in aria-keyshortcuts; the key itself is hidden from screen readers.

```html
<sl:button aria-keyshortcuts="Enter">
  Accept <sl:kbd aria-hidden="true"><sl:icon name="corner-down-left"/></sl:kbd>
</sl:button>
<sl:button variant="outline" aria-keyshortcuts="Escape">
  Cancel <sl:kbd aria-hidden="true">Esc</sl:kbd>
</sl:button>
```

### Attributes

#### sl:kbd

Attributes of `<sl:kbd>`: A key on the keyboard, as text (`Ctrl`) or an `sl:icon`. Several keys pressed together go in an `sl:kbd-group`.

`sl:kbd` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

#### sl:kbd-group

Attributes of `<sl:kbd-group>`: A key combination: `sl:kbd` elements, with any text between them (such as `+`). HTML nests the keys of a combination in an outer `kbd`, as this does.

`sl:kbd-group` declares no props; every attribute passes through.

---

## Separator

> A thin line between content.

Source: https://shadleaf.dev/current/components/separator/

**Example: Separator.**

```html
<sl:separator/>
```

### Vertical

`orientation="vertical"` draws a vertical line that stretches to the height of the row it is in. Put it in a flex
container, such as a toolbar or a row of links.

**Example: Vertical.** A vertical separator stretches to the height of the flex row it is in.

```html
<sl:button as="a" href="/blog" variant="link">Blog</sl:button>
<sl:separator orientation="vertical"/>
<sl:button as="a" href="/docs" variant="link">Docs</sl:button>
<sl:separator orientation="vertical"/>
<sl:button as="a" href="/source" variant="link">Source</sl:button>
```

### Decorative or announced

A separator is decorative by default, as in shadcn/ui: it only draws a line and is hidden from assistive technology
(`role="none"`). When the line divides the content into groups a screen reader user should know about, write
`decorative="false"`. It is then announced as a separator (`role="separator"`, with `aria-orientation` when
vertical).

**Example: Announced.** decorative="false" makes it a separator for assistive technology too.

```html
<sl:separator decorative="false"/>
```

### Attributes

Attributes of `<sl:separator>`: A thin line between content. A vertical one stretches to the height of the row it is in, so it needs a flex container.

| Attribute | Values | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `orientation` | `horizontal`, `vertical` | `horizontal` | Direction of the line. |
| `decorative` | *boolean* | `true` | Hide the line from assistive technology, as most separators only draw a line. With `decorative="false"` it is announced as a separator, for one that divides content into groups. |

Every prop can also be set from an expression: `th:orientation="${...}"`. Any other attribute passes through to the rendered element, and `class` is appended.
